# Case 4: first normalizations for axisymmetric Navier-Stokes singularities with bounded circulation

Status: UNRESOLVED_NOT_ACCEPTED

Final outcome. Neither endpoint of the acceptance gate was reached. No complete theorem is proved (neither GOAL for every
hypothetical singular trajectory nor a direct exclusion of the no-extraction alternative), and no fixed-datum counterexample is
constructed. GOAL is proved, with a rule defined from records, in regime (I) ($\limsup D/M^2>0$). In regime (II) ($D/M^2\to0$,
equivalently $S/M\to0$) the first missing implication is the exclusion of the residual alternative (C) of Theorem 5, or, in the
weaker topology of Section 6A, of the alternative (C*) of Corollary 13, which is contained in (C). (C*) is the disjoint union of the
flat alternative (FLAT$_0$) (historical BMO norm $o(M)$) and of (C*)$\cap$(HD) (a nonzero Reynolds defect), and neither part is
excluded. For (FLAT$_0$), Section 12 splits the late velocity first crossings into a near-axis branch and a toroidal branch, the
latter with two cases; the two estimates (a) and (b) stated under How to resume below would together exclude (FLAT$_0$), and neither
is established. On the route pursued furthest the remaining gap is estimate (a), for the second case of the toroidal branch: an upper
bound $2^{o(M/\theta_b)}M^2/\|\omega^\theta(t)\|_\infty$, along late velocity first crossings and for a fixed $s_0$, for the
dual-weighted swirl source or for the signed dual-weighted compression work of the Remark after Corollary 58. The bounds proved there
are size bounds and do not give this; in particular no bound of the required size is proved for the contribution near the axis.
Excluding (FLAT$_0$) would still leave (C*)$\cap$(HD). The precise statement of the first missing implication is at the end of the
Overview.

Run record. This round of work on the case started at T0 = 2026-10-17T19:11:13Z and ended at T_end = 2026-10-19T19:15:42Z (UTC, system
clock), an elapsed time of 48 h 4 min, spent in consecutive work sessions of about 47.7 hours in total with restarts of under one
minute between them. The prompt's minimum of 24 hours of documented research on this case was met in this round alone. An earlier
round (2026-10-15T23:24Z to 2026-10-16T21:11Z) produced the previously published version of this report, with the same status.

What this round added. The previously published version contained the Setting, Sections 1 to 10, the Limitations and the
Verification, and stated the same first missing implication (the exclusion of (C), or of (C*) in the weaker topology). This round
added the Overview and two new sections. Section 11 (Lemmas 27 to 40A) gives sufficient conditions for GOAL at velocity first
crossings in regime (II), namely a bound $O(M_n^2/D_n)$ on the local meridional vorticity mass, on the swirl source or on the signed
compression work (Lemma 31, Theorem 32), and matching lower bounds showing that under (C) these quantities, divided by $M_n^2/D_n$,
tend to infinity, and that under (FLAT$_0$) the normalized work grows at least like $2^{cM_n/\Theta_0}$ (Propositions 30 and 33); it
also splits the work into a bounded part inherited from a fixed earlier time and a part generated by the swirl source (Proposition
39), introduces the dual representation of Lemmas 40 and 40A, and proves model bounds and counterexamples for prescribed drifts.
Section 12 (Proposition 41 to Corollary 58) proves $H'\le\frac92\|u^\theta\|_\infty^2H$ for the coupled vorticity energy $H$ of Chen,
Fang and Zhang and $\limsup_{t\uparrow T_*}(T_*-t)^{1/2}\|u^\theta(t)\|_\infty\ge3^{-1/2}$ (Proposition 41, and Corollary 42 with the continuation criterion of Chen,
Fang and Zhang), and reduces (FLAT$_0$) to the near-axis and toroidal branches described above: a conditional exclusion on the near-axis branch (Corollary
52(d)), an exponential lower bound in each branch (Corollary 54), and the reduction of the second toroidal case to a dual-weighted
bound (Corollary 58), with the obstructions recorded in the Remarks after it. The one regularity input granted in the earlier version
(a quantitative $\epsilon$-regularity step in Proposition 14B(a)) is no longer needed, since Proposition 14B(a) is now proved
without it, and new items were added to the Limitations (among them
further approaches that were tried and fail) together with the How to resume paragraph of the Overview. Every addition was checked
by hand and, except where the Verification section records otherwise, refereed by an independent second model; the
extent of each check is described in the Verification section.

## Overview

This overview states the outcome in brief; the precise statements, hypotheses and proofs are in the sections cited.

Setting and goal. $u$ is the maximal smooth solution of the Navier-Stokes equations on $\mathbb R^3$ (unit viscosity) with
smooth, axisymmetric, divergence-free data $u_0\in\bigcap_kH^k$. In cylindrical coordinates $(r,\theta,z)$ about the
symmetry axis write $u=u^re_r+u^\theta e_\theta+u^ze_z$; $b=u^re_r+u^ze_z$ is the meridional velocity, $\omega^r$ and
$\omega^\theta$ are the radial and azimuthal components of the vorticity, $\Gamma=ru^\theta$ is the circulation and
$\eta=\omega^\theta/r$. The data have energy $E_0=\|u_0\|_2^2$ and circulation bound
$G=\|ru_0^\theta\|_\infty<\infty$, and $u$ blows up at a first time $T_*<\infty$; $M$, $D$ and $S$ are the historical
maxima of $\|u\|_\infty$, $\|\nabla u\|_\infty$ and $\|u^\theta\|_\infty$. GOAL asks for a rule, defined from records or
from specified observables of the trajectory, that selects rescalings of $u$ converging strongly (including the terminal
time) to a bounded, spatially nonconstant ancient solution of a rigorously derived limit equation with the correct
pressure class. Below, as in the body, "GOAL holds for a normalization" means that these analytic requirements hold for
it; whether the normalization is also given by such a rule is stated separately. All convergence statements below are
local and hold after passage to a subsequence, in the topology of the cited result.

What is proved.
1. Regime split (Theorem 3). Either $\limsup D/M^2>0$ (regime (I)) or $D/M^2\to0$ (regime (II)), and (II) holds if
   and only if $S/M\to0$ (the converse direction uses the Liouville theorems of Koch, Nadirashvili, Seregin and
   Sverak for swirl-free and planar ancient solutions).
2. Regime (I) (Theorem 2). GOAL holds, with a rule defined from records: at first crossings of levels of $\|\nabla u\|_\infty$ (the first
   time at which $\|\nabla u\|_\infty$ reaches a given level, so that $\|\nabla u\|_\infty=D$ there) with $D\ge cM^2$ for a fixed $c>0$, centred at points where $|\nabla u|=D$, the rescalings at amplitude $M$ and length
   $1/M$ converge to a bounded ancient mild Navier-Stokes solution with canonical pressure and nonzero gradient at the
   origin. This extraction uses no Liouville theorem.
3. Regime (II), reductions (Theorems 4 and 5, Corollary 13, Propositions 14 and 14A). GOAL holds with an explicit
   Euler-scale normalization (amplitude $M$, length $\ell$ a fixed multiple of the Lipschitz scale $M/D$, time unit
   $\ell/M$; Theorem 4(c)) unless the plateau alternative (UP) of Theorem 4 holds: $\omega_t(RM/D)/M\to0$ for every
   fixed $R$, where $\omega_t(\rho)$ is the modulus of continuity of $u$ at scale $\rho$, maximized over all times up to $t$. More generally, an admissible
   isotropic normalization (Section 5: amplitude $M$, length at least a fixed multiple of $M/D$, long history) that keeps
   a uniform local $L^2$ translation modulus (L2M) and retains terminal $L^2$ oscillation (L2-OSC) converges to a bounded swirl-free weak
   Euler solution with canonical pressure, axisymmetric about a limiting axis or planar, and GOAL holds for it. Such a
   normalization exists unless the residual alternative (C) holds: every admissible normalization that retains $L^2$
   oscillation fails (L2M). Outside (C) the normalization is in general only shown to exist; explicit rules are given
   under additional conditions (Theorem 4(c), Theorem 5', Proposition 22, and item 4 below). In the weaker topology of strong local $L^2$
   convergence in space-time together with strong terminal $L^2$ convergence, the residual alternative is (C*), which is contained in (C); it is the disjoint union of the flat alternative (FLAT$_0$), in which
   the historical BMO norm $\Theta_0$ of $u$ is $o(M)$ and (C) holds vacuously because no admissible normalization
   retains $L^2$ oscillation, and of (C*)$\cap$(HD), where (HD) means that some admissible joint subsequential limit has a
   nonzero Reynolds defect (the measure $\mathcal R$ in the weak-$*$ limit $U_n\otimes U_n\rightharpoonup\bar U\otimes\bar U+\mathcal R$
   of normalized fields $U_n\rightharpoonup\bar U$). In regime (II), energy gives $\Theta_0\ge cM/\log(E_0M)$ near
   $T_*$ (Proposition 14C), so the flatness can only be logarithmic.
4. Sufficient conditions at velocity records (Lemma 31, Theorem 32). Assume regime (II) and let $t_n\uparrow T_*$ be
   velocity first crossings ($\|u(t_n)\|_\infty=M(t_n)=M_n$, record points $x_n$, $D_n=D(t_n)$). GOAL holds, for the
   normalization at the record points with length $M_n/D_n$, if along one such sequence one of the following is at most
   $CM_n^2/D_n$ with $C$ independent of $n$: the meridional vorticity mass $\int|\omega^\theta/r|\,dx$ over the solid of
   revolution of the meridional disc of radius $2\rho_n$ about $x_n$, for radii $\rho_n$ with $\rho_n/(E_0/M_n^2)^{1/3}\to\infty$
   (a fixed radius is allowed); the swirl source $\int_{s_0}^{t_n}\!\int|\partial_z(u^\theta/r)^2|\,dx\,dt$ for a fixed
   $s_0<T_*$; the signed compression work $W_{s_0}(t_n)=\int_{s_0}^{t_n}\!\int(u^\theta/r)^2(-u^r)\,dx\,dt$ for a fixed
   $s_0<T_*$. In particular it suffices that the scale-invariant mass $\iint|\omega^\theta|\,dr\,dz$ stay bounded along the
   sequence. These conditions are sufficient; they are not known to be necessary.
5. Lower bounds of the same order (Proposition 30, the Remark (a windowed source condition) after Theorem 32,
   Lemma 31, Theorem 32, Proposition 33). In regime (II), for every fixed $s_0$ and along every sequence of velocity first crossings,
   $W_{s_0}(t_n)\ge(\sqrt{2/3}-o(1))M_n^2/D_n$; if in addition $\|\omega_0^\theta\|_{L^1}<\infty$, the source in the
   window of length $2K^2D_n^2/M_n^4$ before $t_n$ ($K=\|\omega_0^\theta\|_{L^1}+E_0/2$) is at least $(\sqrt2-o(1))M_n^2/D_n$. In
   (C), which is contained in (UP), along every sequence of velocity first crossings the quantities of item 4 (for every
   fixed $s_0$ and every admissible choice of the radii), divided by $M_n^2/D_n$, tend to infinity, and under (FLAT$_0$)
   the normalized work $W_{s_0}(t_n)D_n/M_n^2$ is at least of order $2^{cM_n/\Theta_0(t_n)}$.
6. Structure of the work (Section 11). For each fixed $s_0\in(0,T_*)$, the part of the work inherited from the vorticity present at
   time $s_0$ is bounded on $[s_0,T_*)$ (Proposition 39); the rest is generated by the swirl source after $s_0$ and
   carried by the flow. In models in which the swirl is replaced by a weight $0\le f\le G^2r^{-4}$ with source $\partial_zf$ and the produced
   vorticity is carried by a prescribed smooth, axisymmetric, divergence-free and globally Lipschitz drift, the work is at most an absolute constant times $G^2$ times the source $\mathcal I$
   (its space-time $L^1$ norm) without drift and for the linear strains $(b^r,b^z)=\alpha(t)(-\frac r2,z)$ of arbitrary time dependence, for smooth weights supported in one fixed compact set
   (approximation of the actual swirl by this class is not carried out). With the additional cap $f\le D^2$ for a constant $D>0$ and sources
   supported in $\{Dr^2\le G\}$, its inward part is at most $0.934\,G^2\mathcal I$ for global inflow and at most
   $1.867\,G^2\mathcal I$ under a quantitative weak-outflow condition; for global outflow with the swirl transported by the
   same drift and initial cap $|\Gamma|\le\min\{G,Dr^2\}$ it is at most $\frac{G^2}8(1+\log^+(4DT/G))\mathcal I$ on windows of
   length $T$. For general smooth Lipschitz drifts no bound by a constant multiple of $G^2\mathcal I$, uniform over drifts,
   weights and windows, holds for the work or for its positive part (explicit examples, also as families of compactly
   supported drifts); this leaves open bounds for one fixed finite-energy drift or with joint control of the energy and
   the Lipschitz constant. A nonzero finite-energy divergence-free
   meridional drift has radial components of both signs, so the one-signed classes are model statements; the
   additional structure of the Navier-Stokes drift that would suffice is not identified.
7. A coupled vorticity energy (Section 12). The energy $H=\|\omega^r/r\|_2^2+\|\omega^\theta/r\|_2^2$ of Chen, Fang and
   Zhang satisfies $H'\le\frac92\|u^\theta\|_\infty^2H$ (Proposition 41), and with their continuation criterion this gives
   $\limsup_{t\uparrow T_*}(T_*-t)^{1/2}\|u^\theta(t)\|_\infty\ge3^{-1/2}$ (Corollary 42(a)). For (FLAT$_0$) the analysis splits
   by the distance of the record point to the axis, measured in octaves of $M/(16\|\omega^\theta\|_\infty)$. On the
   near-axis branch, under the quantitative geometric and growth hypotheses of Corollary 52(d), flat records are
   incompatible with swirl bounds $\|u^\theta(t)\|_\infty\le c(T_*-t)^{-1/2}\log^k(e/(T_*-t))$ on a set of times defined there;
   this is conditional, and no such swirl bound is established. On the toroidal branch the second alternative of the dichotomy of Corollary 54 holds: a lower bound
   for the enstrophy exponential in $M/\theta_b$ (when $M/\theta_b$ exceeds an absolute constant), where $\theta_b\le\Theta_0$ is the
   BMO norm (mean oscillation) of the meridional velocity $b$ at the crossing. The localization in the Remark on the two branches in Section 12 has two cases: either the
   coupled-energy bound of Proposition 51 holds with a modified octave count, or, under the stated hypotheses, Corollary
   56 gives an alternative on large values of $\omega^\theta/r$ at earlier times or nearer the axis; Lemma 57 bounds the
   part of the localized record velocity produced off the axis during the last $K$ transit times $r_t/M$ ($K$ fixed, $r_t$ the
   distance of the record point to the axis; the bound is $o(\theta_b)$ when evaluated on the scalar
   histories of Limitations 1', and no such relative smallness is proved in general), and Corollary 58 shows that an upper bound for
   the inward compression work weighted by the dual solution of Lemma 40 whose final datum is a smooth cutoff equal to one on the torus swept by the meridional
   disc of radius $r_t/2$ about the record point and supported in the torus swept by the disc of radius $3r_t/4$, of the form $M^2/\|\omega^\theta(t)\|_\infty$ times a factor subexponential in
   $M/\theta_b$ (for one fixed $s_0$, along a sequence of crossings to which the second case applies, with $M/\theta_b\to\infty$), would exclude the second case along that sequence; so would the same bound for the signed form of that work (Remark after Corollary 58). This branch is open.

What is open. Within the strategy of admissible amplitude-$M$ isotropic normalizations, the exclusion of (C) in regime (II) (or of (C*) in
the weaker topology), which is the first missing implication stated below; and, where a successful normalization exists,
a rule selecting it. A bound $W_{s_0}(t_n)=O(M_n^2/D_n)$ along one sequence of velocity first crossings would suffice;
it is not known to be necessary. Proposition 30 proves that this work diverges along such records, and for every
singular trajectory the divergence of $W_0$ follows, conditionally on the criterion of Chen, Fang and Zhang, from the
Remark (relation to the literature) in Section 11; the open estimate concerns the rate of this divergence. Two checks
delimit the available information. A specified list of scalar inequalities (Limitations 1') admits explicit scalar
histories in regime (II) with (FLAT$_0$), all on the toroidal branch; and the family of single-time fields of Proposition
10' satisfies the kinematic conditions used in Section 10 while failing (L2M) at every amplitude-normalized window that
retains oscillation. Neither is a Navier-Stokes evolution; they show only that those scalar inequalities alone, or those
single-time conditions alone, cannot exclude (C). The nonconstant ancient limits obtained at the BMO amplitude
under the respective record hypotheses of Proposition 14B and Theorem 37 are not proved to be globally bounded.

How to resume. Each step below is shown here to suffice for the part of the problem it names; none of the estimates it asks for is
established. The scalar histories of Limitations 1' satisfy every scalar inequality listed there and lie on the toroidal branch; they are
the first test for a new argument on that branch. Work in regime (II). At a velocity first crossing $t$ whose record point lies at distance
$r_t$ from the axis put $A=\|\omega^\theta(t)\|_\infty$, $\rho_0=M/(16A)$ and $h_b=M/\theta_b$; let $q=0$ if $r_t\le\rho_0$ and
$q=\lceil\log_2(r_t/\rho_0)\rceil$ otherwise, and $p_q=\lfloor5h_b/(8C_0)\rfloor-q$, with $C_0$ the constant of Proposition 33; $p_{out}\ge p_q$
is the outer shell count of the Remark on the two branches in Section 12 (the integer part of the sum of the magnitudes of the outer dyadic Biot-Savart
contributions to the record velocity, in units of $C_0\theta_b$). Fix $0<\delta<1/(4C_0)$. Under (FLAT$_0$), $h_b\to\infty$ along velocity
first crossings, and every sufficiently late crossing is near-axis ($p_q\ge1$) or toroidal ($p_q\le0$); on the toroidal branch the
localization in that Remark gives either $p_{out}\ge\delta h_b$ (first case), and then the conclusion of Proposition 51 holds with $p_{out}$
in place of $p_q$, or $p_{out}<\delta h_b$ (second case). So estimates (a) and (b), each covering its class of sufficiently late crossings,
together exclude (FLAT$_0$). (a) Second case: an upper bound $2^{o(h_b)}M^2/A$, for a fixed $s_0$ and along late velocity first
crossings, for the signed dual-weighted compression work $S_\psi(s_0,t)=\int_{s_0}^t\!\int\psi\,(u^\theta/r)^2(-u^r)\,dx\,ds$ of the Remark
after Corollary 58, where $\psi(s)=P(t,s)^*\chi_{\mathcal R}$ is the dual solution of Lemma 40 with final datum the cutoff $\chi_{\mathcal R}$
of the torus swept by the meridional disc of radius $r_t/2$ about the record point (paragraph after Lemma 57). By the integrated identity of
Lemma 40A stated in that Remark, a bound of the same form for the dual-weighted critical swirl dissipation
$\int_{s_0}^t\!\int\psi\,(2|\nabla u^\theta|^2r^{-1}+(u^\theta)^2r^{-3})\,dx\,ds$ is equivalent. Since the proof of Corollary 58 uses only the bound $\int\chi_{\mathcal R}|Z(t)|\,dx\le\int_{s_0}^t\!\int\psi|\partial_z(q^2)|$ of Lemma 40A, with $q=u^\theta/r$ (in (a) $q$ denotes this function, not the octave index above) and $Z(t)$ the part of $\eta(t)$ produced by the source $\partial_z(q^2)$ after $s_0$, the same bound for the dual-weighted source $P_\psi(s_0,t)=\int_{s_0}^t\!\int\psi|\partial_z(q^2)|\,dx\,ds$ also suffices; it follows from the bound for $S_\psi$, since $P_\psi\le2^{-1/2}(H(s_0)+3S_\psi)$ with $H(s_0)=\int\psi(s_0)(u^\theta(s_0))^2r^{-1}dx$ (the dual-weighted swirl energy of Lemma 40A, not the coupled vorticity energy $H$ of item 7), and by $\int|\partial_z(q^2)|\,dx=4\pi\iint|u^\theta||\partial_zu^\theta|r^{-1}\,dr\,dz$ it involves only the axial variation of the swirl. By the Remark on the shell-tested form of Corollary 58, it even suffices to bound $\max_{k''\le k\le j}\rho_k(\int w_kZ(t)\,dx)_+$ by $2^{o(h_b)}M^2/A$, uniformly along the crossings considered, where $w_k$ are smooth kernels of size at most $(2\pi\rho_k)^{-1}$ supported in the torus; each scaled signed pairing $\rho_k\int w_kZ(t)\,dx$ equals $-\rho_k\int_{s_0}^t\!\int(\partial_z\Psi_k)\,q^2\,dx\,ds$, where $\Psi_k(s)=P(t,s)^*w_k$ is the transport of $w_k$ by the dual equation, its absolute value is at most $(2\pi)^{-1}P_\psi$, and for general prescribed sources these finitely many pairings can be much smaller than $P_\psi$. The dual $L^2$ identity gives $\int_{s_0}^t\|\partial_z\Psi_k\|_2^2\,ds\le4r_t$ (that Remark also bounds $\sup_s\|\partial_z\Psi_k(s)\|_2$ and $\int_{s_0}^t\!\int(\partial_z\Psi_k)^4\,dx\,ds$ with no Lipschitz constant of $b$, which gives only a size bound for the pairings through $Q(s_0,t)^{1/4}$) and, for the summed kernel, the necessary condition of item (d) of that Remark, $\frac38M-o(M)\le4(r_tB\,Q(s_0,t))^{1/2}$ for fixed $s_0$, with $Q(s_0,t)=\int_{s_0}^t\|q\|_4^4\,ds$ and $B=1+2(j-k'')\log2$; so $r_tB\,Q(s_0,t)/M^2\to0$ along the crossings would also suffice, but no estimate here bounds this scale-invariant ratio by a quantity tending to zero. Item (e) of that Remark gives the same sufficient condition with $Q(s_0,t)$ replaced by $2(1+8\Lambda)Q_\psi(s_0,t)$, where $Q_\psi(s_0,t)=\int_{s_0}^t\!\int\psi q^4\,dx\,ds$ and $\Lambda>B$ is the logarithm of a ratio of volumes; if a solution with the values of the scalar histories realized the second case, the part of $Q_\psi$ in $\{r\ge r_t/4\}$ would be too small to supply the resulting necessary lower bound, so the contribution from $\{r<r_t/4\}$ would have to supply at least $\frac{9\sqrt2}{16384}\tau^{-2}L^{-5/4}(1-o(1))$. Neither the barrier of item (ix) of the Remark (what the scalar histories leave open for the signed form) nor the quartic estimate of item (vii) of the Remark on signed and cancellation-preserving forms of Lemma 40 excludes this. On these scalar histories, the ceiling obtained from item (vii) by inserting the smoothing bound for $\varepsilon_\psi$ exceeds the requirement just displayed by a factor of order $c_S^4L^{9/4}$. More generally, by item (e) of the Remark (radial transport required by the dual-weighted quartic), at every crossing satisfying the hypotheses stated there and with the record point at distance at least $\sup_{[s_0,t]}\|\nabla u\|_2/\|\eta(t)\|_2$ from the axis, this scalar ceiling is at least a fixed positive multiple of $B^2/\ell$ times $N_0=9M^2/(2048r_tB(1+8\Lambda))$, where $\ell$ is the logarithm of a ratio of scales; item (e) of the Remark on the shell-tested form of Corollary 58 gives $Q_\psi\ge N_0(1-o(1))$ along the crossings considered, and on these histories $N_0$ is at least the displayed requirement times $1-o(1)$, so the comparison applies to the displayed requirement as well. This obstruction concerns the scalar ceiling only, not the majorant retaining the actual $\varepsilon_\psi$. For a solution realizing these scalar histories and the second case, the Remark (radial transport required by the dual-weighted quartic) requires a $\psi q^4$-weighted mean of $r(-u^r)$ at least $\frac49-o(1)$ (for even $p\ge2$ and $-2p<\beta<-2$, the constants $f(b)$ in item (f) of that Remark are at most $\frac{2(\sqrt p-1)}{\sqrt p+1}<2$; no upper bound involving only endpoint terms follows by discarding the entire quadratic form using its pointwise sign; by item (g) of that Remark this mean is not bounded by a universal constant for general solutions, the family there having $V(s_0)/Q_\psi\to\infty$ and a circulation bound that grows, with no crossing of the second case established for it; by item (h), for the Burgers vortex, whose inward flow is a harmonic strain, the mean is unbounded with a fixed circulation bound and $V(s_0)/Q_\psi\to0$; by item (i) the same holds for finite-energy solutions, whose meridional velocity is given by the decaying Biot-Savart law; for their own cutoffs and intervals, (g) has $P_\psi=O(1)$ as $n\to\infty$ for fixed $a$, (h) has $P_\psi=0$ and (i) has $\delta^{-2}P_\psi\to0$ in its iterated limit, which does not settle the source condition of (a) at the crossings) and an outward charge $J_{\varepsilon(t)}\ge\varepsilon(t)^2\frac{9\sqrt2}{16384}\tau^{-2}L^{-5/4}(1-o(1))$, where $\varepsilon(t)=2/\log(D(t)/S(t))$ is held fixed during each time integration. The size estimate in the paragraph after
Corollary 58 on the inward work away from the axis bounds the contribution of $\{r\ge r_t/4\}$ over $[\max\{s_0,t-r_t^2\},t]$ to the
inward work $W_\psi\ge S_\psi$ of Corollary 58 by $288\pi^2G^2Mr_t$, where $Mr_t$ is the Reynolds number at the scale $r_t$; on the scalar
histories this numerical ceiling exceeds the required bound by a factor tending to infinity, and no bound of the required size for the
contribution nearer the axis is proved (when $u^r\le0$ on $\{r<r_t/4,\ |z-z_t|<4M\lambda\}\times[t-\lambda,t]$ for some $0<\lambda\le t-s_0$ with $M\lambda\ge\frac32r_t$, $z_t$ the height of the record point, item (v) of
the second Remark after Corollary 58 bounds that contribution over this window by $8\pi G^2M\lambda/r_t$ plus a Gaussian tail term; for $\lambda=r_t^2$ this is the same circulation scale, which is not of the required size). Over the whole history, item (vi) of that Remark bounds the part of the dual-weighted critical swirl dissipation over $\{r\ge r_t/4\}$ by $288\pi^2G^2+4968\pi^2\int_{s_0}^t\|u^\theta(s)\|_\infty^2ds$; so the bound in (a) follows from bounds $2^{o(h_b)}M^2/A$ for this time integral and for the part of the dissipation over $\{r<r_t/4\}$, and in the scalar histories the time integral is $O(L^3)$, which is $o(M^2/A)$ there. Item (vii) of that Remark bounds the part of $P_\psi(s_0,t)$ over $\{r\ge\rho\}$ by $\frac{9\sqrt2}8\pi^2(r_t/\rho)^3(I(G^2+8I))^{1/2}$ with the same time integral $I$; in the scalar histories this leaves open only the part of $P_\psi$ at distance less than a fixed multiple of $r_tL^{-1/3}$ from the axis, and, for the first choice $D=A=M^2/L^4$ of the scalar histories, a kinematic swirl history given there shows that the conservation of the dual mass, the pointwise upper bounds, the integral inequalities of items (vi) and (vii) and the four listed norm ceilings for the swirl derivatives do not decide that part (the example does not realize the scalar histories; it only stays below their ceilings), so an argument for it has to use more, for instance the swirl dynamics as in Lemma 40A. Item (viii), with $\rho=cr_tL^{-1/3}$ for a fixed $c>0$ and the cutoff $\Phi=\phi(r/\rho)$ defined there, localizes the identity of Lemma 40A to the cylinder $\{r<2cr_tL^{-1/3}\}$ with the localized signed work $W_\Phi=\int_{s_0}^t\!\int\psi q^2(-u^r)(3\Phi-r\Phi')\,dx\,ds$. In the scalar histories $3S_\psi-W_\Phi=O(M^2/A)$, so a bound $2^{o(h_b)}M^2/A$ for $W_\Phi$ is equivalent to a bound of the same form for $S_\psi$, and either implies the source condition; no converse from the source condition to either work bound is proved. The localization thus identifies where the condition lies without weakening it. Item (ix) bounds the part of $P_\psi$ inside $\{r<r_t/4\}$ using a barrier for $\psi$ controlled by the outflow rate $\sup_{s_0\le s\le t}\|(u^r/r)_+(s)\|_\infty$; together with (vii) it yields $P_\psi(s_0,t)\le C\tau^{-3/8}L^{1/8}$ in the scalar histories for fixed $s_0$. This upper bound exceeds every fixed multiple of a threshold of the second case by at least a constant multiple of $\tau^{-1/8}L^{7/8}$, so the estimate does not decide that case. The estimate over the last window uses only pointwise bounds for $u^\theta/r$ and $u^r$ and the conserved mass of $\psi$;
the same paragraph shows that for arbitrary nonnegative weights with prescribed mass and a spatially uniform cap the bound
$\int_{\{r\ge r_t/4\}}\psi r^{-4}dx\le(r_t/4)^{-4}\int\psi\,dx$ cannot be improved, which is not an optimality statement for the actual dual
weight. Possible further inputs are spatial information on $\psi$, the cancellation between inward and outward work along the dual
weight, and the fact that the drift is generated by $\eta$. For the cancellation one candidate mechanism uses the probabilistic representation of $\psi$ in the paragraph after Lemma 57: there the squared radius $R^2$ of the paths satisfies $d(R^2)=2Ru^r\,dt''+2\sqrt2R\,dB$ (the $-R^{-1}$ drift cancels against the Itô term), so it has quadratic variation $8R^2$ per unit time whatever the drift. The quadratic variation alone does not limit how long the inward motion of a path can stay correlated with large values of $q^2$: by Lemma 40, $\psi(s)=r^2g(s)$ with $g$ solving $\partial_sg+b\cdot\nabla g+\Delta_5g+2(u^r/r)g=0$, and since the five-dimensional divergence of $b$ is $2u^r/r$, up to a constant factor, $\psi(s)\,dx$ and the law at time $s$ of the diffusion in $\mathbb R^5$ with generator $\Delta_5-b\cdot\nabla$ run backward from time $t$ (which is not killed) have the same distribution of $(r,z)$; the radial coordinate $\hat R$ of that diffusion satisfies $d\hat R=(3/\hat R-u^r)\,du+\sqrt2\,dB$ in the backward time $u=t-s$, and where $u^r=kr$ near the axis ($k>0$, a forward radial outflow) this drift has an attracting zero at $\hat R=(3/k)^{1/2}$, so the drift can confine the paths radially despite the noise. This does not establish residence in a spatial cell or repeated inflow and outflow stretches. Controlling the weighted work may require further information on the drift or on the swirl dynamics, for instance a cancellation between the inward and outward work along a path. No estimate giving the required bound is proved. (b) Near-axis branch and first case: an upper bound for the swirl record
$S_{s_0}(t)=\sup_{[s_0,t]}\|u^\theta\|_\infty$ that contradicts the lower bound of Corollary 52(b) along these crossings (in the first case with
$p_{out}$ in place of $p_q$, which the proof of Corollary 52(b) permits, since it uses only the lower bound for the coupled energy). By
Corollary 52(d), a swirl bound $\|u^\theta(t)\|_\infty\le c(T_*-t)^{-1/2}$, with one fixed $c$, at all late times of the set $\mathcal G$ of
Lemma 48 excludes near-axis crossings $t_n\uparrow T_*$ at the energy-critical rate $M\ge m_0(T_*-t)^{-3/4}$ with $2^{p_q}\ge f_0h_b^2$
($m_0,f_0>0$ fixed); it does not by itself exclude the other crossings of this branch. (c) Outside (FLAT$_0$): one admissible
isotropic normalization $U_n(y,\tau)=M_n^{-1}u(x_n+\ell_ny,s_n+\tau\ell_n/M_n)$ (Section 5) with (L2-OSC) such that for all $R,a>0$ there
is $C_{R,a}$ with $\int_{E_{n,R}}|\eta(x,s_n+\tau\ell_n/M_n)|\,dx\le C_{R,a}M_n\ell_n$ for all large $n$ and all $\tau\in[-a,0]$, where $E_{n,R}$
is the solid of revolution of the meridional rectangle $\{|r-r(x_n)|\le R\ell_n,\ |z-x_{n,3}|\le R\ell_n\}$; since $\int_{E_{n,R}}|\eta|\,dx$
is $2\pi$ times the meridional mass $\iint|\omega^\theta|\,dr\,dz$ of that rectangle, Corollary 6A(b) then gives (L2M), and this
normalization excludes (C) (Limitations 1). (d) For all branches at once: one of the record conditions of item 4 along one sequence of
velocity first crossings; each of them gives GOAL (item 4) and excludes (C) (item 5).

No counterexample to GOAL is constructed. Status: GOAL, with a rule defined from records, is proved in regime (I). In
regime (II) the analytic requirements are proved outside (C) for an existing normalization, with explicit rules under the
additional conditions cited above. Neither (C) nor (C*) is excluded, and the case is not resolved.

First missing implication: in the regime $D/M^2\to0$, exclusion of the residual cascade alternative (C) of
Theorem 5 below; equivalently, the existence, at some amplitude-$M$ Euler-scale normalization with retained
$L^2$ oscillation, of a uniform local $L^2$ translation modulus for the physical solution (for instance through the
vorticity bound (VL1) of Theorem 6 (the meridional vorticity mass $\int_{B_{R\ell_n}(x_n)}|\omega^\theta|\,dx$, divided by $M_n\ell_n^2$, bounded uniformly in $n$ over the last $A$ Euler times, for all fixed $R,A$), for which, by Corollary 6A(b), it suffices that one signed part of $\omega^\theta$ have bounded normalized meridional mass $(M_n\ell_n)^{-1}\iint(\omega^\theta)_\pm\,dr\,dz$ over the meridional squares $\{|r-r(x_n)|\le R\ell_n,\ |z-x_{n,3}|\le R\ell_n\}$, uniformly in $n$ over the same windows and for all fixed $R,A$). A further sufficient condition proved here is stated at the times of velocity records (Section 11): along some sequence of velocity first crossings $t_n\uparrow T_*$ (times with $\|u(t_n)\|_\infty=M(t_n)$, record point $x_n$), the meridional vorticity mass $N_n=\int_{\{d_m(x,x_n)<2\rho_n\}}|\omega^\theta/r|(x,t_n)\,dx$ satisfies $N_n\le CM_n^2/D_n$ for one choice of radii $\rho_n>0$ with $\rho_nM_n^{2/3}\to\infty$ (a fixed radius is allowed; $d_m$ the distance in the meridional half-plane); a further sufficient condition is the signed compression work bound $\int_{s_0}^{t_n}\!\int(u^\theta/r)^2(-u^r)\,dx\,dt\le CM_n^2/D_n$ for some fixed $s_0\in[0,T_*)$. Either gives GOAL with the Lipschitz-scale normalization at the records of that sequence and excludes the plateau alternative (UP) of Theorem 4, hence (C) (Lemma 31, Theorem 32). Since $N_n\le\|\omega^\theta(t_n)/r\|_{L^1(\mathbb R^3)}=2\pi\iint|\omega^\theta(r,z,t_n)|\,dr\,dz$ and $M_n^2/D_n\to\infty$ in regime (II), the first condition holds in particular when the total meridional vorticity mass $\iint|\omega^\theta|\,dr\,dz$ stays bounded along the sequence; this mass is invariant under the scaling $u\mapsto\lambda u(\lambda x,\lambda^2t)$, does not increase without swirl (the homogeneous $\eta$ equation contracts $L^1$: proof of Lemma 9 and the Remark on the accumulated swirl source after Lemma 9'), and is not known to be bounded with swirl. If $\|\omega_0^\theta\|_{L^1}<\infty$, it suffices that the swirl source accumulated in the last $2\delta_n=2K^2D_n^2/M_n^4$ units of time before $t_n$ be $O(M_n^2/D_n)$, with $K$ the constant of Lemma 9'; this windowed source is always at least $(\sqrt2-o(1))M_n^2/D_n$ (and so is the source in a window shorter by a factor tending to zero, depending on the trajectory), and a bound $O(M_n^2/D_n)$ on the inward compression work $\int\!\int(u^\theta/r)^2(u^r)_-\,dx\,dt$ over the same window also suffices (Remark (a windowed source condition) after Theorem 32). For every fixed $s_0$ the signed compression work $\int_{s_0}^{t_n}\!\int(u^\theta/r)^2(-u^r)\,dx\,dt$ is at least $(\sqrt{2/3}-o(1))M_n^2/D_n$ along every such sequence (Proposition 30), and so is the inward work over the windows just described (Remark (a windowed source condition) after Theorem 32); the single-time constraints of energy, amplitude, gradient, circulation, symmetry and the plateau modulus do not give the mass bound (Remark (no proof from the listed single-time constraints) in Section 11). Neither condition is known to be necessary for GOAL (Limitations 1). At general admissible normalizations a scale form holds (Theorem 38). Fix $s_0\in[0,T_*)$ and let $\mathcal I_{s_0}(t)=\int_{s_0}^t\|\partial_z[(u^\theta/r)^2](s)\|_{L^1(\mathbb R^3)}\,ds$ be the accumulated swirl source. Every admissible isotropic normalization with retained $L^2$ oscillation (L2-OSC) has $M_n\ell_n/S(t_n)\to0$, $S$ the historical swirl record; one with $M_n\ell_n\ge\kappa\mathcal I_{s_0}(t_n)$ for a fixed $\kappa>0$ and all large $n$ satisfies (VL1) and gives GOAL. In (C) every such normalization has $\ell_nD_n/M_n\to\infty$ (since (C) is contained in (UP) by Theorem 32, and under (UP) no oscillation is retained at bounded multiples of $M_n/D_n$) and $M_n\ell_n/\mathcal I_{s_0}(t_n)\to0$; such normalizations exist exactly when (FLAT$_0$) fails, by the proof of Proposition 14A. Moreover $\mathcal I_{s_0}(t)=o(S(t))$ as $t\to T_*$ (Theorem 38(c)), and in (C), along every sequence of velocity first crossings, the record normalization at the scale $\ell_n=\kappa\mathcal I_{s_0}(t_n)/M_n$ converges to zero in $C([-A,0];L^2_{loc})$ for every $A>0$ and every fixed $\kappa>0$, although its value at the origin has modulus one. When the flat alternative (FLAT) of Proposition 14 fails (that alternative states that for each fixed $\epsilon\in(0,1]$ the supremum over $x$, $\rho>0$ and $s\le t$ of the $L^2$ mean oscillation on $B_\rho(x)$ of $u(s)$ mollified at scale $\epsilon\rho$ is $o(M(t))$ as $t\uparrow T_*$), a sufficient condition is the exterior certificate (EC):
a bound, uniform in $n$, on the meridional vorticity mass $\ell_n^{-2}M_n^{-1}\int_{B_{R\ell_n}(x_n)\cap\{r>h\ell_n\}}|\omega^\theta(t)|\,dx$
over the last $A$ Euler times, for all $R,A,h$, at one normalization with the terminal certificate (OSC$_0$) of
Theorem 17 (the $L^2$ mean oscillation on $B_{\ell_n}(x_n)$ of the velocity at the terminal time, mollified at scale $\epsilon\ell_n$, is at least $cM_n$, for fixed $\epsilon\in(0,1]$ and $c>0$; Proposition 22). Proposition 25 shows that the analogous single-time exterior mass condition is
stronger than compactness for single-time fields, so this sufficient condition may be strictly stronger than the
exclusion of (C); necessity of (EC) in the trajectory class is open. In the weaker topology (ST) of Section 6A the first missing implication is the
exclusion of the alternative (C*) of Corollary 13 (which is contained in (C)), that is, of both the flat alternative
(FLAT) and the defect alternative (C*-D) of Proposition 14 (in which (FLAT) fails and every joint subsequential limit that is nonconstant at some time has a nonzero Reynolds defect); by Proposition 14A, equivalently, of (FLAT$_0$) (historical
BMO norm $o(M)$; by Proposition 14C it can hold only with $\Theta_0\ge cM/\log(E_0M)$; at sufficiently late velocity first crossings with $5M\ge16C_0\theta_b$ whose record point lies within $(M/(16A))2^{5M/(8C_0\theta_b)-2}$ of the axis ($\theta_b\le\Theta_0$ the meridional BMO norm, $A=\|\omega^\theta(t)\|_\infty$ the instantaneous meridional vorticity norm, $C_0$ an absolute constant) it forces $S_{s_0}(t)^4\int_{s_0}^{T_*}\|\nabla u\|_2^2dt\ge\frac{128}{2187}(c_5\theta_b^2\,2^{p_q}/L_E(M)-H(s_0))$ for $0\le s_0\le t$, with $S_{s_0}(t)$ the swirl record over $[s_0,t]$, $L_E(M)=(6E_0/(\pi M^2))^{1/3}$ the energy length, $c_5>0$ an absolute constant and $p_q=\lfloor5M/(8C_0\theta_b)\rfloor-q\ge1$, $q$ the number of octaves (rounded up, and $0$ within $M/(16A)$) by which that distance exceeds $M/(16A)$ (Corollary 52); for a record point at distance at most a fixed multiple of $M/A$ from the axis and at the energy-critical rate $M\ge m_0(T_*-t)^{-3/4}$ (for a fixed $m_0>0$) this puts the swirl record above the Type I size by the fourth root of a factor exponential in $M/\theta_b$. No upper bound on the swirl of this strength is known, and when $p_q\le0$ (record point farther from the axis) Proposition 51 gives no bound; this toroidal branch remains open; see the Remark on the two branches in Section 12) and of (C*)$\cap$(HD) (some admissible normalization has a nonzero Reynolds defect; it then has one
with a constant weak limit, a constant nonzero defect and retained terminal $L^2$ oscillation). Outside (C*), GOAL in (ST) holds with the anchor and the
terminal slice chosen by rules applied to each trajectory, not from the limit (Theorem 17, Corollary 18). In both
topologies the exclusion of the residual alternative would not by itself give a selection rule computed from records
for every trajectory: in (B) (some admissible normalization satisfies (L2M) and (L2-OSC), Theorem 5) the defining conditions concern the
rescaled physical solution, in (B*) (some admissible joint subsequential limit has $\mathcal R=0$ and a weak limit that is
nonconstant at some time, Corollary 13) they concern an
admissible joint subsequential limit, and in both the starting normalization is given only by an existence statement. Explicit rules are
proved under additional conditions, among them (L2LS) of Theorem 5' (comparable level scales of the historical local $L^2$ oscillation; Result item 4'), a ratio condition on level scales (Section 5), a
prescribed modulus (the Remark at the end of Section 6), and (EC) for the outputs of a prescribed rule producing
normalizations with the physical certificate (OSC$_0$) (Proposition 22); the mere existence of some normalization with
both certificates gives the analytic requirements only. The first missing implication is stated within the strategy of
admissible amplitude-$M$ isotropic normalizations: neither (C) nor (C*) is shown to be equivalent to the failure of
every first normalization allowed by the goal stated at the beginning of this Overview (for instance, the extractions of Proposition 14B, under their
respective record hypotheses, yield nonconstant limits whose global boundedness is not
proved).

## Result

Let $u$ be the maximal smooth solution of the three-dimensional Navier-Stokes equations (unit viscosity) with smooth,
axisymmetric, divergence-free data $u_0\in\bigcap_kH^k(\mathbb R^3)$, energy $E_0=\|u_0\|_2^2$, and circulation
bound $G=\|ru_0^\theta\|_\infty<\infty$, and suppose $u$ blows up at a finite time $T_*$. Write
$M(t)=\sup_{s\le t}\|u(s)\|_\infty$, $D(t)=\sup_{s\le t}\|\nabla u(s)\|_\infty$, $S(t)=\sup_{s\le t}\|u^\theta(s)\|_\infty$,
and $\omega_t(\rho)=\sup_{s\le t}\sup_{x,|\zeta|\le\rho}|u(x+\zeta,s)-u(x,s)|$ (historical modulus).

Notation for this summary. Asymptotic statements along records refer to sequences $t_n\uparrow T_*$ with $M_n=M(t_n)$ and
$D_n=D(t_n)$; a velocity first crossing is a time $t$ with $\|u(t)\|_\infty=M(t)$, with a record point $x_t$ (or $x_n$) where
$|u|=M(t)$ (that is, a time at which the running maximum is attained; it need not be the first time at which $\|u\|_\infty$ reaches the level $M(t)$, and statements made at every velocity first crossing hold in particular at those first hitting times, which are named explicitly where they are used, as for the dyadic records of Proposition 14D(b)); in this summary and in Section 12, $H$ denotes the coupled vorticity energy of Overview item 7 and Result item 9, not the historical supremum of $\|\nabla^2u\|_\infty$ (elsewhere $H$ is a local symbol, defined where it occurs); constants in bounds along a sequence are independent of $n$. In regime (II), that is $D(t)/M(t)^2\to0$, an
admissible isotropic normalization is $U_n(y,\tau)=M_n^{-1}u(x_n+\ell_ny,s_n+\tau\ell_n/M_n)$, with $\ell_n\ge cM_n/D_n$ for a fixed $c>0$, $0\le s_n\le t_n$ and history depth $s_nM_n/\ell_n\to\infty$, as
defined in Section 5. As in Section 11, $b=u^re_r+u^ze_z$ is the meridional velocity, $\eta=\omega^\theta/r$, $d_m$ is the
distance in the meridional half-plane, $T_\rho(x)=\{y:d_m(y,x)<\rho\}$, $Q_\rho(x,t)=\iint_{\{d_m<\rho\}}|\omega^\theta|\,dr'dz'$ is the
meridional vorticity mass of a disc, so that $2\pi Q_\rho(x,t)=\int_{T_\rho(x)}|\eta(y,t)|\,dy$, and $L_E(M)=(6E_0/(\pi M^2))^{1/3}$ is
the energy length. The meridional BMO norm $\theta_b$, the absolute constants $C_0,C_2$ and the integer
$k_*=\lfloor M/(4C_0\theta_b)\rfloor$ are those of Proposition 33, and $\rho_*=C_2L_E(M)$. The letter $A$ has two uses. In vorticity and mass
estimates (as in $M/(16A)$, $M^2/A$ and $\mathcal I_{s_0}A/M^2$) it is $A(t)=\|\omega^\theta(t)\|_\infty$, as in Section 11, and in item 9 $A_n=A(t_n)$.
In time windows it is a fixed length of rescaled time, as in "$A$ Euler times", $C([-A,0];\cdot)$, $C_{R,A}$ and $M(t-A/D(t))$; the lengths
$A_n\to\infty$ in items 5 and 5' are rescaled time lengths of this kind, unrelated to $A(t_n)$.

The goal (GOAL) is a selection rule, computable from records or from a quantitatively specified observable, and a
physically justified first normalization $U_n$ such that along one subsequence $U_n\to U$ strongly locally including
the terminal time $\tau=0$, $\sup|U|<\infty$, $U$ is spatially nonconstant, and $U$ solves a rigorously derived ancient
PDE with the correct pressure class. This report does not achieve GOAL for every singular trajectory.

Convention. Below, "GOAL holds" for a normalization means that the analytic requirements of GOAL (strong convergence
including $\tau=0$, boundedness, spatial nonconstancy, the limit equation with canonical pressure) hold for that
normalization; how the normalization is selected is stated separately in each case. Explicit rules, defined from records or
observables of the trajectory without reference to any limit, are given in regime (I) (first crossings, Theorem 2), when
(UP) fails (near-maximizers of the oscillation at a fixed multiple of the Lipschitz scale at which (UP) fails, Theorem
4(c)), under (L2LS) (Theorem 5'), and for any prescribed rule from physical observables that produces normalizations with
the physical certificate (OSC$_0$), provided its outputs satisfy (EC) (Proposition 22). These rules are computable from
records or observables only in the sense that they are defined from them alone; algorithmic computability with
certified errors is not claimed. The existence of some normalization with both certificates gives the analytic
requirements but does not supply such a rule. In alternative
(B) in general the normalization is only shown to exist; it is not chosen from the limit, since its defining
conditions, including the oscillation certificate, concern the rescaled physical solution, but no explicit rule
producing it is given. In (B*) the defining conditions (a vanishing defect and a weak limit that is not constant at
some time) concern a joint subsequential limit, so a (B*) normalization is characterized through its limit; Corollary
18 applies rules computed from the trajectory to such a normalization but does not select it. The end-time shift of Theorem 12 is chosen from the weak limit, that is, retrospectively.

What is proved:

1. (Regime split.) Exactly one of (I) $\limsup_{t\uparrow T_*}D/M^2>0$ and (II) $D/M^2\to0$ holds, and (II) is
   equivalent to $S/M\to0$ (Theorem 3).
2. (Regime I.) In regime (I), GOAL holds with the parabolic normalization at first-crossing records of
   $\|\nabla u\|_\infty$; the limit is a bounded ancient mild Navier-Stokes solution with canonical BMO pressure and
   $|\nabla U(0,0)|\ge c>0$ (Theorem 2). No Liouville theorem is needed.
3. (Reduction to a plateau.) In regime (II), GOAL holds with an explicit Euler-scale normalization unless the uniform
   plateau alternative (UP) holds: $\omega_t(RM/D)/M\to0$ for every fixed $R$ (Theorem 4). Under (UP), every
   amplitude-$M$ normalization at the Lipschitz scale $M/D$ has only space-time constant limits.
4. (Main reduction.) GOAL also holds whenever some admissible amplitude-$M$ Euler-scale normalization has a uniform
   local $L^2$ translation modulus (L2M) and retained $L^2$ oscillation (L2-OSC); the limit is a bounded, swirl-free
   weak Euler solution with canonical pressure, axisymmetric about a limiting axis or planar. So GOAL holds unless the
   residual alternative (C) holds: every admissible amplitude-$M$ normalization with retained $L^2$ oscillation
   fails (L2M) (Theorem 5); in (B) the normalization is given by an existence statement. The convergence obtained is in $C([-A,0];L^2_{loc})$ for every $A$ (strong local
   convergence including the terminal trace, not locally uniform). (C) is stated literally; no equivalence with a condition on
   Reynolds defects is proved.
4'. (Observable rule.) A sufficient condition for GOAL in regime (II), stated through an explicit observable, is
   the $L^2$ level-scale condition (L2LS) of Theorem 5': there are $c\in(0,1]$ and $t_n\uparrow T_*$ such that the level
   scale $\lambda_c(t_n)$, the smallest radius at which the historical local $L^2$ oscillation reaches $cM(t_n)$, is defined
   and $\liminf_n\lambda_{c'}(t_n)/\lambda_c(t_n)>0$ for every $c'\in(0,c)$, the lower bound being allowed to depend on $c'$.
   The selection is then $\ell_n=\lambda_c(t_n)$ with an observation time $s_n\le t_n$ and a center $x_n$ at which the local
   $L^2$ oscillation at radius $\ell_n$ is at least $(1-\frac1n)cM(t_n)$. Necessity of (L2LS) is not proved.
4''. (Defect-free reduction.) In regime (II) it suffices that some admissible amplitude-$M$ normalization has a
   vanishing Reynolds defect and a weak limit that is spatially nonconstant at some time (Theorem 12). After an end-time
   shift by a bounded number of Euler times, taken from a full-measure subset (obtained after choosing a subsequence) of an interval on which the weak limit is nonconstant (a
   retrospective choice, which does not meet the selection requirement), the analytic requirements of GOAL then
   hold with strong convergence in $L^2_{loc}$ of space-time and strong $L^2_{loc}$ convergence of the terminal slice
   (topology (ST)), which is weaker than the $C_tL^2_{loc}$ convergence of Theorem 5. No choice from the limit is in
   fact needed: re-anchoring at a maximizer of a weighted oscillation observable and then selecting a terminal slice
   within one Euler time by a threshold rule on translation differences, both computed from each rescaled trajectory,
   gives GOAL in (ST) for the normalization so obtained from the given one; the slices converge strongly there, and the limit there is nonconstant by a relative-energy
   argument (Theorem 17, Corollary 18). In this weaker sense the
   alternatives are (A) (regime (I)), (B*) (some admissible joint subsequential limit is defect-free and nonconstant; it contains
   (B)) and (C*) (Corollary 13); GOAL holds in (A), and in (B*) for a normalization given by an existence statement. The remaining alternative (C*) is contained in (C) and
   splits into the flat alternative (FLAT) (at every scale the velocity mollified at a fixed relative resolution has
   oscillation $o(M)$; then every weak limit of an admissible amplitude-$M$ isotropic normalization is constant, $E_0D^3/M^5\to\infty$, and every velocity
   record sits in an approximately uniform jet extending over unboundedly many octaves above the Lipschitz scale;
   every joint limit is one space-time constant vector with a Leray-free defect, and, along each joint convergent
   subsequence, the normalizations at fixed multiples of the Lipschitz scale around velocity records converge strongly,
   without defect, to a constant unit vector equal to the limiting normalized record direction) and the defect alternative (C*-D) (Proposition 14). Exact bounded Navier-Stokes families show that neither branch
   can be excluded using the limit equation alone. Re-zooming at a Lebesgue point of a nonzero defect (Proposition 14A)
   shows that (C*) is the disjoint union of (FLAT$_0$), historical BMO norm $o(M)$, in which every admissible
   normalization converges in (ST) along subsequences to constants, and of (C*)$\cap$(HD), where (HD) provides an
   admissible normalization of the same trajectory with a constant weak limit, a constant nonzero defect annihilating
   the azimuthal direction (rank at most two),
   mollified fields converging locally uniformly to that constant and retained terminal $L^2$ oscillation; (C*-D) is
   contained in (HD). This is a reduction, not an exclusion.
4'''. (BMO amplitude.) Write $\Theta_0$ for the historical BMO norm (Proposition 14A). The bound
   $\|\nabla p\|_\infty\le C\|u\|_{BMO}\|\nabla u\|_\infty$ holds at every time, and
   for every fixed $s_1\in(0,T_*)$ and $s_1\le t<T_*$, $D(t)\le\max\{D(s_1),4/s_1,C\Theta_0(t)^2\}$ (Proposition 14B(a), by a blow-up at the gradient scale and linear parabolic estimates). Ancient Navier-Stokes solutions with bounded
   gradient, bounded BMO norm and the absolutely convergent, curl-free increment pressure are constant if they are
   invariant in one direction or axisymmetric without swirl (Proposition 14B(b)). Consequently, if $D/\Theta_0^2$ does not
   tend to $0$, there are $\delta>0$ and record times with $D(t_n)\ge\delta\Theta_0(t_n)^2$ for which the normalizations at
   gradient records, with amplitude $\Theta_0(t_n)$, length $1/\Theta_0(t_n)$, unit viscosity and the constant Galilean
   frame of the local mean, have a subsequence converging locally with first derivatives through $\tau=0$ to a
   nonconstant ancient solution of this class, whose pressure gradient is exactly the increment pressure; after a
   further constant horizontal boost the limit is axisymmetric about a fixed line, with circulation bounded by $G$ and
   nonzero swirl (Proposition 14B(c)). BMO records, that is $t_n\uparrow T_*$, $s_n\le t_n$, points $x_n$ and radii $\rho_n$ with
   $L^2$ mean oscillation $O_0(x_n,s_n,\rho_n)\ge\frac12\Theta_0(t_n)$ (Proposition 14A) and $\kappa_n=\Theta_0(t_n)\rho_n$ bounded
   above, have $\kappa_n$ bounded below by a positive constant, and the normalizations at length $\rho_n$ in the constant
   Galilean frame of the local mean have subsequential limits along which $\kappa_n\to\kappa>0$, with viscosity $1/\kappa$ and
   circulation bound $G/\kappa$ (Proposition 14B(d)). These normalizations are computed from records, and their limits have the convergence, nonconstancy and
   limit equation required by GOAL, but at the amplitude $\Theta_0$ rather than $M$, and global boundedness is not
   established: the limits satisfy $|v|\le C_A(1+\log(2+|y|))$ on each slab $-A\le\sigma\le0$. Rigidity with
   swirl in this class is not proved here; it would settle the bounded-circulation case of the conjecture of Koch,
   Nadirashvili, Seregin and Sverak that bounded ancient axisymmetric mild solutions are constant, a case described as
   open in Q. S. Zhang's survey (Remark (iii) after Proposition 14B), and the scalar rates of Remark (ii) after Proposition 14B satisfy both
   $D/\Theta_0^2\to0$ and (FLAT$_0$); so the present arguments exclude neither (FLAT$_0$) nor (C). Finite energy restricts (FLAT$_0$) quantitatively:
   if $\Theta_0\le M/32$, then $D^3\ge c_0E_0^{-1}M^2\Theta_0^32^{3M/(16\Theta_0)}$, and, since $D\le M^2$ near $T_*$ in regime (II),
   $\Theta_0\ge cM/\log(E_0M)$ near $T_*$ (Proposition 14C); the scalar rates above satisfy this.
   Following the local mean along its own path shows that, at sufficiently flat dyadic velocity records, a large mean
   persists for a time longer by a factor $e^{c M/\Theta_0}$ than the elementary pressure bound gives, with a lower bound for
   $M(t)^2(T_*-t)$ in terms of the flatness ratio at the next dyadic record, which tends to infinity under (FLAT$_0$)
   (Proposition 14D; that $M^2(T_*-t)\to\infty$ in regime (II) is already Proposition 7(c)); the dissipation this certifies is summable, so it does not exclude (FLAT$_0$).
   Use the dyadic records of Proposition 14D(b): $m_n=2^nm_0$ with $m_0>\|u_0\|_\infty$, $t_n$ the first time with
   $\|u(t_n)\|_\infty=m_n$, and $h_n=m_n/\Theta_0(t_n)$. At every sufficiently large such record with $h_n\ge h_*$ ($h_*$ the absolute
   constant of Proposition 14D(b)), the signed pressure impulse along the backward-diffusing packet, over the age interval
   $\varsigma_n=KE_0^{2/3}m_n^{-4/3}<t_n$ ($K$ absolute), is at least $m_n/2$, and the older averaged velocity has norm at most
   $m_n/4$ (Remark (pressure impulse at flat records), after Proposition 14D). The bound $O(\Theta_0)$ for this impulse that would exclude (FLAT$_0$) is not proved, and the upper bound
   that is proved does not suffice.
5. (Vorticity certificates.) (L2M) holds at any admissible scale $\ell$ at which
   $\int_{B_{R\ell}}|\omega^\theta|\le C_{R,A}M\ell^2$ on every window of $A$ Euler times, for every fixed $R$ and $A$, with
   $C_{R,A}$ independent of $n$ (Theorem 6). By the circulation
   bound $|\int\!\!\int_P\omega^\theta\,dr\,dz|\le M\operatorname{perimeter}(P)$ on meridional rectangles, a bound on one signed part of
   $\omega^\theta$ suffices; so in (C), for every admissible normalization with retained oscillation, there are a radius, a
   subsequence and times in a bounded Euler window at which the meridional vorticity on the corresponding rectangle has both
   signs with unbounded normalized masses that cancel up to a bounded amount (Corollary 6A). For each fixed $h>0$ the
   signed meridional masses on the exterior rectangles $[\max\{h,d_n-R\},d_n+R]\times[-R,R]$ ($d_n$ the rescaled axis
   distance) are, up to $o(1)$, carried by fluid that stayed outside a tube around the axis of normalized radius
   $a_n\to0$ during the preceding $A_n\to\infty$ Euler times (Lemma 26A); this concerns particle paths, not where the
   vorticity was generated, and it bounds neither signed exterior mass. Near the
   axis the bound of Theorem 6 is implied by $\int_{B_{R\ell}}|\omega^\theta/r|\le CM\ell$; the global bound proved here for
   $\|\omega^\theta/r\|_{L^1}$ (Lemma 9, under the additional integrability of the data and of $\omega^\theta/r$ assumed
   there) exceeds $M\ell$, for normalizations with retained oscillation (L2-OSC), by at least a factor $c(E_0M)^{1/2}\to\infty$, with $c>0$
   depending only on the oscillation constant (comparison after Lemma 9; this compares upper bounds and says nothing about the actual mass). Without that integrability, the accumulated swirl source
   $\int_0^t\|\partial_z[(u^\theta/r)^2]\|_{L^1}$ up to the observation times $t=t'_n$ in the windows of Corollary 6A exceeds $2\pi M\ell$ times
   their normalized mass up to $o(1)$, and at least half of this lower bound comes from a physical tube around the axis whose radius tends to zero
   but is much larger than $\ell$ (Remark after Lemma 9'); both facts are compatible with the available upper bound and
   do not locate the production within the preceding history. For Navier-Stokes trajectories a
   much weaker certificate suffices. Off the axis the meridional vorticity mass in a ball of fixed radius can grow, over
   a bounded elapsed Euler time $\Delta\tau$, only by transport from the ball enlarged by $\Delta\tau$ plus a collar of fixed
   width $w\in(0,1]$, provided this whole enlarged ball stays at normalized distance at least $d'>0$ from the axis, up to
   the factor $e^{\Delta\tau/d'}$ and an error $O(D/M^2+GD/M^2)$ whose constant depends on the radius, $w$, $\Delta\tau$ and
   $d'$ (Lemma 21); the rescaled swirl source of $\omega^\theta/r$ tends to zero in $W^{-1,1}_{loc}$ and uniformly
   off the axis (Lemma 19). Consequently the exterior certificate (EC), a bound on the meridional vorticity mass over the
   last $A$ Euler times outside every tube $\{r\le h\ell\}$ around the axis, with constants that may blow up as $h\to0$
   (in time-integrated form or, equivalently by Proposition 22(a), as a bound uniform over all times in each window of $A$ Euler times), implies (L2M) (Proposition 22). With the terminal
   certificate (OSC$_0$) $O_\epsilon(x_n,s_n,\ell_n)\ge cM_n$ of Theorem 17 ($O_\epsilon(x,s,\rho)$ the $L^2$ mean oscillation on $B_\rho(x)$ of $u(s)$ mollified at scale $\epsilon\rho$; a physical selection, available exactly when
   (FLAT) fails), (EC) gives case (B) of Theorem 5: GOAL in $C([-A,0];L^2_{loc})$ at the physical terminal time, with no
   terminal-slice rule. The time-integrated full-ball bound (VC) of Theorem 6' (the meridional vorticity mass of $B_{R\ell_n}(x_n)$ integrated over the last $A$ Euler times, divided by $\ell_n^3$, bounded for all fixed $R,A$) implies (EC) (Proposition 20); the
   terminal-slice rules of Theorem 17(d) and Proposition 20(b), which give GOAL in (ST) under (VC) or (EC), remain correct
   but are not needed for this conclusion. In (C) every normalization with (OSC$_0$) has $\ell_nD_n/M_n\to\infty$ and
   violates (EC) along every subsequence. A historical bound $O(M)$ for $\|\omega^\theta\|_3$ would give (VC) for every
   admissible normalization and so exclude the part of (C) outside (FLAT); one for $\|\omega^\theta/r\|_{3/2}$ would do so
   given a certified normalization with bounded rescaled axis distance (whose existence is not proved). Neither bound is
   available, and the argument through (VC) does not reach (FLAT); small critical norms are compatible with flatness at a single time (Section 9B, Remarks). Section 9D tests this route: energy makes $\sum_nM_n\ell_n^2\le N_{\rm ov}E_0/(2c_2)$, hence
   the sum $\sum_n\ell_n$ of the scales, finite over every family of certified windows with fixed certificate constants $c,\epsilon$ ($c_2$
   depending on them), restricted to the depth and viscosity conditions of Proposition 24, whose persistence cylinders overlap at most
   $N_{\rm ov}$ times (Proposition 24), so the local
   budget of Lemma 9' alone gives no uniform bound on the exterior mass along windows approaching $T_*$; for single-time
   fields, failure of the exterior mass bound at every certified scale is compatible with compactness of the certified
   rescalings (Proposition 25), so (EC) may be strictly stronger than what is needed (a strict separation for
   trajectories is not proved); and certified windows approach the axis (Corollary 26).
5'. (Sub-amplitude criterion, conditional.) A normalization at an amplitude $B_n$ with $D_n/B_n^2\to0$ (of interest
   for $B_n=o(M)$) at the Lipschitz scale $B_n/D_n$, in the constant Galilean frame of the observed central velocity,
   gives GOAL with locally uniform convergence through $\tau=0$ provided, with constants $C,c>0$ independent of $n$, it
   is bounded by $C$ on cylinders $B_{R_n}\times[-A_n,0]$ with $R_n,A_n\to\infty$ and $A_n$ at most the rescaled depth
   $s_nD_n$, satisfies $\sup_{B_1}|v_n(\cdot,0)|\ge c$, and has a time-integrated tail
   $\int_{-A_n}^0\int_{|y|>R_n}|v_n|^2|y|^{-4}$ tending to zero (Proposition 15). These hypotheses are not derived from the
   a priori estimates. The time part of the local bound on $B_{\rho_n}\times[-A_n,0]$ follows from the terminal bound on
   the ball of radius $(\rho_n+A_n)e^{A_n}+A_n+1$ if $A_n$ times the rescaled pressure gradient on the cylinder over a
   ball one unit larger tends to zero and $A_n\nu_n^{1/2}\to0$, $\nu_n=D_n/B_n^2$ (Lemma 15', a Lagrangian estimate), but the available a priori bound for the pressure gradient (the increment integral) is
   bounded below by a positive constant in rescaled units at any scale where the terminal oscillation holds, so the
   needed smallness would require cancellation in the pressure integral, and the terminal quiet zone is not given by the present estimates (a
   functional family shows that increment behaviour alone cannot give it).
5''. (Weighted relative energy.) For a smooth bounded Navier-Stokes solution $v$ with canonical pressure and bounded
   $\nabla v$, $\nabla^2v$, $\partial_\tau v$, compared with a bounded $C^1$ Euler solution $V$ with canonical pressure and
   bounded $\nabla V$, $\partial_\tau V$, the relative energy in the weight $(1+|y|^2/L^2)^{-\alpha/2}$, $3<\alpha<5$, satisfies
   a Gronwall inequality (Lemma 16, Proposition 16(a)). Hence strong
   convergence on earlier slices propagates to $\tau=0$ whenever the limit is classical on a terminal interval with bounded
   gradient and time derivative (this regularity of the unknown limit is not derived), and a limit that is constant at a slice of strong
   convergence stays constant at later times, which is the nonconstancy argument of Theorem 17. Under (FLAT)
   the weighted defect energy $e_1(\tau)=\int(1+|y|^2)^{-\alpha/2}\operatorname{tr}\mathcal R(y,\tau)\,dy$ ($3<\alpha<5$ fixed, $\mathcal R$ the Reynolds defect) of Proposition 16(c) satisfies $e_1(t)\le C_\alpha(1+t-a)^\alpha e_1(a)$ for $a<t$ in a set of
   full measure, so it can decrease backward in time at most polynomially, and exact swirl-free pipe flows carry
   stationary defects, so this estimate does not exclude a defect.
6. (Dynamics under (UP).) Under (UP), $\|\nabla p(t)\|_\infty=o(M(t)D(t))$ and $M(t-A/D(t))/M(t)\to1$ for every fixed
   $A>0$: the velocity record is frozen over bounded multiples of the Lipschitz-scale Euler time $1/D(t)$ (Proposition 7); in particular the temporal certificate of Section 5 (a drop of the velocity record within a fixed multiple of the time $1/D$) fails under (UP). In regime
   (II), $M(t)^2(T_*-t)\to\infty$, so (C) can occur only for velocity Type II blow-up; no velocity Type I bound is a standing
   hypothesis (velocity Type I is excluded from regime (II) by a short argument, Proposition 7(c)), and the swirl upper
   bounds in item 9 are additional hypotheses of conditional results.
7. (Single-time test.) An explicit one-dimensional profile (Proposition 10) satisfies, uniformly in a parameter $N$,
   amplitude and Lipschitz normalizations, the plateau modulus of (UP) (after a dilation into the regime
   $D/M^2\to0$), retained $L^2$ oscillation at the energy scale, and failure of (L2M) at the energy scale and on every
   interval of length $2\pi4^{-j}$ where $L^2$ oscillation is retained. Its three-dimensional transfer (Proposition 10')
   is a family of smooth, compactly supported, divergence-free, axisymmetric, swirl-free fields of fixed energy with
   $M,D\to\infty$, $D/M^2\to0$, the plateau modulus of (UP) and $L^2$ oscillation at a scale comparable to the energy
   length, for which every amplitude-normalized window that retains $L^2$ oscillation, at any centre and any scale,
   fails the $L^2$ translation modulus. So the single-time inequalities used in this report do not by themselves
   exclude (C)-type behaviour, in one or in three dimensions. This is a single-time test: the fields are not a
   Navier-Stokes trajectory, and it does not show that no single-time argument using other information about
   three-dimensional finite-energy fields could succeed.
8. (Velocity records and compression work.) In regime (II), at velocity first crossings $t_n\uparrow T_*$ the record point
   approaches the axis, and a velocity record of size $M_n$ needs swirl source $\int_{s_0}^{t_n}\|\partial_z(u^\theta/r)^2\|_1\ge(2-o(1))M_n^2/\|\omega^\theta(t_n)\|_\infty$
   produced after any fixed time $s_0$; through an exact identity for the critical swirl energy $\int(u^\theta)^2r^{-1}dx$
   with a Hardy inequality, the signed compression work $W_{s_0}(t_n)=\int_{s_0}^{t_n}\!\int(u^\theta/r)^2(-u^r)$ is at least
   $(\sqrt{2/3}-o(1))M_n^2/D_n$ (Lemmas 27 to 29, Proposition 30). If the plateau alternative (UP) holds, then along every sequence of velocity first
   crossings $t_n\to T_*$ and for every choice of radii $\rho_n>0$ with $\rho_n/L_E(M_n)\to\infty$, the meridional vorticity mass $N_n=\int_{T_{2\rho_n}(x_n)}|\eta(t_n)|\,dx$
   near the records satisfies $N_nD_n/M_n^2\to\infty$, and for every $s_0$ the source and the work also exceed every multiple of $M_n^2/D_n$ (Lemma 31). Hence every singular trajectory is in regime (I), or GOAL holds with the Lipschitz-scale normalization
   at velocity records along any sequence of velocity first crossings $t_n\uparrow T_*$ where the source (or the work) is $O(M_n^2/D_n)$, or the residual alternative (R) holds (regime (II), and for every fixed $s_0$ and every sequence of velocity first crossings $t_n\to T_*$ the source and the work from $s_0$, divided by $M_n^2/D_n$, and $S(t_n)A_n/M_n^2$ with $A_n=\|\omega^\theta(t_n)\|_\infty$ tend to infinity), and
   (C) $\subset$ (UP) $\subset$ (R) (Theorem 32). In regime (II), along every sequence of velocity first crossings $t_n\uparrow T_*$,
   $S(t_n)\|\omega^\theta(t_n)\|_\infty/M_n^2\to\infty$ with $S$ the historical swirl record (Lemma 34(b)), and for every fixed $s_0\in[0,T_*)$ and all sufficiently large $n$ the
   inward strain clock satisfies $\int_{s_0}^{t_n}\|(u^r/r)_-(s)\|_\infty ds\ge\frac13\log(M_n^2/D_n)-C(s_0)\to\infty$ (Corollary 35); under (FLAT$_0$) the work is exponentially large in $M/\Theta_0$
   (Proposition 33), so a bound $C(1+M/\Theta_0)^p$ with fixed $C,p>0$ at every sufficiently late velocity first crossing on $W_{s_0}D/M^2$, on
   $\mathcal I_{s_0}A/M^2$, or on the single-time quantity $Q_{4\rho_*}(x_t,t)A/M^2$ (the meridional vorticity mass within $4C_2L_E(M)$ of the
   record point) would exclude (FLAT$_0$). No such bound is proved, and the particular scalar constraints listed in Limitations 1'
   (scalar forms of the main proved inequalities on (FLAT$_0$)) admit explicit histories with $M/\Theta_0\to\infty$ at a logarithmic rate, so
   that specified list alone does not exclude (FLAT$_0$); compatibility with every scalar consequence of the equations is not asserted. As $t\to T_*$, $\mathcal I_{s_0}(t)=o(S(t))$; every admissible normalization with retained $L^2$ oscillation has $M_n\ell_n=o(S(t_n))$, one with
   $M_n\ell_n\ge\kappa\mathcal I_{s_0}(t_n)$ satisfies (VL1) and gives GOAL, and in (C) the record normalization at every fixed multiple
   of the source scale $\mathcal I_{s_0}(t_n)/M_n$ satisfies (L2M) and converges to zero in $C([-A,0];L^2_{loc})$ although its value at the origin
   has modulus one (Theorem 38). For every fixed $s_0\in(0,T_*)$, the part $W^d$ of the compression work inherited from the vorticity
   at time $s_0$ satisfies $|W^d(t)|\le C_d$ for $s_0\le t<T_*$ (Proposition 39), so the work bound concerns only the retarded part generated by the swirl source after $s_0$; that part
   cannot be signed pairwise. In a model in which the swirl is replaced by a weight $0\le f\le G^2r^{-4}$ with source $\partial_zf$ and the produced vorticity is carried by a
   prescribed drift, this memory work is at most a constant times $G^2$ times the source (its space-time $L^1$ norm) without drift and for linear strains of arbitrary time dependence (for weights that are smooth and supported in one fixed compact set),
   while for general smooth Lipschitz drifts neither its absolute value nor its positive part $\max\{W_T,0\}$ ($W_T$ the memory work over a time interval of length $T$) is bounded by a multiple of $G^2$ times the source over windows
   long in units of the Lipschitz time, uniformly over drifts and weights (one fixed drift suffices for the absolute value; the example for the positive part is a family of drifts; both conclusions persist for compactly supported drifts, as families with velocity and Lipschitz bounds comparable to those of the untruncated drifts and with unbounded energies or, after a parabolic rescaling, with energy one and unbounded Lipschitz constants). With the additional cap $f\le D^2$ (for the Navier-Stokes solution $|u^\theta|\le Dr$ with $D$ the supremum of $\|\nabla u\|_\infty$) and an arbitrary signed source
   supported in $\{Dr^2\le G\}$, its inward part is at most $0.934\,G^2$ times the source for drifts whose radial component is nonpositive at all points and times,
   and at most $1.867\,G^2$ times the source if only $r(b^r)_+\le\theta(r)$ everywhere, with $\theta(r)=(2(1+\log^+(\ell/r)))^{-1}$ and $\ell=\frac{28}3(G/D)^{1/2}$ (outflow of
   small radial Reynolds number); for an arbitrary drift, under the same assumptions, the excess over $0.934\,G^2$ times the source is at most an occupation charge of the outflow. (Except for the linear strains, whose coefficient need only be locally integrable in time, all drifts
   here are smooth, axisymmetric, divergence-free and globally Lipschitz.) If $f=\Gamma^2r^{-4}$ and $\Gamma$ solves $\partial_t\Gamma+b\cdot\nabla\Gamma=(\partial_r^2-\frac1r\partial_r+\partial_z^2)\Gamma$ with the same drift, vanishes on the axis and starts with $|\Gamma|\le\min\{G,Dr^2\}$, the inward part is at most $\frac{G^2}8(1+\log^+(4N/G))$ times the source
   on windows of length $N/D$ when the radial component is nonnegative everywhere (with no restriction on the support of the source), and, if $|\Gamma|\le Dr^2$
   throughout and $\Gamma(0)\in L^1$, at most a constant times $G^2\Lambda$ times the source whatever the signs, where $\Lambda=\frac DG(\|\Gamma(0)\|_{L^1}/G)^{2/3}$.
   A nonzero divergence-free meridional drift of finite energy has both signs of its radial component, and for a steady drift every bounded steady circulation that is continuous up to the axis and vanishes there is zero
   if it has finite circulation mass or if $\sup|X||b(X)|<\infty$ (Remark (the memory part without transport)). A localized version of the critical swirl budget with the backward adjoint weight is
   Lemma 40, and Lemma 40A gives it with no derivative of the weight; assuming in addition $\|\omega_0^\theta\|_{L^1}<\infty$, on the record windows of item (d) of the
   Remark (the windowed conditions concern a thin cylinder about the axis) the part of the adjoint-weighted inward work that these
   estimates do not control is the work at radial Reynolds number $r(u^r)_->\frac16$ on a shell about the axis between $(6D_n)^{-1/2}$ (many viscous lengths) and $o(D_n/M_n^2)$
   (Remark (compression at small radial Reynolds number), part (c)); the Biot-Savart decomposition of this work and the failure on the record windows of the smallness hypothesis of a signed pairing are parts (f) and (g) of that Remark, and an elementary kinematic bound in (g), $\sup_x|r\varphi(x,t)|\ge M^2/(128D)$ at velocity records with $4GD\le M^2$ ($r\varphi$ is the Stokes stream function divided by $r$, a scale-invariant quantity), shows that $\sup|r\varphi|\to\infty$ along first crossings in regime (II), so the smallness hypothesis of the signed pairing fails on every sufficiently late selected record window (that $\sup|r\varphi|$ is unbounded near $T_*$ on every singular trajectory also follows from the $BMO^{-1}$ regularity criterion of Lei and Zhang). An explicit family of single-time fields shows that the mass bound does not follow from the single-time
   constraints of energy, amplitude, gradient, circulation, symmetry and the plateau modulus (Remark (no proof from the listed single-time constraints) in Section 11). For the anisotropic rescalings of
   Proposition 36 (amplitude $B_n$, constant Galilean frames, end times $s_n\uparrow T_*$ with $s_n/T_n\to\infty$ for the time
   unit $T_n$), if $B_n/[\Theta_0(s_n)(1+\log_2\chi_n)]\to\infty$, with $\chi_n$ the aspect ratio (denoted $\Lambda_n$ there), every weak
   local $L^2$ limit at a fixed time is spatially constant; if such convergence holds at almost every time of an interval and
   the bounded limit solves the limit equation with a pressure gradient vanishing on spatial constants, it is one constant
   on that interval, and so is its terminal value when it is weakly continuous there (Proposition 36). In regime (II), if for some fixed $R\ge1$, $c\in(0,1]$ and $t_n\uparrow T_*$ the supremum of the $L^2$ mean
   oscillation $O_0$ over balls of radii $\rho\le R\Theta_0(s)/D(s)$ at times $s\le t_n$ is at least $c\Theta_0(t_n)$, the
   normalization selected there at the BMO amplitude (from points with oscillation at least $\frac c2\Theta_0(t_n)$) has
   Lipschitz, spatially nonconstant ancient cluster limits, and GOAL holds for it exactly when some cluster limit is globally bounded (Theorem 37).
   Under (FLAT$_0$), if every sufficiently late slice had signed mean drops (SD) of size $c_1\Theta_0$ in a fixed direction on all radii $\rho$ between
   $a(s)=\Theta_0(s)/D(s)$ and $2^{c_2M(s)/\Theta_0(s)}a(s)$, at all centres within distance $\rho$ of a path $X(s)$, every isotropic normalization anchored to those octaves (both
   conditions of Proposition 37A(b)) would fail GOAL, and bounded anisotropy is treated in the same way (Proposition 37A(c) and the Remark after
   it); such trajectories are not shown to exist.

9. (Coupled vorticity energy and the size of the swirl.) The energy $H=\|\omega^r/r\|_2^2+\|\omega^\theta/r\|_2^2$ of the coupled system of Chen,
   Fang and Zhang (related to the formulation of Hou and Li) satisfies $H'\le\frac92\|u^\theta\|_\infty^2H$ (the energy method of Chen, Fang and Zhang with explicit constants, Proposition 41). Consequently
   every singular trajectory has $\limsup_{t\uparrow T_*}(T_*-t)^{1/2}\|u^\theta(t)\|_\infty\ge3^{-1/2}$, and in regime (II) the near-record vorticity mass of
   Proposition 33 forces $\frac94\int_{s_0}^{t_n}\|u^\theta\|_\infty^2\ge k_*(n)\log2+\log\Xi_n-C(s_0)$ along velocity first crossings, with
   $\Xi_n=(M_n^2/A_n)\rho_{*,n}^{-1}(r(x_n)+4\rho_{*,n})^{-1/2}\to\infty$, $\rho_{*,n}=C_2L_E(M_n)$ (Corollary 42(c), where it is denoted $\Lambda_n$). This is a necessary condition,
   not an exclusion: (FLAT$_0$) would be excluded on a trajectory along which the left side minus $\log\Xi_n$ stays bounded above on one
   sequence of such crossings, and no such bound is proved. A Hardy-type inequality $H^2\le\|\nabla u\|_2^2J$, $J=\|\nabla(\omega^r/r)\|_2^2+\|\nabla(\omega^\theta/r)\|_2^2$ (Proposition 41(d)), also gives the bound
   $H(t)\le\max\{H(s_0),9\sup_{s_0\le s\le t}\|u^\theta(s)\|_\infty^2\|\nabla u(s)\|_2^2\}$ without the exponential, and with it a second necessary condition of the same kind.
   Since $\|\nabla u(t)\|_2^2\le\|\nabla u_0\|_2^2+E_0D(t)$ (energy inequality, proof of Corollary 44), this gives $H\le\max\{H(s_0),9S^2(\|\nabla u_0\|_2^2+E_0D)\}$ in terms of the historical
   records alone. At points with $b(x,t)\ne0$, in particular near the axis, the meridional velocity is controlled by $\|\omega^\theta/r\|_2$ and the enstrophy:
   $|b(x)|^2\le24(\|\eta\|_2\|\nabla u\|_2+r(x)\|\eta\|_2^2)(1+\log_+(\|\nabla u\|_\infty R_x/|b(x)|))$ with $R_x=\max(r(x),\|\nabla u\|_2/\|\eta\|_2)$
   (Lemma 43, with a sharp kernel constant). Combining it with the two bounds above gives, in regime (II) at late velocity first crossings,
   $M^2\le189E_0DS(1+3r_tS)K_t$ with $K_t=1+\log_+(8D\max(r_t,1/(3S))/(7M))$, and hence
   $D/M^2\ge\min\{(378E_0SK_t)^{-1},(4536E_0^2S^2K_t)^{-1/3}\}$ unless $M\le567E_0S^2K_t$ (Corollary 44).
   These are again necessary conditions, not exclusions, and the swirl growth they force is weaker than that of Lemma 34(b). The differential inequalities of Proposition 41 with Lemma 43 also show that
   $\|u^\theta\|_\infty\le c(T_*-t)^{-1/2}$ at all late times is incompatible with a meridional velocity at least $\gamma(T_*-t)^{-3/4}$ within $C(T_*-t)^{1/2}$ of the axis, with
   $\|\nabla u\|_\infty\le(T_*-t)^{-p}$ (fixed $c,C,\gamma,p>0$), on any measurable set of times $\mathcal T\subset[\max\{0,T_*-1\},T_*)$ with $\int_{\mathcal T}dt/((T_*-t)\log(e/(T_*-t)))=\infty$, in particular at all late times
   (Corollary 45, a conditional statement, via $H'\le\frac{2187}{128}\|u^\theta\|_\infty^4\|\nabla u\|_2^2$, Corollary 46); the scalar histories of
   Limitations 1' are therefore stated with swirl $S=c_S(T_*-t)^{-1/2}\log(e/(T_*-t))$, $c_S\ge1$. Let $U_{\rm ax}(t)$ be the supremum of the moduli $|(u(t))_{B_\rho(x_0)}|$ of all ball means of the velocity centred
   at points $x_0$ of the axis, $\rho\ge0$ (point values included). Then $\|\omega^\theta/r\|_2^2\ge13E_0^{-1/3}U_{\rm ax}^{8/3}$ (Lemma 47, an exact kernel), and with a Riccati form of Proposition 41 (Lemma 48)
   this excludes a lower bound $U_{\rm ax}\ge\gamma(T_*-t)^{-3/4}$ at all late times together with $\|u^\theta\|_\infty\le c(T_*-t)^{-1/2}\log^{1/2}(e/(T_*-t))$ on the set $\mathcal G$ of times where
   $H<36\|u^\theta\|_\infty^2\|\nabla u\|_2^2$, with no gradient hypothesis (Theorem 49, sharp for its scalar inputs; integrated forms in Theorem 49(c) and Corollary 50). If $t_1\in[\max\{0,T_*-1\},T_*)$,
   $c>0$, $k\ge0$ and $\|u^\theta(t)\|_\infty\le c(T_*-t)^{-1/2}\log^k(e/(T_*-t))$ at every $t\in\mathcal G\cap[t_1,T_*)$, then $(T_*-t)^2H\log^{-4k}(e/(T_*-t))\to0$ (Theorem 49(e)), and at every $t$ with
   $2t-T_*\ge t_1$ also $(T_*-t)^2H\log^{-4k}(e/(T_*-t))\le C(c)\int_{2t-T_*}^t\|\nabla u\|_2^2$ with $C(c)=((9c^2/2)/(1-\exp(-9c^2/2)))^2$ (Theorem 49(f)). In regime (II), at a sufficiently late
   velocity first crossing (so that $|b(x_t,t)|\ge\frac78M$, by Theorem 3), an $L^2$ form of Proposition 33 gives $H\ge\Lambda:=c_5\,\theta_b^2\,2^{p_q}/L_E(M)$, $c_5=2\pi C_0^2/(11C_2)$, whenever
   $p_q\ge1$, with $\theta_b\le\Theta_0$ the meridional BMO norm and $p_q=\lfloor5M/(8C_0\theta_b)\rfloor-q$, $q$ the number of octaves (rounded up, and $0$ within $M/(16A)$) by which the
   distance of the record point to the axis exceeds $M/(16A)$ (Proposition 51). So on the near-axis branch of (FLAT$_0$) the swirl record $S_{s_0}(t)=\sup_{s_0\le s\le t}\|u^\theta(s)\|_\infty$ satisfies
   $S_{s_0}(t)^4\int_{s_0}^{T_*}\|\nabla u\|_2^2\ge\frac{128}{2187}(\Lambda-H(s_0))$ for every $0\le s_0\le t$ (Corollary 52(b)), again a necessary condition and not an exclusion; a sequence of such crossings
