$\Delta(u^\theta e_\theta)=(\Delta u^\theta-r^{-2}u^\theta)e_\theta$, the swirl equation says that the smooth Cartesian field $w$ satisfies $w_t+b\cdot\nabla w=\Delta w-aw$.
Fix $s_0\in(0,T_*)$, $t\ge s_0$, $B=D_b(t)$, $\ell=(B+4/s_0)^{-1/2}$, $s\in[s_0,t]$ and $x$; let $X'=b(X,\cdot)$, $X(s)=x$, and
$W(y,\tau)=w(X(s+\ell^2\tau)+\ell y,s+\ell^2\tau)$ on $B_2\times(-4,0]$ (a time interval inside $[0,t]$). Then $W_\tau+\beta\cdot\nabla W=\Delta W-cW$ with
$\beta=\ell(b(X+\ell y,\cdot)-b(X,\cdot))$ and $c=\ell^2a$, so $|\beta|\le2$, $|c|\le1$ and $|W|\le S(t)$. Interior $W^{2,1}_p$ estimates for each component ($p>5$;
bounded lower-order coefficients are absorbed by interpolation; the terminal slice is included) and the parabolic embedding give
$|\nabla W(0,0)|\le CS(t)$, that is $|\nabla w(x,s)|\le CS(t)(B+4/s_0)^{1/2}$. Hence $D(t)\le B+D(s_0)+C\max\{S(0),GB^{1/2}\}(B+4/s_0)^{1/2}$; since
$D(t)\to\infty$ this forces $B\to\infty$, and dividing by $B$ gives (b). Consequently $M/D\le M/D_b\le(1+CG+o(1))M/D$ near $T_*$. In Theorem 4, Lemmas 28 and 31, Theorem 32 and Theorem 38, where $D$ enters
through the length $M/D$, the threshold $M^2/D$ or the regime condition $D/M^2\to0$, replacing $D$ by $D_b$ changes constants only (for
instance $|\nabla U_n|\le1$ for the record normalization becomes $|\nabla U_n|\le1+CG+o(1)$), so the residual (R) is unchanged; and the gradient
record is never carried by the swirl gradient alone. For the Burgers vortex $u^r=-\gamma r/2$, $u^z=\gamma z$, $ru^\theta=G(1-e^{-\gamma r^2/4})$
(stationary, of infinite energy, outside the class considered) the Frobenius norms on the axis give $D_b/D\le(12/(12+G^2))^{1/2}$, so a
dependence on $G$ is natural; its necessity for singular trajectories is not shown.

**Remark (no proof from the listed single-time constraints).** The condition $N\le CM^2/D$ does not follow from the single-time
constraints listed below (here $M,D,S$ are instantaneous norms). For $n\to\infty$ let
$u_n=m\,v_0(x/a)+h\,v_k(x/a)+\delta^{-1}w(x/\delta)$ with $m=n^{12}$, $a=n^{-8}$, $h=n^{11}$, $k=n^3$, $\delta=n^{-9}$, where $v_0$ is a fixed smooth
compactly supported swirl-free axisymmetric divergence-free field supported in $\{1<r<2,|z|<1\}$ with $\|v_0\|_\infty=1$, $v_k$ is
the swirl-free field with Stokes stream function $k^{-1}r\phi(r,z)\sin(kr)$ for a fixed cutoff $\phi$ supported in $\{3<r<4,|z|<1\}$
and equal to one on a smaller rectangle, and $w=r\chi_1(r^2,z)e_\theta$ is a pure swirl field with $\chi_1$ smooth, nonzero and
compactly supported, $w$ supported in $\{r<1\}$. The supports are disjoint, $\|ru_n^\theta\|_\infty=\|r^2\chi_1\|_\infty$ is fixed, the energy is
bounded ($m^2a^3=1$, $h^2a^3=n^{-2}$, $\delta=n^{-9}$ for the swirl part), $M_n=n^{12}$, $\|\nabla u_n\|_\infty\asymp\|\omega_n^\theta\|_\infty\asymp hk/a=n^{22}$ (from $v_k$, whose vorticity is
$k\sin(kr)+O(1)$ on the rectangle), $\|u_n^\theta\|_\infty\asymp n^9$. So $D/M^2\asymp n^{-2}$, the Lipschitz length is $n^{-10}\ll a$, the
normalization at a maximum point of the first component converges locally uniformly to a constant unit vector, the
instantaneous plateau modulus holds, and $N_n\asymp hak=n^6$ for every $\rho_n$ with $\rho_n/L_E\to\infty$ (the radii of Lemma 31; $L_E\asymp a$), so $N_nD_n/M_n^2\asymp n^4\to\infty$. Note that the first
component alone has mass $\asymp ma=n^4\gg M_n^2/D_n=n^2$: under a plateau the record is generated by vorticity at scales much
larger than $M/D$, as Lemma 31 says. These fields are not a Navier-Stokes trajectory and are not claimed to be first
crossings; an estimate that uses the evolution, the record history or further single-time identities of actual solutions
is neither proved nor refuted. (For $\rho_n$ merely comparable to $L_E$ the mass can be smaller: $\rho_n=a/4$ gives $N_n=O(n^4)$.) This is the three-dimensional analogue, for
the mass functional, of Proposition 10'.

**Proposition 33 (flat records force exponentially large source).** Let $\theta_b(t)=\sup_{x,\rho}\fint_{B_\rho(x)}|b(t)-(b(t))_{B_\rho(x)}|$. There
are absolute constants $C_0,C_2,c_1>0$ such that at a velocity first crossing $t$ with $|b(x_t,t)|\ge\frac78M$, $M=M(t)$, $A=A(t)$,
$\rho_0=M/(16A)$ and every $\rho_*\ge C_2L_E(M)$, with $k_*=\lfloor M/(4C_0\theta_b(t))\rfloor$: $2^{k_*}\rho_0<\rho_*$ and
$Q_{4\rho_*}(x_t,t)\ge c_1(M^2/A)2^{k_*}$. Always $\theta_b(t)\le\Theta_0(t)$ (with $\Theta_0$ of Proposition
14A), and in regime (II) under (FLAT$_0$), along velocity first crossings, $\frac1{\sqrt3}(\Sigma'(s_0)+3W_{s_0}(t_n))\ge\mathcal I_{s_0}(t_n)\ge2\pi c_1(M_n^2/A_n)2^{k_*(n)}-o(1)$ with
$k_*(n)\to\infty$.

Proof. Let $\rho_k=2^k\rho_0$, $\psi_k=\phi(\cdot/\rho_{k+1})-\phi(\cdot/\rho_k)$ and $J$ the integer with $\rho_*\le\rho_{J+1}<2\rho_*$. Then exactly
$b(x)=B_{core}+\sum_{k=0}^JB_k+T$ with kernels $\phi(\cdot/\rho_0)K$, $\psi_kK$, $(1-\phi(\cdot/\rho_{J+1}))K$. Core: $|B_{core}|\le(4\pi)^{-1}A\int_{|z|<2\rho_0}|z|^{-2}=2A\rho_0=M/8$.
Tail: $|T|\le C_1E_0^{1/2}\rho_*^{-3/2}\le M/8$ for $C_2$ large (Lemma 27(b)). Shells, oscillation bound: after the integration by parts of
Lemma 27(b), $B_k=\int N_k(x-y)b(y)\,dy$ with $|N_k|\le C\rho_k^{-3}$, supported in $|z|\le4\rho_k$, $\int N_k=0$, so $|B_k|\le C_0\theta_b(t)$. Shells, mass
bound: the kernel is at most $(4\pi)^{-1}\rho_k^{-2}$ on its support, and a horizontal circle about the axis meets $B_s(x)$ in length at most $2\pi s$
(proof of Lemma 31), so $\int_{B_{4\rho_k}(x)}|\omega^\theta|\le8\pi\rho_kQ_{4\rho_k}(x)$ and $|B_k|\le2Q_{4\rho_k}(x)/\rho_k$. Since $|b(x_t)|\ge\frac78M$,
$\sum_k|B_k|\ge\frac58M$; the shells $k<k_*$ contribute at most $C_0\theta_bk_*\le M/4$, so $\sum_{k_*\le k\le J}2Q_{4\rho_k}/\rho_k\ge\frac38M$ (in particular
$k_*\le J$, which is the impossibility statement, since $\rho_J<\rho_*$), and with $Q_{4\rho_k}\le Q_{4\rho_*}$ for $k\le J$ and
$\sum_{k\ge k_*}\rho_k^{-1}\le2\rho_{k_*}^{-1}$, $Q_{4\rho_*}\ge\frac3{32}M\rho_{k_*}=c_1(M^2/A)2^{k_*}$ with $c_1=\frac3{512}$. For $\theta_b\le\Theta_0$ (this holds at every
time): if $J'$ is the reflection through a plane containing the axis, $J'u(J'x)$ has the same meridional part as $u(x)$ and the
opposite swirl, so $b(x)=\frac12(u(x)+J'u(J'x))$, and the mean oscillation of $J'u(J'\cdot)$ on a ball is that of $u$ on the
reflected ball; the $L^1$ mean oscillation is at most the $L^2$ one. In regime (II), $|b(x_n,t_n)|\ge\frac78M_n$ eventually (Theorem 3);
take $\rho_*=C_2L_E(M_n)\to0$ and conclude as in Proposition 30 with Lemma 29(b). $\square$

Under (FLAT$_0$) this makes $W_{s_0}(t_n)D_n/M_n^2$ at least of order $2^{cM_n/\Theta_0(t_n)}$. In regime (II), where $D\le M^2$ near $T_*$, the
inequality $2^{k_*}\rho_0\le C_2L_E(M)$ gives $M/\theta_b(t)\le C(1+\log(E_0M))$ at velocity first crossings, which is the strength of
Proposition 14C(c); this is a necessary condition, not an exclusion of (FLAT$_0$).

**Lemma 34 (swirl-weighted work ceiling; a swirl lower bound in regime (II)).** (a) For $s_0<t<T_*$,
$W^+_{s_0}(t)\le\frac12\int_{s_0}^t\|u^\theta(s)\|_\infty\|\nabla u(s)\|_2^2\,ds\le\frac12S(t)\epsilon(s_0)$, where
$\epsilon(s_0)=\int_{s_0}^{T_*}\|\nabla u\|_2^2\,ds\le E_0/2$ tends to zero as $s_0\to T_*$.
(b) In regime (II), along every sequence of velocity first crossings,
$\liminf_nA_nM_n^{-2}\int_{s_0}^{t_n}\|u^\theta\|_\infty\|\nabla u\|_2^2\,ds\ge4/\sqrt3$ for every $s_0$, hence $S(t_n)A_n/M_n^2\to\infty$ and $S(t_n)D_n/M_n^2\to\infty$.

Proof. (a) The angular derivatives contribute $((u^r)^2+(u^\theta)^2)r^{-2}$ to the Frobenius norm $|\nabla u|^2$, so
$q^2|u^r|=|u^\theta||u^\theta u^r|r^{-2}\le\frac{|u^\theta|}2((u^\theta)^2+(u^r)^2)r^{-2}\le\frac{|u^\theta|}2|\nabla u|^2$; integrate and use the energy equality.
(b) Proposition 30 and Lemma 29(b) give $\sqrt3(2-o(1))M_n^2/A_n\le\Sigma'(s_0)+\frac32\int_{s_0}^{t_n}\|u^\theta\|_\infty\|\nabla u\|_2^2$, and $A_n/M_n^2\to0$. The
integral is at most $S(t_n)\epsilon(s_0)$, so $\liminf S(t_n)A_n/M_n^2\ge4/(\sqrt3\epsilon(s_0))$; let $s_0\to T_*$. $\square$

This is a necessary condition, not a contradiction: Lemma 1(c) gives only $S\le(GD)^{1/2}$, and $G^{1/2}D^{3/2}/M^2\to\infty$ by Lemma 2.
A bound $S(t_n)\le CM_n^2/A_n$ along one sequence of velocity first crossings would exclude regime (II).

**Remark (swirl disc).** For $s<T_*$ put $v(s)=\|u^\theta(s)\|_\infty$ and $d(s)=\|\nabla u(s)\|_\infty$. Then $Y(s)\ge\pi^2v^6/(18G^2d^2)$ and
$\Sigma'(s)\ge\pi^2v^4/(8d^2)$, and for $s_0<t<T_*$
$$\frac{\pi^2}{36G^2}\int_{s_0}^t\frac{v^6}{d^2}\,ds\le W_{s_0}(t)+\frac13\Sigma'(s_0)\le\frac12\int_{s_0}^tv\|\nabla u\|_2^2\,ds+\frac13\Sigma'(s_0).$$
Proof. Let $0<v'<v$ and pick $(r_0,z_0)$ in the meridional half-plane with $|u^\theta(r_0,z_0)|\ge v'$. Since $u^\theta$ vanishes on the axis and
its meridional gradient $(\partial_ru^\theta,\partial_zu^\theta)$ consists of components of $\nabla u$, $v'\le dr_0$; and $r_0v'\le|\Gamma(r_0,z_0)|\le G$. On the disc of radius
$a=v'/(2d)\le r_0/2$ about $(r_0,z_0)$ one has $|u^\theta|\ge v'/2$ and $0<r\le\frac32r_0\le3G/(2v')$, so
$Y=2\pi\iint(u^\theta)^2r^{-2}dr\,dz\ge2\pi\cdot\pi a^2(v'/2)^2(2v'/(3G))^2=\pi^2v'^6/(18G^2d^2)$ and $\Sigma'=2\pi\iint(u^\theta)^2dr\,dz\ge\pi^2v'^4/(8d^2)$; let
$v'\to v$. By Lemma 29(a), $\Sigma'(t)\ge0$ and $\mathcal K\ge\frac34Y$, $3W_{s_0}(t)\ge\frac32\int_{s_0}^tY\,ds-\Sigma'(s_0)$; the last inequality is Lemma 34(a)
with $W\le W^+$. $\square$

The time-integrated swirl is therefore below the Lipschitz scale relative to the dissipation, in the sense
$\int v^6d^{-2}\le CG^2(\Sigma'(s_0)+\int v\|\nabla u\|_2^2)$; with $D\ge d$ the same holds with $D$ in place of $d$. This is a lower bound on the work
and excludes nothing by itself; it is used in Limitations 1'.

**Remark (a BMO form of the work ceiling).** Let $\theta_b$ be as in Proposition 33 and $w=u^\theta e_\theta$. For $s<T_*$,
$$\Bigl|\int u^rq^2\,dx\Bigr|\le C_*\theta_b(s)\|q(s)\|_2\|\nabla w(s)\|_2\le C_*\theta_b(s)\|\nabla u(s)\|_2^2$$
with an absolute constant $C_*$. Hence the total variation of $W_{s_0}$ on $[s_0,t]$ is at most
$\int_{s_0}^t\min\{C_*\theta_b(s),\frac12\|u^\theta(s)\|_\infty\}\|\nabla u(s)\|_2^2\,ds\le\min\{C_*\Theta_0(t),\frac12S(t)\}\epsilon(s_0)$.
Proof. $B=qe_\theta$ is bounded near the axis ($u^\theta=r\,h(r^2,z)$ there), lies in $L^2$, and is divergence-free on $\mathbb R^3$ in
the sense of distributions: $\int B\cdot\nabla\phi\,dx=\iint q\,(\int_0^{2\pi}\partial_\theta\phi\,d\theta)\,dr\,dz=0$. Off the axis
$|\nabla u|^2=|\nabla b|^2+|\nabla w|^2$ and $|\nabla w|^2=(\partial_ru^\theta)^2+(\partial_zu^\theta)^2+q^2$, so $\|q\|_2\le\|\nabla w\|_2\le\|\nabla u\|_2$. Since
$(e_\theta\cdot\nabla)w=r^{-1}\partial_\theta(u^\theta e_\theta)=-qe_r$, one has $B\cdot\nabla w_j=-q^2(e_r)_j$ and $u^rq^2=-\sum_jb_j\,B\cdot\nabla w_j$. Each $\nabla w_j$
is a curl-free $L^2$ field, so by the div-curl theorem of Coifman, Lions, Meyer and Semmes $B\cdot\nabla w_j$ lies in the Hardy space
$\mathcal H^1(\mathbb R^3)$ with norm at most $C\|B\|_2\|\nabla w_j\|_2$. As $b_j(s)$ is bounded, Fefferman's $\mathcal H^1$-BMO duality applies to the
absolutely convergent integral $\int b_j\,B\cdot\nabla w_j$ (through an atomic decomposition, which converges in $L^1$), with
$\|b_j\|_{BMO}\le\theta_b$; the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality in $j$ gives the bound. The minimum with $\frac12\|u^\theta\|_\infty$ is the pointwise
bound of Lemma 34(a) taken before the time integration, and $\theta_b\le\Theta_0$ by Proposition 33. $\square$

None of the consequences is an exclusion. (i) With Proposition 33 and Lemma 29(b), along velocity first crossings in regime (II),
$2^{k_*(n)}\le C\epsilon(s_0)A_n\Theta_0(t_n)/M_n^2$ for large $n$, so $M_n/\Theta_0(t_n)\le C(1+\log(A_n\Theta_0(t_n)/M_n^2))\le C(1+\log(D_n/M_n))$. Since
$D\le M^2$ near $T_*$ and $D/M\ge(\pi M^2/(24E_0))^{1/3}$ (Lemma 2), $\log(D_n/M_n)$ is comparable with $\log(E_0M_n)$, so this has the strength of the
bound after Proposition 33 and of Proposition 14C(c). (ii) The analogue of Lemma 34(b), $\Theta_0(t_n)A_n/M_n^2\to\infty$, already follows from
$\theta_b(t_n)\ge cM_n/\log(E_0M_n)$ and from $\frac78M\le2A\rho+C_1E_0^{1/2}\rho^{-3/2}$ for every $\rho>0$ (the core, shell and tail bounds in the proof of
Proposition 33), which gives $M\le CA^{3/5}E_0^{1/5}$. (iii) For an axisymmetric cutoff $\psi$, $\psi B$ is again divergence-free and
$(\psi B)\cdot\nabla(\psi w_j)=\psi^2B\cdot\nabla w_j$ exactly; but on a torus of cross-section $\ell$ at distance $r_0\gg\ell$ from the axis the relevant
BMO norm of the cut-off field $b$ is of the size of $|u^r|$ there, not of the oscillation of $(u^r,u^z)$ on the cross-section, because $e_r$
rotates; subtracting $u^r(r_0,z_0)e_r+u^z(r_0,z_0)e_z$ instead gives the bound $C(\alpha+|u^r(r_0,z_0)|\ell/r_0)\|\psi q\|_2\|\nabla(\psi w)\|_2$ for the localized
work, $\alpha$ the supremum of that difference on the support. We found no window estimate from it beyond the Remark (where the critical work is not done) below.

**Remark (radial form of the work ceiling).** Put $\rho(t)=(G/D(t))^{1/2}$ and $\mathcal E(R)=\int_{s_0}^{T_*}\!\int_{\{r<R\}}|\nabla u|^2dx\,ds$. Then
$W^+_{s_0}(t)\le\frac G2\int_{\rho(t)}^\infty\mathcal E(R)R^{-2}\,dR$: for $s\le t$, $|u^\theta(r,z,s)|\le\min\{D(t)r,G/r\}\le G/\max\{r,\rho(t)\}$, and
$1/\max\{r,\rho\}=\int_\rho^\infty\mathbf 1_{\{r<R\}}R^{-2}dR$, so Lemma 34(a) and Tonelli give the bound. Hence, by Lemma 29(b), a bound of the
right side by $CM_n\ell_n$ at an admissible normalization with (L2-OSC) gives GOAL (Theorem 38(b) below), and a bound by $CM_n^2/D_n$
along velocity first crossings gives (V) of Theorem 32. Since $\mathcal E(R)\le\epsilon(s_0)$, the part $R\ge R_0$ contributes at most
$G\epsilon(s_0)/(2R_0)$; under a power bound $\mathcal E(R)\le HR^\alpha$ for $R\le R_0$ with $0<\alpha<1$, and $\rho(t)\le R_0$, the right side is at most
$\frac{H}{2(1-\alpha)}G^{(1+\alpha)/2}D(t)^{(1-\alpha)/2}+\frac{G\epsilon(s_0)}{2R_0}$. No such power bound is known: finite dissipation alone gives
none (a radial dissipation measure with $\mathcal E(R)=1/\log(1/R)$ near $0$ is finite and violates every power bound).

**Corollary 35 (inward strain clock).** In regime (II), along velocity first crossings,
$\int_{s_0}^{t_n}\|(u^r/r)_-(s)\|_\infty\,ds\ge\frac13\log(M_n^2/D_n)-C(s_0)\to\infty$.

Proof. With $k(s)=\|(u^r/r)_-(s)\|_\infty$, $(u^r)_-q^2\le k\,q^2r$ and $\int q^2r\,dx=\Sigma'$, so $\frac d{dt}W^+_{s_0}\le k\Sigma'\le k(\Sigma'(s_0)+3W^+_{s_0})$ by Lemma
29(a). Gronwall gives $W^+_{s_0}(t)\le\frac13\Sigma'(s_0)(e^{3\int_{s_0}^tk}-1)$; insert Proposition 30 ($W^+\ge W$) and $A_n\le\sqrt2D_n$. $\square$

**Remark (where the critical work is not done).** In the record normalization of Lemma 31 the rescaled circulation is
$\nu_n\Gamma$, so with $d_n(y)$ the distance of $y$ from the rescaled axis the rescaled swirl angular velocity is at most $\min\{1,G\nu_n/d_n^2\}$,
while $|U_n^r|\le d_n$ (Lipschitz, zero on the axis). The physical work is $M_n^2\ell_n^2=\nu_n^{-2}$ times the rescaled integral, and
integrating over the whole horizontal plane (split at $d_n=(G\nu_n)^{1/2}$) bounds the rescaled integral over
$\{|y|<\varrho\}\times[-A,0]$ by $\frac{16\pi}3\varrho A(G\nu_n)^{3/2}$, uniformly in the position of the axis; so the compression work done there is
at most $\frac{16\pi}3\varrho AG^{3/2}(M_n^2/D_n)^{1/2}=o(M_n^2/D_n)$. So the critical work required by Proposition 30 is done more than any fixed number of Euler times
$1/D_n$ before $t_n$, or at distances much larger than $M_n/D_n$ from the record.

**Remark (memory form of the work).** Split $\eta=\eta^d+Z$ from $s_0$ and $\varphi$ accordingly. By Lemma 29(c),
$W_{s_0}(t)=W^d(t)+\int_{s_0}^t\int_{s_0}^\tau\mathcal M(\tau,s)\,ds\,d\tau$ with $\mathcal M(\tau,s)=\int q^2(\tau)(-u^r[P(\tau,s)F(s)])\,dx$, where $P(\tau,s)$ is the
homogeneous propagator and $u^r[\cdot]$ the radial velocity of a vorticity. The sup bound $\|b[Z(\tau)]\|_\infty\le(\frac12\|rZ\|_\infty\|Z\|_1)^{1/2}$
of Lemma 27(a) gives $W-W^d\le(\sup(A+\|r\eta^d\|_\infty)\mathcal I_{s_0}(t)/2)^{1/2}\epsilon(s_0)$, and with Lemma 29(b) the closed bound
$\mathcal I_{s_0}(t)\le\frac2{\sqrt3}(\Sigma'(s_0)+3\sup W^d)+\frac32\sup(A+\|r\eta^d\|_\infty)\epsilon(s_0)^2$ (here $\sup_\tau|W^d(\tau)|\le C_d<\infty$ by
Proposition 39 below). Here $w=r\eta^d$ solves $(\partial_t+b\cdot\nabla-\Delta+r^{-2})w=u^r\eta^d$ with $|\eta^d|\le C_0$, so the maximum principle gives
$\|r\eta^d(t)\|_\infty\le A(s_0)+C_0\int_{s_0}^tM\,ds$ and $\sup(A+\|r\eta^d\|_\infty)=O(D(t))$. Compared with Proposition 30 this is consistent in regime (II): it misses the target $\mathcal I\le CM^2/D$ by the factor
$(D\epsilon(s_0)/M)^2\to\infty$. The loss is in the sup bound, which is sharp at the records: the inner Biot-Savart operator with a nonincreasing radial cutoff
is $T_Rc=c-\chi_R*c$ with $\chi_R=-\partial_r[\phi(r/R)]/(4\pi r^2)\ge0$, $\int\chi_R=1$, so $\|T_Rc\|_\infty\le2\|c\|_\infty$; at the radii $R_n$ of
Proposition 30, $|T_{R_n}b(x_n,t_n)|=(1-o(1))M_n$ and $|T_{R_n}b^d(x_n,t_n)|\le2C_0R_n(r(x_n)+2R_n)=o(1)$, hence $\|b[Z(t_n)]\|_\infty\ge(\frac12-o(1))M_n$;
what is missing is that the inward radial velocity it generates is small where $q^2$ is large.

**Remark (the memory part without transport).** (Letters introduced in one proof or example of this Remark are local to it, except that $a$, $\ell$, $\theta$, $k$ and $\mathcal H$, once introduced with the two classes of drifts below, keep their meanings to the end of the Remark. Integrals $dx$ and the norms of $L^p(dx)$ refer to volume in $\mathbb R^3$, integrals $dX$ to volume in $\mathbb R^5$.) Replace the propagator $P(\tau,s)$ in the memory form of the work by the heat semigroup
$e^{(\tau-s)\Delta_5}$ of $\Delta_5=\partial_r^2+\frac3r\partial_r+\partial_z^2$, that is, drop the transport term of the equation for the produced vorticity and keep
the swirl as it is. Then for $s_0<t<T_*$
$$\Big|\int_{s_0}^t\!\!\int_{s_0}^\tau\!\!\int q^2(\tau)\big(-u^r[e^{(\tau-s)\Delta_5}F(s)]\big)\,dx\,ds\,d\tau\Big|\le\frac3{16}\,G^2\,\mathcal I_{s_0}(t).$$
Only $0\le q^2\le G^2r^{-4}$ at all times is used: no sign of $F$ or of the work, and no relation between the swirl and the drift. We prove it
for histories whose lifts $f=q^2$ (see below) are jointly smooth on $[s_0,t]\times\mathbb R^5$, invariant under rotations of the first four
coordinates and supported in one fixed compact set for all times in $[s_0,t]$, with $0\le f\le G^2r^{-4}$ (a model class; the approximation
of the actual swirl is not carried out). Proof. Lift axisymmetric functions to $\mathbb R^5\ni X=(x,z)$, $x\in\mathbb R^4$, $r=|x|$, so that $\int h\,dx=\pi^{-1}\int hr^{-2}\,dX$, $A=-\Delta_5$ is the
Laplacian of $\mathbb R^5$ with sign, $B=A^{-2}$ has the kernel $(16\pi^2|X|)^{-1}$, and $k_\sigma=A^{-1}e^{-\sigma A}$ has a positive kernel. With $f=q^2$ and Lemma 29(c),
$\pi$ times the left side is the absolute value of $\int\!\int_{s_0<s<\tau<t}\int\!\!\int\partial_zf(X,\tau)\,r_X^{-1}k_{\tau-s}(X-Y)\,\partial_zf(Y,s)\,dY\,dX\,ds\,d\tau$. Three facts: (a)
$k_\sigma=\int_\sigma^\infty e^{-\lambda A}d\lambda$, so $\int_0^\infty k_\sigma\,d\sigma=B$, and $\partial_z$ of the heat kernel has the sign of $-z$ for every $\lambda$, so $\int_0^\infty|\partial_zk_\sigma|\,d\sigma=|\partial_zB|$; (b)
$\int_{\mathbb R}|\partial_zB(x,z)|\,dz=(8\pi^2|x|)^{-1}$ and $\int_{\mathbb R}\partial_zk_\sigma(x,z)\,dz=0$, so $|\int g\,\partial_zk_\sigma\,dz|\le\frac m2\int|\partial_zk_\sigma|\,dz$ whenever $0\le g\le m$; (c) $|x-y|^{-1}$ is
superharmonic on $\mathbb R^4$, so its average over $\{|x|=R\}$ is at most $R^{-1}$ when $|y|\le R$, whence $\int_{|x|\ge\rho}|x|^{-4}|x-y|^{-1}d^4x\le2\pi^2/\rho$ and
$\int_{|x|\ge\rho}|x|^{-5}|x-y|^{-1}d^4x\le\pi^2/\rho^2$ for $|y|=\rho$. On $\{r_Y\ge r_X\}$ fix $(X,\tau)$ and move $\partial_z$ from $f(Y,s)$ to the kernel (the region does
not depend on $z$); (b) with $m=G^2r_Y^{-4}$, then (a) (after extending the time integral of the nonnegative majorant $|\partial_zk_\sigma|$ to $\sigma\in(0,\infty)$), (b) and (c) bound the inner integral by $\frac{G^2}2\cdot\frac1{8\pi^2}\cdot\frac{2\pi^2}{r_X}=\frac{G^2}{8r_X}$, so this region
contributes at most $\frac{G^2}8\int\!\int|F|r^{-2}\,dX\,d\tau=\frac\pi8G^2\mathcal I_{s_0}(t)$. On $\{r_Y<r_X\}$ fix $(Y,s)$ and move $\partial_z$ from $f(X,\tau)$ to the kernel; with
$fr_X^{-1}\le G^2r_X^{-5}$ the same steps give $\frac{G^2}2\cdot\frac1{8\pi^2}\cdot\frac{\pi^2}{r_Y^2}=\frac{G^2}{16r_Y^2}$ and a contribution at most $\frac\pi{16}G^2\mathcal I_{s_0}(t)$. Adding the
two regions gives the claim. $\square$
So without transport the vorticity produced by the swirl source does compression work at most $\frac3{16}G^2$ times that source, for
every history in the model class; the bound is uniform in the length of the time interval. On the record windows of the Remark (a windowed source
condition) Lemma 29(b) forces $W_{s_n}(t_n)\ge\mathcal I_{s_n}(t_n)/\sqrt3-o(M_n^2/D_n)$; a bound of the above form for the true propagator, together with
a bound $O(M_n^2/D_n)$ for the work $W^d$ of the inherited vorticity, would give $\mathcal I_{s_n}(t_n)=O(M_n^2/D_n)$ when $G^2<16/(3\sqrt3)$, hence
$N_n\le\|\eta(s_n)\|_{L^1}+\mathcal I_{s_n}(t_n)=O(M_n^2/D_n)$ by the splitting in that Remark (where $s_n$ is chosen with $\|\eta(s_n)\|_{L^1}\le M_n^2/D_n$), and GOAL by Lemma 31 (for $G^2\ge16/(3\sqrt3)$ these
inequalities alone give no bound). Neither required estimate is established here. The proof above uses axial cancellation and weighted
bounds on the time-integrated absolute axial derivatives of the response kernel; extending it requires corresponding bounds for the
kernel of $A^{-1}P(\tau,s)$ in both the source and the observation variables, uniform along windows with $D_n(t_n-s_n)\to\infty$, where
$D_n(t_n-s_n)$ is the length of the window in units of the Lipschitz time $1/D_n$.
The same bound, with the constant $\frac38$, holds in the model class above when the produced vorticity is also transported by a linear
strain with arbitrary time dependence, that is, when $P(\tau,s)$ is replaced by the propagator $U(\tau,s)$ of $\partial_t+b\cdot\nabla-\Delta_5$ with
$(b^r,b^z)=\alpha(t)(-\frac r2,z)$ and $\alpha$ locally integrable. With $\kappa(\tau,\sigma)=\exp(-\int_\sigma^\tau\alpha)$, $\kappa=\kappa(\tau,s)$, $h=\int_s^\tau\kappa(\tau,\sigma)\,d\sigma$ and
$k=\int_s^\tau\kappa(\tau,\sigma)^{-2}d\sigma$, the characteristics are $x\mapsto\sqrt\kappa\,x$, $z\mapsto z/\kappa$, and the kernel of $A^{-1}U(\tau,s)$ is
$\kappa H_{h,k}(x-\sqrt\kappa\,y,z-w/\kappa)$ for $Y=(y,w)$, where $H_{h,k}(d,\zeta)=\int_0^\infty p^{(4)}_{h+\lambda}(d)\,p^{(1)}_{k+\lambda}(\zeta)\,d\lambda$ and $p^{(n)}_a$ is the heat kernel of
$\mathbb R^n$ at time $a$ (the factor $\kappa$ is the Jacobian of the flow on $\mathbb R^5$). Since $H_{h,k}(d,\cdot)$ is even and decreasing on $(0,\infty)$,
$|\int g\,\partial_\zeta H_{h,k}(d,\zeta)\,d\zeta|\le mH_{h,k}(d,0)\le mJ_h(d)$ whenever $0\le g\le m$, where $J_h(d)=\int_0^\infty p^{(4)}_{h+\lambda}(d)(4\pi\lambda)^{-1/2}d\lambda$; by
substitution $J_h(\sqrt\kappa\,d)=\kappa^{-3/2}J_{h/\kappa}(d)$ and $\int_0^\infty J_a(d)\,da=(8\pi^2|d|)^{-1}$. Split the bilinear form at the transported radius. On
$\{\sqrt\kappa|y|\ge|x|\}$ move $\partial_z$ from $f(Y,s)$ to the kernel, substitute $v=\sqrt\kappa\,y$ and use $-\partial_sh=\kappa$; on $\{|x|>\sqrt\kappa|y|\}$ move $\partial_z$ from
$f(X,\tau)$, substitute $x=\sqrt\kappa\,u$ and use $\partial_\tau(h/\kappa)=\kappa^{-1}$. With (c) the two regions contribute at most $\frac\pi4G^2\mathcal I_{s_0}(t)$ and
$\frac\pi8G^2\mathcal I_{s_0}(t)$. So time-dependent compression of the produced vorticity does not by itself make the memory work large compared
with $G^2\mathcal I_{s_0}(t)$; the argument does not cover drifts whose strain varies in space, as the meridional drift does.
For general drifts no time-uniform bound of this form for the absolute memory work holds, even with a constant depending on $G$. For each
fixed $G>0$ the following example uses one steady smooth bounded drift $b$, divergence-free in $\mathbb R^3$ and globally Lipschitz, and a steady
$f=q^2$ in the model class above; with the propagator $U(\tau,s)$ of $\partial_t+b\cdot\nabla-\Delta_5$ in place of $P(\tau,s)$, its memory work satisfies $W_T\le0$,
while $|W_T|/(G^2\mathcal I_0(T))$ is at least a positive constant times $\log(1+\log(1+T/2))$ for all $T>0$; the drift and its Lipschitz constant are fixed as $T\to\infty$.
Example ($s_0=0$). Sets specified in $(r,z)$, such as $K$ and $\Omega$ below, also denote their rotation-invariant realizations in three or five
dimensions, and in diffusion probabilities and expectations the starting point is a meridional point $\xi=(r,z)$. Let $S$ be smooth and nondecreasing with
$S=0$ on $(-\infty,0]$ and $S=1$ on $[1,\infty)$, let $\nu(\zeta)=(\zeta-1)S(4\zeta-2)S(6-4\zeta)$ and $V(\zeta)=\nu(\zeta)-\nu(-\zeta)$ (odd, supported in $\{\frac12\le|\zeta|\le\frac32\}$, and
$V(\zeta)=\zeta-1$ for $\frac34\le\zeta\le\frac54$), let $c(r)=256S(2r-1)(64+\log r)$ for $r>\frac12$ and $c=0$ otherwise, and let $b$ have the stream function $-rc(r)V(z/r)$,
that is, $b^r=\frac crV'(\zeta)$ and $b^z=\frac cr(\zeta V'(\zeta)-V(\zeta))-c'V(\zeta)$ with $\zeta=z/r$. Then $b$ is smooth, divergence-free in $\mathbb R^3$, zero for $r<\frac12$, bounded and
globally Lipschitz (its first derivatives are bounded functions of $\zeta$ times $c/r^2$, $c'/r$ or $c''$, which are bounded), $b^r$ is even and $b^z$ is odd in $z$;
for $r\ge1$, in the two cones $|\zeta-1|\le\frac14$ and $|\zeta+1|\le\frac14$, its radial component is outward with $rb^r=256(64+\log r)$.
Let $f=(\frac49)^4G^2a(r)g(z)$ with $a(r)=S(16(r-2))S(16(\frac94-r))$ and $g(z)=S(9-4|z|)$, independent of time; then
$0\le f\le G^2r^{-4}$, and on $\{z>0\}$ the function $h=-F\ge0$ is supported in $K=[2,\frac94]^2$, has $m=\int_{z>0}h\,dx\ge128\pi G^2/6561$, and $\mathcal I_0(T)=2mT$. The produced
vorticity $\eta_b(\tau)=\int_0^\tau U(\tau,s)F\,ds$ is odd in $z$, and $\vartheta=-\eta_b$ solves $\partial_t\vartheta+b\cdot\nabla\vartheta=\Delta_5\vartheta+h$ on $\{z>0\}$ with zero initial data and zero
boundary values on $\{z=0\}$, so $\vartheta\ge0$ there by the maximum principle in that half space of $\mathbb R^5$. In $\Omega=\{r>1,\ |z/r-1|<\frac14\}$ one has $b^r=c/r$ and
$b^z=c/r-c'(z-r)/r$ with $c=256\ell$, $\ell=64+\log r$. For $r>0$, $\frac2r\partial_r\vartheta=\operatorname{div}_3(2\vartheta e_r/r)$; consequently the density $\rho=2\pi r\vartheta$ for $dr\,dz$ satisfies
$\partial_t\rho=\mathcal L^*\rho+2\pi rh$, $\rho(0)=0$, where $\mathcal L^*$ is the formal adjoint for $dr\,dz$ of $\mathcal L=\partial_r^2+\partial_z^2+(b^r-\frac1r)\partial_r+b^z\partial_z$. Let $\sigma$ be the exit time
from $\Omega$ of the diffusion with generator $\mathcal L$. With $y=z/r-1$, $H=\frac1{16}-y^2$, $E=e^{-\ell H}$ and $\beta(y)=1+(1+y)^2$, a direct computation gives
$r^2\mathcal LE/E=H^2+2H-4(1+y)y+\ell\,[4(1+y)yH+2\beta(y)+6y]-16\ell+[(4\beta(y)-512)\ell^2-250\ell]\,y^2$; for $|y|\le\frac14$ the terms independent of $\ell$ are at most $\frac{225}{256}$,
the bracket multiplying $\ell$ is at most $\frac{429}{64}<7$ and $(4\beta(y)-512)\ell^2-250\ell\le0$, so $\mathcal LE\le(1-9\ell)r^{-2}E\le0$ in $\Omega$; also $\mathcal L(r^{-1})=(3-256\ell)r^{-3}\le0$.
Hence $p=1-E-r^{-1}$ satisfies $\mathcal Lp\ge0$ and $-1\le p\le1$ in $\Omega$, $p=-E\le0$ on $\{r=1\}$ and $p=-r^{-1}\le0$ on the lateral boundary; the stopped diffusion is
nonexplosive (its drift is bounded on $\{r\ge1\}$), and Itô's formula gives $p(\xi)\le\mathbb E_\xi p(X_{u\wedge\sigma})\le\mathbb P_\xi(\sigma>u)$. On $K$, $|y|\le\frac18$ and $\ell H\ge3$, so
$\mathbb P_\xi(\sigma>u)\ge\frac12-e^{-3}>\frac25$ for all $u\ge0$. Since $\mathcal L(r^2)=2c$ and $r\ge1$ along stopped paths, Jensen's inequality gives
$\frac d{du}\mathbb E_\xi r_{u\wedge\sigma}^2\le32768+256\log\mathbb E_\xi r_{u\wedge\sigma}^2$, hence $\mathbb E_\xi r_{u\wedge\sigma}^2\le65536(1+u)(1+\log(1+u))$, and the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality
$\mathbb P_\xi(\sigma>u)^2\le\mathbb E_\xi[r_u^{-2};\sigma>u]\,\mathbb E_\xi[r_u^2;\sigma>u]$ gives $\mathbb E_\xi[r_u^{-2};\sigma>u]\ge k/((1+u)(1+\log(1+u)))$ with $k=4/(25\cdot65536)$, for $\xi\in K$.
The solution killed on leaving $\Omega$ has the same source and smaller boundary values, so by comparison and Duhamel's formula
$Q(\tau):=\int_\Omega\vartheta(\tau)r^{-2}\,dx\ge\int_0^\tau\!\int_K2\pi rh\,\mathbb E_{(r,z)}[r_u^{-2};\sigma>u]\,dr\,dz\,du\ge mk\log(1+\log(1+\tau))$. The potential $\varphi=A^{-1}\eta_b$ is odd in $z$, and for $X$ with $z_X>0$, with
$Y^*$ the reflection of $Y$ in $\{z=0\}$, $-\varphi(X)=\frac1{8\pi^2}\int_{z_Y>0}(|X-Y|^{-3}-|X-Y^*|^{-3})\vartheta(Y)\,dY$. For $X\in K$ and $Y\in\Omega$ the bracket is at least
$6z_Xz_Y|X-Y^*|^{-5}\ge\frac{9z_X}{2\cdot6^5}r_Y^{-4}$ (use $|X-Y^*|\le|X|+|Y|\le6r_Y$ and $z_Y\ge\frac34r_Y$), so, using the
angular integration identity $\int_\Omega\vartheta r^{-4}\,dY=\pi Q(\tau)$ and $r_Xz_X\ge4$, $-r_X\varphi(X)\ge\frac9{4\pi6^5}Q(\tau)$ on $K$. Therefore the memory work is
$W_T=-\int_0^T\!\!\int F\,r\varphi\,dx\,d\tau=-2\int_0^T\!\!\int_{z>0}h\,r(-\varphi)\,dx\,d\tau\le-\frac{9m^2k}{2\pi6^5}\int_0^T\log(1+\log(1+\tau))\,d\tau$, and with $\mathcal I_0(T)=2mT$ and the
bound for $m$, $|W_T|\ge c_0\,G^2\mathcal I_0(T)\log(1+\log(1+T/2))$ for all $T>0$, with $c_0=1/145118822400$. In this example $W_T\le0$: the integrated
memory work is decompressive (each sign of the produced vorticity that survives is carried away from the source in its own cone). The example
rules out a time-uniform estimate of the absolute memory work based only on $0\le q^2\le G^2r^{-4}$ and on properties shared by all smooth axisymmetric
divergence-free Lipschitz drifts (among them the contraction of all $L^p(dx)$ norms and smoothing estimates without spatial derivatives, uniform in $b$).
It does not refute a one-sided bound $W\le\gamma\,G^2\mathcal I+O(M_n^2/D_n)$, which is the form relevant to Lemma 29(b). The prescribed swirl is independent of
the drift (a smooth compactly supported steady solution of $b\cdot\nabla\Gamma=(\partial_r^2-\frac1r\partial_r+\partial_z^2)\Gamma$ with $\Gamma=0$ on the axis vanishes by the strong maximum
principle), and the drift has infinite kinetic energy ($\int_\Omega|b|^2dx\ge\pi\int_1^\infty c^2\,dr=\infty$). The compressive part can also be made large
(next paragraph); whether further structure (transport of the swirl by the same drift, the Navier-Stokes coupling, or a bound on a scale-invariant energy; finite energy alone does not suffice, by the truncation after the next example) restores a bound
is not settled by these examples.
The positive part of the memory work is not bounded either when the window is long in units of the Lipschitz time. There are a steady $f$ in
the model class with $G=1$, a steady smooth bounded globally Lipschitz drift $b_*$, divergence-free in $\mathbb R^3$, and constants $c,\kappa>0$ such that for
the drifts $b_M=Mb_*$ and the horizons $T_M=\exp(e^{\kappa M/4})$ one has $W_{T_M}\ge c\,\mathcal I_0(T_M)\log N_M/\log\log N_M$ for all large $M$, where
$N_M=\|\nabla b_M\|_\infty T_M\to\infty$. Replacing $f$ by $G^2f$ multiplies $W$ by $G^4$ and $\mathcal I_0$ by $G^2$. The parabolic rescaling with $\lambda^2=\|\nabla b_M\|_\infty$
(drift $\lambda^{-1}b_M(X/\lambda)$, lift $\lambda^{-4}f(X/\lambda)$, time $\lambda^2t$) keeps the model class and leaves $W$, $\mathcal I_0$ and $\|b\|_\infty^2/\|\nabla b\|_\infty$ unchanged, so for drifts of
Lipschitz constant $1$ no bound $W_T\le\gamma G^2\mathcal I_0(T)+C+C\|b\|_\infty^2/\|\nabla b\|_\infty$ holds with $\gamma$ and $C$ independent of the drift, of the source in the model class and of the window length (the
rescaled sources have supports $\lambda_M\operatorname{supp}f$, and for the unrescaled family the source is fixed; here
$\|b_M\|_\infty^2/\|\nabla b_M\|_\infty=O(M)$, while $\mathcal I_0(T_M)=T_M\|F\|_{L^1}$).

Proof ($G=1$). The window starts at time $0$, with $\eta_b(0)=0$. Source. Let $a\in C_c^\infty((49.99,50.01))$ with $0\le a\le1$ and $a\ne0$, let $g$ be smooth, even and nonincreasing on $[0,\infty)$ with $g=1$ on $[0,9.99]$ and
$g=0$ on $[10.01,\infty)$, and let $f=11^{-4}a(r^2/2)g(z)$. Then $0\le f\le r^{-4}$, and $F=\partial_zf$ is nonnegative on its part $P_-$ near $(10,-10)$ and nonpositive on its
part $P_+$ near $(10,10)$.

Observation kernel. Let $\bar n(r,z;s,w)$ be the average over $\omega\in S^3$ of $(r^2+s^2-2rs\omega_1+(z-w)^2)^{-3/2}$. For the produced vorticity $\eta_b$ and the
measure $d\mu=2\pi s\,\eta_b(s,w)\,ds\,dw$, the Newton kernel of $\mathbb R^5$ gives $\varphi(r,z)=\frac1{8\pi}\int s^2\bar n(r,z;s,w)\,d\mu(s,w)$; integrating by parts in $z$,
$W_T=\int_0^T\!\int fr\partial_z\varphi\,dx\,d\tau=\int_0^T\!\int K\,d\mu_\tau\,d\tau$ with
$K(s,w)=\frac{s^2}4\int fr^2\partial_z\bar n\,dr\,dz=\frac{s^2}4\int_{r,z>0}r^2(-\partial_zf)\,[\bar n(r,z;s,w)-\bar n(r,-z;s,w)]\,dr\,dz$. (The average $\bar n$ has a logarithmic singularity on the diagonal $(r,z)=(s,w)$, $s>0$; the integration by parts is justified by regularizing the Newton kernel,
whose first derivatives are locally integrable.) So $K$ is odd in $w$, positive for $s>0$, $w>0$, and $K(0,w)=0$.
Since $a^{-3/2}-(a+d)^{-3/2}\ge\frac32d(a+d)^{-5/2}$, the bracket is at least $6zw((r+s)^2+(z+w)^2)^{-5/2}$ before averaging; on $C_+=\{s>12,\ |w/s-1|<\frac18\}$ one has $w\ge\frac78s$
and $(r+s)^2+(z+w)^2\le9s^2$ on the support of $F$, so $K\ge k_0s^{-2}$ there with $k_0=\frac7{1296}\int_{r,z>0}r^2z(-\partial_zf)\,dr\,dz>0$. Finally
$K(s,w)=s^2\int_{\mathbb R^5}r_X^{-1}f(X)\,\partial_{z_X}N_5(X-Y)\,dX$ with $Y=(se_1,w)$ and $N_5(Z)=(8\pi^2|Z|^3)^{-1}$, where $f/r_X$ is smooth with compact support, so
$|K|\le C(1+s^2+w^2)^{-1}$ by the local integrability and the decay $|Z|^{-4}$ of $\nabla N_5$.

Diffusion representation and a drift-independent bound. As in the first example, the density of $\mu_\tau$ for $dr\,dz$ evolves by the forward equation of
$\mathcal L$ (for the drift $b$) with source $2\pi rF$; the diffusion $Y$ with generator $\mathcal L$ is killed at the axis, and $K(Y_u)=0$ after killing. For the steady
source this gives $W_T=\int\big(\int_0^T(T-u)\,\mathbb E_\xi K(Y_u)\,du\big)F\,dx$, with $\xi$ the meridional point of $x$. The forward operator
$\Delta_5-b\cdot\nabla=\Delta_3+\frac2r\partial_r-b\cdot\nabla$ satisfies $\int vPv\,dx=-\int|\nabla v|^2dx-2\pi\int v(0,z)^2dz$, its adjoint for $dx$, $\Delta_3-\frac2r\partial_r+b\cdot\nabla$ with zero values
on the axis, satisfies $\int vP^*v\,dx=-\int|\nabla v|^2dx$, and both semigroups are contractions of $L^1(dx)$ (the mass of a nonnegative density does not increase,
and the maximum principle holds for the forward equation). Nash's inequality $\|v\|_2^{10/3}\le C\|v\|_1^{4/3}\|\nabla v\|_2^2$ on $\mathbb R^3$ gives $L^1\to L^2$ bounds $Ct^{-3/4}$
for both, so the transition density of $Y$ for $dx$ is at most $Ct^{-3/2}$, with $C$ independent of $b$. (These statements are first proved on the domains
$\{r>\epsilon,\ |x|<R\}$ with Dirichlet conditions, where $\operatorname{div}_3(\frac2re_r-b)=0$ makes the two operators adjoint, the maximum principle and duality give both
contractions, and $\frac d{dt}\|v\|_2^2=-2\|\nabla v\|_2^2$, with constants independent of $\epsilon$, $R$ and $b$. In the limit the diffusion of the forward operator does not reach the axis,
since $(\Delta_3+\frac2r\partial_r-b\cdot\nabla)r^{-2}=2b^rr^{-3}\le2\|\nabla b\|_\infty r^{-2}$, while the diffusion of the adjoint is killed there.) Splitting at $|x|=\sqrt t$ and using mass contraction outside,
$\sup_\xi\mathbb E_\xi|K(Y_t)|\le C/(1+t)$ and $\sup_\xi\mathbb E_\xi\int_0^T|K(Y_u)|\,du\le C\log(1+T)$; by the strong Markov property, paths restarted at a stopping time
contribute at most this per unit mass. (In particular $|W_T|\le C\mathcal I_0(T)\log(1+T)$ for this source and every divergence-free drift; this does not exclude unbounded growth
for one fixed drift, but the lower bound below uses a family of drifts.)

Routing drift. Let $\eta_0$ be an even smooth cutoff, equal to $1$ on $[-\frac14,\frac14]$ and supported in $(-\frac12,\frac12)$, let $V(\zeta)=\eta_0(\zeta-1)(\zeta-1)+\eta_0(\zeta+1)(\zeta+1)$, let
$\chi_0$ be smooth with $\chi_0=0$ on $(0,\frac12]$ and $\chi_0=1$ on $[1,\infty)$, and let $B$ be the drift with stream function $-r\chi_0(r)V(z/r)$. It is smooth, bounded,
globally Lipschitz and divergence-free in $\mathbb R^3$, and $B=(\frac1r,\pm\frac1r)$ in the cone cores $\{r\ge1,\ |z/r\mp1|\le\frac14\}$. Let $h$ be the diffeomorphism of the
$(p,z)$ plane, $p=r^2/2$, that in polar coordinates $(\varrho,\theta)$ about $(50,0)$ is $(\varrho,\theta)\mapsto(\varrho,\theta+\vartheta_0(\varrho))$, with $\vartheta_0$ smooth, $\vartheta_0=\pi$ for $\varrho\le11$ and
$\vartheta_0=0$ for $\varrho\ge12$; it preserves area, so the corresponding map $H$ of the meridional half plane preserves $r\,dr\,dz$. It moves only points with $38\le p\le62$,
so $H$ is the identity for $r\ge12$, and $H(r,z)=(\sqrt{200-r^2},-z)$ near the support of $F$. Let $b_*=(DH)^{-1}B\circ H$: it is smooth, bounded, globally Lipschitz with
some constant $L_*$, divergence-free in $\mathbb R^3$, equal to $B$ for $r\ge12$, and its flow is $H^{-1}\Phi^s_BH$. In the upper core the flow of $B$ moves points by
$r(s)^2=r(0)^2+2s$ with $z-r$ constant (in the lower core $z+r$ is constant). $H$ maps $P_-$ into the upper core near $(10,10)$ and $P_+$ into the lower core near
$(10,-10)$, so at flow time $400$ the $b_*$-images of $P_-$ and $P_+$ lie near $(30,30)$ and $(30,-30)$, at distance more than $\frac1{10}$ inside
$E_\pm=\{29\le r\le31,\ |z/r\mp1|\le\frac1{32}\}$, and the paths stay in $\{r\ge8\}$.

Routing step. For the drift $b_M$ and $\tau_M=400/M$, in the time variable $s=Mt$ the diffusion solves $dY=b_*(Y)\,ds-(MR)^{-1}e_r\,ds+\sqrt{2/M}\,d\beta_s$ for a
Brownian motion $\beta$. By Gronwall's inequality, up to the first time $\theta_4$ at which $R\le4$ and up to $s=400$, its distance from the $b_*$-path from the same point is at most
$e^{400L_*}(\sqrt{2/M}\sup_{s\le400}|\beta_s|+100/M)$. When this is less than $\frac1{20}$, $\theta_4$ does not occur before $s=400$ (the $b_*$-path stays in $\{r\ge8\}$) and the path is in
$E_\pm$ at time $\tau_M$. So the probability that a path started in $P_\mp$ is not in $E_\pm$ at time $\tau_M$ is at most $\delta_M\le Ce^{-c_{\rm r}M}$ for large $M$, with a fixed $c_{\rm r}>0$.

Cone survival. In $C_+$ one has $b_M=M(\frac1r,\frac1r)$ and $\mathcal L=\partial_r^2+\partial_z^2+\frac{M-1}r\partial_r+\frac Mr\partial_z$. Direct computation gives $\mathcal Lr^2=2M$,
$\mathcal L\log r=(M-2)r^{-2}$ and $\mathcal Lr^{2-M}=0$, and with $Q=z/r-1$ and $\mathsf a(Q)=1+(1+Q)^2$, $r^2\mathcal LQ=3-(M-3)Q$ and $r^2|\nabla Q|^2=\mathsf a(Q)$. Let $M\ge12$,
$\beta=\frac1{64}$ and $V_M=e^{\beta MQ^2}$. Then $r^2\mathcal LV_M=V_M\,[2\beta M\mathsf a+4\beta^2M^2Q^2\mathsf a+2\beta MQ(3-(M-3)Q)]$; for $|Q|\le\frac18$ (where $\mathsf a\le\frac{145}{64}$, and
$6MQ\le\frac12M^2Q^2+18$) the bracket is at most $\beta M(7-\frac12MQ^2)$, and since $e^{\beta x}(7-\frac x2)\le7$ for $x\ge0$, $r^2\mathcal LV_M\le7\beta M$ in $C_+$. Let $\sigma$ be the exit
time from $C_+$ and $v_t=\int_0^{t\wedge\sigma}R_u^{-2}\,du$. From $\mathcal L\log r$ and Jensen's inequality, $\mathbb E_\xi v_t\le\log(1+2Mt/r_0^2)/(2(M-2))$, and $\mathbb E_\xi R_{t\wedge\sigma}^2\le r_0^2+2Mt$, where $r_0$ is the radius of $\xi$.
For $\xi\in E_+$ the harmonic function $(12/r)^{M-2}$ bounds the probability of leaving through $\{r=12\}$ by $(12/29)^{M-2}$, and since $V_M=e^{M/4096}$ on the lateral boundary
and $V_M\le e^{M/65536}$ on $E_+$, Itô's formula gives $\mathbb P_\xi(\sigma\le t)\le(\frac{12}{29})^{M-2}+e^{-M/4096}(e^{M/65536}+7\beta M\,\mathbb E_\xi v_t)\le C(1+\log(1+Mt))e^{-\kappa_{\rm c}M}$, $\kappa_{\rm c}=\frac{15}{65536}$.
Fix $0<\kappa\le\min\{c_{\rm r},\kappa_{\rm c}\}$; then $\delta_M\le Ce^{-\kappa M}$ and $\mathbb P_\xi(\sigma\le t)\le\varepsilon_{M,t}:=C(1+\log(1+Mt))e^{-\kappa M}$. By the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, if
$\varepsilon_{M,T}\le\frac12$ then $\mathbb E_\xi[R_u^{-2};u<\sigma]\ge(1-\varepsilon_{M,T})^2/(961+2Mu)$ for $u\le T$ and $\xi\in E_+$. The lower cone $C_-$ is the mirror image.

Assembly. Split each path at $\tau_M$, at the routing event and at its exit from $C_\pm$ after $\tau_M$. On $[0,\tau_M]$, $|K|\le C$, which contributes at least $-(C/M)\mathcal I_0(T)$.
Paths that are not routed (probability at most $\delta_M$) and paths after their exit contribute at least $-CT(\delta_M+\varepsilon_{M,T})\log(1+T)$ per unit of $|F|\,dx$, by the
drift-independent bound. Before its exit, a routed path from $P_-$ (where $F\ge0$) is in $C_+$, where $K>0$, and one from $P_+$ (where $F\le0$) is in $C_-$, where $K<0$; all
these contributions are nonnegative, and those with $u-\tau_M\le T/4$, where $T-u\ge T/2$ when $T\ge4\tau_M$, give at least
$\frac T2k_0(1-\delta_M)(1-\varepsilon_{M,T})^2\int_0^{T/4}(961+2Mu)^{-1}\,du$ per unit of $|F|\,dx$. Hence, for $T\ge1600/M$, $\varepsilon_{M,T}\le\frac12$ and $M$ so large that $\delta_M\le\frac12$,
$$\frac{W_T}{\mathcal I_0(T)}\ge\frac cM\log(1+cMT)-\frac CM-Ce^{-\kappa M}\big(1+\log(1+MT)\big)\log(1+T),$$
and at $T=T_M$ the last term is $O(e^{-\kappa M/2})$ while the first is at least $ce^{\kappa M/4}/M$. Since $\log N_M=e^{\kappa M/4}+O(\log M)$ and $\log\log N_M=\frac{\kappa M}4+o(1)$, this gives the claim. $\square$

The gain comes from routing: the vorticity produced at the lower step, which is positive, is carried into the upper cone, and the negative vorticity
of the upper step into the lower cone, so that each sign ends up on the side where the kernel $K$ has the same sign; in the previous example each
sign stays on its own side and the work is negative. As there, the swirl is prescribed independently of the drift and the drifts have infinite
kinetic energy.
Finite energy alone does not change these conclusions. Here the drift and the weight are steady, the weight is smooth with compact support away from the axis, and $\rho_F$ is the largest
distance from the origin of a point of $\operatorname{supp}F$. For a meridional drift $b=b^re_r+b^ze_z$ of the class let $\Psi(r,z)=\int_0^rs\,b^z(s,z)\,ds$ be its stream function vanishing on the axis, so that
$b^r=-\partial_z\Psi/r$ and $b^z=\partial_r\Psi/r$; since $\Psi/r^2=\int_0^1\theta\,b^z(\theta r,z)\,d\theta$ is smooth, $b=\operatorname{curl}((\Psi/r)e_\theta)$. Let $\chi_1$ be smooth on $[0,\infty)$ with $0\le\chi_1\le1$, $\chi_1=1$ on $[0,1]$ and
$\chi_1=0$ on $[2,\infty)$, and for $R>\rho_F$ let $b_R=\operatorname{curl}(\chi_1(|X|/R)(\Psi/r)e_\theta)$. Then $b_R$ is smooth, axisymmetric and divergence-free in $\mathbb R^3$, equal to $b$ on $\{|X|\le R\}$ and zero outside
$\{|X|<2R\}$, so it has finite energy, and with $B_R=\sup_{|X|\le2R}|b|$ one has $\|b_R\|_\infty\le CB_R$ and $\|\nabla b_R\|_\infty\le\|\nabla b\|_\infty+CB_R/R$ (use $|\Psi|\le\frac12r^2B_R$,
$\partial_r(\Psi/r)=b^z-\Psi/r^2$ and $\partial_z(\Psi/r)=-b^r$). Drive the diffusions of $\mathcal L$ for $b$ and for $b_R$ by the same Brownian motion. By pathwise uniqueness for $r>0$ the two
paths coincide up to their first exit from $\{|X|<R\}$ or their common killing at the axis, so, with $p_R(T)$ the supremum over $\xi\in\operatorname{supp}F$ of the
probability that the path from $\xi$ reaches $\{|X|=R\}$ before time $T$ and before killing (paths that leave the ball and return are included in this event), the representation of $W_T$ in the proof above gives
$|W_T(b)-W_T(b_R)|\le\sup|K_f|\,\mathcal I_0(T)\,T\,p_R(T)$; here $K_f$ is the observation kernel of that proof (written $K$ there) formed with the weight $f$ under consideration, which is bounded for every such weight, with $\sup|K_f|\le CG^2$ for the weight of the
first example. Since $\mathcal L(r^2+z^2)=2+2(rb^r+zb^z)$, Itô's formula stopped at the exit from $\{|X|<R\}$ and at killing (first also at $\{r=\epsilon\}$, then $\epsilon\to0$), with Jensen's inequality in the first example and Gronwall's inequality otherwise, bounds the expectation of $r^2+z^2$ at the stopped time, and
$p_R(T)$ is at most this bound divided by $R^2$. In the first example $|X||b|\le C\log(e+|X|)$, which gives $p_R(T)\le C(1+T)(1+\log(1+T))R^{-2}$; with $R(T)=100(1+T)^2$ the error is
at most $CG^2\mathcal I_0(T)(1+\log(1+T))(1+T)^{-2}$, so $|W_T(b_{R(T)})|\ge\frac{c_0}2G^2\mathcal I_0(T)\log(1+\log(1+T/2))$ for all large $T$, the drifts $b_{R(T)}$ have velocity and
Lipschitz constant bounded independently of $T$ (on the cutoff shell $|b_R|\le C(1+\log R)/R$ and $|\nabla b_R|\le C(1+\log R)/R^2$), and $W_T(b_{R(T)})\le0$ exactly, because the cutoff is even in $z$, so
that $b_R^r$ stays even and $b_R^z$ odd and the maximum-principle and reflection argument of the first example applies unchanged. In the second example, for $M\ge1$, $|X||b_M|\le CM$, which gives $p_R(T)\le C(1+MT)R^{-2}$; with
$R_M=100(1+M)(1+T_M)^2$ the error is at most $C\mathcal I_0(T_M)(1+M)^{-1}(1+T_M)^{-2}$, so $W_{T_M}(b_M^{(R_M)})\ge c\,\mathcal I_0(T_M)\log N_M/\log\log N_M$ for large $M$ (here
$b_M^{(R_M)}$ is the truncation of $b_M$), and since $\|\nabla b_M^{(R_M)}\|_\infty$ is comparable to $\|\nabla b_M\|_\infty$ (the derivatives on the cutoff shell are $O(M/R_M^2)$, because $H$ preserves $r\,dr\,dz$ and moves only points of a compact subset of $\{r>0\}$, the stream function of $b_*$ vanishing on the axis is $-r\chi_0(r)V(z/r)$ composed with $H$, which equals that of $B$ near the shell) and $\|b_M^{(R_M)}\|_\infty\le CM$, the
rescaled statement holds for compactly supported drifts (rescale with $\lambda^2=\|\nabla b_M^{(R_M)}\|_\infty$). For a general meridional drift of the class, $\mathcal L(1+r^2+z^2)\le a_b(1+r^2+z^2)$ with $a_b=3+|b(0)|^2+2\|\nabla b\|_\infty$, so
$p_R(T)\le(1+\rho_F^2)e^{a_bT}R^{-2}$, and $R(T)=R_0e^{(a_b+1)T/2}$ with $R_0>\rho_F$ makes the error $O(Te^{-T})\,\mathcal I_0(T)$. At these velocity and Lipschitz scales the energies of the truncations tend to infinity with the radius
($\|b_R\|_{L^2}^2$ is comparable to $R(1+\log R)^2$ in the first example and to $M^2R$ for $b_M$, for large $R$, with constants independent of $R$ and $M$, by the shell bounds and the cone cores inside the ball). The parabolic rescaling above multiplies the energy by $\lambda$
and leaves $W$, $\mathcal I_0$, $G$ and the window length in Lipschitz units unchanged, so with $\lambda=\|b_R\|_{L^2}^{-2}$ both examples become compactly supported families of energy one (with Lipschitz constants
tending to infinity and varying sources), and neither $|W_T|\le C(G,\|b\|_{L^2})G^2\mathcal I_0(T)$ nor $\max\{W_T,0\}\le C(G,\|b\|_{L^2})G^2\mathcal I_0(T)$ holds uniformly over compactly supported drifts of energy one and weights of the model class (the first example excludes the first inequality, the second example the second). The quantity $\|b\|_{L^2}^2\|\nabla b\|_\infty^{1/2}$ is invariant under the rescaling and is
unbounded along these families. We have no counterexample with one fixed finite-energy drift, or with simultaneous bounds on the energy and the Lipschitz constant; bounds depending on
$\|b\|_{L^2}^2\|\nabla b\|_\infty^{1/2}$ are not excluded. For the Navier-Stokes velocity at a velocity first crossing, $|u|\ge M_n/2$ on a ball of radius $M_n/(2D_n)$ (as in the proof of Lemma 2) gives
$\|u(t_n)\|_{L^2}^2D_n^{1/2}\ge\frac\pi{24}(M_n^2/D_n)^{5/2}$, which tends to infinity in regime (II).
Together the two examples show that, for general divergence-free Lipschitz drifts, compactly supported ones included (also with energy one), neither the absolute value nor the positive
part of the memory work is bounded by a multiple of $G^2\mathcal I$ uniformly over these drifts and sources as the window length measured in Lipschitz times tends to infinity
(the second example uses a family of drifts, not one fixed drift, and the compactly supported versions of both examples are families). A bound for the true propagator therefore has to use properties that these examples
do not have: for the true solution $f=q^2$ is transported by the same drift and satisfies
$\partial_tf+u\cdot\nabla f=\Delta_5f-2|\nabla q|^2-4\frac{u^r}rf$, and the drift is generated by the vorticity it carries. Finite energy of the drift is not such a property: the truncated examples have it, even with energy one. Quantitative control of a scale-invariant quantity that is unbounded along the rescaled truncated families, such as $\|b\|_{L^2}^2\|\nabla b\|_\infty^{1/2}$, would be one. Which of these properties suffice is not settled here.
Two classes of drifts do admit bounds, provided the weight also satisfies $f\le D^2$ for a constant $D>0$ (for the Navier-Stokes solution
$|u^\theta|\le\|\nabla u\|_\infty r$, so this holds with $D$ the supremum of $\|\nabla u(t)\|_\infty$ over the window). The drifts are, as above, smooth, axisymmetric, divergence-free in $\mathbb R^3$ and
globally Lipschitz, and the sign conditions on $b^r$ below are required at all times of the window and at all points. In both classes the source $F\in L^1(dx\,d\tau)$ producing $\eta_b$ is arbitrary (it need not be $\partial_zf$). Let $W^+_T=\int_0^T\!\int f\,(u^r_{\rm new})_-\,dx\,d\tau$ with $u^r_{\rm new}=-r\partial_z\varphi$ be the inward part of the memory
work, so that $W_T\le W^+_T$ when $F=\partial_zf$ (for another source $-\int_0^T\!\int f\,u^r_{\rm new}\,dx\,d\tau$ takes the place of $W_T$ and is also at most $W^+_T$), and let $a=(G/D)^{1/2}$ (with $G,D>0$). (i) If $b^r\le0$ everywhere (inflow), then for every weight $0\le f\le\bar f(r):=\min\{D^2,G^2r^{-4}\}$,
$W^+_T\le\int_0^T\!\int\mathcal H(r)|F|\,dx\,d\tau$, where $\mathcal H(\rho)=DG\rho^2(\frac38+\frac14\log\frac\ell\rho)$ for
$\rho\le\ell=\frac{28}3a$ and $\mathcal H(\rho)=\frac12DG\ell\rho-\frac18DG\ell^2$ for $\rho\ge\ell$; in particular $W^+_T\le(\frac38+\frac14\log\frac{28}3)G^2\mathcal I_0(T)\le0.934\,G^2\mathcal I_0(T)$ if $F$ vanishes
for $r>a$, uniformly in $T$ and $D$. (ii) If $b^r\ge0$ everywhere (outflow) and the swirl is transported by the same drift, that is, $f=\Gamma^2r^{-4}$ with
$\partial_t\Gamma+b\cdot\nabla\Gamma=(\partial_r^2-\frac1r\partial_r+\partial_z^2)\Gamma$, $\Gamma=0$ on the axis and $|\Gamma(0)|\le\min\{G,Dr^2\}$, then
$W^+_T\le\frac{G^2}8\big(1+\log^+\frac{4DT}G\big)\mathcal I_0(T)$.

Proof. (i) For a signed $g$ and $a_g=-\partial_zA^{-1}g$, the kernel of $-\partial_{z_X}A^{-1}$ is $3(z_X-z_Y)(8\pi^2)^{-1}|X-Y|^{-5}$; the negative part of $a_g(X)$ is at most the
integral over $Y$ of the negative parts, and integrating $f\le\bar f$ over the half line in $z_X$ on which a given $Y$ contributes ($\int_0^\infty3\zeta(d^2+\zeta^2)^{-5/2}d\zeta=d^{-3}$) gives
$\int f\,(ra_g)_-\,dx\le\int|g|\,\mathcal K(r)\,dx$ with $\mathcal K(\rho)=\frac{\rho^2}{8\pi^2}\int_{\mathbb R^4}\bar f(|y|)|y|^{-1}|y-\rho e|^{-3}\,d^4y$, $e$ a unit vector. Since $r^2\bar f\le DG$ and $\int_{\mathbb R^4}|y|^{-3}|y-\rho e|^{-3}d^4y=4\pi^2\rho^{-2}$,
$\mathcal K\le\frac12DG$; for $\rho\ge a$, splitting at $|y|=\rho/2$ and using $\int_{\mathbb R^4}\bar f(|y|)|y|^{-1}d^4y=\frac{8\pi^2}3G^2a^{-1}$ on the inner part and $r^2\bar f\le4G^2\rho^{-2}$ on the outer part,
$\mathcal K\le\frac{8G^2}{3a\rho}+\frac{2G^2}{\rho^2}\le\frac{14G^2}{3a\rho}$. Hence $\mathcal K\le k(\rho):=\frac12DG\min\{1,\ell/\rho\}$. The function $\mathcal H(\rho)=\frac12\int_0^\rho sk(s)\,ds+\frac{\rho^2}2\int_\rho^\infty k(s)s^{-1}ds$ is the one
displayed; it is nonnegative and increasing, of class $C^1$ on $[0,\infty)$ and $C^2$ on $(0,\infty)$ (not $C^2$ at $0$, where $\mathcal H''\to\infty$), with $\mathcal H''-\mathcal H'/\rho=-k$ for $\rho>0$, so $\mathcal L\mathcal H=-k+b^r\mathcal H'\le-k$ when $b^r\le0$. By positivity of the propagator, Duhamel's formula and
duality, $W^+_T\le\int_0^T\!\int|F(\sigma)|\,\mathbb E_{\xi,\sigma}\big[\int_\sigma^{T\wedge T_0}k(R_\tau)\,d\tau\big]\,dx\,d\sigma$, where $(R,Z)$ is the diffusion with generator $\mathcal L$ started at the meridional point $\xi$
of $x$ at time $\sigma$ and $T_0$ is its hitting time of the axis; Itô's formula for $\mathcal H(R)$, stopped near the axis and at large radius, and Fatou's lemma bound the
expectation by $\mathcal H(r)$. Finally $\mathcal H(a)=G^2(\frac38+\frac14\log\frac{28}3)$. The sign of $b^r$ entered only through $b^r\mathcal H'\le0$. Since $\mathcal H'(\rho)=\frac12DG\rho(1+\log\frac\ell\rho)$ for $\rho\le\ell$ and
$\mathcal H'=\frac12DG\ell$ for $\rho\ge\ell$, the weaker condition $r(b^r)_+\le\theta(r):=\big(2(1+\log^+\frac\ell r)\big)^{-1}$ everywhere (inflow of any strength, outflow of small
radial Reynolds number) gives $b^r\mathcal H'\le\frac12k$, hence $\mathcal L\mathcal H\le-\frac12k$ and $W^+_T\le2\int_0^T\!\int\mathcal H(r)|F|\,dx\,d\tau$, which is at most $1.867\,G^2\mathcal I_0(T)$ if $F$ vanishes for $r>a$. For any drift as above, without a sign condition on $b^r$, $\mathcal H'(\rho)=\rho k/(2\theta)$ for $\rho>0$ on both pieces gives $\mathcal L\mathcal H=-k+q_+-q_-$ with $q_\pm=k\,(rb^r)_\pm/(2\theta)\ge0$, and the same argument, in which the stopped identity $\mathbb E\mathcal H(R_{S_n})+\mathbb E\int(k+q_-)\,d\tau=\mathcal H(r)+\mathbb E\int q_+\,d\tau$ (with time integrals from $\sigma$ to $S_n$, the minimum of $T$ and of the exit time of $(R,Z)$ from $\{n^{-1}<r<n,\ |z|<n\}$) is used with $\mathbb E\mathcal H(R_{S_n})$ and $\mathbb E\int q_-\,d\tau$ discarded and monotone convergence is applied separately to the occupation integrals of $k$ and of $q_+$, gives
$W^+_T\le\int_0^T\!\int\mathcal H(r)|F|\,dx\,d\tau+\frac12\int_0^T\!\int|F(\sigma)|\,\mathbb E_{\xi,\sigma}\big[\int_\sigma^{T\wedge T_0}k(R_\tau)\,\theta(R_\tau)^{-1}(R_\tau b^r)_+\,d\tau\big]\,dx\,d\sigma$, with $b^r$ evaluated at $(R_\tau,Z_\tau,\tau)$ (the right side may be infinite): the excess over the bound for
inflowing drifts is at most the outflow occupation cost, weighted by $k(rb^r)_+/(2\theta)$, of the positive propagation of the absolute source $|F|$. (ii) $\Gamma(X,\tau)=\mathbb E[\Gamma(0,Y_\tau);T_0>\tau]$ for the diffusion $Y=(R,Z)$ with generator
$\partial_r^2+\partial_z^2-(b^r(\tau-s)+\frac1r)\partial_r-b^z(\tau-s)\partial_z$ started at $X$ and killed at the axis, so $|\Gamma(X,\tau)|\le G\,\mathbb P(T_0>\tau)$. When $b^r\ge0$, compare $R$ with the
solution of $d\tilde R=\sqrt2\,dB-\tilde R^{-1}ds$ driven by the same Brownian motion: $R-\tilde R$ has no martingale part and $\frac d{ds}(R-\tilde R)\le(R-\tilde R)/(R\tilde R)$, so $R\le\tilde R$ by
Gronwall's inequality before either reaches a small radius, and $\tilde R^2$ is a squared Bessel process of dimension $0$ at double speed; hence
$\mathbb P(T_0>\tau)\le1-e^{-r^2/(4\tau)}\le r^2/(4\tau)$. Also $|\Gamma|\le Dr^2$ persists: the generator of $Y$ applied to $r^2$ gives $-2rb^r\le0$, so $R^2$, set equal to $0$ after $T_0$, is a nonnegative supermartingale (by localization and Fatou's lemma), and
$|\Gamma(X,\tau)|\le D\,\mathbb E[R_\tau^2;T_0>\tau]\le Dr^2$. So
$r^2f\le\min\{D^2r^2,G^2r^{-2},G^2r^2/(16\tau^2)\}\le m(\tau):=\min\{DG,G^2/(4\tau)\}$, and the first step of (i) with $\bar f$ replaced by $m(\tau)r^{-2}$ gives
$\int f\,(u^r_{\rm new})_-\,dx\le\frac12m(\tau)\|\eta_b(\tau)\|_{L^1}\le\frac12m(\tau)\mathcal I_0(T)$ by the $L^1$ contraction. Since $\int_0^Tm\,d\tau=\frac{G^2}4(\min\{x,1\}+\log^+x)$ with $x=4DT/G$, the bound
follows. $\square$
In (i) a unit of source mass injected at radius $r$ contributes at most $\mathcal H(r)$ to the bound for the inward work, through the expected occupation cost
$\mathbb E\int k(R_\tau)\,d\tau$ up to absorption at the axis, whatever the window length. In (ii) the backward radial paths stay below the drift-free paths driven by the same
Brownian motion, so they reach the axis, where $\Gamma=0$, no later; then $\Gamma^2r^{-2}\le G^2/(4\tau)$, and the logarithm is the time integral of this bound. The argument does not show whether the logarithm in (ii) is needed. Apart from the outflow charge, neither statement covers drifts that have both inflow and outflow
violating $r(b^r)_+\le\theta(r)$, which is what both examples above use. The two global sign conditions are conditions on drifts of infinite energy: if the meridional drift $b=b^re_r+b^ze_z$ is in $L^2(\mathbb R^3)$
and $b^r\le0$ everywhere (or $b^r\ge0$ everywhere), then $b=0$. Indeed, for $\psi\ge0$ smooth, nonincreasing in $r$ and compactly supported, and a cutoff $\chi\ge0$ equal to $1$ near $0$, the divergence condition gives
$\int b^r\psi'(r)\chi(z/R)\,dx=-R^{-1}\int b^z\psi(r)\chi'(z/R)\,dx=O(\|b\|_2R^{-1/2})$; the left integrand has one sign, so letting $R\to\infty$ gives $b^r\psi'=0$, hence $b^r=0$, then
$\partial_zb^z=0$ and $b^z=0$. So (i) and (ii) apply to model drifts such as the linear strain above (with $\alpha\ge0$ at all times for (i) and $\alpha\le0$ for (ii)), but not to a nonzero meridional drift of finite energy; the weak-outflow condition has no such
restriction (it holds for small multiples of any smooth compactly supported drift, since $rb^r=O(r^2)$ at the axis).
Without a sign condition on $b^r$ there is a bound in terms of the initial circulation mass. Suppose that the swirl is transported as in (ii), with
$|\Gamma(0)|\le G$ and $\Gamma(0)\in L^1(dx)$, and that $|\Gamma|\le Dr^2$ at all times (for the Navier-Stokes solution this is the cap above). Then, with an absolute constant $C$
and $t_*=(C\|\Gamma(0)\|_{L^1}/G)^{2/3}$, $W^+_T\le\frac32DGt_*\,\mathcal I_0(T)$, that is $W^+_T\le C'G^2\Lambda\,\mathcal I_0(T)$ with the scale-invariant number
$\Lambda=\frac DG(\|\Gamma(0)\|_{L^1}/G)^{2/3}$; if moreover $b^r\ge0$, $W^+_T\le\frac{G^2}8(\frac53+\log^+\frac{4Dt_*}G)\mathcal I_0(T)$. Proof. The operator $Q_b=\partial_r^2-\frac1r\partial_r+\partial_z^2-b\cdot\nabla$ of
the swirl equation, with zero values on the axis, and its adjoint for $dx$, $\Delta_5+b\cdot\nabla$ with the regular axis condition, satisfy $\int uQ_bu\,dx=-\|\nabla u\|_2^2$ and
$\int vQ_b^*v\,dx=-\|\nabla v\|_2^2-2\pi\int v(0,z)^2dz$; both evolutions are positive and contract $L^1(dx)$, since $\int Q_bu\,dx=0$ and $\int Q_b^*v\,dx=-4\pi\int v(0,z)\,dz\le0$ for $u,v\ge0$.
With the same justification by exhaustion as in the paragraph on the diffusion representation in the proof of the second example (with $b$ replaced by $-b$, since $\Delta_5-b\cdot\nabla$ is $Q_{-b}^*$), Nash's inequality gives $L^1\to L^2$ bounds $Ct^{-3/4}$ for both evolutions, uniformly over time-dependent drifts. The adjoint of the evolution of $Q_b$ from time $s$ to time $t$ is the evolution of $Q_b^*$ run with the reversed coefficient path $\tau\mapsto b(t-\tau)$, so it obeys the same bound; hence by duality and
composition at the midpoint $\|\Gamma(t)\|_\infty\le\min\{G,C\|\Gamma(0)\|_{L^1}t^{-3/2}\}$, with $C$ independent of $b$. With the cap, $r^2f=\Gamma^2r^{-2}\le D\|\Gamma(t)\|_\infty\le DG\min\{1,(t_*/t)^{3/2}\}$, and the first
step of (i) gives $W^+_T\le\frac12\mathcal I_0(T)\int_0^\infty DG\min\{1,(t_*/t)^{3/2}\}\,dt=\frac32DGt_*\mathcal I_0(T)$. If $b^r\ge0$, the proof of (ii) also gives $|\Gamma|\le Gr^2/(4t)$, so
$r^2f\le G\min\{1,(t_*/t)^{3/2}\}\min\{D,G/(4t)\}$, whose integral over $(0,\infty)$ is at most $\frac{G^2}4(\frac53+\log^+\frac{4Dt_*}G)$. $\square$
This bound is uniform in the window length whatever the sign of $b^r$, so in the coupled model $W^+_T/(G^2\mathcal I_0(T))\to\infty$ along a sequence of configurations and windows only if $\Lambda\to\infty$ along it; here
$\Lambda=(L_\Gamma/a)^2$ with $L_\Gamma=(\|\Gamma(0)\|_{L^1}/G)^{1/3}$ and $a=(G/D)^{1/2}$, so this requires a swirl reservoir whose size $L_\Gamma$ is large compared with $a$. Keeping the time integral up to $T$
gives $W^+_T\le\frac12DG(3t_*-2t_*^{3/2}T^{-1/2})\mathcal I_0(T)$ for $T>t_*$, which improves on the trivial bound $W^+_T\le\frac12DGT\,\mathcal I_0(T)$ exactly when $T>t_*$, that is, when the circulation mass at the
start of the window is less than $C^{-1}GT^{3/2}$; on the record windows of the main argument $T_n=t_n-s_n\to0$, so the improvement would need $\|\Gamma(s_n)\|_{L^1}<C^{-1}GT_n^{3/2}$,
which nothing in the argument provides.
Every nonzero bounded steady circulation has infinite circulation mass: if $b$ is a steady drift as above, with no sign condition on $b^r$, and $\Gamma$ is a bounded $C^2$ solution of $Q_b\Gamma=0$ for $r>0$, continuous up to the axis and zero there, with $\Gamma\in L^1(dx)$, then $\Gamma=0$, since Itô's formula for the diffusion generated by $Q_b$, killed at the axis (localized away from the axis and from infinity, then passing to the limit by boundedness), shows that $\Gamma$ equals its own evolution under the swirl equation, so that the drift-independent smoothing bound above gives $\|\Gamma\|_\infty\le C\|\Gamma\|_{L^1}t^{-3/2}$ for every $t>0$. Without the mass condition, a nonzero bounded steady circulation requires $\sup|X|\,|b(X)|=\infty$ (which does not require $|b|$ itself to be unbounded). Let $b$ be steady, smooth, axisymmetric and globally Lipschitz with
$M_b=\sup|X|\,|b(X)|<\infty$, where $|X|^2=r^2+z^2$ (divergence-free is not needed here), and let $\Gamma$ be bounded, continuous up to the axis, zero there, and a $C^2$ solution of $Q_b\Gamma=0$ for $r>0$. Then $\Gamma=0$.
Indeed, let $Y$ be the diffusion with generator $Q_b$ killed at the axis, write $r=R\sin\vartheta$, $z=R\cos\vartheta$ with $0<\vartheta<\pi$, and change time by $ds=dt/R^2$: the
radial part of $-\frac1r\partial_r$ cancels the first-order term of the planar Laplacian, and $d\log R=\sqrt2\,dB^1-(1+Rb_R)\,ds$, $d\vartheta=\sqrt2\,dB^2-(\cot\vartheta+Rb_\vartheta)\,ds$, where
$b_R,b_\vartheta$ are the polar components of $b$, so $|Rb_R|,|Rb_\vartheta|\le M_b$. The function $p(x)=\sin x\,e^{-M_bx}\int_x^{\pi/2}e^{M_bu}(\sin u)^{-1}du$ is nonnegative on $(0,\frac\pi2]$ and solves
$p'+(M_b-\cot x)p=-1$, so $V(\vartheta)=\int_0^{\min\{\vartheta,\pi-\vartheta\}}p(x)\,dx$ is bounded, of class $C^2$ on $(0,\pi)$ (since $p(\frac\pi2)=0$ and $p'(\frac\pi2)=-1$), and satisfies
$V''-\cot\vartheta\,V'+M_b|V'|=-1$; Itô's formula, stopped near $\{0,\pi\}$ and at bounded times, shows that the exit time $\sigma$ of $\vartheta$ from $(0,\pi)$ satisfies $\mathbb E\sigma\le V(\vartheta_0)$, and $\vartheta$ has a limit $0$ or $\pi$ at $\sigma$ (the coefficients of $d\cos\vartheta=-\sqrt2\sin\vartheta\,dB^2+Rb_\vartheta\sin\vartheta\,ds$ are bounded, and an interior limit would allow continuation).
Since $\log R$ has bounded drift, it stays finite up to $\sigma$, so $Y$ reaches the axis at the finite time $\int_0^\sigma R^2\,ds$, and the bounded martingale $\Gamma(Y)$ has terminal
value $0$; hence $\Gamma=0$. This excludes every compactly supported drift, but not inflow growing at infinity: the Burgers vortex $b=\alpha(-\frac r2,z)$ with $\alpha>0$, $\Gamma=G(1-e^{-\alpha r^2/4})$, is steady with $F=0$, and it can be perturbed into a steady example of class (i) with a nonzero source. Take $\alpha=2$, $G=1$, $D=4$ and $y=r^2/2$; then $Q_b=2y\partial_y^2+\partial_z^2-b^y\partial_y-b^z\partial_z$ with $b^y=rb^r=-\partial_z\Psi$ and $b^z=\partial_y\Psi$ for the stream function $\Psi$ ($\Psi=2yz$ for the Burgers drift). Let $w=y+\varepsilon\chi(y)\zeta(z)$ with $\chi\in C_c^\infty((0,\frac1{32}))$, $\zeta\in C_c^\infty(\mathbb R)$ and $\int\chi\,dy\int\zeta\,dz\ne0$, and $\Gamma=1-e^{-w}$. For $\Psi=S(w,z)$ one has $b\cdot\nabla\Gamma=-e^{-w}w_y\,\partial_zS$, so $Q_b\Gamma=0$ becomes $\partial_zS=J$ with $J=-(2y(w_{yy}-w_y^2)+w_{zz}-w_z^2)/w_y$ written in the variables $(w,z)$ (a change of coordinates for small $|\varepsilon|$), and $S$ is obtained by integrating in $z$ from below the support of $\zeta$, where $S=2wz$. For small $\varepsilon\ne0$ the drift is smooth and globally Lipschitz, equals the Burgers drift outside the perturbed set and the column above it, and has $b^r\le0$ everywhere; above the perturbed set $\Psi=2yz+C_\varepsilon(y)$ with $C_\varepsilon$ compactly supported, an additional bounded axial shear $C_\varepsilon'(y)$, which is not zero, since expanding $(J-2w)w_y$ and integrating by parts give $\int_0^\infty C_\varepsilon\,dy=-4\varepsilon\int\chi\int\zeta+\varepsilon^2\iint(2y((\chi\zeta)_y)^2+((\chi\zeta)_z)^2)\,dy\,dz$. Moreover $0\le\Gamma\le\min\{1,r^2\}\le\min\{1,4r^2\}$, and the source $\partial_z(\Gamma^2r^{-4})$ is nonzero and supported in $\{r^2<\frac1{16}\}\subset\{4r^2<1\}$. So this is a steady example of class (i). It is not a steady Navier-Stokes solution: above the perturbed set the meridional vorticity divided by $r$ is $-C_\varepsilon''(y)$, the source vanishes there, and the steady equation for it reduces to $2yC_\varepsilon''''+(2y+4)C_\varepsilon'''=0$, which forces $C_\varepsilon'''=Ke^{-y}y^{-2}$ and then, by the compact support, $C_\varepsilon=0$. For swirl carried without diffusion along the streamlines of a steady drift,
$\Gamma=\gamma(S)$ with $b^r=-\partial_zS/r$, $b^z=\partial_rS/r$, the source is $F=-H'(S)b^r/r^3=b\cdot\nabla(H'(S)/(2r^2))$ with $H=\gamma^2$, so along a trajectory of $\dot X=b(X)$ with $r\to\infty$ at both ends the time integral of $F$, taken as the sum of the two one-sided improper integrals, is zero; with diffusion, if $H\in C^3$ and $h_S=H'(S)/(2r^2)$, as a function on $\mathbb R^5$, is $C^2$ and bounded with bounded derivatives up to order two, then $F=\Delta_5h_S-(\Delta_5-b\cdot\nabla)h_S$ gives, for the propagator $U(\tau,s)$ of $\partial_t+b\cdot\nabla-\Delta_5$, $\eta_b(t)=\int_0^tU(t,s)F\,ds=h_S-U(t,0)h_S+\int_0^tU(t,s)\Delta_5h_S\,ds$, where $\Delta_5h_S=(2r^2)^{-1}\big(H'''(S)|\nabla S|^2+H''(S)\mathcal DS\big)$ with $\mathcal D=\partial_r^2-\frac1r\partial_r+\partial_z^2$ (from $\Delta_5(g\,r^{-2})=r^{-2}\mathcal Dg$); the last term is the vorticity produced by the steady source $\Delta_5h_S$. This is a pointwise identity; the corresponding work integrals require in addition the integrability of each term. The source $\Delta_5h_S$ vanishes on $\{S\in I\}$ if $H$ is affine on an open interval $I$ (the vorticity it produces need not vanish there), and the work of the last term over long windows is not estimated here.

**Proposition 36 (amplitude above the historical BMO norm gives constant limits).** Let
$U_n(y,\tau)=B_n^{-1}(u(x_n+L_ny+V_nT_n\tau,s_n+T_n\tau)-V_n)$ with $s_n\uparrow T_*$, $T_n>0$, $s_n/T_n\to\infty$, $B_n>0$, constant vectors $V_n$, and
$L_n=\operatorname{diag}(\ell_{\perp,n},\ell_{\perp,n},\ell_{z,n})$ of aspect ratio $\Lambda_n=\max(\ell_{\perp,n}/\ell_{z,n},\ell_{z,n}/\ell_{\perp,n})$, and suppose
$B_n/(\Theta_0(s_n)(1+\log_2\Lambda_n))\to\infty$, with $\Theta_0$ the historical $L^2$ mean oscillation of Proposition 14A. (a) If
$U_n(\cdot,\tau)\rightharpoonup U(\tau)$ weakly in $L^2_{loc}$ for some $\tau\le0$, then $U(\tau)$ is a.e. constant. (b) If this holds for a.e.
$\tau\in(-A,0]$ and $U$ is bounded and solves the limit equation with a pressure whose gradient vanishes on spatially constant
fields, $U$ coincides a.e. with one constant on $\mathbb R^3\times(-A,0)$, and $U(0)$ is that constant if $U$ is weakly continuous
in time at $0$ (an interior distributional equation does not determine an arbitrarily assigned value at $\tau=0$; if
$U_n(\cdot,0)\rightharpoonup U(0)$ weakly in $L^2_{loc}$, then $U(0)$ is spatially constant by (a)). (c) Under (FLAT$_0$) the hypothesis holds for every amplitude-$M$ normalization of
bounded aspect ratio, in any constant Galilean frame and at any centre.

Proof. In this proof and in that of Theorem 37, $\operatorname{osc}_Ef=(\fint_E|f-(f)_E|^2dx)^{1/2}$ is the $L^2$ mean oscillation on $E$ (the quantity $O_0$ of Proposition 14A when $E$ is a ball), not the pointwise oscillation of Lemma 2. For $f$ with $\beta=\sup_{x,\rho}\operatorname{osc}_{B_\rho(x)}f<\infty$ and an ellipsoid $E=x_0+L B_1$ of aspect ratio $\Lambda$, $\operatorname{osc}_E(f)\le
C(1+\log_2\Lambda)\beta$: cover $E$ by the cubes of side $\min(\ell_\perp,\ell_z)$ of a lattice meeting it (their union $Q$ has $|Q|\le(48/\pi)|E|$
and lies in a ball of radius $N\min(\ell_\perp,\ell_z)$, $N\le4\Lambda$); the dyadic chain $|(f)_{B_\rho}-(f)_{B_{2\rho}}|\le\sqrt8\operatorname{osc}_{B_{2\rho}}f$ compares
each cube mean with the mean over a ball of radius $5N\min(\ell_\perp,\ell_z)$ with error $C(1+\log_2N)\beta$, and
$\operatorname{osc}_E f\le(|Q|/|E|)^{1/2}\operatorname{osc}_Qf$. By the change of variables, $\operatorname{osc}_{B_\rho(y_0)}U_n(\tau)=B_n^{-1}\operatorname{osc}_E u(s)$ for an ellipsoid $E$ of
aspect ratio $\Lambda_n$ and $s\le s_n$, which is at most $C(1+\log_2\Lambda_n)\Theta_0(s_n)/B_n\to0$; weak lower semicontinuity of the
oscillation gives (a). (b) is Theorem 4(b) in this generality. (c) is the definition of (FLAT$_0$). $\square$

So under (FLAT$_0$) GOAL can be met only at amplitudes $B_n\le C\Theta_0(s_n)(1+\log_2\Lambda_n)$; Proposition 14A(c) is the isotropic,
amplitude-$M$, fixed-frame case.

**Theorem 37 (extraction at the BMO amplitude and its Lipschitz scale).** Assume regime (II). For $R\ge1$ let
$a(s)=\Theta_0(s)/D(s)$ and $\Theta_0^{(R)}(t)=\sup\{O_0(x,s,\rho):s\le t,\ \rho\le Ra(s)\}$, with $O_0$ of Proposition 14A, and suppose
(C$_\Theta$): $\Theta_0^{(R)}(t_n)\ge c\Theta_0(t_n)$ for some $R\ge1$, $c\in(0,1]$ and $t_n\uparrow T_*$. Choose $s_n\le t_n$, $x_n$ and $\rho_n\le Ra(s_n)$
with $O_0(x_n,s_n,\rho_n)\ge\frac c2\Theta_n$, $\Theta_n=\Theta_0(t_n)$, put $\ell_n=Ra(s_n)$, $T_n=\ell_n/\Theta_n$, $V_n=\int\phi_{\ell_n}(x-x_n)u(x,s_n)\,dx$ for a
fixed radial mollifier $\phi$, and $v_n(y,\tau)=\Theta_n^{-1}[u(x_n+\ell_ny+V_nT_n\tau,s_n+T_n\tau)-V_n]$. Then $\nu_n=T_n/\ell_n^2=D(s_n)/(R\Theta_0(s_n)\Theta_n)$
is bounded, and along a subsequence $\nu_n\to\nu\in[0,\infty)$ and $v_n\to v$ locally uniformly on $\mathbb R^3\times(-\infty,0]$, where
$\|\nabla v\|_\infty\le R$, the $L^2$ mean oscillation of $v(\tau)$ is at most $1$ on every ball, $v(0)$ has $L^2$ mean oscillation at least $c/2$ on a
ball of radius in $[c_0/R,1]$ with $c_0=\frac c2(5/3)^{1/2}$, and $v$ solves $\partial_\tau v+\nabla\cdot(v\otimes v)+\Pi[v]=\nu\Delta v$, $\operatorname{div}v=0$, with $\Pi$ the increment
integral of Lemma 3. After a constant horizontal boost $v$ is axisymmetric about a vertical line with circulation at most
$G\nu$, or it is invariant in a horizontal direction. Consequently, GOAL holds for this explicitly selected normalization if
and only if some cluster limit is bounded on $\mathbb R^3\times(-\infty,0]$ (then $\Pi[v]$ is the canonical pressure gradient).

Proof. The bounds $|\nabla v_n|\le\ell_nD(s_n)/\Theta_n\le R$, $L^2$ mean oscillation at most $1$, terminal $L^2$ mean oscillation at least $c/2$ on
$B_{\rho_n/\ell_n}$ with $\rho_n/\ell_n\ge c_0/R$ (Lipschitz functions have $\operatorname{osc}_{B_\rho}\le(3/5)^{1/2}\|\nabla f\|_\infty\rho$) and $\int\phi v_n(0)=0$ follow from
the definitions; $\|u(s_n)\|_\infty\ge\frac c2\Theta_n\to\infty$ (Proposition 14C(c)) gives $s_n\to T_*$ and $s_n/T_n\ge s_nD(s_n)/R\to\infty$. Boundedness of
$\nu_n$: if $\nu_n\to\infty$, the viscous-time rescaling $w_n(y,\sigma)=v_n(y,\sigma/\nu_n)$ solves the heat equation up to terms of size
$\beta_n=1/\nu_n$ times locally bounded quantities, plus a pressure gradient bounded by $CR/\nu_n$ (Proposition 14B(a):
$\|\nabla p\|_\infty\le C\theta_0\|\nabla u\|_\infty$); the weighted mean $h_n=\int\phi w_n$ satisfies $|h_n'|\le C_R(1+|h_n|)$, $h_n(0)=0$, so $w_n$ grows at most
linearly on slabs; testing the equation against a mollifier $\chi_\delta$ gives $|\partial_\sigma(w_n*\chi_\delta)|\le C(1+\delta^{-1})$ on compact
sets, which with $\|w_n-w_n*\chi_\delta\|_\infty\le CR\delta$ and $\delta=|\sigma-\sigma'|^{1/2}$ gives a uniform time modulus; a locally uniform limit is an ancient heat
solution with gradient at most $R$ and mean oscillation at most $1$, whose derivatives are bounded ancient caloric functions,
hence constant, so it is affine, and an affine field with bounded mean oscillation is constant, contradicting the terminal
oscillation. With $\nu_n$ bounded the same slab bounds, the dyadic logarithmic growth bound $|v_n(y)-h_n|\le C(1+\log(2+|y|))$, the
mollified time modulus (whose constants do not depend on a lower bound for $\nu_n$) and Arzela-Ascoli give the convergence; $\Pi[v_n]$ equals the rescaled pressure gradient (Lemma 3 is
invariant under the rescaling), $|\Pi[v_n]|\le CR$, and its integrand is dominated by $C|z|^{-4}\min\{R^2|z|^2,(1+\log^+(R|z|))^2\}$, so
$\Pi[v_n]\to\Pi[v]$ locally uniformly by dominated convergence. The symmetry statement is the rotation covariance about the
rescaled axis, applied to the unsubtracted field $\Theta_n^{-1}u(x_n+\ell_ny,s_n+T_n\tau)$, whose circulation about that axis is $\nu_n\Gamma$
(subtracting a non-vertical constant would break the symmetry); vertical boosts preserve it, and the horizontal part of
$V_n/\Theta_n$ is at most $C(1+\log(2+d_n))$ with $d_n=\operatorname{dist}(x_n,\text{axis})/\ell_n$, by the dyadic chain comparing $V_n$ with the
(vertical) weighted mean at the axis point at the height of $x_n$. If $d_n$ is bounded this gives the axisymmetric
alternative with circulation at most $G\nu$; if $d_n\to\infty$, the angular derivative of the rescaled field is
$O((1+\log(2+d_n))/d_n)$ on compact sets, which gives the planar alternative, as in Proposition 14B(c). For bounded $v$, the
remark after Lemma 3 identifies $\Pi[v]$ with the canonical pressure gradient. $\square$

Theorem 37 covers the inviscid case $D/\Theta_0^2\to0$, where Proposition 14B(d) does not apply. The complementary alternative
(UP$_\Theta$) (for every $R$, $\Theta_0^{(R)}/\Theta_0\to0$) is the plateau alternative one amplitude level down, and single-time fields
realize both alternatives, so single-time bounds decide neither: the family of the Remark after Theorem 32 has BMO norm
$\asymp M$ and satisfies the single-time form of (UP$_\Theta$), and the same family without its oscillatory component has
$\Theta/D\asymp a$ and satisfies the single-time form of (C$_\Theta$); neither is a trajectory or satisfies (FLAT$_0$). Under (FLAT$_0$), Proposition 36 and Theorem 37 together say
that if GOAL fails for every normalization of bounded aspect ratio, then either (UP$_\Theta$) holds or every limit of Theorem 37
is unbounded; this is a necessary condition for total failure, not a classification.

**Proposition 37A (signed octave drops and anchored normalizations).** Let $s<T_*$, $X\in\mathbb R^3$, $0<a\le R$, $c_1>0$ and
$e$ a unit vector. Say that $u(s)$ has signed drops on $(X,a,R)$ in the direction $e$ if
$$e\cdot\big[(u(s))_{B_\rho(x)}-(u(s))_{B_{2\rho}(x)}\big]\ge c_1\Theta_0(s)\qquad\text{for all }\rho\in[a,R]\text{ and all }x\text{ with }|x-X|\le\rho.\tag{SD}$$
(a) Then $c_1\Theta_0(s)(1+\lfloor\log_2(R/a)\rfloor)\le2M(s)$ and $a\ge c_1\Theta_0(s)/(4D(s))$.
(b) Let $U_n(y,\tau)=B_n^{-1}(u(x_n+\ell_ny+V_nT_n\tau,s_n+T_n\tau)-V_n)$ be an isotropic rescaling as in Proposition 36, let $u(s_n)$
have signed drops on $(X_n,a_n,R_n)$ in the direction $e$ with $c_1$ and $e$ fixed, put $d_n=\max(a_n,|x_n-X_n|)$ and
$J_n=\{j\in\mathbb Z:d_n\le2^j\ell_n\le R_n/2\}$, and call the rescaling anchored if $|J_n|\to\infty$ and there is $K$ with
$J_n\cap[-K,K]\ne\emptyset$ for all large $n$. If it is anchored, $B_n\le C\Theta_0(s_n)$ and $U_n(\cdot,0)\rightharpoonup U(0)$ weakly in
$L^2_{loc}$ along a subsequence, then there is $j_0\in[-K,K]$ such that one of the following holds:
$$e\cdot\big[(U(0))_{B_{2^{j_0}}}-(U(0))_{B_{2^{j_0+k}}}\big]\ge\frac{c_1k}C\qquad\text{for all }k\ge1,\quad\text{or}\quad
e\cdot\big[(U(0))_{B_{2^{j_0-k}}}-(U(0))_{B_{2^{j_0}}}\big]\ge\frac{c_1k}C\qquad\text{for all }k\ge1;$$
in particular $U(0)\notin L^\infty(\mathbb R^3)$. If only $J_n\cap[-K,K]\ne\emptyset$ for large $n$ and $B_n/\Theta_0(s_n)\to0$, no subsequence
of $U_n(\cdot,0)$ converges weakly in $L^2_{loc}$.
(c) Suppose that (FLAT$_0$) holds and that, for fixed $c_1,c_2>0$, a direction $e$ and some function $X(s)$, every slice $u(s)$ near
$T_*$ has signed drops on $(X(s),a(s),R_*(s))$ with $a=\Theta_0/D$ and $R_*=2^{c_2M/\Theta_0}a$ (a signed logarithmic jet along $X$). Then GOAL
fails for every isotropic normalization anchored at $(X(s_n),a(s_n),R_*(s_n))$, for every amplitude, time scale and constant frame. In
particular, if the centres of the normalization of Theorem 37 satisfy $|x_n-X(s_n)|\le Ca(s_n)$, its cluster limits are unbounded.

Proof. (a) Sum (SD) at $x=X$ over $\rho=2^ja$, $0\le j\le\lfloor\log_2(R/a)\rfloor$; the two extreme means have modulus at most $M(s)$. For the
second inequality, $|(f)_{B_\rho}-(f)_{B_{2\rho}}|\le\sup_{B_{2\rho}}|f-(f)_{B_{2\rho}}|\le4\rho\|\nabla f\|_\infty$. (b) The frame cancels in differences of
means, and $(U_n(0))_{B_r}-(U_n(0))_{B_{r'}}=B_n^{-1}[(u(s_n))_{B_{r\ell_n}(x_n)}-(u(s_n))_{B_{r'\ell_n}(x_n)}]$. If $j,j+k\in J_n$, the radii $2^{j+i}\ell_n$,
$0\le i<k$, lie in $[d_n,R_n/4]\subset[a_n,R_n]$ and are at least $|x_n-X_n|$, so (SD) at the centre $x_n$ applies to each consecutive pair and
$e\cdot[(U_n(0))_{B_{2^j}}-(U_n(0))_{B_{2^{j+k}}}]\ge c_1k\Theta_0(s_n)/B_n$. By pigeonhole over $[-K,K]$ a fixed $j_0$ lies in $J_n$ along a
subsequence, and since $|J_n|\to\infty$ a further subsequence has $\max J_n-j_0\to\infty$ or $j_0-\min J_n\to\infty$; means over fixed balls
converge under weak $L^2_{loc}$ convergence, which gives the dichotomy, and a bounded $U(0)$ would give $2\|U(0)\|_\infty\ge c_1k/C$
for all $k$. If $B_n/\Theta_0(s_n)\to0$, the drop between $B_{2^{j_0}}$ and $B_{2^{j_0+1}}$ diverges along every subsequence, which is
incompatible with weak convergence. (c) Along a subsequence $B_n/\Theta_0(s_n)$ tends to $\infty$, to a finite limit or to $0$, and GOAL is excluded in the
first case by Proposition 36(b) and in the other two by (b). For Theorem 37, $\ell_n=Ra(s_n)$ and $|x_n-X(s_n)|\le Ca(s_n)$ put an
integer of modulus at most $|\log_2R|+\log_2(1+C)+1$ in $J_n$, and $|J_n|\to\infty$ since $R_*(s)/a(s)=2^{c_2M(s)/\Theta_0(s)}\to\infty$; and $\frac c2\Theta_0(t_n)\le O_0(x_n,s_n,\rho_n)\le\Theta_0(s_n)\le\Theta_0(t_n)$ gives
$1\le\Theta_0(t_n)/\Theta_0(s_n)\le2/c$, so (b) applies with $B_n=\Theta_0(t_n)$. $\square$

Only the terminal slice and its weak convergence are used in (b), so the time scale, the viscosity of the limit and the frame
play no role; bounded anisotropy can be treated in the same way with ellipsoids, whose indicators converge in $L^2$ after extraction of
a convergent aspect ratio. In the outward alternative the signed means over expanding dyadic balls decrease at least linearly in the
octave number; in the inward alternative those over shrinking dyadic balls increase at least linearly. Both exclude boundedness;
no pointwise growth law is asserted (a logarithmic singularity at the origin would be compatible with $L^p_{loc}$ for every $p<\infty$). Scope: no trajectory with signed
logarithmic jets is shown to exist, and the signed drops are a hypothesis on the trajectory, not a consequence of (FLAT$_0$).
The exclusion at all amplitudes needs both anchoring conditions, and it does not extend in general to the following cases at
amplitudes comparable to $\Theta_0(s_n)$: centres at distance at least $2^{-C}R_*(s_n)$ from the path (then $|J_n|$ stays bounded, although
the integer window may stay fixed, so that the clause of (b) for $B_n/\Theta_0(s_n)\to0$ still applies), scales below $2^{-k_n}a(s_n)$ or above
$2^{k_n}R_*(s_n)$ with $k_n\to\infty$ (then $J_n$ leaves every fixed window of integers), and unbounded anisotropy (then the pulled-back
ellipsoids degenerate). The dynamic constraint on such trajectories (for $e=e_z$ in regime (II), the Duhamel formula for
$\eta$ from a fixed time $s_0$, tested against the rotation-averaged drop kernel and combined with the energy packing of the drops, forces
$\mathcal I_{s_0}(t)\ge(c_1-o(1))\Theta_0(t)R_*(t)$ for each fixed $s_0$, and Lemma 29(b) then gives $W_{s_0}(t)\ge(c_1/\sqrt3-o(1))\Theta_0(t)R_*(t)$) is of the same exponential type as Proposition 33, which needs no signed
drops.
