**Remark (relation to the literature).** If $\sup_{0<t<T_*}W_0(t)<\infty$, Lemma 29(a) gives $\int_0^{T_*}\mathcal K\,dt<\infty$ and hence $\int_0^{T_*}\|q\|_3^2\,dt\le C\int_0^{T_*}\mathcal K\,dt<\infty$,
since $\|q\|_3^2\le\|u^\theta r^{-3/2}\|_2\|u^\theta r^{-1/2}\|_6\le CY^{1/2}(X+Y)^{1/2}\le C\mathcal K$ (Hölder, Sobolev for $\Gamma r^{-3/2}$, and $X\le3\mathcal K$,
$Y\le\frac43\mathcal K$ from the proof of Lemma 29). This is a scale-invariant bound on $q$. With $|\Gamma|\le G$ it converts into a criterion: $\|u^\theta\|_6^6=\int|\Gamma|^3|q|^3dx\le G^3\|q\|_3^3$,
so $\|u^\theta\|_6^4\le G^2\|q\|_3^2$ and $u^\theta\in L^4_tL^6_x$ on $(0,T_*)$, which is the case $d=0$, $(a,b)=(4,6)$ of the criterion of Chen, Fang
and Zhang quoted in Limitations 6 (their Remark 1.3 also states the version $q\in L^2_tL^3_x$ under bounded circulation directly). Hence,
conditional on that criterion (its statement was checked, its proof was not), $\int_0^t\mathcal K\,ds\to\infty$ as $t\uparrow T_*$ at a first singular
time in every regime: otherwise $q\in L^2_tL^3_x$ and $u^\theta\in L^4_tL^6_x$ on $(0,T_*)$, and the criterion, applied on $(0,T_*)$ to the axisymmetric
Leray-Hopf solution with the same $H^2$ datum (which coincides with $u$ there by weak-strong uniqueness), continues the solution past $T_*$. Since
$\Sigma'\ge0$, Lemma 29(a) then gives $W_{s_0}(t)\ge\frac13(2\int_{s_0}^t\mathcal K\,ds-\Sigma'(s_0))\to\infty$ for every fixed $s_0$; $W_{s_0}$ need not be
monotone, but this lower bound diverges, so the full limit is $+\infty$. This says nothing about the windows of (C) or the size of $W$ relative to $M^2/D$. In
regime (II) Proposition 30 gives the divergence of $W$ at velocity first crossings directly, without any criterion. The partial Type I theorem of
Q. S. Zhang discussed in Limitations 6 gives $\limsup\sqrt{T_*-t}\,\|(u^r)_-(t)\|_\infty=\infty$; Corollary 35 concerns the different,
scale-invariant clock $\int\|(u^r/r)_-\|_\infty\,dt$. We know of no estimate of $W$ of order $M^2/D$ under finite energy and bounded
circulation.

**Theorem 38 (scale constraints on oscillating normalizations in regime (II)).** Assume regime (II), fix $s_0<T_*$, write
$\mathcal I_{s_0}(t)=\int_{s_0}^t\|F\|_1\,ds$, $C_0=\|\eta(s_0)\|_\infty$, and for $\kappa>0$ let
$\Theta_0^{[\kappa]}(t)=\sup\{O_0(x,s,\rho):s\le t,\ x\in\mathbb R^3,\ \rho\ge\kappa S(t)/M(t)\}$ and
$\Theta_{\mathcal I}^{[\kappa]}(t)=\sup\{O_0(x,s,\rho):s\le t,\ x\in\mathbb R^3,\ \rho\ge\kappa\mathcal I_{s_0}(t)/M(t)\}$ ($O_0$ as in Proposition 14A).
(a) Every admissible isotropic normalization with (L2-OSC) has $M_n\ell_n/S(t_n)\to0$. Equivalently,
$\Theta_0^{[\kappa]}(t)/M(t)\to0$ as $t\to T_*$ for every $\kappa>0$, for every trajectory in regime (II).
(b) If an admissible isotropic normalization with (L2-OSC) has $M_n\ell_n\ge\kappa\,\mathcal I_{s_0}(t_n)$ for some $\kappa>0$ and all
large $n$, then it satisfies (VL1), hence (L2M), and GOAL holds for it. If $\limsup_{t\to T_*}\Theta_{\mathcal I}^{[\kappa]}(t)/M(t)>0$ for some
$\kappa>0$, GOAL holds with a normalization selected from near-maximizers of $\Theta_{\mathcal I}^{[\kappa]}$. In alternative (C) of
Theorem 5, every admissible isotropic normalization with (L2-OSC) has $M_n\ell_n/\mathcal I_{s_0}(t_n)\to0$, and
$\Theta_{\mathcal I}^{[\kappa]}(t)/M(t)\to0$ for every $\kappa>0$.
(c) $\mathcal I_{s_0}(t)=o(S(t))$ as $t\to T_*$. Along velocity first crossings $t_n\uparrow T_*$ with record points $x_n$, for every choice of
$L_n\le1$ with $L_nM_n/(1+\mathcal I_{s_0}(t_n))\to\infty$ (for instance some $L_n=o(S(t_n)/M_n)$),
$$b(x_n,t_n)=\frac1{4\pi}\int_{|y-x_n|<L_n}\frac{\omega^\theta(y,t_n)e_\theta(y)\times(x_n-y)}{|x_n-y|^3}\,dy+o(M_n).$$
(d) For every $t\in[s_0,T_*)$, $x\in\mathbb R^3$ and $0<P\le\frac12$,
$$\Big|\fint_{B_P(x)}b(t)\Big|\le\frac5{2\pi}\,\frac{N(x,t)}{P}+C_1E_0^{1/2},\qquad N(x,t)=\int_{T_2(x)}|\eta(t)|\,dy\le C_0|T_2(x)|+\mathcal I_{s_0}(t),$$
with $T_2(x)=\{y:d_m(y,x)<2\}$ and an absolute constant $C_1$.
(e) In alternative (C), along every sequence of velocity first crossings, for every $\kappa>0$ the record normalization at the
source scale, $U_n(y,\tau)=M_n^{-1}u(x_n+\ell_ny,t_n+\tau\ell_n/M_n)$ with $\ell_n=\kappa\mathcal I_{s_0}(t_n)/M_n$, is admissible, satisfies
(L2M), and $U_n\to0$ in $C([-A,0];L^2_{loc}(\mathbb R^3))$ for every $A$, although $|U_n(0,0)|=1$.

Proof. Step 0. For $t<T_*$ let $t'\le t$ be the first time with $\|u(t')\|_\infty=M(t)$; it is a velocity first crossing, $t'\to T_*$
as $t\to T_*$, and $M(t')=M(t)$. By Lemma 34(b), monotonicity of $S$ and $D$, and $A\le\sqrt2D$,
$S(t)D(t)/M(t)^2\ge S(t')D(t')/M(t')^2\to\infty$; as $D/M^2\to0$, $S(t)\to\infty$.
(a) Let the normalization satisfy (L2-OSC) on $B_{R_0}$ with constant $c_0$. Then $\int_{B_{R_0\ell_n}(x_n)}|u(s_n)|^2\ge c_0M_n^2\ell_n^3$, and
rotating this ball about the axis as in the proof of Lemma 28 gives $(1+r(x_n)/\ell_n)M_n^2\ell_n^3\le CE_0$, with $C$ depending on
$R_0,c_0$. Let $E_{n,R}$ be the solid of revolution of the rectangle $P_{n,R}$ of Corollary 6A; then
$|E_{n,R}|\le8\pi R^2(r(x_n)/\ell_n+R)\ell_n^3=O(M_n^{-2})$, and $N^+_{n,R}+N^-_{n,R}=(2\pi M_n\ell_n)^{-1}\int_{E_{n,R}}|\eta|\,dx$. (L2-OSC) forces
$\|u(s_n)\|_\infty\ge cM_n$, so $s_n\to T_*$, and since the depth tends to infinity the window times $t=s_n+\tau\ell_n/M_n$, $\tau\in[-A,0]$,
eventually lie in $(s_0,t_n]$. Split $\eta=\eta^d+Z$ from $s_0$ as in the Remark after Theorem 6' (accumulated swirl source):
$|\eta^d|\le C_0$ and $\|Z(t)\|_1\le\mathcal I_{s_0}(t)$. By Lemma 29(b) and Lemma 34(a),
$\mathcal I_{s_0}(t)\le(\Sigma'(s_0)+\frac32S(t)\epsilon(s_0))/\sqrt3$, and $S(t)\le S(t_n)$ on the window. Hence, uniformly on the window,
$$N^+_{n,R}+N^-_{n,R}\le\frac{C_0|E_{n,R}|+(\Sigma'(s_0)+\frac32S(t_n)\epsilon(s_0))/\sqrt3}{2\pi M_n\ell_n}.$$
Suppose $M_n\ell_n\ge\kappa S(t_n)$ along a subsequence. Then $M_n\ell_n\to\infty$, so the limit superior of the right side is at most
$\sqrt3\epsilon(s_0)/(4\pi\kappa)$ for every $s_0$, and it tends to $0$ as $s_0\to T_*$. By the first bound of Corollary 6A(a), $(M_n\ell_n^2)^{-1}\int_{B_{R\ell_n}(x_n)}|\omega^\theta|\le2\pi R(N^+_{n,R}+N^-_{n,R})$ (the circle bound, valid for every position of $x_n$), the normalized
$|\omega^\theta|$ mass on every $B_R$ tends to $0$ uniformly on the window; no bound on $r(x_n)/\ell_n$ is used. So (VL1) holds, (L2M) holds by Theorem 6, and by Theorem 5
$U_n\to U$ in $C([-A,0];L^2(B_R))$ along a further subsequence. The rescaled meridional field $V_n=M_n^{-1}b(x_n+\ell_n\cdot,s_n)$ is
divergence free with $\|\operatorname{curl}V_n\|_{L^1(B_R)}=(M_n\ell_n^2)^{-1}\int_{B_{R\ell_n}(x_n)}|\omega^\theta(s_n)|\to0$, and $|U_n(\cdot,0)-V_n|\le S(t_n)/M_n\to0$ (Theorem 3).
So $U(\cdot,0)$ is bounded, divergence free and curl free in $\mathcal D'(\mathbb R^3)$; its components are bounded harmonic functions,
hence constant, and strong convergence at $\tau=0$ contradicts (L2-OSC). Therefore $M_n\ell_n/S(t_n)\to0$. For the second form: if
$\Theta_0^{[\kappa]}(t_k)\ge2cM(t_k)$ along $t_k\to T_*$, choose $s_k\le t_k$, $x_k$, $\rho_k\ge\kappa S(t_k)/M(t_k)$ with $O_0(x_k,s_k,\rho_k)\ge cM_k$,
$M_k=M(t_k)$. The normalization $M_k^{-1}u(x_k+\rho_ky,s_k+\tau\rho_k/M_k)$ is admissible: $O_0(x,s,\rho)\le\min\{D(s)\rho,\|u(s)\|_\infty\}$
gives $\rho_k\ge cM_k/D(t_k)$ and $\|u(s_k)\|_\infty\ge cM_k$, so $s_k\to T_*$; $c^2M_k^2|B_{\rho_k}|\le E_0$ gives $\rho_k\to0$ and depth
$s_kM_k/\rho_k\to\infty$; it is bounded by $1$ on the past and has terminal oscillation $|B_1|^{1/2}O_0/M_k\ge|B_1|^{1/2}c$ on $B_1$. It
contradicts the first form. Conversely, (L2-OSC) on $B_{R_0}$ gives $O_0(x_n,s_n,R_0\ell_n)\ge c'M_n$, so the second form implies the first.
(b) On the window $\mathcal I_{s_0}(t)\le\mathcal I_{s_0}(t_n)\le M_n\ell_n/\kappa$, so the display above, with $\mathcal I_{s_0}$ in place of its bound,
gives $N^+_{n,R}+N^-_{n,R}\le o(1)+1/(2\pi\kappa)$, since $M_n\ell_n\ge cM_n^2/D_n\to\infty$. Corollary 6A(a), Theorem 6 and Theorem 5(a) give
(VL1), (L2M) and GOAL. The selection uses the witnesses of (a) with $\rho_k\ge\kappa\mathcal I_{s_0}(t_k)/M(t_k)$, which are admissible by the
same argument and satisfy the hypothesis of the first sentence. In (C) no admissible normalization has both (L2M) and (L2-OSC),
so the first sentence, applied to subsequences, gives $M_n\ell_n/\mathcal I_{s_0}(t_n)\to0$, and the second gives the flatness. Changing
$s_0$ changes $\mathcal I_{s_0}$ by a constant only, so the vanishing argument of (a) does not apply here; the normalized mass is
bounded, not small.
(c) Let $J(t)=\int_{s_0}^t\|u^\theta\|_\infty\|\nabla u\|_2^2\,ds$. For $s_0<s_1<t$, $J(t)\le J(s_1)+S(t)\epsilon(s_1)$; since $S(t)\to\infty$,
$\limsup J/S\le\epsilon(s_1)$ for every $s_1$, so $J=o(S)$, and $\mathcal I_{s_0}\le(\Sigma'(s_0)+\frac32J)/\sqrt3=o(S)$ by Lemma 29(b) and the
pointwise bound in the proof of Lemma 34(a). At velocity first crossings $r(x_n)\to0$ (Lemma 28), so $N_n=N(x_n,t_n)\le C+\mathcal I_{s_0}(t_n)$.
As in the proof of Lemma 31(b) with radius $1$: the part of the Biot-Savart integral from $|y-x_n|\ge1$ is at most
$C_1E_0^{1/2}+N_n/(2\pi)$ (the kernel $(1-\phi)K$ by Lemma 27(b), the remainder by the circle bound $\mu_n(B_s(x_n))\le sN_n$), and the part from
$L_n\le|y-x_n|<1$ is at most $(4\pi)^{-1}\int_{|y-x_n|\ge L_n}|x_n-y|^{-2}d\mu_n\le N_n/(2\pi L_n)$ by layer-cake integration. Both are $o(M_n)$,
because $N_n=o(S(t_n))=o(M_n)$ and $L_nM_n/(1+\mathcal I_{s_0}(t_n))\to\infty$. The choice $L_n=(S(t_n)(1+\mathcal I_{s_0}(t_n)))^{1/2}/M_n$ is admissible
and is $o(S(t_n)/M_n)$.
(d) Let $\phi_1=\phi(\cdot-x)$ with $\phi$ the cutoff of Lemma 27 ($\phi=1$ on $B_1$, supported in $B_2$), so that $\phi_1$ is supported in $B_2(x)\subset T_2(x)$, since the
meridional distance is at most the Euclidean distance. For $x'\in B_P(x)$ the field generated by $(1-\phi_1)\omega^\theta e_\theta$ is at most
$C_1E_0^{1/2}$: write $(1-\phi_1)\operatorname{curl}b=\operatorname{curl}((1-\phi_1)b)+\nabla\phi_1\times b$ and integrate by parts against the kernel, which is
smooth with $|\nabla K(x'-y)|\le C|x'-y|^{-3}$ on $|x'-y|\ge\frac12$. Let $\mu=\phi_1|\omega^\theta|\,dy$; the circle bound gives $\mu(B_s(x))\le sN(x,t)$.
The mean over $x'\in B_P(x)$ of $(4\pi)^{-1}\int|x'-y|^{-2}d\mu(y)$ is at most $(4\pi)^{-1}(3P^{-2})\mu(B_{2P}(x))\le\frac3{2\pi}N/P$ from
$y\in B_{2P}(x)$ (since $\int_{B_P(x)}|x'-y|^{-2}dx'\le4\pi P$), and at most $(4\pi)^{-1}\cdot4\int_{|y-x|\ge2P}|y-x|^{-2}d\mu\le\frac1\pi N/P$ from the rest
(there $|x'-y|\ge|y-x|/2$, and layer-cake integration gives $\int_{|y-x|\ge L}|y-x|^{-2}d\mu\le2N/L$). The bound on $N$ is the split of (a).
(e) In (C) $\subset$ (R) (Theorem 32) $\mathcal I_{s_0}(t_n)D_n/M_n^2\to\infty$, so $\ell_n\ge M_n/D_n$ eventually, $\ell_n=o(S(t_n)/M_n)\to0$ by (c),
the depth $t_nM_n/\ell_n$ tends to infinity, $|U_n|\le1$ on the past, and $\mathcal I_{s_0}(t_n)\to\infty$. The mass bound in the proof of (b) uses (L2-OSC) only to get $C_0|E_{n,R}|=o(M_n\ell_n)$ and window times after $s_0$; here these
follow from $r(x_n)\to0$ (Lemma 28) and $\ell_n\to0$, so the normalization satisfies (VL1) and (L2M). By Theorem 5 every subsequence has a
further subsequence with $U_n(\cdot,0)\to U(\cdot,0)$ in $L^2_{loc}$. If $U(\cdot,0)$ were nonconstant, that subsequence would satisfy (L2-OSC)
and (L2M), which (C) excludes; so $U(\cdot,0)=c$ is constant. By (d) and $|U_n-M_n^{-1}b|\le S(t_n)/M_n$,
$|c|=\lim|\fint_{B_R}U_n(\cdot,0)|\le\limsup\frac5{2\pi}\frac{C+\mathcal I_{s_0}(t_n)}{R\ell_nM_n}=\frac5{2\pi\kappa R}$ for every $R\ge1$, so $c=0$. The
same holds at every $\tau_0\in[-A,0]$: the normalization recentered at $(x_n,t_n+\tau_0\ell_n/M_n)$ with the same $M_n,\ell_n$ is admissible
and satisfies (L2M) (its windows lie in windows of the original one), so $U(\cdot,\tau_0)$ is constant, and (d) at the time
$t_n+\tau_0\ell_n/M_n\le t_n$, where $N\le C+\mathcal I_{s_0}(t_n)$ and $|U_n-M_n^{-1}b|\le S(t_n)/M_n$, gives $U(\cdot,\tau_0)=0$. Since every
subsequence has a further subsequence converging to $0$ in $C([-A,0];L^2(B_R))$, the whole sequence does. $\square$

Theorem 38(a) is a constraint, not a route to GOAL: the swirl scale $S/M$ lies above every oscillating scale, and $\Theta_0^{[\kappa]}$
gives no selection. Part (b) is the contrapositive of the Remark after Theorem 6' (accumulated swirl source in (C)) in scale form,
and every normalization with (L2-OSC) in (C) has $M_n/D_n\ll\ell_n\ll\mathcal I_{s_0}(t_n)/M_n\ll S(t_n)/M_n$ (the lower separation from (C) $\subset$ (UP):
a bounded subsequence of $\ell_nD_n/M_n$ would make the oscillation on every fixed normalized ball tend to zero; the middle one from (b);
the upper one from (c)). This window does not assert that such a normalization exists, and it does not apply to the source-scale
normalization of (e), which has no (L2-OSC). Part (e) says that in (C) the record speed is carried by a set that is negligible in $L^2$ at every fixed multiple of the
source scale $\mathcal I_{s_0}(t_n)/M_n$, while by (UP) it is a plateau at every fixed multiple of the Lipschitz scale $M_n/D_n$. In
particular the source-scale record normalization gives no GOAL limit even under the weaker reading that asks for nonconstancy
at some time $\tau\le0$: its limit is $U\equiv0$ on $(-\infty,0]$. Part (c)
is a statement about the signed Biot-Savart contribution only.

**Proposition 39 (the inherited compression work is bounded).** Fix $s_0\in(0,T_*)$, let $\eta^d$ solve the homogeneous equation
$(\partial_t+b\cdot\nabla)\eta^d=(\Delta+\frac2r\partial_r)\eta^d$ from $\eta^d(s_0)=\eta(s_0)$, let $u^r[h]$ be the radial velocity of the vorticity
$rh\,e_\theta$, and $W^d(t)=\int_{s_0}^t\int q^2(-u^r[\eta^d])\,dx\,ds$. Put $B_0=\|\eta(s_0)\|_\infty$ and $B_2=\|\eta(s_0)\|_2\le\sqrt2\|\nabla^2u(s_0)\|_2$, finite since the maximal solution of data $u_0\in\bigcap_kH^k$ lies in $H^2$ at $s_0<T_*$.
(a) For axisymmetric $h\in L^2\cap L^\infty$, $\|u^r[h]/r\|_\infty\le4\|h\|_\infty^{1/3}\|h\|_2^{2/3}$.
(b) $\|\eta^d(t)\|_\infty\le B_0$ and $\|\eta^d(t)\|_2\le B_2$ for $s_0\le t<T_*$.
(c) $|W^d(t)|\le C_d:=4B_0^{1/3}B_2^{2/3}E_0^{1/2}((T_*-s_0)\epsilon(s_0))^{1/2}$ for $s_0\le t<T_*$.
Hence $W_{s_0}=W^d+W^p$, where $W^p$ is the work of the radial velocity of $Z=\eta-\eta^d$ (the retarded source term in the Remark
(memory form of the work) above), $|W^d|\le C_d$, and the work bound $W_{s_0}(t_n)\le CM_n^2/D_n$ of Lemma 31 is equivalent to the same
bound for $W^p$.

Proof. (a) For smooth compactly supported $h$, at $x=(r,0,z)$ the radial Biot-Savart component is
$(4\pi)^{-1}\int(z-y_3)r'\cos\theta\,h(r',y_3)|x-y|^{-3}dy$ with $y=(r'\cos\theta,r'\sin\theta,y_3)$. Integrating by parts in $\theta$,
$\int_0^{2\pi}\cos\theta\,d^{-3}d\theta=3rr'\int_0^{2\pi}\sin^2\theta\,d^{-5}d\theta$ for $d^2=(r-r')^2+(z-y_3)^2+2rr'(1-\cos\theta)$, so
$u^r[h](x)/r=\frac3{4\pi}\int(z-y_3)y_2^2|x-y|^{-5}h(y)\,dy$ (here $y_2=(y-x)\cdot e_\theta(x)$ is the coordinate of the frame in which $x=(r,0,z)$; the axis value is the continuous limit), and $|u^r[h]/r|\le\frac3{4\pi}\int|x-y|^{-2}|h(y)|dy\le3R\|h\|_\infty+\frac3{2\sqrt\pi}R^{-1/2}\|h\|_2$.
With $R=(\|h\|_2/\|h\|_\infty)^{2/3}$ the coefficient is $3+3/(2\sqrt\pi)<4$. Truncation extends this to $L^2\cap L^\infty$ (the far field
converges by the same Cauchy-Schwarz bound), and for $h=\eta^d$, $Z$ these kernels add to the actual radial velocity since $\eta,\eta^d\in
L^2\cap L^\infty$ on $[s_0,t]$. (b) The maximum principle, and
$\frac12\frac d{dt}\|h\|_2^2=\int h(\Delta+\frac2r\partial_r)h\,dx=-\|\nabla h\|_2^2-2\pi\int h(0,z)^2dz$ (transport drops out by incompressibility; the
term $\frac2r\partial_r$ gives $4\pi\iint hh_r\,dr\,dz$), justified with cutoffs on compact time intervals. (c) $|u^r[\eta^d]|\le K_0r$ with
$K_0=4B_0^{1/3}B_2^{2/3}$, $\int q^2r\,dx=\int|u^\theta||q|\,dx\le E_0^{1/2}\|q\|_2$, and $\int_{s_0}^{T_*}\|q\|_2^2\le\epsilon(s_0)$ because $q^2$ is one of the
angular terms of $|\nabla u|^2$; Cauchy-Schwarz in time. The bound on $B_2$: $|r^{-1}\partial_\theta\omega|^2=r^{-2}((\omega^r)^2+(\omega^\theta)^2)\ge\eta^2$ and
$|r^{-1}\partial_\theta\omega|\le|\nabla\omega|$. $\square$

With Lemma 29(a) and $\Sigma'\ge0$, a bound $W^p(t_n)\le\alpha\int_{s_0}^{t_n}\mathcal K+CM_n^2/D_n$ with $0\le\alpha<\frac23$ would give
$W_{s_0}(t_n)\le(2C_d+\alpha\Sigma'(s_0)+2CM_n^2/D_n)/(2-3\alpha)$ (eliminate $\int\mathcal K$ with Lemma 29(a) and drop $-\alpha\Sigma'(t_n)\le0$) and exclude (UP); for $\alpha<0$ directly $W_{s_0}(t_n)\le C_d+CM_n^2/D_n$, since $\mathcal K\ge0$. It is not proved, and it cannot be proved by pairwise
signs. The instantaneous contribution is never compressive: by Lemma 29(c), $\int q^2(-u^r[F])\,dx=-\int r\varphi F\,dx$ with
$-\Delta_5\varphi=F$, and $\int r\varphi F\,dx=2\pi\iint(r^2|\nabla\varphi|^2+\frac12\varphi^2)\,dr\,dz\ge0$. But for $f=\chi^2$ with $\chi$ supported in
$\{1<r<2,|z|<\frac1{10}\}$ and $g=f(\cdot,\cdot-10)$, the second axial derivative of the five-dimensional fundamental solution,
$\frac3{8\pi^2}(4(z-z')^2-|X_h-Y_h|^2)(|X_h-Y_h|^2+(z-z')^2)^{-7/2}$, is positive on the separated supports, hence $u^r[\partial_zf]<0$ on $\operatorname{supp}g$ and $\int g(-u^r[\partial_zf])>0$, and by continuity this persists for a short propagation time: a later swirl square can be compressed by the
vorticity an earlier one produced. This rules out a general pairwise sign rule for the retarded kernel; it does not exhibit two swirl
profiles of one Navier-Stokes trajectory.

**Lemma 40 (localized critical swirl budget).** Let $s_0<t<T_*$, $0\le\chi\le1$ smooth, axisymmetric, with compact support, and $\psi(s)=P(t,s)^*\chi$
the dual of the propagator $P$ of the Remark after Theorem 6' (accumulated swirl source), that is, the solution of $\partial_s\psi+b\cdot\nabla\psi+\Delta\psi-\frac2r\partial_r\psi=0$ on $[s_0,t)$ with $\psi(t)=\chi$, i.e. $\psi=r^2g$ with $g$ the
five-dimensional adjoint solution with data $\chi r^{-2}$ (smooth for $s<t$; $\psi=0$ on the axis for $s<t$, which is the boundary condition of the dual domain; $0\le\psi\le1$ by positivity and the $L^1$ contraction of $P$; $\int\psi(s)\,dx=\int\chi\,dx$ by duality and $P(t,s)1=1$). Then,
with values in $[0,\infty]$,
$$\int\chi|Z(t)|\,dx\le\int_{s_0}^t\!\!\int\psi|F|\le\frac1{\sqrt3}\Big[\int\psi(s_0)\frac{\Gamma(s_0)^2}{r^3}+\frac32\int_{s_0}^t\!\!\int\psi|u^\theta||\nabla u|^2+\int_{s_0}^t\!\!\int q^2(\partial_r\psi)_+\Big].$$

Proof. The first inequality is positivity of $P$ and duality. With $H(s)=\int\psi\Gamma^2r^{-3}dx$, the equation of $\Gamma$ and of $\psi$ give,
after integration by parts (with the cutoffs of Lemma 29; the axis terms vanish since $\Gamma=O(r^2)$, $\Gamma_r=O(r)$, $\psi=O(r^2)$ and $\psi_r=O(r)$ for $s<t$, so that the boundary factors are $O(r^3)$),
$H'+2\int\psi(|\nabla\Gamma|^2r^{-3}-\frac32\Gamma^2r^{-5})=3\int\psi q^2(-u^r)-2\int\Gamma^2r^{-4}\partial_r\psi$. Completing the square,
$\int\psi\Gamma_r^2r^{-3}=\int\psi r^{-3}(\Gamma_r-\frac{3\Gamma}{2r})^2+\frac94\int\psi\Gamma^2r^{-5}-\frac32\int\Gamma^2r^{-4}\partial_r\psi$, so
$H'+2\int\psi r^{-3}(\Gamma_r-\frac{3\Gamma}{2r})^2+2X'+\frac32Y'=3\int\psi q^2(-u^r)+\int q^2\partial_r\psi$ with $X'=\int\psi\Gamma_z^2r^{-3}$, $Y'=\int\psi\Gamma^2r^{-5}$
($\Gamma^2r^{-4}=q^2$). As in Lemma 29(b), $\int\psi|F|\le2(X'Y')^{1/2}\le\frac1{\sqrt3}(2X'+\frac32Y')$, and $3\psi q^2|u^r|\le\frac32\psi|u^\theta||\nabla u|^2$. Integrate on
$[s_0,t-\delta]$, drop $H(t-\delta)\ge0$, and let $\delta\to0$ (all right-side integrands are nonnegative). $\square$

Without the weight, the radial term is absent and Lemma 40 is Lemma 29(b) with Lemma 34(a). The unweighted Hardy inequality
cannot be multiplied by $\psi$, so the radial term cannot be removed from the completion of the square used in the proof of Lemma 40 (Lemma 40A below avoids that completion): take $\chi\ge0$ nonzero, axisymmetric, supported in an annulus
away from the axis, and $\Gamma$ smooth with compact support away from the axis and equal to $1$ near $\operatorname{supp}\chi$; then
$\int\chi\Gamma_r^2r^{-3}=0<\int\chi\Gamma^2r^{-5}$, and since $\psi_\delta=P(t,t-\delta)^*\chi$ converges to $\chi$ against smooth functions with compact
support away from the axis as $\delta\downarrow0$, $\int\psi_\delta\Gamma_r^2r^{-3}<\int\psi_\delta\Gamma^2r^{-5}$ for all small ages $\delta>0$.

**Lemma 40A (localized critical swirl budget without a radial term).** Under the hypotheses of Lemma 40 put $v=u^\theta$, $H(s)=\int\psi v^2r^{-1}dx$ (the $H$ of the
proof of Lemma 40), $X'=\int\psi v_z^2r^{-1}dx$ and $Y'=\int\psi v^2r^{-3}dx$. Then for $s_0\le s<t$, with $\nabla v=(\partial_rv,\partial_zv)$,
$$H'(s)+2\int\psi\frac{|\nabla v|^2}r\,dx+Y'(s)=3\int\psi q^2(-u^r)\,dx,$$
and consequently, with values in $[0,\infty]$,
$$\int\chi|Z(t)|\,dx\le\int_{s_0}^t\!\!\int\psi|F|\le\frac1{\sqrt2}\Big[\int\psi(s_0)\frac{\Gamma(s_0)^2}{r^3}+3\int_{s_0}^t\!\!\int\psi q^2(u^r)_-\Big]\le\frac1{\sqrt2}\Big[\int\psi(s_0)\frac{\Gamma(s_0)^2}{r^3}+\frac32\int_{s_0}^t\!\!\int\psi|u^\theta||\nabla u|^2\Big].$$

Proof. Put $f=v^2/r$. The swirl solves $\partial_sv+b\cdot\nabla v+\frac{u^r}rv=\Delta v-\frac v{r^2}$ with $\Delta=\partial_{rr}+\frac1r\partial_r+\partial_{zz}$, and
$b\cdot\nabla r^{-1}=-u^rr^{-2}$, so $\partial_sf+b\cdot\nabla f=-3u^rv^2r^{-2}+2vr^{-1}\Delta v-2v^2r^{-3}$. Multiply by $\psi$, add $f$ times the equation of $\psi$, use
$\operatorname{div}b=0$, and write $2\psi vr^{-1}\Delta v=\operatorname{div}(2\psi vr^{-1}\nabla v)-2vr^{-1}\nabla\psi\cdot\nabla v-2\psi r^{-1}|\nabla v|^2+2\psi vv_rr^{-2}$ and
$-f\Delta\psi=-\operatorname{div}(f\nabla\psi)+2vr^{-1}\nabla v\cdot\nabla\psi-v^2r^{-2}\partial_r\psi$. The terms with $\nabla\psi\cdot\nabla v$ cancel, and the remaining terms with one
radial derivative, $2r^{-1}f\partial_r\psi-v^2r^{-2}\partial_r\psi+2\psi vv_rr^{-2}$, equal $\operatorname{div}(\psi v^2r^{-2}e_r)+\psi v^2r^{-3}$. Hence, pointwise for $s<t$ and $r>0$,
$$\partial_s(\psi f)+\operatorname{div}V=-\frac{2\psi|\nabla v|^2}r-\frac{\psi v^2}{r^3}+3\psi q^2(-u^r),\qquad V=b\psi f+f\nabla\psi-\frac{2\psi v}r\nabla v-\frac{\psi v^2}{r^2}e_r.$$
Since $v=rq$ with $q$ smooth and $\psi=r^2g$ with $g$ smooth for $s<t$, $V=O(r^2)$ near the axis, and the flux through $\{r=\epsilon\}$ over a bounded axial
interval is $O(\epsilon^3)$; integrate first over bounded cylinders and remove the outer cutoffs as in Lemma 29, using the decay of the solution and of the
adjoint for $s\le t-\delta$. This gives the identity. The terminal datum is attained only off the axis ($\psi(s,0,z)=0$ for $s<t$), so integrate the identity on
$[s_0,t-\delta]$ and drop $H(t-\delta)\ge0$ and the radial part of $|\nabla v|^2$: $\int_{s_0}^{t-\delta}(2X'+Y')\,ds\le H(s_0)+3\int_{s_0}^{t-\delta}\!\int\psi q^2(u^r)_-$. Pointwise
$|F|=2|v||v_z|r^{-2}\le\frac1{\sqrt2}(2v_z^2r^{-1}+v^2r^{-3})$, the difference being $\frac1{\sqrt2}(\sqrt2|v_z|r^{-1/2}-|v|r^{-3/2})^2$. Let $\delta\to0$ (all integrands
are nonnegative) and use the first inequality of Lemma 40. The last inequality follows from $|\nabla u|^2\ge((u^r)^2+(u^\theta)^2)r^{-2}$ (the full gradient of
$u$), which gives $q^2(u^r)_-\le\frac12|u^\theta||\nabla u|^2$. $\square$

Lemma 40 follows from the identity of Lemma 40A: integrating $\psi vv_rr^{-2}$ by parts in $r$ gives
$\int\psi v_r^2r^{-1}=\int\psi r^{-1}(v_r-\frac v{2r})^2+\frac14Y'-\frac12\int q^2\partial_r\psi$, and $r(v_r-\frac v{2r})=\Gamma_r-\frac{3\Gamma}{2r}$. So the radial term of Lemma 40
comes from this completion of the square, and replacing $\partial_r\psi$ by its positive part loses a cancellation that Lemma 40A keeps; the price is the
constant $1/\sqrt2$ in place of $1/\sqrt3$. Before the sign of the work is dropped, the same steps give
$\int_{s_0}^t\!\int\psi|F|\le\frac1{\sqrt2}[H(s_0)+3\liminf_{\delta\to0}\int_{s_0}^{t-\delta}\!\int\psi q^2(-u^r)]$.

**Remark (power weights).** Let $\psi$ be as in Lemma 40, $q=u^\theta/r$ and $0\le a\le2$. Then for $s_0\le s<t$
$$\frac d{ds}\int\psi r^aq^2\,dx+2\int\psi r^a\Big(q_z^2+\big(q_r+\frac arq\big)^2\Big)dx+a(2-a)\int\psi r^{a-2}q^2\,dx=(4-a)\int\psi r^aq^2\Big(-\frac{u^r}r\Big)dx .$$
Here $q$ solves $\partial_sq+b\cdot\nabla q=\Delta_5q-2\frac{u^r}rq$ with $\Delta_5=\partial_{rr}+\frac3r\partial_r+\partial_{zz}$, and the spatial operator in the equation of $\psi$ is
$\Delta_5^*w=w_{rr}-\frac1rw_r+w_{zz}$, the adjoint of $\Delta_5$ with respect to $dx$ when the test function $w$ vanishes quadratically at the axis. Since $\operatorname{div}b=0$ and
$b\cdot\nabla r^a=ar^{a-1}u^r$, the transport terms leave $(a-4)\int\psi r^aq^2u^r/r$; and
$\int(2\psi r^aq\Delta_5q-r^aq^2\Delta_5^*\psi)=\int\psi(2r^aq\Delta_5q-\Delta_5(r^aq^2))=-\int\psi(2r^a|\nabla q|^2+4ar^{a-1}qq_r+a(a+2)r^{a-2}q^2)$, while
$2r^a|\nabla q|^2+4ar^{a-1}qq_r+a(a+2)r^{a-2}q^2=2r^a(q_z^2+(q_r+aq/r)^2)+a(2-a)r^{a-2}q^2$. The axis terms vanish because $\psi=O(r^2)$ and $q$ is smooth;
the integrals are finite because $0\le\psi\le1$ and $r^aq^2\le q^2+(u^\theta)^2$; and the integration is justified as in Lemma 40A (on $[s_0,t-\delta]$, then $\delta\to0$).
No derivative of $\psi$ occurs. For $a=1$, $r(q_r+q/r)=\partial_ru^\theta$ and $rq^2=(u^\theta)^2/r$, and the identity is that of Lemma 40A.

Since $|F|=2|q||q_z|\le2(r^aq_z^2)^{1/2}(r^{-a}q^2)^{1/2}$, the exponent $a=1$ is the only one for which the weight $r^{-a}$ in this factorization is the
coercive zero-order weight $r^{a-2}$; for $0\le a<1$ the identity is combined with the dissipation. Put $J=\int_{s_0}^t\|\nabla u\|_2^2$, $H_a(s)=\int\psi(s)r^aq(s)^2$ and
$A_a=H_a(s_0)+(4-a)\int_{s_0}^t\!\int\psi r^aq^2(u^r)_-/r$. Integrating the identity gives $2\int\!\int\psi r^aq_z^2+a(2-a)\int\!\int\psi r^{a-2}q^2\le A_a$, and
$\int\!\int\psi q^2\le J$ (as $|q|\le|\nabla u|$). For $a=0$, Cauchy-Schwarz gives $(\int_{s_0}^t\!\int\psi|F|)^2\le2A_0J$. For $0<a\le1$, Hölder's inequality gives
$\int\!\int\psi r^{-a}q^2\le(\int\!\int\psi r^{a-2}q^2)^{a/(2-a)}J^{2(1-a)/(2-a)}$, and Cauchy-Schwarz followed by maximization under the linear constraint gives
$$\Big(\int_{s_0}^t\!\!\int\psi|F|\Big)^{2-a}\le2^{-a/2}(2-a)^{1-a}A_aJ^{1-a}\qquad(0\le a\le1),$$
which for $a=1$ is the consequence in Lemma 40A. For $1<a<2$ the Hölder exponent $a/(2-a)$ exceeds $1$ and this argument fails; at $a=2$ the zero-order coercive coefficient $a(2-a)$ vanishes.

On the windows of item (d) of the Remark (the windowed conditions concern a thin cylinder about the axis), with $L_n=M_n^2/D_n$, choose $s_n\in I_n$ as in the
Remark (a windowed source condition) with $\lambda=\lambda_n\le1$, and in addition outside the set of $s\in I_n$ with $\|\nabla u(s)\|_2^2\ge2\varepsilon_nE_0/\delta_n'$, which has
measure at most $\delta_n'/4$ because $\int_{I_n}\|\nabla u\|_2^2\le\varepsilon_nE_0/2$; the three exceptional sets cover at most $\frac{11}{12}\delta_n'$ of $I_n$. Here $\varepsilon_n>0$, since otherwise
the dissipation would vanish on an interval before $t_n$ and $u(t_n)=0$. Then $H_0(s_n)\le\|\nabla u(s_n)\|_2^2<2\varepsilon_nE_0/\delta_n'\le2\sqrt{\varepsilon_n}E_0/\delta_n$, and since
$M_n^4/D_n^2=K^2/\delta_n$ the ratio of $H_0(s_n)$ to $L_n^2/(\varepsilon_nE_0)$ is at most $2E_0^2\varepsilon_n^{3/2}/K^2\to0$; also $H_1(s_n)\le\Sigma'(s_n)=o(L_n)$, so by Hölder's inequality
$H_a(s_n)\le H_0(s_n)^{1-a}H_1(s_n)^a=o(L_n^{2-a}(\varepsilon_nE_0)^{-(1-a)})$; and $\int_{s_n}^{t_n}\|\nabla u\|_2^2\le\varepsilon_nE_0/2$. With the positivity and duality step of item (d)
this gives: if for some $0\le a\le1$ and some $C$ independent of $n$
$$\int_{s_n}^{t_n}\!\!\int\psi_nr^aq^2\frac{(u^r)_-}r\,dx\,ds\le C\,L_n^{2-a}(\varepsilon_nE_0)^{-(1-a)}$$
along a sequence of velocity first crossings, for the times $s_n$ just chosen and the adjoint weights $\psi_n$ of item (d), then
$N_n\le\big((\kappa_aC)^{1/(2-a)}+\frac Kc+o(1)\big)L_n$ with $\kappa_a=2^{a/2-1}(2-a)^{1-a}(4-a)$ ($\kappa_0=4$, $\kappa_1=3/\sqrt2$), and GOAL holds with the record
normalization along it (Lemma 31). The integral is over all space: $\psi_n(s)$ is not supported in the cylinder for $s<t_n$. For $a=1$ this is the condition of item (d).
For $a=0$ the weight is the compression rate $(u^r)_-/r\le D_n$ ($D$ is historical and $|u^r|\le r\|\partial_ru^r\|_\infty$), and the elementary bound
$\int\!\int\psi_nq^2(u^r)_-/r\le D_n\varepsilon_nE_0/2$ meets the condition only if $\varepsilon_n^2\le2CM_n^4/(E_0^2D_n^3)$, which by Lemma 2 requires $\varepsilon_n^2\le48C/(\pi E_0M_n)$; the
preceding estimates give no rate for $\varepsilon_n$. The conditions for different $a$ involve different radial weights and are ordered. Write $B_a$ for the left side of the condition at $a$; since $rq^2(u^r)_-=\frac{|\Gamma|}r\,\frac{|u^\theta|(u^r)_-}r\le\frac G2|\nabla u|^2$ and $\psi_n\le1$, also $B_2\le G\varepsilon_nE_0/4$, and for $0\le a<b\le1$ Hölder's inequality with $r^b=(r^a)^{(2-b)/(2-a)}(r^2)^{(b-a)/(2-a)}$ gives $B_b\le B_a^{(2-b)/(2-a)}B_2^{(b-a)/(2-a)}$. So the condition at $a$ with constant $C$ implies the condition at $b$ with constant $C^{(2-b)/(2-a)}(G/4)^{(b-a)/(2-a)}$, and the condition at $a=1$ is the weakest. The reverse implications are not shown, and none of the conditions is proved.

**Remark (compression at small radial Reynolds number).** Under the hypotheses of Lemma 40A put $W=\int_{s_0}^t\!\int\psi q^2(u^r)_-$, $I=\int_{s_0}^t\!\int\psi v^2r^{-3}$ and
$S_0=H(s_0)$, with the time integrals taken on $[s_0,t-\delta]$ and $\delta\to0$ as in Lemma 40A (monotonically for nonnegative integrands); the integrands are nonnegative except the kernel integrals in (f) and the work and pairing integrals in (g), and the rearrangement in (g) is performed on the truncated intervals, where all its terms are finite. The product $r(u^r)_-$ is the radial Reynolds
number of the inflow (the viscosity is $1$).

(a) Let $0\le\theta<1$ and $\Omega_\theta=\{(x,s):3r(u^r)_-(x,s)>\theta\}$. Then
$$\int\chi|Z(t)|\,dx\le\int_{s_0}^t\!\!\int\psi|F|\le\frac1{\sqrt{2(1-\theta)}}\Big[S_0+3\int\!\!\int_{\Omega_\theta}\psi q^2(u^r)_-\Big].$$
Off $\Omega_\theta$, $3q^2(u^r)_-=\frac{v^2}{r^3}\,3r(u^r)_-\le\theta\frac{v^2}{r^3}$, so the identity of Lemma 40A, with $H(t-\delta)\ge0$ and the radial part of $|\nabla v|^2$ dropped, gives
$\int(2X'+(1-\theta)Y')\le S_0+3\int\!\int_{\Omega_\theta}\psi q^2(u^r)_-$; and $|F|=2|v||v_z|r^{-2}\le\kappa(2v_z^2r^{-1}+(1-\theta)v^2r^{-3})$ with $\kappa=(2(1-\theta))^{-1/2}$, the difference
being $\kappa(\sqrt2|v_z|r^{-1/2}-\sqrt{1-\theta}|v|r^{-3/2})^2$. For $\theta=0$ this is Lemma 40A.

(b) Put $A=\int_{s_0}^t\!\int\psi q^2(u^r)_-\,r(u^r)_-=\int_{s_0}^t\!\int\psi v^2(u^r)_-^2r^{-1}$, the inward work weighted by the radial Reynolds number. Then
$$I\le S_0+3W,\qquad W\le3A+\sqrt{AS_0}.$$
The first inequality is the integrated identity of Lemma 40A. By Cauchy-Schwarz, $W\le(IA)^{1/2}$, so $W^2\le A(S_0+3W)$ and $W\le\frac12(3A+(9A^2+4AS_0)^{1/2})\le3A+\sqrt{AS_0}$.
Conversely $A\ge W^2/(S_0+3W)$ whenever $S_0+3W>0$.

(c) On the windows of item (d) of the Remark (the windowed conditions concern a thin cylinder about the axis), with $L_n=M_n^2/D_n$: since $u^r$ and
$u^\theta$ vanish on the axis and $D$ is historical, $(u^r)_-\le\min\{M_n,D_nr\}$ and $|u^\theta|\le D_nr$ on $[s_n,t_n]$ (for large $n$, $\lambda_n\le1$, so $[s_n,t_n]\subset[t_n-2\delta_n,t_n]$). So for fixed $0<\theta<1$ the set $\Omega_\theta$ lies in
$\{r>(\theta/(3D_n))^{1/2}\}$, and $(\theta/(3D_n))^{1/2}M_n\to\infty$ in regime (II): compression at distance at most $(\theta/(3D_n))^{1/2}$ from the axis contributes at most a
$\theta$-fraction of the zero-order dissipation $I$ (precisely, three times the inward work over $\{r\le(\theta/(3D_n))^{1/2}\}$ is at most $\theta I$), and needs no separate estimate; with $\theta=\frac12$, the bound of item (d) of that Remark holds in the form
$$N_n\le\|\eta(s_n)\|_{L^1}+\Sigma'(s_n)+3\int_{s_n}^{t_n}\!\!\int_{\{6r(u^r)_->1\}}\psi q^2(u^r)_-+\frac Kc\,\frac{M_n^2}{D_n}.$$
The elementary estimates do not show that the inward work over the excluded region is small: the bound $q^2(u^r)_-\le\frac{|u^\theta|}2\frac{(u^r)^2+(u^\theta)^2}{r^2}\le\frac{D_n\rho}2|\nabla u|^2$ on
$\{r<\rho\}$ gives $\int_{s_n}^{t_n}\!\int_{\{r<\rho\}}\psi q^2(u^r)_-\le\frac14D_n\rho\,\varepsilon_nE_0$, which at $\rho=(\theta/(3D_n))^{1/2}$ is $O(L_n)$ precisely when $\varepsilon_nD_n^{3/2}/M_n^2$ is bounded; this gives no necessary condition for the actual work to be $O(L_n)$. Similarly, $q^2(u^r)_-\le\frac{|u^\theta|}2|\nabla u|^2\le\frac G{2r}|\nabla u|^2$ gives $\int_{s_n}^{t_n}\!\int_{\{r\ge\rho\}}\psi q^2(u^r)_-\le G\varepsilon_nE_0/(4\rho)$ for every $\rho>0$. Assume $G>0$
(for $G=0$ the source $F$ vanishes) and put $\rho_n^*=G\varepsilon_nE_0D_n/(4M_n^2)$ (if $\varepsilon_n=0$ the work vanishes), so that $\rho_n^*M_n^2/D_n\to0$. Splitting $\{6r(u^r)_->1\}$ at $r=\rho_n^*$, the same bound holds in the form
$$N_n\le\|\eta(s_n)\|_{L^1}+\Sigma'(s_n)+3\int_{s_n}^{t_n}\!\!\int_{\mathcal S_n}\psi q^2(u^r)_-+\Big(3+\frac Kc\Big)\frac{M_n^2}{D_n},\qquad\mathcal S_n=\{6r(u^r)_->1\}\cap\{r<\rho_n^*\}\subset\{(6D_n)^{-1/2}<r<\rho_n^*\}.$$
So the part of the inward-work term not controlled by these estimates is the work on a shell about the axis whose inner radius $(6D_n)^{-1/2}$ is many viscous lengths $1/M_n$
and whose outer radius is $o(D_n/M_n^2)$. If $\varepsilon_nD_n^{3/2}/M_n^2\le4/(\sqrt6\,GE_0)$ along a sequence of velocity first crossings, the shell is empty, and since $\|\eta(s_n)\|_{L^1}$ and $\Sigma'(s_n)$ are
$o(L_n)$ by the choice of $s_n$, $N_n=O(M_n^2/D_n)$ and GOAL follow (a special case of part (d) of the present Remark); the limits above do not imply this inequality.

(d) The same rate controls the full work and implies GOAL. Since $|u^\theta|\le\min\{D_nr,G/r\}$, $r(u^\theta)^2=|\Gamma||u^\theta|\le G^{3/2}D_n^{1/2}$, and $(u^r/r)^2\le|\nabla u|^2$, so
$A\le G^{3/2}D_n^{1/2}\varepsilon_nE_0/2$ on the window, and by (b) and item (d) of the Remark (the windowed conditions concern a thin cylinder about the axis) a bound $\varepsilon_nD_n^{3/2}/M_n^2\le C$ along a sequence of velocity first crossings gives
$W=O(L_n)$ and GOAL with the record normalization along it (Lemma 31); by Lemma 2 the rate condition implies $\varepsilon_n=O(M_n^{-1/2})$ (a necessary condition for it, not a sufficient one). The rate is restrictive: Leray's bound
$\|\nabla u(t)\|_2^2\ge c^2(T_*-t)^{-1/2}$ gives $\varepsilon_n\ge\frac{4c^2}{E_0}\delta_n(T_*-t_n+2\delta_n)^{-1/2}$; if $T_*-t_n\le2\delta_n$ this is at least $\frac{2c^2K}{E_0}D_n/M_n^2$, which exceeds
$CM_n^2/D_n^{3/2}$ for large $n$ (as $D_n^{5/2}/M_n^4\ge cM_n^{1/6}$ by Lemma 2), and otherwise the rate forces $T_*-t_n\ge c'D_n^7/M_n^{12}\ge c''M_n^{-1/3}$. So the rate fails along
every sequence with $M_n(T_*-t_n)^3\to0$. Conversely, under (UP) (which contains (C)), Theorem 32 gives $N_nD_n/M_n^2\to\infty$ along every sequence of velocity first crossings, so $W/L_n\to\infty$ by the same item (d), and part (b) with $S_0=o(L_n)$ forces $A\ge(\frac13-o(1))W$, so $A/L_n\to\infty$ and
$(D_n^{3/2}/M_n^2)\int_{s_n}^{t_n}\!\int\psi(u^r/r)_-^2\to\infty$.

(e) A second weight removes the work at an exponential cost. Let $\mathcal V\ge3(u^r/r)_-$ be smooth and bounded on $[s_0,t]$ and let $h$ solve $\partial_sh+b\cdot\nabla h+\Delta_5^*h+\mathcal Vh=0$ on
$[s_0,t)$, $h(t)=\chi$, constructed like $\psi$ (so $h=r^2g_h$ with $g_h$ smooth for $s<t$). The computation of Lemma 40A with $h$ in place of $\psi$ gives
$\int\chi\frac{v(t)^2}r+\int_{s_0}^t\!\int h\big(\frac{2|\nabla v|^2}r+\frac{v^2}{r^3}+(\mathcal V+3\frac{u^r}r)\frac{v^2}r\big)=\int h(s_0)\frac{v(s_0)^2}r$, with $\mathcal V+3u^r/r\ge0$, and $\psi\le h$. If
$h\le C_h$ on $[s_0,t)$, then $\int_{s_0}^t\!\int\psi|F|\le C_h\Sigma'(s_0)/\sqrt2$, and on the windows of item (d) of the Remark (the windowed conditions concern a thin cylinder about the axis) $N_n\le\|\eta(s_n)\|_{L^1}+C_h\Sigma'(s_n)/\sqrt2+(K/c)M_n^2/D_n$, so a bound
$h\le C_h$ uniform in $n$ gives GOAL. By the Feynman-Kac formula, $h(s,x)=\mathbb E_{s,x}\big[\mathbf 1_{\{\tau_0>t\}}\chi(X_t)\exp\int_s^t\mathcal V(\sigma,X_\sigma)\,d\sigma\big]$, where $X$ is the diffusion with
generator $b\cdot\nabla+\partial_{rr}-r^{-1}\partial_r+\partial_{zz}$ and $\tau_0$ its first hitting time of the axis (an unnormalized expectation with a survival indicator). By Khasminskii's lemma,
$h\le(1-\beta)^{-1}$ if $\mathbb E_{a,x}\big[\int_a^{t\wedge\tau_0}\mathcal V(\sigma,X_\sigma)\,d\sigma\big]\le\beta<1$ for all $s_0\le a<t$ and all starting points $x$ off the axis (paths killed before $t$ included). The admissible choice
$\mathcal V\equiv3D_n$ gives only $h\le e^{3D_n(t_n-s_n)}$, and $D_n(t_n-s_n)\ge D_n\delta_n'=K^2\lambda_n^2D_n^3/M_n^4\ge\frac{\pi K^2}{24E_0}\lambda_n^2M_n\ge\frac{\pi K^2}{24E_0}M_n^{1/2}\to\infty$; this shows that
the elementary upper bound diverges, not that $h$ is unbounded. Khasminskii's hypothesis fails on long windows already for a steady strain, and not only far from the axis: for $b=(-\alpha r,2\alpha z)$ with $\alpha>0$ (infinite energy, a prescribed drift) and $\mathcal V=3\alpha$, the survival law of the absorbing process in the strain example of item (iv) of the Remark (signed and cancellation-preserving forms of Lemma 40) below gives, at a starting point at distance $r$ from the axis and with $R=\alpha r^2$ (the radial Reynolds number $r(u^r)_-$ there), $\mathbb E_{a,x}\int_a^{t\wedge\tau_0}\mathcal V\,d\sigma=3\int_0^{\alpha(t-a)}\big(1-\exp(-R/(2(e^{2k}-1)))\big)dk$. Since survival tends to one as $r\to\infty$, the supremum over starting points is $3\alpha(t-s_0)$, so the hypothesis holds exactly when $3\alpha(t-s_0)<1$. At fixed $R$ the limit of the expectation as $\alpha(t-a)\to\infty$ is $\frac32\big(\log(R/2)+\gamma+e^{R/2}E_1(R/2)\big)$ ($\gamma$ Euler's constant, $E_1$ the exponential integral), which is increasing in $R$, equals $\frac32(\log(R/2)+\gamma)+O(R^{-1})$ as $R\to\infty$, and exceeds $1$ for $R\ge\frac34$ (it equals $1$ at $R\approx0.7219$); hence the expectation exceeds $1$ on sufficiently long windows from every starting point with $\alpha r^2\ge\frac34$, that is, at moderate radial Reynolds number. This is the failure of a sufficient criterion: on a fixed window $h\le e^{3\alpha(t-s)}\sup\chi$ for this drift.

(f) The vorticity producing the inflow. For integrable axisymmetric $f$ put $a_f=-\partial_z(-\Delta_5)^{-1}f$, so that $u^r/r=a_\eta$. Then
$$\int(a_f)_-\,\frac{dx}r\le\frac12\int|f|\,dx,$$
and the constant is sharp: defining the operator on finite axisymmetric measures by superposition of circles of positive radius, equality holds for every nonnegative such measure supported in one plane $\{z=z_0\}$ and giving the axis zero mass, and smooth densities approach it. Indeed $a_f(X)=\frac3{8\pi^2}\int(z-\zeta)|X-Y|^{-5}f(Y)\,dY$ in $\mathbb R^5$, so by the triangle inequality it suffices to take for $f$ the uniform unit mass ($\int f\,dx=1$) on the circle $\{r=s,z=0\}$, $s>0$, or its negative (by the reflection $z\mapsto-z$ the positive and negative parts of the field of one circle have the same integral). Then $a_f(r,z)=\frac{3s^2}{16\pi^3}\,z\int_{S^3}(|re-s\Omega|^2+z^2)^{-5/2}d\Omega$ for a fixed unit vector $e\in\mathbb R^4$, which is negative exactly for $z<0$. Since $\int_0^\infty t(A+t^2)^{-5/2}dt=\frac13A^{-3/2}$, $\int(a_f)_-\,dz=\frac{s^2}{16\pi^3}\int_{S^3}|re-s\Omega|^{-3}d\Omega$, and by rotation invariance $\int_0^\infty\!\int_{S^3}|re-s\Omega|^{-3}d\Omega\,dr=\int_{\mathbb R^4}|y|^{-3}|y-y_0|^{-3}dy$ with $|y_0|=s$, which is $4\pi^2s^{-2}$ by the composition rule $I_1I_1=I_2$ of the Riesz potentials $I_\alpha g=c_\alpha|y|^{\alpha-4}*g$ in $\mathbb R^4$, where $c_1=c_2=(4\pi^2)^{-1}$. With $dx=2\pi r\,dr\,dz$ the left side is $2\pi\cdot\frac{s^2}{16\pi^3}\cdot\frac{4\pi^2}{s^2}=\frac12$. For a nonnegative measure in one plane, every circular contribution to $a_f$ has the sign of $z-z_0$, so the negative parts add. Since $q^2(u^r)_-=v^2(a_\eta)_-/r$ and, on the windows of part (c), $v^2\le\min\{D_n^2r^2,G^2r^{-2}\}\le GD_n$, splitting $\eta(s)=\eta\mathbf 1_{\Omega(s)}+\eta\mathbf 1_{\Omega(s)^c}$ for any measurable family of axisymmetric regions $\Omega(s)$ gives
$$\int_{s_n}^{t_n}\!\!\int\psi q^2(u^r)_-\le\frac{GD_n}2\int_{s_n}^{t_n}\!\!\int_{\Omega(s)}|\eta|\,dx\,ds+\int_{s_n}^{t_n}\!\!\int\psi\,\frac{v^2}r\big(a_{\eta\mathbf 1_{\Omega(s)^c}}\big)_-\,dx\,ds.$$
For $\Omega(s)$ the meridional tube about $x_n$, the first term is at most $\frac12GD_n(t_n-s_n)$ times the largest tube mass on the window, and $\frac12GD_n(t_n-s_n)\ge\frac{\pi K^2G}{48E_0}\lambda_n^2M_n\to\infty$ by part (e) (for $G>0$), so this is not a bound by an $o(1)$ multiple of the tube masses. If the free-model decay $\psi\le r^2/(4(t_n-s))$ of part (d) of the Remark (the windowed conditions concern a thin cylinder about the axis) were available (it is not established for a general drift), then $\psi v^2/r\le\min\{GD_n,G^2/(4(t_n-s))\}/r$, and the coefficient would become $\frac12\int_0^{t_n-s_n}\min\{GD_n,\frac{G^2}{4\sigma}\}d\sigma$, which equals $\frac{G^2}8\big(1+\log\frac{4D_n(t_n-s_n)}G\big)$ once $4D_n(t_n-s_n)\ge G$, as holds for large $n$, and still tends to infinity. These are comparisons of upper-bound coefficients, not lower bounds for the work. The same operator bound controls the second term by $\frac12GD_n\int_{s_n}^{t_n}\int_{\Omega(s)^c}|\eta|\,dx\,ds$, but gives no control of it by the mass inside the tube: vorticity outside the tube can induce inflow on a part of the shell where $\eta$ vanishes, and no bound for this exterior contribution at the required order is established here.

(g) A signed pairing. Integrating by parts in $z$ at fixed $r$ (the localized form of Lemma 29(c), as in the Remark (signed and cancellation-preserving forms of Lemma 40)), $\int\psi q^2(-u^r)\,dx=-\int r\varphi(\psi F+q^2\partial_z\psi)\,dx$ for $s<t$, and the identity of Lemma 40A gives its integrated bound with the signed work $\mathcal W_\psi=\int_{s_0}^t\!\int\psi q^2(-u^r)$ in place of the inward work: $\int_{s_0}^t\!\int\psi|F|\le\frac1{\sqrt2}(S_0+3\mathcal W_\psi)$. Hence, if $m=\sup|r\varphi|$ over $[s_0,t)\times\mathbb R^3$ satisfies $m<\sqrt2/3$, then
$$\int_{s_0}^t\!\!\int\psi|F|\le\frac{S_0+3\int_{s_0}^t\!\int|r\varphi|\,q^2|\partial_z\psi|}{\sqrt2-3m}.$$
Here $2\pi r\cdot r\varphi=2\pi\int_0^r\sigma u^z(\sigma,z)\,d\sigma$ is the axial flux through the disc of radius $r$, so $r\varphi$ is half of $r$ times the disc mean of $u^z$, a signed Reynolds number of the meridional flow of the same kind as the radial Reynolds number in (a)-(c). Besides $|r\varphi|\le\|u\|_\infty r/2$, the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality in $r$ and the one-dimensional bound $\sup g^2\le\|g\|_2\|g'\|_2$ in $z$ give $|r\varphi|^2\le\frac1{4\pi}\|u^z\|_2\|\partial_zu^z\|_2$, so $m$ is finite on every interval ending before $T_*$, but this does not make it small near $T_*$. On the selected record windows this hypothesis fails for all large $n$. Let $s_n$ be as in the Remark (a windowed source condition), $\mathcal I_n=\mathcal I_{s_n}(t_n)$ and $m_n=\sup|r\varphi|$ over $[s_n,t_n)\times\mathbb R^3$. Integrating Lemma 29(c) over $[s_n,t_n)$ gives $W_{s_n}(t_n)\le m_n\mathcal I_n$, and Lemma 29(b) gives $\sqrt3\,\mathcal I_n\le\Sigma'(s_n)+3W_{s_n}(t_n)$, so $(\sqrt3-3m_n)\mathcal I_n\le\Sigma'(s_n)$. There $\Sigma'(s_n)=o(M_n^2/D_n)$ and $\mathcal I_n\ge(\sqrt2-o(1))M_n^2/D_n$, hence $m_n\ge\frac1{\sqrt3}-o(1)$, and $\frac1{\sqrt3}>\frac{\sqrt2}3$. So on these windows $|r\varphi|$ reaches at least $\frac1{\sqrt3}-o(1)$ somewhere (a proved necessary condition). More precisely, since $-r\varphi F\le|r\varphi||F|$, the same two inequalities give $\int_{s_n}^{t_n}\!\int|r\varphi||F|\ge\frac1{\sqrt3}\mathcal I_n-\frac13\Sigma'(s_n)$: the source-weighted mean of $|r\varphi|$ on the window is at least $\frac1{\sqrt3}-o(1)$; as $|r\varphi|\le M_nr/2$ there, this contains the mean radius bound of item (b) of the Remark (the windowed conditions concern a thin cylinder about the axis). Since $m_n\ge\frac1{\sqrt3}-o(1)>\frac{\sqrt2}3$, the absorption argument above does not apply on these windows, whatever bound holds for the term containing $\partial_z\psi$ (for which no bound of order $M_n^2/D_n$ is established either). The bound $m_n\ge\frac1{\sqrt3}-o(1)$ is, however, much weaker than an elementary kinematic fact, by which $m_n\to\infty$ in regime (II); the information specific to Lemma 29 is the source-weighted bound, which concerns where the swirl source lies and not only the supremum. Let $\Psi(r,z)=\int_0^r\sigma u^z(\sigma,z)\,d\sigma$ be the Stokes stream function, so that $r\varphi=\Psi/r$, $\partial_r\Psi=ru^z$ and $\partial_z\Psi=-ru^r$; $r\varphi$ is invariant under the Navier-Stokes scaling. Let $t<T_*$ be a time with $\|u(t)\|_\infty=M(t)$, write $M=M(t)$, $D=D(t)$, and assume $4GD\le M^2$. The maximum of $|u(t)|$ is attained, since $u(t)$ is Lipschitz and square integrable; let $P=(r_0,z_0)$ be a meridional point where it is attained, and put $\rho=M/(8D)$. Since $u^r$ and $u^\theta$ vanish on the axis, $|u^r|,|u^\theta|\le Dr$; with $|u^\theta|\le G/r$ this gives $|u^\theta(P)|\le\sqrt{GD}\le M/2$, so the meridional velocity $U=(u^r,u^z)(P)$ has $|U|\ge\frac{\sqrt3}2M$, and on the meridional disc of radius $\rho$ about $P$ (intersected with $\{r\ge0\}$) the meridional velocity differs from $U$ by at most $D\rho=M/8$, since $(u^r,u^z)=(u_1,u_3)$ on the half plane $\{x_2=0,\ x_1\ge0\}$. If $r_0\ge\rho$, let $\nu=U/|U|$ and $e=(\nu^z,-\nu^r)$; then $\partial_e\Psi=r\,\nu\cdot(u^r,u^z)\ge r(|U|-\frac M8)\ge\frac38rM$ on that disc, and along the segment $\{P+se:|s|\le\rho/2\}$, on which $\frac12r_0\le r\le\frac32r_0$, $\Psi$ increases by at least $\frac3{16}r_0M\rho$; at one endpoint $|\Psi|\ge\frac3{32}r_0M\rho$ and $r\le\frac32r_0$, so $|r\varphi|\ge M\rho/16$. If $r_0<\rho$, the points $(r,z_0)$ with $0\le r\le\rho$ lie in that disc, $|U^r|=|u^r(P)|\le Dr_0<\frac M8$, so $|U^z|>\frac58M$ and $u^z(\cdot,z_0)$ has a fixed sign and modulus at least $\frac M2$ on $[0,\rho]$; hence $|\Psi(\rho,z_0)|\ge M\rho^2/4$ and $|r\varphi(\rho,z_0)|\ge M\rho/4$. In both cases
$$\sup_x|r\varphi(x,t)|\ge\frac{M(t)^2}{128\,D(t)}.$$
Velocity first crossings are such times. In regime (II), $4GD\le M^2$ eventually, so $\sup_x|r\varphi(x,t_n)|\ge M_n^2/(128D_n)\to\infty$, and $m_n\to\infty$ since $r\varphi$ is continuous. Conversely, a bound $\limsup_{t\uparrow T_*}\sup_x|r\varphi(x,t)|=K<\infty$ would give, at every velocity first crossing close to $T_*$, either $4GD>M^2$ (possible only if $G>0$, and then $D/M^2>1/(4G)$) or $D/M^2\ge1/(128(K+1))$, hence regime (I). This converse is vacuous for singular trajectories: by Theorem 1.4 of Lei and Zhang (J. Funct. Anal. 261 (2011), 2323-2345), a suitable weak solution on $\mathbb R^3\times[0,T]$ with initial data in $L^2$ and bounded initial circulation is smooth on $\mathbb R^3\times(0,T]$ if $u(t)=\operatorname{curl}B(t)$ with $\sup_{0<t<T}\|B(t)\|_{BMO}<\infty$. Here $b=u^re_r+u^ze_z=\operatorname{curl}\big((\Psi/r)e_\theta\big)$, and the divergence-free swirl part $u^\theta e_\theta$ is the curl of a field with $BMO$ seminorm at most $CG$, by the standard bound for the Riesz potential of order one of a field whose mass in every ball of radius $R$ is at most $CGR^2$, which holds since $|u^\theta|\le G/r$; so a bound on $\sup|r\varphi|$ up to $T_*$ (it is finite on every interval ending before $T_*$, by the estimate above) gives such a potential, and $u$ would be smooth up to $T_*$. Thus $\sup_x|r\varphi|$ is unbounded near $T_*$ on every singular trajectory; what the kinematic bound adds in regime (II) is the rate $M_n^2/(128D_n)$ along first crossings, and the conclusion that, for each fixed $m_*<\infty$, the bound $\sup_{[s_n,t_n)\times\mathbb R^3}|r\varphi|\le m_*$ (such as the absorption hypothesis above) fails for all sufficiently large $n$ in regime (II). The result of Lei and Zhang is used only for this contextual regularity observation; the reductions, window estimates and kinematic argument are independent of it.
The estimates above are proved; the additional uniform bounds in (b)-(f) that would give GOAL are not established, and the smallness hypothesis of (g) fails on the selected record windows. The terminal support of $\chi$ does not confine
$\psi$ or $h$ to the cylinder about the axis; none of the bounds above uses such a confinement.

A sufficient condition for (VL1)
at an admissible normalization is that, for a time $s_0<T_*$ fixed independently of $n$ and axisymmetric terminal data $\chi_n$ at the window times with $1_{E_{n,R}}\le\chi_n\le1$ and
$\int\chi_n\le C_R|E_{n,R}|$ (an enlarged meridional rectangle provides one), the three quantities $C_0|E_{n,R}|$,
$\int\psi_n(s_0)\Gamma(s_0)^2r^{-3}\le\|u^\theta(s_0)q(s_0)\|_\infty\int\chi_n$ and $\int\!\int\psi_n|u^\theta||\nabla u|^2$ are at most
$C_{R,A}M_n\ell_n$ for all $R,A$ (by Lemma 40A; with Lemma 40 the radial term $\int\!\int q^2(\partial_r\psi_n)_+$ would be a fourth quantity); then $\int_{E_{n,R}}|\eta|\le C'_{R,A}M_n\ell_n$ on the windows and Corollary 6A(a) gives (VL1). (For the conclusion (L2M), uniformity in the window times can be replaced by a window average: since $\int_{B_{R\ell_n}(x_n)}|\omega^\theta|\,dx\le R\ell_n\int_{E_{n,R}}|\eta|\,dx$ (a horizontal circle meets $B_{R\ell_n}(x_n)$ in length at most $2\pi R\ell_n$), the window average bound $\int_{s_n-A\ell_n/M_n}^{s_n}\int_{E_{n,R}}|\eta|\,dx\,dt\le C_{R,A}\ell_n^2$ for all $R,A$ gives (VC) of Theorem 6', hence (L2M) by Propositions 20(c) and 22(a).) With (L2-OSC) the first
two are $o(M_n\ell_n)$, since $|E_{n,R}|=O(M_n^{-2})$ (proof of Theorem 38(a)) and $M_n\ell_n\to\infty$, so only the time integral needs a
bound, and then GOAL follows; without (L2-OSC) the first two need a separate argument. The condition is not proved, it is sufficient
and not known to be necessary, and in (C) it fails at every normalization with (L2-OSC). The positive adjoint
does not decay by itself: for the strain $b=(\kappa r,-2\kappa z)$, $P(t,s)^*1=1-\exp(-\kappa r^2/(2(1-e^{-2\kappa(t-s)})))\ge1-e^{-c^2/2}$ at
$r=c\kappa^{-1/2}$ for all ages, and a finite-energy cutoff of this strain keeps half of this up to age $\kappa^{-1}\log\lambda$, $\lambda\ge1$, at the point $(r,z)=(c\kappa^{-1/2},0)$ (for a starting height $z_0\ne0$ the axial exit bound below acquires the additional term $2z_0^2/L^2$).
Precisely, let $p_c=1-e^{-c^2/2}$, $C(c)^2\ge4(c^2+e^{-1})/p_c$, $L=C(c)\lambda\kappa^{-1/2}$, $\zeta$ smooth with $\zeta=1$ on $[0,1]$ and $\zeta=0$ on
$[4,\infty)$, and let $b_L$ have Stokes stream function $-\kappa r^2z\,\zeta((r^2+z^2)/L^2)$ ($b^r=-r^{-1}\partial_z\Psi$, $b^z=r^{-1}\partial_r\Psi$); it is smooth,
divergence free, equal to the strain on $B_L$ and supported in $B_{2L}$. The weight $P(t,s)^*1$ is the survival probability of the diffusion
$dR=\sqrt2\,dB+(b^r-R^{-1})dv$, $dZ=\sqrt2\,dW+b^z\,dv$ absorbed at the axis, started at $(r,z)$. For the strain, $e^{-2\kappa v}R_v^2$ is a nonnegative
local martingale started at $c^2/\kappa$, so by the maximal inequality $R$ reaches $L/\sqrt2$ before age $H\le\kappa^{-1}\log\lambda$ with probability at
most $2c^2/C(c)^2$; optional stopping for $d(Z^2)=2\sqrt2Z\,dW+(2-4\kappa Z^2)dv$ bounds the probability that $|Z|$ reaches $L/\sqrt2$ by
$4H/L^2\le2/(eC(c)^2)$. The two diffusions, driven by the same Brownian motions, agree until exit from $B_L$, so the cutoff weight is at
least $p_{\rm strain}(H)-p_c/2\ge\frac12p_{\rm strain}(H)\ge p_c/2$. This concerns prescribed drifts and one observation point; the energy of $b_L$
grows with $L$, and $b_L$ is not shown to be part of a Navier-Stokes solution. In general $0\le P(t,s)^*\chi\le1-\exp(-r^2/(4a_k(s,t)))$ when $b^r\le kr$,
with $a_k(s,t)=\int_s^t\exp(-2\int_s^vk)\,dv$.
A localized form holds when the cutoff avoids a cylinder: if $\chi=0$ on $\{r<\rho_0\}$, $0<\alpha<2$ and
$k_\alpha(v)=\sup_{r<\rho_0}(u^r/r-(2-\alpha)r^{-2})_+$, then $P(t,s)^*\chi\le(r/\rho_0)^\alpha\exp(\alpha\int_s^tk_\alpha)$ on $\{r<\rho_0\}$. Proof: $w=(r/\rho_0)^\alpha h(s)$,
$h(s)=\exp(\alpha\int_s^tk_\alpha)\ge1$, satisfies $\partial_sw+b\cdot\nabla w+\Delta w-\frac2r\partial_rw=\alpha w(u^r/r-(2-\alpha)r^{-2}-k_\alpha)\le0$ there; $w\ge1\ge\psi$
at $r=\rho_0$ and $w\ge0=\psi$ at $s=t$, where $\psi=P(t,s)^*\chi$; $\psi=r^2g$, where $g$ solves $\partial_sg+b\cdot\nabla g+\Delta_5g+2(u^r/r)g=0$, so
$\|g(s)\|_\infty\le\rho_0^{-2}\exp(2\int_s^t\|(u^r/r)_+\|_\infty)$ and $w\ge\psi$ at $r=\delta$ for small $\delta$ since $\alpha<2$; the maximum principle on
$\{\delta<r<\rho_0\}$ (with $\epsilon e^{\lambda(t-s)}(1+z^2)$ added, $\lambda>2M(t)+2$, $\epsilon\to0$) gives $w\ge\psi$. Since $|u^r/r|\le D$, $k_1(v)$ only sees outward strain in
the annulus $D(v)^{-1/2}\le r<\rho_0$, and with $\mathfrak K=\int_{s_0}^tk_1$ the production in a core $\{r<\rho_c\}$, $\rho_c<\rho_0$, reaches $\chi$ with weight at most
$e^{\mathfrak K}\rho_c/\rho_0$; for the swirl core $\rho_c=(G/D)^{1/2}$ and $\rho_0\ge cM/D$ this is at most $Ce^{\mathfrak K}(GD)^{1/2}/M$, whose factor $D^{1/2}/M$ tends to zero in regime (II), but decay of the product requires an additional estimate. No bound on $\mathfrak K$ is known (the
elementary one is $\mathfrak K\le D(t)(t-s_0)$), the production between the core and the window is not reached by this barrier, and since
$\sup_{r<\rho_0}u^r/r\ge0$ always (otherwise $\partial_zu^z\ge c>0$ on the axis, contradicting the boundedness of $u^z$) a signed version cannot record
inflow.

**Remark (signed and cancellation-preserving forms of Lemma 40).** (i) Before the signs are dropped, the proof of Lemma 40 gives
$$\int_{s_0}^t\!\!\int\psi|F|\le\frac1{\sqrt3}\Big[\int\psi(s_0)\frac{\Gamma(s_0)^2}{r^3}+3\mathcal W_\psi+\int_{s_0}^t\!\!\int q^2\partial_r\psi\Big],\qquad
\mathcal W_\psi=\int_{s_0}^t\!\!\int\psi q^2(-u^r),$$
with the signed integrals understood as limits of integrals over $[s_0,t-\delta]$. Splitting $u^r=u^r[\eta^d]+u^r[Z]$ as in Proposition 39,
the inherited part of $\mathcal W_\psi$ is at most $C_d$ in absolute value, because the proof of Proposition 39 bounds the absolute
integrand $q^2|u^r[\eta^d]|\le K_0q^2r$ and $0\le\psi\le1$. Localization destroys the instantaneous sign of the Remark after
Proposition 39: $\int\psi f(-u^r[\partial_zf])=-\int r\psi\varphi\,\partial_zf-\int r\varphi f\partial_z\psi$ has no sign, and for two nonnegative bumps
of $f=q^2$ with separated axial supports, the first much larger, a cutoff selecting the second gives positive localized work by
the kernel computation in that remark. (ii) The exact identity
$$\int\chi|Z(t)|\,dx=\sup\Big\{\Big|\int_{s_0}^t\!\!\int q^2\,\partial_z(P(t,s)^*h)\,dx\,ds\Big|:h\ \text{smooth, axisymmetric},\ |h|\le\chi\Big\}$$
(duality, Duhamel, one integration by parts in $z$, and approximation of $\chi\operatorname{sign}Z(t)$) keeps the cancellations of the
source that Lemma 40 discards; it is a reformulation, not a bound. (iii) For the steady Burgers column $u^r=-\gamma r/2$, $u^z=\gamma z$,
$\Gamma=G(1-e^{-\gamma r^2/4})$ (infinite energy) one has $F=0$, while the unweighted work per unit length grows linearly in time; for
a fixed compact terminal cutoff the adjoint-weighted work is bounded uniformly in the elapsed time, by $\|V\|_\infty\int\chi$ with
$V(r)=\int_r^\infty m(a)^{-1}\int_0^am(v)w(v)\,dv\,da$, $m=r^3e^{\gamma r^2/4}$, $w=q^2(-u^r)$, which solves $V''+(3/r+\gamma r/2)V'=-w$. So replacing
the axial variation $2(X_zY)^{1/2}$ by work loses information, but not through linear growth of the localized budget. (iv) None of
these supplies the needed transmission bound: $0\le\psi_n\le1$ gives only $\int\!\int\psi_n|F|\le\mathcal I_{s_0}(t_n)$, and in (C) the bound
$C_{R,A}M_n\ell_n$ is $o(\mathcal I_{s_0}(t_n))$ at every normalization with (L2-OSC) (Theorem 38(b)). Transmission can be much smaller than production: in the strain $b=(-\gamma r,2\gamma z)$ (infinite energy) the absorbing adjoint is $P(t,s)^*1=1-\exp[-\gamma r^2/(2(e^{2\gamma(t-s)}-1))]$, and $\Gamma=G(1-e^{-\gamma r^2/2})f(t,z)$ with $f=A^{-1/2}\exp(-\gamma e^{-4\gamma t}z^2/A)$, $A=2-e^{-4\gamma t}$, solves the swirl equation with $\|F(t)\|_1=2A^{-1}\pi G^2\gamma\log2$ for all $t\ge0$, while $\int|Z(t)|\,dx\le\int_0^t\!\int P(t,s)^*1\,|F|\le CG^2$ uniformly in $t$; this is an example in a prescribed drift, not a trajectory, and gives no bound in (C). The rate exponents
$D=M^{19/10}$, $S=M^{4/5}$, $\mathcal I\asymp W\asymp M^{2/5}$, $\ell=M^{-3/4}$ satisfy $M/D\ll\ell\ll\mathcal I/M\ll S/M$, $\mathcal ID/M^2\to\infty$ and the
relation $E_0/(M^2(\mathcal I/M)^3)=E_0M^{-1/5}\to0$, so the energy bound $M_n^2\ell_n^3\le CE_0$ that retained oscillation requires (proof of Theorem 38(a)) fails at the source scale, consistently with Theorem 38(e); this is bookkeeping of exponents, not a trajectory. (v) The $L^2$
form needs a rate that is not available. Testing the equation of $Z$ with $Z$ (the axis term has a sign, and $\int ZF=-\int q^2\partial_zZ$)
gives $\|Z(t)\|_2^2\le\frac12\int_{s_0}^t\|q\|_4^4\,ds$; dually, $\int\psi(s_0)^2+2\int_{s_0}^t\!\int|\nabla\psi|^2=\int\chi^2$, since the term from
$-\frac2r\partial_r\psi$ is a multiple of $\int\psi(0,z,s)^2dz=0$. Hence $\int\chi_n|Z(t)|\le(\int\chi_n)^{1/2}(\frac12\int_{s_0}^t\|q\|_4^4)^{1/2}$,
and $\int_{s_0}^t\|q\|_4^4=o(D(t)^2)$ by Limitations 6(iv). But $\int\chi_n\ge|E_{n,R}|\ge2\pi R^3\ell_n^3$, and $\ell_n\ge cM_n/D_n$ at every
normalization with (L2-OSC), because $O_0(x,s,\rho)\le D(s)\rho$. Put $Q(t)=\int_{s_0}^t\|q(s)\|_4^4\,ds$. The window times satisfy $t\le s_n$, so for this Cauchy-Schwarz majorant to be $O(M_n\ell_n)$
throughout the window it is necessary that $Q(s_n)\int\chi_n\le CM_n^2\ell_n^2$, hence $Q(s_n)\le CM_n^2/\ell_n\le C'M_nD_n$ (with the larger
common majorant using $Q(t_n)$ the same rate is required of $Q(t_n)$). These are requirements for this majorant, not necessary conditions
for the vorticity mass bound or for GOAL. Since $M_nD_n=o(D_n^2)$, the available little-o estimate does not give either rate; no such rate
is proved, and the $L^1$ and $L^2$ forms together leave the window of the Remark after Theorem 38 unchanged. (vi) An entropy form localizes the quartic at a logarithmic cost. Let $0\le\chi\le1$ and $\psi$ be as in Lemma 40, $v=\int\chi$,
$\mathcal H(s)=\int\psi(s)\log\psi(s)\,dx\le0$ and $\mathcal J=\int_{s_0}^t\!\int|\nabla\psi|^2/\psi$. In $\mathcal H'$ the transport term vanishes since
$\operatorname{div}b=0$, the term from $-\frac2r\partial_r\psi$ is $4\pi\int\!\int\partial_r(\psi\log\psi)\,dr\,dz=0$ since $\psi=O(r^2)$, and the axis flux of the
Laplacian is $O(r^2|\log r|)$; so $\mathcal H'=\int|\nabla\psi|^2/\psi$ and $\mathcal J\le\mathcal H(t)-\mathcal H(s_0)\le-\mathcal H(s_0)$ (for rough terminal data integrate on
$[s_0,t-\delta]$ and use $\mathcal H\le0$). With $x_0$ on the axis and $m_2(s)=\int\psi(s)|x-x_0|^2dx$ one has $-m_2'=-2\int\psi\,b\cdot(x-x_0)+10v$ (the
Laplacian gives $6v$, the radial term $4v$), so by comparison $m_2(s_0)^{1/2}\le m_2(t)^{1/2}+v^{1/2}(\int_{s_0}^t\|u\|_\infty+(10(t-s_0))^{1/2})$, and the
Gaussian bound for the entropy at given second moment gives $-\mathcal H(s_0)\le v\log(1/v)+\frac32v\log(2\pi e\,m_2(s_0)/(3v))$. Split $\chi=\chi_++\chi_-$
with $\chi_+=\chi1_{\{Z(t)\ge0\}}$, $\chi_-=\chi1_{\{Z(t)<0\}}$. By duality, $\int\chi_\pm Z(t)=-\int_{s_0}^t\!\int q^2\partial_z\psi_\pm$ (bounded approximation, and
integration by parts as in Lemma 40, justified by $\int\!\int q^2|\partial_z\psi_\pm|\le(Q_\psi\mathcal J_\pm)^{1/2}$); the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality with weight
$\psi_\pm$, the bound on $\mathcal J_\pm$ and $-\alpha\log\alpha-\beta\log\beta\le-(\alpha+\beta)\log(\alpha+\beta)+(\alpha+\beta)\log2$ give
$$\int\chi|Z(t)|\le\big((v\log2-\mathcal H(s_0))\,Q_\psi\big)^{1/2},\qquad Q_\psi=\int_{s_0}^t\!\int q^4\psi\le Q(t).$$
Since $q^4=u^\theta q\,\Gamma^2r^{-5}$, $|q|\le|\nabla u|$ and $|u^\theta|\le S$, the identity in the proof of Lemma 40 and $\int\!\int q^2(\partial_r\psi)_+\le(Q_\psi\mathcal J)^{1/2}$
give $Q_\psi\le\frac23SD(H_0+\frac32S\varepsilon_\psi+(Q_\psi\mathcal J)^{1/2})$, hence
$$Q_\psi\le\tfrac43SD\,H_0+2S^2D\,\varepsilon_\psi+\tfrac49S^2D^2\mathcal J,\qquad H_0=\int\psi(s_0)\frac{\Gamma(s_0)^2}{r^3},\quad\varepsilon_\psi=\int_{s_0}^t\!\int\psi|\nabla u|^2,$$
with $S=S(t)$, $D=D(t)$; the radial term of Lemma 40 is absorbed. With Lemma 40A in place of the identity in the proof of Lemma 40 the same steps give
$Q_\psi\le SD\,H_0+\frac32S^2D\,\varepsilon_\psi$, with no term in $\mathcal J$. At the window solids take $1_{E_{n,R}}\le\chi_n\le1$ with $\operatorname{supp}\chi_n\subset E_{n,2R}$
(so $v_n=\int\chi_n\le C|E_{n,R}|$) and $x_0$ the axis point at the height of the window centre. Then $|E_{n,R}|=O(M_n^{-2})$ and $|E_{n,R}|\ge2\pi R^3\ell_n^3$
with $\ell_n\ge cM_n/D_n$, so $v_n\ge c_RM_n^{-3}$ eventually; the distance of the window centre to the axis is $O(D_n^2/M_n^4+\ell_n)$ (the solid has volume at
least $c\,r\ell_n^2$ if that distance $r$ exceeds $2R\ell_n$), so $m_2(t)/v_n=O(1)$; with $\int_{s_0}^t\|u\|_\infty\le M_n(t-s_0)$ this gives
$-\mathcal H(s_0)\le C_Rv_n\log M_n$. (Without a support condition the entropy is not controlled by the mass: a bump of fixed small mass spread at small
amplitude over a large region has arbitrarily large $-\mathcal H$.) Since $H_0\le\|u^\theta(s_0)q(s_0)\|_\infty v_n$, the produced part satisfies $\int\chi_n|Z(t)|\le
C_{R,A}M_n\ell_n$ on the windows as soon as, with the historical records $M_n=M(t_n)$, $D_n=D(t_n)$, $S_n=S(t_n)$ (which bound the values at every window time $t\le s_n\le t_n$),
$$\Lambda_n=S_nD_nv_n\log M_n\le C_{R,A}M_n\ell_n\qquad\text{and}\qquad S_n\Lambda_n\varepsilon_{\psi_n}\le C_{R,A}M_n^2\ell_n^2;$$
since $\varepsilon_\psi\le\epsilon(s_0)\le E_0/2$, the second condition is implied by the stronger condition $S_n\Lambda_n\le C_{R,A}M_n^2\ell_n^2$, and in (C), where $M_n\ell_n\ll S_n$, the stronger condition implies the first.
Compared with (v), the quartic is localized by $\psi$ at the cost of the factor $\log M_n$, and no strain clock enters. The conditions are not proved: with
$v_n\ge c\ell_n^3$ and $\ell_n\ge cM_n/D_n$ the stronger condition $S_n\Lambda_n\le CM_n^2\ell_n^2$ forces $S_n^2\log M_n=O(M_n)$, and in the bookkeeping of (iv) one has
$S\Lambda\ge cM^{5/4}\log M$ against $M^2\ell^2=M^{1/2}$ and $\Lambda\ge cM^{9/20}\log M$ against $M\ell=M^{1/4}$. The dissipation can be localized: the forward
and adjoint $L^2$ identities have no drift or axis contribution of the wrong sign, so the Nash inequality, duality and composition at the midpoint
give $\|\psi(s)\|_\infty\le\min\{1,Cv(t-s)^{-3/2}\}$ with an absolute $C$; splitting the time integral at a backward age $\delta$ gives
$\varepsilon_\psi\le vD^2\delta+Cv\delta^{-3/2}\epsilon(s_0)$, hence $\varepsilon_\psi\le\min\{\epsilon(s_0),Cv D^{6/5}\epsilon(s_0)^{2/5}\}$, and $\varepsilon_{\psi_n}\to0$ at the
windows (first $n\to\infty$ on $[s_0,s_1]$, then $s_1\uparrow T_*$). In the bookkeeping of (iv) $vD^{6/5}\ge\ell^3D^{6/5}=M^{3/100}$ does not decay, and no bound on
$\varepsilon_\psi$ affects the condition on $\Lambda_n$. No estimate established here yields these conditions throughout (C), and their necessity is not established. (vii) A quartic identity removes the Fisher term from the budget of (vi). Put $h=r^2q^4=(u^\theta)^4r^{-2}$ and $L=\partial_s+b\cdot\nabla-\Delta_5$, $\Delta_5=\partial_{rr}+\frac3r\partial_r+\partial_{zz}$, and $a=u^r/r$ (in this item $a$ is not an exponent). From $Lq=-2aq$, $L(r^2)=2ar^2-8$ and completion of the radial square, $Lh+\frac83q^4+12r^2q^2[(q_r+\frac{2q}{3r})^2+q_z^2]=-6ah$ pointwise. Since $h=O(r^2)$ and $\psi=O(r^2)$ for $s<t$, $\frac d{ds}\int\psi h=\int\psi Lh$ with no derivative on $\psi$ (the axis terms are $O(r^3)$ as in Lemma 40; integrate on $[s_0,t-\delta]$ and let $\delta\to0$). Dropping $\int\chi h(t)\ge0$ and the square gives $\frac83Q_\psi\le\int\psi(s_0)h(s_0)-6\int_{s_0}^t\!\int\psi ah$, and $|a|\le|\nabla u|$, $h\le S^2q^2$, Cauchy-Schwarz with weight $\psi$ and Young give $$Q_\psi\le\tfrac34\|r^2q(s_0)^4\|_\infty v+\tfrac{81}{16}S^4\varepsilon_\psi .$$ With the bound of (vi) the condition on $\Lambda_n$ is no longer needed: on the windows $\int_{E_{n,R}}|Z(t)|\le C_{R,A}M_n\ell_n$ follows from the single condition $S_n^4v_n\varepsilon_{\psi_n}\log M_n\le C_{R,A}M_n^2\ell_n^2$, with the fixed $s_0$, the cutoffs, the historical record $S_n=S(t_n)$ and the entropy bound of (vi) (the datum term contributes $O(v_n(\log M_n)^{1/2})=o(M_n\ell_n)$), and this condition is implied by the second condition of (vi) since $S^2\le GD$. It asks for a rate of the localized dissipation, $\varepsilon_{\psi_n}\le C_{R,A}M_n^2\ell_n^2/(S_n^4v_n\log M_n)$; since $v_n\ge c\ell_n^3$, in the bookkeeping of (iv) this necessarily requires $\varepsilon_{\psi_n}\le CM_n^{-9/20}(\log M_n)^{-1}$ (and is equivalent to it if $v_n$ is comparable to $\ell_n^3$), while the adjoint smoothing gives only $\varepsilon_{\psi_n}\to0$ without a rate. Keeping the sign of the work instead, the part $a^d$ of $a$ generated by $\eta^d$ is bounded, $|a^d|\le K_d$, and is absorbed (since $h=|\Gamma||q|^3\le G|q|^3$, $6K_dh\le\frac43q^4+C(K_dG)^4$, at the cost of $C(K_dG)^4v(t-s_0)$), so that the open term is the compression work $-\int_{s_0}^t\!\int\psi a^Zh$ of the meridional velocity generated by $Z$ on the quartic; no bound for it at the windows is established here. The dissipation condition is not established on the windows, and its necessity for the window bound or for GOAL is not established.

**Remark (Jacobian form of the produced vorticity).** Use $p=r^2/2$ and $z$, so that $dx=2\pi\,dp\,dz$, $v=(ru^r,u^z)$ satisfies
$\operatorname{div}_{p,z}v=0$, and with $\mathcal D=\partial_t+v\cdot\nabla_{p,z}$, $L_0=2p\partial_p^2+\partial_z^2$, $L_1=L_0+4\partial_p$ the equations read
$\mathcal D\Gamma=L_0\Gamma$ and $\mathcal DZ=L_1Z+F$, where $q=\Gamma/(2p)$ and $F=\partial_z(q^2)$. Let $a(t)=\int_{s_0}^tP(t,s)q(s)\,ds$, the bounded
solution of $\mathcal Da=L_1a+q$ with $a(s_0)=0$ on compact subintervals of $[s_0,T_*)$ (a diffused form of the accumulated rotation
angle; in the five-dimensional lift in which $L_1$ is the Laplacian it is smooth, and it need not vanish on the axis), and put
$J(f,g)=f_pg_z-f_zg_p$. Since $\mathcal DJ(f,g)=J(\mathcal Df,g)+J(f,\mathcal Dg)-(\operatorname{div}v)J(f,g)$ and $J(\Gamma,q)=F$, both $Z$ and
$J(\Gamma,a)-\int_{s_0}^tP(t,s)\mathcal C\,ds$ are bounded solutions of $\mathcal Dh=L_1h+F$ with zero data at $s_0$ (the factors of
$J(\Gamma,a)=(q+\partial_ru^\theta)a_z-(\partial_zu^\theta)a_r$ are bounded), and uniqueness of bounded solutions gives
$$Z(t)=J(\Gamma(t),a(t))-\int_{s_0}^tP(t,s)\mathcal C(s)\,ds,\qquad\mathcal C=J(L_0\Gamma,a)+J(\Gamma,L_1a)-L_1J(\Gamma,a),$$
and $\mathcal C=-4pJ(\Gamma_p,a_p)-2J(\Gamma_z,a_z)-2\Gamma_{pp}a_z-2\Gamma_za_{pp}+4\Gamma_{pz}a_p$. At regular points of a level set of $\Gamma$,
$J(\Gamma,a)=|\nabla_{p,z}\Gamma|\,\partial_{\mathbf t}a$ with $\mathbf t=(-\Gamma_z,\Gamma_p)/|\nabla_{p,z}\Gamma|$, so by the coarea formula
$\int_E(\sigma J(\Gamma,a))_+dx=2\pi\mathcal V_\sigma(E,t)$, $\mathcal V_\sigma(E,t)=\int_{-G}^G\int_{U_E\cap\{\Gamma(t)=c\}}(\sigma\partial_{\mathbf t}a)_+\,d\mathcal H^1\,dc$, $U_E$
the meridional section of $E$ in the variables $(p,z)$, $\sigma=\pm1$. With $|\eta^d|\le C_0$, positivity of $P$, and
$\int_E|\eta|\le2\int_E(\sigma\eta)_++|\int_E\eta|$, $|\int_{E_{n,R}}\eta|\le16\pi RM_n\ell_n$ (Corollary 6A(a)), one gets: if at an admissible
normalization with (L2-OSC), for all $R,A$ and uniformly in the window times, for one sign $\sigma$ (which may depend on the time)
$2\pi\mathcal V_\sigma(E_{n,R},t)+\int_{s_0}^t\int\psi_{n}(-\sigma\mathcal C)_+\le K_{R,A}M_n\ell_n$, with $\psi_n=P(t,s)^*1_{E_{n,R}}$, then
$\int_{E_{n,R}}|\eta|\le(2K_{R,A}+16\pi R)M_n\ell_n+2C_0|E_{n,R}|$, so (VL1) and GOAL hold. This condition is not proved, and it is
not weaker than the bound $\int_{E_{n,R}}|\eta|\le C_{R,A}M_n\ell_n$ itself: if $B_\sigma$ denotes its left side and $\mathcal W_{\mathcal C}=\int_{s_0}^t\int\psi_n|\mathcal C|$,
then exactly $B_++B_-=\int_{E_{n,R}}|J(\Gamma,a)|+\mathcal W_{\mathcal C}$ and $B_+-B_-=\int_{E_{n,R}}Z$, with $|\int_{E_{n,R}}Z|\le16\pi RM_n\ell_n+C_0|E_{n,R}|$; since
$J(\Gamma,a)=Z+\int P\mathcal C$, the condition holds (with some constants) if and only if that bound holds together with
$\mathcal W_{\mathcal C}\le K'_{R,A}M_n\ell_n$ on the windows. Resetting $s_0$ to the start of the window removes the history from $a$ and $\mathcal C$ but
replaces $C_0$ by the uncontrolled $\|\eta\|_\infty$ at that time. Bounded circulation limits the range of levels, not the variation of
$a$ along a level set, even when the levels are material: in the inviscid scalar model $\mathcal D\Gamma=0$, $\mathcal Da=\Gamma/(2p)$ with
$N\in\mathbb N_{>0}$ and $v=(\frac18\sin Nz,0)$ on $1<p<2$ (extended smoothly, divergence free and bounded independently of $N$, away from the axis) and $\Gamma(s_0,p,z)=g(p)$, where $g\in C_c^\infty((\frac12,\frac52))$ is fixed independently of $N$ and $g(p)=p$ on $[1,2]$, the levels $c\in[\frac54,\frac74]$ stay in $\frac98\le p\le\frac{15}8$ for $t-s_0\le1$ and give
$\mathcal V_\pm\ge N(t-s_0)^2/90$ on the solid of revolution of $\{1<p<2,\ 0<z<2\pi\}$, while $\Gamma$, $a$ and $q$ stay bounded independently of $N$; $\|a(t)\|_\infty\le\int_{s_0}^t\|q\|_\infty$ controls
neither $\mathcal V_\sigma$ nor $\mathcal C$; and $\mathcal C$ has no sign even at the start of a coupled evolution: for data with
$\Gamma_0=2p(1+pz)$ near $(p,z)=(1,0)$ (cut off away from the axis, with any smooth compactly supported meridional field),
$a(s_0)=0$ and $\partial_ta(s_0)=q_0$ give $\partial_t\mathcal C(s_0)=-8pz$, by bilinearity of $\mathcal C$ in $(\Gamma,a)$. For the steady Burgers
column of item (iii) $q$ and $a$ do not depend on $z$ and $F=J(\Gamma,a)=\mathcal C=Z=0$, although the compression work is positive.

### 12. A coupled vorticity energy and the size of the swirl

Notation as in Section 11. Put $\xi=\omega^r/r=-\partial_zq$, $v=u^r/r=-\partial_z\varphi$ with $\varphi$ as in Lemma 29(c),
$\Delta_5=\partial_r^2+\frac3r\partial_r+\partial_z^2$, $D_t=\partial_t+b\cdot\nabla$, and
$$H=\|\xi\|_2^2+\|\eta\|_2^2,\qquad J=\|\nabla\xi\|_2^2+\|\nabla\eta\|_2^2,\qquad P=\|q\|_2^2,\qquad V(s)=\|u^\theta(s)\|_\infty .$$

**Proposition 41 (coupled vorticity energy).** For $s_0<t<T_*$:
(a) $\frac12H'+J\le3V(HJ)^{1/2}$; hence $H'\le\frac92V^2H$ and $H(t)\le H(s_0)\exp(\frac92\int_{s_0}^tV^2ds)$;
(b) $\|F\|_1\le2P^{1/2}\|\xi\|_2$ and $P^2\le\Sigma'Y$;
(c) for every $x$ and $\rho>0$, $Q_\rho(x,t)\le2^{-1/2}\rho\,(r(x)+\rho)^{1/2}\|\eta(t)\|_2$, and if $r(x)>\rho$ also
$Q_\rho(x,t)\le\rho\,\|\nabla u(t)\|_2\,(2(r(x)-\rho))^{-1/2}$;
(d) $H^2\le\|\nabla u\|_2^2J$; hence $H(t)\le\max\{H(s_0),\,9\sup_{s_0\le s\le t}V(s)^2\|\nabla u(s)\|_2^2\}$.

Proof. Finiteness. For a smooth axisymmetric vector field $U$ and $e\in\{e_r,e_z\}$, $(e\cdot\nabla)((e_\theta\cdot\nabla)U)=\nabla^2U(e,e_\theta)$
because $e_\theta$ is constant along the directions $e_r$ and $e_z$, and $(e_\theta\cdot\nabla)U=r^{-1}\partial_\theta U$ in Cartesian components. For
$U=fe_\theta$ this is $-(f/r)e_r$, and for $U=b$ it is $ve_\theta$. Hence $|f/r|\le|\nabla U|$ and $|\nabla(f/r)|\le\sqrt2|\nabla^2U|$ pointwise, and
likewise $|v|\le|\nabla b|$, $|\nabla v|\le\sqrt2|\nabla^2b|$. With $U=u^\theta e_\theta$, $\partial_z(u^\theta e_\theta)$ and $\omega^\theta e_\theta=\operatorname{curl}b$ (where
$u^\theta e_\theta$ and $b$ are half the difference and half the sum of $u$ and its image under a reflection in a plane through the axis, so
lie in every $H^k$ at times $t<T_*$), this gives $q,\xi,\eta,v\in H^1$ at each $t<T_*$, with norms bounded on compact subintervals of $[0,T_*)$;
the same argument applied to higher and time derivatives gives the regularity used below. $H^1$ alone does not give values on the axis;
these come from the smoothness of $u$: for instance on the positive $x_1$-axis $q(r,z)=\int_0^1\partial_1(u^\theta e_\theta)_2(sr,0,z)\,ds$, so $q$ is
continuous up to the axis with $q(0,z)=\partial_1(u^\theta e_\theta)_2(0,0,z)$, and likewise for $\xi,\eta,v$. The integrations by parts are done
with cutoffs in $r<\varepsilon$ and $|x|>R$ as in Lemma 29; the errors at $|x|=R$ tend to zero by the $L^2$ bounds above. Equations. Insert $\omega^r=r\xi$, $\omega^\theta=r\eta$ and $u^r=rv$ into the
equations $D_t\omega^r=(\Delta-r^{-2})\omega^r+\omega^r\partial_ru^r+\omega^z\partial_zu^r$ and $D_t\omega^\theta=(\Delta-r^{-2})\omega^\theta+v\omega^\theta+r^{-1}\partial_z(u^\theta)^2$,
and use $(\Delta-r^{-2})(rf)=r\Delta_5f$ and $D_tr=u^r$: the terms $v\omega^r$ and $v\omega^\theta$ cancel, and
$$D_t\xi=\Delta_5\xi+(\omega^r\partial_r+\omega^z\partial_z)v,\qquad D_t\eta=\Delta_5\eta+F,\qquad F=\partial_z(q^2)=-2q\xi,\qquad\Delta_5v=\partial_z\eta,$$
the last from $-\Delta_5\varphi=\eta$. This is the coupled system (1.6) of Chen, Fang and Zhang (arXiv 1505.00905), related to the formulation of
Hou and Li (Comm. Pure Appl. Math. 61 (2008)); their energy identities (1.7)-(1.8) are followed here with explicit constants. For a smooth axisymmetric $f$ with the regularity above,
$-\int f\Delta_5f\,dx=\|\nabla f\|_2^2+2\pi\int f(0,z)^2dz\ge\|\nabla f\|_2^2$ (the axis term need not vanish; it is nonnegative and is discarded) and $\int f^2r^{-1}dx\le2\|f\|_2\|\partial_rf\|_2$ (Lemma 29); transport terms integrate
to zero since $\operatorname{div}b=0$. Testing $\Delta_5v=\partial_z\eta$ with $v$ gives $\|\nabla v\|_2^2\le\int\eta\,\partial_zv\,dx$, so $\|\nabla v\|_2\le\|\eta\|_2$. Since
$\omega^r\partial_rv+\omega^z\partial_zv=r^{-1}(\partial_r\Gamma\,\partial_zv-\partial_z\Gamma\,\partial_rv)$ and
$(\partial_r\Gamma\,\partial_zv-\partial_z\Gamma\,\partial_rv)\xi-\Gamma(\partial_rv\,\partial_z\xi-\partial_zv\,\partial_r\xi)=\partial_r(\Gamma\,\partial_zv\,\xi)-\partial_z(\Gamma\,\partial_rv\,\xi)$ with $\Gamma=0$ on the axis,
$$\int(\omega^r\partial_rv+\omega^z\partial_zv)\xi\,dx=\int u^\theta(\partial_rv\,\partial_z\xi-\partial_zv\,\partial_r\xi)\,dx,$$
whose modulus is at most $V\|\nabla v\|_2\|\nabla\xi\|_2\le V\|\eta\|_2\|\nabla\xi\|_2\le V(HJ)^{1/2}$. By $|q|\le V/r$, Cauchy-Schwarz and the weighted bound,
$|\int F\eta\,dx|=2|\int q\xi\eta\,dx|\le2V(\int\xi^2r^{-1})^{1/2}(\int\eta^2r^{-1})^{1/2}\le4V(\|\xi\|_2\|\eta\|_2\|\nabla\xi\|_2\|\nabla\eta\|_2)^{1/2}\le2V(HJ)^{1/2}$.
So $\frac12\frac d{dt}\|\xi\|_2^2+\|\nabla\xi\|_2^2\le V\|\eta\|_2\|\nabla\xi\|_2$ and
$\frac12\frac d{dt}\|\eta\|_2^2+\|\nabla\eta\|_2^2\le4V(\|\xi\|_2\|\eta\|_2\|\nabla\xi\|_2\|\nabla\eta\|_2)^{1/2}$; adding them gives (a); $3V(HJ)^{1/2}\le J+\frac94V^2H$ gives $H'\le\frac92V^2H$, and Gronwall the last bound.
(b) $\|F\|_1=2\int|q\xi|\le2\|q\|_2\|\xi\|_2$, and $P=\int(\Gamma^2r^{-3})^{1/2}(\Gamma^2r^{-5})^{1/2}dx\le(\Sigma'Y)^{1/2}$.
(c) $|T_\rho(x)|=2\pi\iint_{\{d_m<\rho\}}r'\,dr'\,dz'\le2\pi^2\rho^2(r(x)+\rho)$ and $2\pi Q_\rho(x,t)=\int_{T_\rho(x)}|\eta|\le|T_\rho(x)|^{1/2}\|\eta\|_2$. If $r(x)>\rho$, then
$r'>r(x)-\rho$ on the disc, so $Q_\rho\le(\pi\rho^2)^{1/2}(\iint|\omega^\theta|^2dr'dz')^{1/2}$ and $\iint|\omega^\theta|^2dr'dz'=\int_{T_\rho(x)}|\omega^\theta|^2(2\pi r')^{-1}dx\le
\|\omega\|_2^2/(2\pi(r(x)-\rho))$, with $\|\omega\|_2=\|\nabla u\|_2$ for divergence-free $u\in H^1$.
(d) For $f=\xi$ or $f=\eta$, integrating $\partial_r(r^2f^2)$ in $r$ (the boundary term vanishes at the axis because of the factor $r^2$, and at
infinity by a cutoff) gives $\|f\|_2^2=-\int rf\,\partial_rf\,dx$; with $r\xi=\omega^r$, $r\eta=\omega^\theta$ and Cauchy-Schwarz,
$H=-\int(\omega^r\partial_r\xi+\omega^\theta\partial_r\eta)\,dx\le(\|\omega^r\|_2^2+\|\omega^\theta\|_2^2)^{1/2}J^{1/2}\le\|\nabla u\|_2J^{1/2}$. Let $K$ be the maximum
on the right of (d). At a time $s$ with $H(s)>9V(s)^2\|\nabla u(s)\|_2^2$ one has $J^{1/2}\ge H/\|\nabla u\|_2>3VH^{1/2}$, so (a) gives
$\frac12H'\le J^{1/2}(3VH^{1/2}-J^{1/2})<0$. If $H(t)>K$, let $t_1=\sup\{s\in[s_0,t]:H(s)\le K\}$; then $t_1<t$, $H(t_1)=K$ and $H>K$ on $(t_1,t]$, so
$H$ decreases there and $H(t)<K$, a contradiction. $\square$

**Corollary 42 (size of the swirl).** (a) If $u$ blows up at $T_*$, then $\limsup_{t\uparrow T_*}(T_*-t)^{1/2}\|u^\theta(t)\|_\infty\ge3^{-1/2}$.
(b) For $s_0<t<T_*$, $\|F(t)\|_1^2\le4(\Sigma'(t)Y(t))^{1/2}H(s_0)\exp(\frac92\int_{s_0}^tV^2ds)$.
(c) At a velocity first crossing $t>s_0$ with $|b(x_t,t)|\ge\frac78M$, with $\rho_*=C_2L_E(M)$ and $k_*$ as in Proposition 33,
$$c_1\frac{M^2}{A}2^{k_*}\le2\sqrt2\,\rho_*\,(r(x_t)+4\rho_*)^{1/2}H(s_0)^{1/2}\exp\Big(\frac94\int_{s_0}^tV^2ds\Big).$$
Hence in regime (II), along every sequence of velocity first crossings $t_n\uparrow T_*$, with $\Lambda_n=M_n^2A_n^{-1}\rho_{*,n}^{-1}(r(x_{t_n})+4\rho_{*,n})^{-1/2}$ and for all large $n$,
$$\frac94\int_{s_0}^{t_n}V^2ds\ge k_*(n)\log2+\log\Lambda_n-C(s_0),\qquad\Lambda_n\to\infty,$$
where $C(s_0)$ does not depend on $n$, and under (FLAT$_0$) also $k_*(n)\to\infty$.
(d) At a crossing as in (c), $c_1\frac{M^2}{A}2^{k_*}\le2\sqrt2\,\rho_*(r(x_t)+4\rho_*)^{1/2}\max\{H(s_0),9\sup_{s_0\le s\le t}V(s)^2\|\nabla u(s)\|_2^2\}^{1/2}$, and, if
$r(x_t)>4\rho_*$, also $c_1\frac{M^2}{A}2^{k_*}\le2\sqrt2\,\rho_*\|\nabla u(t)\|_2(r(x_t)-4\rho_*)^{-1/2}$.

Proof. (a) Otherwise there are $c<3^{-1/2}$ and $s_0<T_*$ with $V(s)\le c(T_*-s)^{-1/2}$ on $[s_0,T_*)$, and Proposition 41(a) gives
$H(t)\le H(s_0)((T_*-s_0)/(T_*-t))^\gamma$ with $\gamma=\frac92c^2<\frac32$, so $\int_{s_0}^{T_*}\|\eta\|_2^{4/3}dt<\infty$. Let $f=(u^\theta)^2/r=\Gamma^2r^{-3}$.
Multiplying the equation $D_t\Gamma=(\Delta-\frac2r\partial_r)\Gamma$ by $\Gamma^3r^{-6}$ and integrating (as in Lemma 3.1 of Chen, Fang and Zhang; transport
contributes $\frac14\int\Gamma^4b\cdot\nabla r^{-6}=-\frac32\int vf^2$) gives
$$\frac14\frac d{dt}\|f\|_2^2+\frac34\|\nabla f\|_2^2+\frac34\|q\|_4^4=-\frac32\int vf^2\,dx .$$
By Hölder's inequality, $\|v\|_6\le C\|\nabla v\|_2\le C\|\eta\|_2$, the interpolation $\|f\|_{12/5}^2\le\|f\|_2^{3/2}\|f\|_6^{1/2}$ and Young's inequality,
$\frac32|\int vf^2|\le\frac14\|\nabla f\|_2^2+C\|\eta\|_2^{4/3}\|f\|_2^2$. Since $\|f(s_0)\|_2\le V(s_0)\|q(s_0)\|_2<\infty$, Gronwall's inequality gives
$\sup_{[s_0,T_*)}\|f\|_2<\infty$ and $\int_{s_0}^{T_*}\|q\|_4^4dt<\infty$; as $u$ is smooth on $[0,s_0]$, $u^\theta/r\in L^4(\mathbb R^3\times(0,T_*))$, and the solution
continues beyond $T_*$ by Lemma 2.5 of Chen, Fang and Zhang (arXiv 1505.00905), which collects results of Kreml and Pokorny, of Neustupa and
Pokorny and of P. Zhang and T. Zhang; its statement was read in the arXiv version, its proof was not checked here. This is a contradiction.
(b) Proposition 41(a), (b) and $\|\xi\|_2^2\le H$.
(c) Proposition 33 with this $\rho_*$, then Proposition 41(c) with $\rho=4\rho_*$, and finally $\|\eta\|_2^2\le H$ with the Gronwall bound of Proposition 41(a). In regime (II), $|b(x_{t_n},t_n)|\ge\frac78M_n$
eventually (Theorem 3), $M_n^2/A_n\ge M_n^2/(\sqrt2D_n)\to\infty$, $\rho_{*,n}\to0$ and $r(x_{t_n})\to0$ (Lemma 28); take logarithms.
(d) Proposition 33, then Proposition 41(c) with $\rho=4\rho_*$, using Proposition 41(d) for the first bound. $\square$

**Remark (scope of Corollary 42).** (i) Part (a) is a lower bound of Type I for the swirl component alone. The endpoint
condition $u^\theta\in L^2_tL^\infty_x$ of Chen, Fang and Zhang is the case $\int^{T_*}V^2dt<\infty$, which a bound $V\le c(T_*-t)^{-1/2}$ alone does
not imply; (a) gives the explicit threshold $3^{-1/2}$ obtained from the same estimate, which is not optimized. The constant was not found in the
arXiv text of Chen, Fang and Zhang; the three earlier papers cited there were not read, and no priority is claimed.
(ii) Part (c) excludes (FLAT$_0$) on any trajectory along which, for some fixed $s_0<T_*$, $\frac94\int_{s_0}^{t_n}V^2ds-\log\Lambda_n$ stays bounded above on one sequence of
velocity first crossings $t_n\uparrow T_*$, for example when $V\le c(T_*-t)^{-1/2}$ near $T_*$ and $\Lambda_n\ge(T_*-t_n)^{-\beta}$ with $\frac94c^2\le\beta$. No a priori bound
