of this kind on $\int V^2$ is proved, so (c) is a necessary condition, not an exclusion. It has the form met elsewhere in this report:
$2^{k_*}$, exponential in $M/\theta_b$, is bounded by $\Lambda_n^{-1}\exp(\frac94\int V^2)$, a power of $T_*-t$ under a Type I swirl bound, and, as scalar arithmetic,
flatness at a logarithmic rate is compatible with such a bound when the swirl constant is large (the family of Limitations 1' has a
swirl larger by a logarithmic factor; see the Remark after Corollary 44).
(iii) For a scalar history with swirl exactly $c_S(T_*-t)^{-1/2}$, parts (a) and (b) of Proposition 41 bound $\|F\|_1^2/(4P)\le\|\xi\|_2^2$ by
$H(s_0)((T_*-s_0)/(T_*-t))^{9c_S^2/2}$ and so bound the swirl constant from below in terms of the source rate: if $\|F\|_1^2/(4P)\ge c(T_*-t)^{-\beta}$
near $T_*$ with $c>0$, then $\beta\le\frac92c_S^2$.
(iv) Part (d) replaces the exponential of $\int V^2$ by the enstrophy. Since $\|\nabla u(t)\|_2^2\le\|\nabla u_0\|_2^2+E_0D(t)$ (proof of Corollary 44),
it also gives $H(t)\le\max\{H(s_0),9S(t)^2(\|\nabla u_0\|_2^2+E_0D(t))\}$. Part (d) is again a bound of the form $2^{k_*}\le$ (powers of the scales), and the second bound in (d) shows that, at a record point farther than $4\rho_*$ from the
axis, $\|\nabla u(t)\|_2$ at the crossing is at least $(c_1/(2\sqrt2))(M^2/A)2^{k_*}(r(x_t)-4\rho_*)^{1/2}\rho_*^{-1}$.

**Lemma 43 (meridional velocity near the axis).** Let $t<T_*$, let $x$ be a point with $r_0=r(x)$, write $D_\infty=\|\nabla u(t)\|_\infty$ (Frobenius norm)
and take all norms at time $t$. For every $R\ge r_0$ and $0<\delta\le R$,
$$|b(x,t)|\le\frac12\Big[2\sqrt2\,\pi D_\infty\delta+\|\eta\|_2\sqrt{2R\log(R/\delta)}+\|\eta\|_2\sqrt{9R/\pi}+\frac{\|\nabla u\|_2}{2\sqrt R}\Big].$$
The same holds with $D_\infty$, $\|\eta\|_2$, $\|\nabla u\|_2$ replaced by any upper bounds $D_*$, $A_*$, $W_*$, and then, if $b(x,t)\neq0$,
$$|b(x,t)|^2\le24\,(A_*W_*+r_0A_*^2)\Big(1+\log_+\frac{D_*R_*}{|b(x,t)|}\Big),\qquad R_*=\max(r_0,W_*/A_*).$$
In particular $|b(x,t)|^2\le24(\|\eta\|_2\|\nabla u\|_2+r_0\|\eta\|_2^2)(1+\log_+(D_\infty R_x/|b(x,t)|))$ with $R_x=\max(r_0,\|\nabla u\|_2/\|\eta\|_2)$.

Proof. The field $u^\theta e_\theta$ is the decaying divergence-free field with curl $\omega^re_r+\omega^ze_z$, so by the Biot-Savart law for the
decaying field $u$, $b=\operatorname{curl}(\psi e_\theta)$ with $\psi e_\theta=(4\pi)^{-1}\int\omega^\theta(y)e_\theta(y)|x-y|^{-1}dy$; in meridional coordinates
$b(r,z)=\iint K(r,z;r',z')\,\omega^\theta(r',z')\,dr'dz'$, $K=(-\partial_zK_0,\,r^{-1}\partial_r(rK_0))$, with
$K_0=\frac{r'}{4\pi}\int_0^{2\pi}\cos\alpha\,s(\alpha)^{-1}d\alpha$, $s(\alpha)^2=r^2+r'^2-2rr'\cos\alpha+h^2$, $h=z-z'$. Put $a=r'$, $d^2=(r-a)^2+h^2$, $Q^2=(r+a)^2+h^2$.
Differentiating under the integral and using $\int_0^{2\pi}(\cos\alpha\,s^{-1}-ra\sin^2\alpha\,s^{-3})d\alpha=\int_0^{2\pi}\partial_\alpha(\sin\alpha\,s^{-1})d\alpha=0$ gives, for $d>0$,
$K=\frac a{4\pi}\int_0^{2\pi}(h\cos\alpha,\ a-r\cos\alpha)\,s(\alpha)^{-3}d\alpha$ (for $r>0$, and at $r=0$ by continuity). Since
$h^2\cos^2\alpha+(a-r\cos\alpha)^2=s^2-(r^2+h^2)\sin^2\alpha\le s^2$ and $\int_0^{2\pi}(X-Y\cos\alpha)^{-1}d\alpha=2\pi(X^2-Y^2)^{-1/2}$ with $X=r^2+a^2+h^2$, $Y=2ra$,
$X^2-Y^2=d^2Q^2$, one gets $|K|\le\frac a{4\pi}\int_0^{2\pi}s^{-2}d\alpha=\frac a{2dQ}\le\frac1{2d}$. (The constant is sharp: at $r=h=0$, $K=(0,1/(2a))$ and $d=a$.)
Hence $|b(x,t)|\le\frac12\iint|\omega^\theta|d^{-1}dr'dz'$. Split the half-plane into $\{d<\delta\}$, $\{\delta\le d<R\}$, $\{d\ge R,\,r'\le3R\}$ and
$\{r'>3R\}$; the last set lies in $\{d\ge2R\}$, since $d\ge r'-r_0$ and $r_0\le R$. On the first, $|\omega^\theta|\le\sqrt2D_\infty$ and $\iint_{d<\delta}d^{-1}\le2\pi\delta$.
On the second, $r'\le r_0+R\le2R$, so $\iint_{d<R}(\omega^\theta)^2=\iint_{d<R}r'^2\eta^2\le2R\iint r'\eta^2=\frac R\pi\|\eta\|_2^2$, while
$\iint_{\delta\le d<R}d^{-2}\le2\pi\log(R/\delta)$; Cauchy-Schwarz gives $\|\eta\|_2(2R\log(R/\delta))^{1/2}$. On the third, write $|\omega^\theta|d^{-1}=(r'^{1/2}|\eta|)(r'^{1/2}d^{-1})$;
for fixed $r'$, $\int_{d\ge R}d^{-2}dz'\le\int_{|h|\le R}R^{-2}dh+\int_{|h|>R}h^{-2}dh=4/R$, so $\iint_{d\ge R,\,r'\le3R}r'd^{-2}\le18R$ and the contribution is at most
$(2\pi)^{-1/2}\|\eta\|_2(18R)^{1/2}=\|\eta\|_2(9R/\pi)^{1/2}$. On the fourth, write $|\omega^\theta|d^{-1}=(r'^{1/2}|\omega^\theta|)(r'^{-1/2}d^{-1})$, use
$\iint(\omega^\theta)^2r'=(2\pi)^{-1}\|\omega^\theta\|_2^2\le(2\pi)^{-1}\|\nabla u\|_2^2$ ($\|\operatorname{curl}u\|_2=\|\nabla u\|_2$ for divergence-free $u\in H^1$) and, as
$r'-r_0\ge\frac23r'$, $\int_{3R}^\infty r'^{-1}\int d^{-2}dz'dr'=\pi\int_{3R}^\infty\frac{dr'}{r'(r'-r_0)}\le\frac{3\pi}2\int_{3R}^\infty r'^{-2}dr'=\frac\pi{2R}$, which gives
$\|\nabla u\|_2/(2\sqrt R)$. Every term is nondecreasing in the norms, so upper bounds may be inserted. For the second form let $v=|b(x,t)|>0$,
$a_0=2\sqrt2\pi$, $c_0=3\pi^{-1/2}+\frac12$, $R=R_*$, $\delta=\min(R_*,v/(a_0D_*))$. The first term is at most $v/2$ and $W_*/(2\sqrt{R_*})\le A_*\sqrt{R_*}/2$, so
$v\le A_*\sqrt{R_*}\,[(2\log(R_*/\delta))^{1/2}+c_0]$ with $\log(R_*/\delta)\le\log a_0+\ell$, $\ell=\log_+(D_*R_*/v)$. By Cauchy-Schwarz in $\mathbb R^2$,
$v^2\le A_*^2R_*(2+c_0^2)(1+\log a_0)(1+\ell)$; finally $A_*^2R_*\le A_*W_*+r_0A_*^2$, and $c_0<\frac94$, $a_0<9$, $\log9<\frac94$ give
$(2+c_0^2)(1+\log a_0)<\frac{113}{16}\cdot\frac{13}4<24$. $\square$

**Corollary 44 (velocity, gradient and swirl records).** Let $S=S(t)$ and $D=D(t)$ be the historical records. There is $t_0<T_*$ such that for
$t_0\le t<T_*$ and every $x$ with $v=|b(x,t)|>0$,
$$v^2\le144\,E_0DS\,(1+3r(x)S)\Big(1+\log_+\frac{D\max(r(x),1/(3S))}{v}\Big).$$
In regime (II), at velocity first crossings $t\ge t_0$ with $|b(x_t,t)|\ge\frac78M$ (eventually, Theorem 3), $M^2\le189\,E_0DS(1+3r_tS)K_t$, where $r_t=r(x_t)$
and $K_t=1+\log_+(8D\max(r_t,1/(3S))/(7M))$. Consequently, at such crossings, unless $M\le567\,E_0S^2K_t$,
$$\frac D{M^2}\ge\min\Big\{\frac1{378\,E_0SK_t},\ \Big(\frac1{4536\,E_0^2S^2K_t}\Big)^{1/3}\Big\}.$$

Proof. The enstrophy $e=\|\nabla u\|_2^2=\|\omega\|_2^2$ satisfies $\frac12e'+\|\nabla\omega\|_2^2=\int\omega\cdot(\nabla u)\omega\,dx\le\|\nabla u\|_\infty e$, so
$e(t)\le e(0)+2D(t)\int_0^te\,ds\le e(0)+E_0D(t)$ by the energy inequality. Proposition 41(d) with a fixed $s_0\in(0,T_*)$ gives
$\|\eta(t)\|_2^2\le H(t)\le\max\{H(s_0),9S(t)^2(e(0)+E_0D(t))\}$. Since axisymmetric data without swirl give a global solution, $S(t)\ge\|u_0^\theta\|_\infty>0$,
and $D(t)\to\infty$ (Beale-Kato-Majda); choose $t_0>s_0$ with $e(0)\le E_0D(t_0)$ and $H(s_0)\le9S(t_0)^2E_0D(t_0)$. For $t\ge t_0$, Lemma 43 applies
with $D_*=D$, $W_*=(2E_0D)^{1/2}$ and $A_*=3S(2E_0D)^{1/2}$, so $A_*W_*=6E_0DS$, $A_*^2=18E_0DS^2$ and $W_*/A_*=1/(3S)$; this is the first bound.
At the crossings, $M^2\le\frac{64}{49}v^2$ and $\log_+(D\max(r_t,1/(3S))/v)\le K_t-1$, and $\frac{64}{49}\cdot144<189$. For the dichotomy put $X=D/M^2$, so
$1\le189E_0SK_tX(1+3r_tS)$, and use Lemma 28: if $r_t\le M/(2D)$, then $3r_tS\le\frac{3S}{2XM}$ and $1\le189E_0SK_tX+\frac{567}2E_0S^2K_t/M$, so either
$M\le567E_0S^2K_t$ or $X\ge(378E_0SK_t)^{-1}$; if $r_t\le12E_0D^2/(\pi M^4)=12E_0X^2/\pi$, then $3r_tS\le12E_0SX^2$ and
$1\le189E_0SK_tX+2268E_0^2S^2K_tX^3$, so one of the two terms is at least $\frac12$. $\square$

**Remark (consequences of Lemma 43 and Corollary 44).** (i) Corollary 44 bounds the gradient-scale ratio $M^2/D$ at velocity first crossings by a
multiple of $E_0S$ times a logarithm of the records, unless $M\le567E_0S^2K_t$. Along crossings where that exception fails, degeneration $D/M^2\to0$
therefore requires the swirl record to grow at least like $(M^2/D)/(E_0K_t)$. This is weaker than the condition $S(t_n)A_n/M_n^2\to\infty$ of
Lemma 34(b) ($A\le\sqrt2D$), so Corollary 44 adds no new necessary condition on the swirl record in regime (II); what is new is the pointwise
bound of Lemma 43 with the enstrophy bound, which Corollary 45 uses. It is not an exclusion of (FLAT$_0$), and in regime (II) $S\to\infty$ is not excluded.
(ii) With exactly Type I swirl the near-axis meridional velocity is limited. If $\|u^\theta(t)\|_\infty\le c\tau^{-1/2}$ ($\tau=T_*-t$) and
$\|\nabla u\|_2^2$ is nondecreasing, the past supremum in Proposition 41(d) is at most $c^2\tau^{-1}\|\nabla u(t)\|_2^2$, and Lemma 43 gives
$\|\nabla u(t)\|_2^2\ge c'\tau^{-1}(\log(e/\tau))^{-1}$ at every time at which some point $x_t$ has $|b(x_t,t)|\ge\tau^{-3/4}$ and $r(x_t)\le C\tau^{1/2}$ (if also
$\|\nabla u\|_\infty\le\tau^{-p}$); if this happens at all late times, the enstrophy is not integrable. Corollary 45 removes the monotonicity.
The bound of Proposition 41(d) as stated does not suffice for that: a scalar history with Type I swirl, $M=\tau^{-3/4}$, record radius $\tau^{1/2}$,
$\|\eta\|_2^2$ of order $\tau^{-2}L^{-1}$ ($L=\log(e/\tau)$), $\|\nabla u\|_\infty=\tau^{-4}$, and enstrophy equal to $1$ plus smooth pulses of height of order $\tau_n^{-1}L_n^{-1}$ and width of order $\tau_n^2$ at $\tau_n=2^{-n}$
satisfies that bound, Lemma 43 and the energy inequality, but not the differential inequalities of Proposition 41 used in the proof of
Corollary 45 (between pulses $H\ge36\|u^\theta\|_\infty^2\|\nabla u\|_2^2$, so $H$ decreases fast).

**Corollary 45 (Type I swirl and near-axis meridional velocity).** Let $C,p,\gamma>0$, let $a_0\in[\max\{0,T_*-1\},T_*)$, and let
$\mathcal T\subset[a_0,T_*)$ be a measurable set such that for every $t\in\mathcal T$, with $\tau=T_*-t$ and $L=\log(e/\tau)$, some point $x_t$ has
$|b(x_t,t)|\ge\gamma\tau^{-3/4}$ and $r(x_t)\le C\tau^{1/2}$, and $\|\nabla u(t)\|_\infty\le\tau^{-p}$. There are $\kappa_1>0$, depending only on $C,p,\gamma$, and
$a\in[a_0,T_*)$ such that, with $\tau_a=T_*-a$,
$$\int_{\mathcal T\cap[a,T_*)}\frac{dt}{\tau L}\le\int_a^{T_*}\|\nabla u\|_2^2\,dt+\frac{\tau_a^2H(a)}{2\kappa_1}+\frac{2187}{256\,\kappa_1}\int_a^{T_*}\tau^2\|u^\theta\|_\infty^4\|\nabla u\|_2^2\,dt.$$
In particular, if $\|u^\theta(t)\|_\infty\le c\tau^{-1/2}$ near $T_*$ (or only $\int^{T_*}\tau^2\|u^\theta\|_\infty^4\|\nabla u\|_2^2\,dt<\infty$), then $\int_{\mathcal T}dt/(\tau L)<\infty$.
Hence there are no $c,C,p,\gamma>0$ and $t_1<T_*$ such that for every $t\in[t_1,T_*)$: (a) $\|u^\theta(t)\|_\infty\le c\tau^{-1/2}$; (b) some point $x_t$ has
$|b(x_t,t)|\ge\gamma\tau^{-3/4}$ and $r(x_t)\le C\tau^{1/2}$; (c) $\|\nabla u(t)\|_\infty\le\tau^{-p}$.

Proof. Write $V=\|u^\theta\|_\infty$ and $e=\|\nabla u\|_2^2$. Step 1. By Proposition 41(a), $H'\le-2J+6V(HJ)^{1/2}$, and by Proposition 41(d),
$H^{1/2}\le e^{1/4}J^{1/4}$; so $H'\le-2y^4+6Ve^{1/4}y^3$ with $y=J^{1/4}\ge0$, whose maximum over $y\ge0$ is attained at $y=\frac94Ve^{1/4}$ and equals
$K_*V^4e$, $K_*=\frac{2187}{128}$. Hence $H(t)\le H(s)+K_*\int_s^tV^4e$ for $s\le t$. Step 2. Let $t\in\mathcal T$ with $e(t)<\tau^{-1}L^{-1}$ (then $e(t)>0$,
since $b(t)\ne0$). Apply Lemma 43 at $x_t$ with $D_*=\tau^{-p}$, $W_*=e^{1/2}$ and $A_*=\max\{\|\eta\|_2,\tau^{-1}L^{-2}\}$: $W_*/A_*\le\tau^{1/2}L^{3/2}$, so
$D_*R_*/|b(x_t,t)|\le\gamma^{-1}\max\{C,1\}L^{3/2}\tau^{5/4-p}$ and, as $0<\tau\le1$ and $\log L\le L-1$, $1+\log_+(D_*R_*/|b|)\le C_1L$ with
$C_1=\frac52+\log_+(\max\{C,1\}/\gamma)+(p-\frac54)_+$. Lemma 43 gives $\gamma^2\tau^{-3/2}\le24C_1L(A_*e^{1/2}+C\tau^{1/2}A_*^2)$, so with $y=\tau A_*$,
$\gamma^2\le24C_1(L^{1/2}y+CLy^2)$; one of the two terms is at least $\frac12\gamma^2$, whence $y^2\ge\kappa_1/L$ with
$\kappa_1=\min\{\gamma^4/(2304C_1^2),\gamma^2/(48C_1C)\}$. Choose $a\ge a_0$ with $L^3>1/\kappa_1$ on $[a,T_*)$; then the floor ($y=L^{-2}$) is excluded, so
$A_*=\|\eta\|_2$ and $H(t)\ge\|\eta(t)\|_2^2\ge\kappa_1\tau^{-2}L^{-1}$. Step 3. Consequently, for every $t\in\mathcal T\cap[a,T_*)$,
$\frac1{\tau L}\le e(t)+\frac{\tau H(t)}{\kappa_1}$. By Step 1 and Tonelli (all integrands are nonnegative),
$\int_a^{T_*}\tau H\,dt\le\frac12\tau_a^2H(a)+K_*\int_a^{T_*}V(s)^4e(s)\int_s^{T_*}(T_*-t)\,dt\,ds=\frac12\tau_a^2H(a)+\frac{K_*}2\int_a^{T_*}\tau^2V^4e\,ds$.
Integrating the pointwise bound over $\mathcal T\cap[a,T_*)$ gives the estimate. Under the Type I bound $\tau^2V^4e\le c^4e$, which is integrable by the
energy inequality; and $\int_{a_0}^{T_*}dt/(\tau L)=\int^\infty d\sigma/(1+\sigma)=\infty$ with $a_0=\max\{t_1,T_*-1\}$ ($\sigma=\log(1/\tau)$), which gives the last assertion. $\square$

Remark. (i) The exponent $\frac34$ is critical for this argument: if $\gamma\tau^{-3/4}$ is replaced by $\gamma\tau^{-\beta}$ with $\beta<\frac34$, the same steps
bound only $\int_{\mathcal T}\tau^{1/2-2\beta}L^{-1}dt$, which is finite even for $\mathcal T=[t_1,T_*)$. (ii) With $\|u^\theta\|_\infty\le c\tau^{-1/2}L^k$, $k>0$, one has only
$\tau^2V^4e\le c^4L^{4k}e$, which the energy inequality does not control ($e=\tau^{-1}L^{-1-2k}$ is integrable while $L^{4k}e$ is not), and the argument gives no
contradiction; the scalar family of Limitations 1' has $k=1$. (iii) Since $d\log L/dt=1/(\tau L)$, the condition $\int_{\mathcal T}dt/(\tau L)=\infty$ says that the image of
$\mathcal T$ under $t\mapsto\log\log(e/\tau)$ has infinite Lebesgue measure; a positive lower density in that variable suffices, while accumulation of
$\mathcal T$ at $T_*$ alone does not. An earlier proof of the case $\mathcal T=[t_1,T_*)$ counted, in the measure $dt/\tau$, the set where $H<36V^2e$
(off which $H'\le-J$); the integrated form above is shorter. (iv) Corollary 45 is a conditional statement. In regime (II) no lower bound $M\ge\gamma(T_*-t)^{-3/4}$ is known, the record radius is
bounded by Lemma 28 and not by $(T_*-t)^{1/2}$, and no Type I bound on the swirl is known; it does not exclude (FLAT$_0$). No priority is
claimed for it; conditional regularity results under Type I bounds on the whole velocity are known (Chen, Strain, Tsai and Yau; Koch,
Nadirashvili, Seregin and Sverak), as is regularity under $u^\theta\in L^2_tL^\infty_x$ (Chen, Fang and Zhang), which the Type I bound does not imply;
no published statement identical to Corollary 45 was found, but the literature on hypotheses on the swirl alone was not surveyed systematically.

**Corollary 46 (a swirl bound for the coupled energy without the enstrophy).** For $s_0\le t<T_*$, with $S_{s_0}(t)=\sup_{s_0\le s\le t}\|u^\theta(s)\|_\infty$
and $\epsilon(s_0)=\int_{s_0}^{T_*}\|\nabla u\|_2^2\,ds\le E_0/2$,
$$H(t)\le H(s_0)+\frac{2187}{128}\int_{s_0}^t\|u^\theta(s)\|_\infty^4\|\nabla u(s)\|_2^2\,ds\le H(s_0)+\frac{2187}{128}\,S_{s_0}(t)^4\,\epsilon(s_0).$$

Proof. With $V=\|u^\theta\|_\infty$ and $e=\|\nabla u\|_2^2$, Proposition 41(a) gives $H'\le-2J+6V(HJ)^{1/2}$ and Proposition 41(d) gives $H^{1/2}\le e^{1/4}J^{1/4}$,
so $H'\le-2y^4+6Ve^{1/4}y^3$ with $y=J^{1/4}\ge0$. The right side is maximal at $y=\frac94Ve^{1/4}$, where it equals $(\frac94)^3\cdot\frac32V^4e=\frac{2187}{128}V^4e$;
integrating gives the bound. $\square$ A last-passage form (with $\mathcal G=\{s\in[0,T_*):H(s)<36V(s)^2e(s)\}$; by Proposition 41(a), (d), $H'\le\frac92V^2H$ everywhere, and
$H'\le-J\le0$ off $\mathcal G$, since there $J^{1/2}\ge He^{-1/2}\ge6VH^{1/2}$) gives the weaker dichotomy $H(t)\le2H(s_0)$, or $H(t)\le\frac{324}{\log2}S_{s_0}(t)^4\epsilon(s_*)$ with $s_*$ as defined next: suppose $H(t)>2H(s_0)$, put $h=H(t)/2$ and $s_*=\sup\{s\in[s_0,t]:H(s)\le h\}$; then $s_*<t$,
$H(s_*)=h$ and $H\ge h$ on $[s_*,t]$ (if $H$ vanishes somewhere, it vanishes identically afterwards, so $H(t)>0$ gives $H>0$ on $[s_0,t]$).
Hence $\log2=\log(H(t)/H(s_*))\le\frac92S_{s_0}(t)^2|\mathcal G\cap[s_*,t]|$. On $\mathcal G\cap[s_*,t]$, $e>H/(36V^2)\ge h/(36S_{s_0}(t)^2)$, so
$|\mathcal G\cap[s_*,t]|\le36S_{s_0}(t)^2h^{-1}\int_{s_*}^te\,ds\le36S_{s_0}(t)^2\epsilon(s_*)/h$. Together, $h\le162S_{s_0}(t)^4\epsilon(s_*)/\log2$.

Remark. (i) Compared with Proposition 41(a), (d), the exponential of $\int V^2$ and the enstrophy are replaced by the fourth power of the
swirl record and the dissipation after $s_0$, which tends to $0$ as $s_0\to T_*$; the last-passage form has $\epsilon(s_*)$ in place of $\epsilon(s_0)$. With
Lemma 43 and the enstrophy bound it gives, at a point $x$ with $|b(x)|>0$ and at times $t$ late enough that $\|\nabla u(t)\|_2^2\le2E_0D(t)$ and $H(s_0)\le\frac{2187}{128}S_{s_0}(t)^4\epsilon(s_0)$,
$|b(x)|^2\le24(A_1W+r(x)A_1^2)(1+\log_+(D\max(r(x),W/A_1)/|b(x)|))$ with $A_1=(\frac{2187}{64})^{1/2}S_{s_0}(t)^2\epsilon(s_0)^{1/2}$ and $W=(2E_0D)^{1/2}$.
Compared with Corollary 44 (with the same swirl record, which is the case for fixed $s_0$ in regime (II) eventually, since $S\to\infty$) this is an improvement
when $S^2\epsilon(s_0)\ll E_0D$; in regime (II) it again yields only necessary conditions. (ii) On the scalar
family of Limitations 1' the ratio of $H$ to $S^4\epsilon(t)$, where $\epsilon(t)=\int_t^{T_*}\|\nabla u\|_2^2=2(\log(e/\tau))^{-1/2}$ there, tends to $0$ like
$L^{-7/2}$, so for every $c>0$ the family satisfies $H(t)\le cS^4\epsilon(t)$ eventually, a fortiori both forms of Corollary 46 for late $s_0$ (the integral form: for fixed $s_0$, as $t\uparrow T_*$, $\int_{s_0}^tS^4e$ is of order $c_S^4\tau^{-2}L^{5/2}$ there).

**Axis-centred means, a Riccati ceiling and the near-axis branch of (FLAT$_0$).** The next results connect axis-centred velocity means with the
coupled energy and apply the resulting bounds at flat records near the axis; their origin is recorded in the Verification section. All proofs below were
re-derived by hand, and the scalar examples were checked by a symbolic computation. Throughout, $\tau=T_*-t$, $L=1+\log(1/\tau)$, $V=\|u^\theta\|_\infty$, $e=\|\nabla u\|_2^2$ and
$\epsilon(s)=\int_s^{T_*}e\,dt\le E_0/2$.

**Lemma 47 (axis-centred means).** Let $x_0$ be a point of the axis, $\rho\ge0$, $s=|y-x_0|$, and write $(u)_{B_0(x_0)}=u(x_0)$. At every $t<T_*$,
$(u)_{B_\rho(x_0)}=m_\rho e_z$ with
$$m_\rho=\frac1{4\pi}\int\omega^\theta(y)\,\frac{r(y)}{\max(s,\rho)^3}\,dy,$$
and for $0\le\rho\le R$ with $R>0$, $|m_\rho|\le(3R/(35\pi))^{1/2}\|\eta\|_{L^2(B_R(x_0))}+(3/(4\pi))^{1/2}\|u\|_2R^{-3/2}$. Consequently, with
$U(t)=\sup\{|(u(t))_{B_\rho(x_0)}|:x_0\text{ on the axis},\ \rho\ge0\}$,
$$U\le0.3802\,E_0^{1/8}\|\eta\|_2^{3/4},\qquad\text{so}\qquad H\ge\|\eta\|_2^2\ge13\,E_0^{-1/3}\,U^{8/3}.$$

Proof. By rotation invariance about the axis, the mean of $u$ over an axis-centred ball has no horizontal component, and the mean of the swirl
part $u^\theta e_\theta$ vanishes; the vertical component comes from the meridional part, $b(x)=\frac1{4\pi}\int\omega^\theta(y)e_\theta(y)\times(x-y)|x-y|^{-3}dy$.
By Newton's theorem $\Phi_\rho(y)=\fint_{B_\rho(x_0)}|x-y|^{-1}dx$ equals $s^{-1}$ for $s\ge\rho$ and $(3\rho^2-s^2)/(2\rho^3)$ for $s\le\rho$, so the mean of $(x-y)|x-y|^{-3}=-\nabla_x|x-y|^{-1}$
over $x\in B_\rho(x_0)$ is $\nabla_y\Phi_\rho(y)=(x_0-y)\max(s,\rho)^{-3}$. Since $(e_\theta(y)\times(x_0-y))_3=r(y)$ for $x_0$ on the axis, the formula for $m_\rho$ follows
(for $\rho=0$ it is the Biot-Savart law at $x_0$; the integrand is $O(r^2s^{-3})$ near $x_0$). For $\rho\le R$ the kernels of $m_\rho$ and $m_R$ agree on
$\{s\ge R\}$, and $0\le\max(s,\rho)^{-3}-R^{-3}\le s^{-3}-R^{-3}$ on $\{s<R\}$. With $|\omega^\theta|=r|\eta|$, Cauchy-Schwarz, $\int_{|y-x_0|=s}r^4\,dS=\frac{32\pi}{15}s^6$ and
$\int_0^1(1-x^3)^2dx=\frac9{14}$,
$$|m_\rho-m_R|\le\frac1{4\pi}\|\eta\|_{L^2(B_R(x_0))}\Big(\frac{32\pi}{15}\int_0^R(1-s^3R^{-3})^2ds\Big)^{1/2}=\frac1{4\pi}\Big(\frac{48\pi R}{35}\Big)^{1/2}\|\eta\|_{L^2(B_R(x_0))}=\Big(\frac{3R}{35\pi}\Big)^{1/2}\|\eta\|_{L^2(B_R(x_0))},$$
and $|m_R|\le|B_R|^{-1/2}\|u\|_2$. If $\|\eta\|_2=0$, then $\omega^\theta=0$, every $m_\rho$ vanishes and $U=0$; otherwise put $a=(3/(35\pi))^{1/2}\|\eta\|_2$ and $\beta=(3/(4\pi))^{1/2}E_0^{1/2}\ge(3/(4\pi))^{1/2}\|u(t)\|_2$. The function $aR^{1/2}+\beta R^{-3/2}$ is minimal at
$R_*=(3\beta/a)^{1/2}$, with value $\frac43aR_*^{1/2}=\frac43\,3^{1/4}a^{3/4}\beta^{1/4}$; for $\rho\le R_*$ this bounds $|m_\rho|$, and for $\rho>R_*$, $|m_\rho|\le\beta\rho^{-3/2}<\beta R_*^{-3/2}=\frac13aR_*^{1/2}$.
Numerically $\frac43\,3^{1/4}(3/(35\pi))^{3/8}(3/(4\pi))^{1/8}=0.3801209\ldots$, and $0.3802^{-8/3}=13.18\ldots>13$. $\square$

**Lemma 48 (differential inequalities and a Riccati ceiling).** Let $\mathcal G=\{t\in[0,T_*):H(t)<36V(t)^2e(t)\}$. For a solution that blows up at $T_*$, $H>0$ and
$e>0$ on $[0,T_*)$, and:
(a) $H'+\frac{H^2}e\le H'+J\le9V^2H\,1_{\mathcal G}$;
(b) for $0\le s<t<T_*$, $H(t)\le\max\{36\sup_{[s,t]}V^2e,\ (t-s)^{-2}\int_s^te\,dt'\}$;
(c) in the variable $\sigma=\log(1/\tau)$, with $\hat h=\tau^2H$, $\hat e=\tau e$ and $\hat v=\tau V^2$ (so $\int e\,dt=\int\hat e\,d\sigma$ and $\int V^2dt=\int\hat v\,d\sigma$),
$$(\log\hat h)_\sigma+2+\frac{\hat h}{\hat e}\le9\hat v\,1_{\mathcal G},\qquad(\log(1+\hat h))_\sigma+\frac{\hat h^2}{(1+\hat h)\hat e}\le9\hat v\,1_{\mathcal G} .$$

Proof. If $u(t)=0$ at some $t$, then $u\equiv0$ afterwards, so $u(t)\ne0$ and $e(t)>0$; if $H(t)=0$, then $\omega^\theta=0$ (so $b=0$) and $\omega^r=-\partial_zu^\theta=0$
(so $u^\theta=0$ by decay), i.e. $u(t)=0$. (a) Proposition 41(a) with $3V(HJ)^{1/2}\le\frac12J+\frac92V^2H$ gives $H'+J\le9V^2H$, and Proposition 41(d) gives $J\ge H^2/e$. Off $\mathcal G$,
$J^{1/2}\ge He^{-1/2}\ge6VH^{1/2}$, so $3V(HJ)^{1/2}\le\frac12J$ and Proposition 41(a) gives $H'+J\le0$. (b) If $H>36V^2e$ on all of $[s,t]$, then $H'\le-H^2/e$ there, so
$(1/H)'\ge1/e$ and $1/H(t)\ge\int_s^tdt'/e\ge(t-s)^2/\int_s^te$ by Cauchy-Schwarz. Otherwise let $s_1$ be the largest $t'\in[s,t]$ with $H(t')\le36V(t')^2e(t')$ ($H,V,e$ are
continuous on $[0,T_*)$); then $H(s_1)\le36\sup_{[s,t]}V^2e$, and $H$ is nonincreasing on $[s_1,t]$ by (a). (c) $d\sigma=dt/\tau$ and $(\log\hat h)_\sigma=-2+\tau H'/H$; multiply
$H'/H+H/e\le9V^21_{\mathcal G}$ (from (a)) by $\tau$. For the second form, $(\log(1+\hat h))_\sigma=\frac{\hat h}{1+\hat h}(\log\hat h)_\sigma$; insert the first form and drop $-\frac{2\hat h}{1+\hat h}\le0$. $\square$

**Theorem 49 (swirl on $\mathcal G$ against a lower bound for $H$).** Let $t_1\in[\max\{0,T_*-1\},T_*)$ and $\sigma_1=\log(1/(T_*-t_1))\ge0$.
(a) There are no $h_0,c>0$ and $k\le\frac12$ such that for all $t\in[t_1,T_*)$: (i) $H(t)\ge h_0\tau^{-2}$, and (ii) $V(t)\le c\tau^{-1/2}L^k$ if $t\in \mathcal G$.
Hypothesis (i) holds with $h_0=13E_0^{-1/3}\gamma^{8/3}$ if at every such $t$ some axis-centred ball mean (Lemma 47, any radius, point values included) has modulus at least $\gamma\tau^{-3/4}$.
(b) The same holds with (i) replaced by: there is $\delta>0$ such that for arbitrarily large integers $j$ the set $\{\sigma\in[2^j,2^{j+1}]:\hat h(\sigma)\ge h_0\}$ has measure at least $\delta2^j$.
(c) If $H\ge h_1(\sigma)\tau^{-2}$ on $[t_1,T_*)$ with $h_1>0$ nondecreasing, then for every integer $N\ge1$, with $t^{(N)}=T_*-e^{-\sigma_1-N}$,
$$\int_{[t_1,t^{(N)}]\cap \mathcal G}V^2dt\ \ge\ \frac2{9E_0}\Big(\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}h_1(\sigma_1+j)^{1/2}\Big)^2-\frac19\log\frac{\hat h(\sigma_1)}{h_1(\sigma_1)} .$$
(d) If $H(t_n)\to\infty$ along some sequence $t_n\uparrow T_*$, there is no $\kappa>0$ with $V^2\le\kappa\tau H$ on $\mathcal G\cap[t_1,T_*)$.
(e) If $V\le c\tau^{-1/2}L^k$ on $\mathcal G\cap[t_1,T_*)$ for some $c>0$ and $k\ge0$, then $\tau^2H(t)L(t)^{-4k}\to0$ as $t\uparrow T_*$, and
$\int_{t_1}^{T_*}\tau HL^{-4k}dt\le\frac12\tau_1^2H(t_1)L(t_1)^{-4k}+\frac{81}{16}c^4E_0$, where $\tau_1=T_*-t_1$.
(f) For $t_1\le s<t<T_*$ let $K=\sup(\{0\}\cup\{V(r)^2:r\in[s,t]\cap \mathcal G\})$. Then
$$H(t)\le\Big(\frac{9K/2}{1-\exp(-9K(t-s)/2)}\Big)^2\int_s^te\,dr,$$
where the first factor is read as $(t-s)^{-2}$ if $K=0$. Consequently, if $V\le c\tau^{-1/2}L^k$ on $\mathcal G\cap[t_1,T_*)$ for some $c>0$ and $k\ge0$, then at every $t$ with $t-\tau\ge t_1$,
$$\tau^2H(t)L(t)^{-4k}\le C(c)\int_{t-\tau}^te\,dr,\qquad C(c)=\Big(\frac{9c^2/2}{1-\exp(-9c^2/2)}\Big)^2,$$
and $\int_{t_2}^{T_*}\big(\int_{t-\tau}^te\,dr\big)\frac{dt}\tau\le\log2\int_{t_1}^{T_*}e\,dt$ for $t_2\in[\frac12(T_*+t_1),T_*)$.
(g) For $0\le s<t<T_*$ let $\Phi_t(r)=\frac92\int_r^tV(r')^2\,1_{\mathcal G}(r')\,dr'$ for $r\in[s,t]$. Then
$$H(t)\Big(\int_s^t\exp(-\Phi_t(r))\,dr\Big)^2\le\int_s^te\,dr .$$
In particular, if $0<\lambda\le t$ and $\int_{[t-\lambda,t]\cap \mathcal G}V^2dr\le\frac29\log2$, then $\lambda^2H(t)\le4\int_{t-\lambda}^te\,dr$.

Proof. (a) Since $L\ge1$ we may assume $0\le k\le\frac12$. By Lemma 48(c), (ii) and $L=1+\sigma$, integrating the first form over $[\sigma_1,2S]$ for $S\ge\sigma_1$ and dropping $\int_{\sigma_1}^S\hat h/\hat e\ge0$ and the term $2$,
$h_0\int_S^{2S}d\sigma/\hat e\le\int_S^{2S}\hat h/\hat e\le\log\frac{\hat h(\sigma_1)}{\hat h(2S)}+9c^2\int_{\sigma_1}^{2S}(1+\sigma)^{2k}d\sigma\le C_1S^{2k+1}$ for $S\ge S_0$, using $\hat h(2S)\ge h_0$. By
Cauchy-Schwarz $S^2\le\int_S^{2S}\hat e\,d\sigma\int_S^{2S}\hat e^{-1}d\sigma$, so $\int_S^{2S}\hat e\,d\sigma\ge(h_0/C_1)S^{1-2k}\ge h_0/C_1$. The blocks $[2^j,2^{j+1}]$ are disjoint, so
$\int_{t_1}^{T_*}e\,dt=\infty$, contradicting the energy inequality. For the second sentence use Lemma 47. (b) Put $w=\hat h(1+\hat h)^{-1/2}$, so $w\ge w_0=h_0(1+h_0)^{-1/2}$ where
$\hat h\ge h_0$. Integrating the second form of Lemma 48(c) as in (a) gives $\int_S^{2S}w^2/\hat e\le\log(1+\hat h(\sigma_1))+9c^2\int_{\sigma_1}^{2S}(1+\sigma)^{2k}\le C_1S^{2k+1}$, and
$(\delta w_0S)^2\le(\int_S^{2S}w)^2\le\int_S^{2S}\hat e\int_S^{2S}w^2/\hat e$ for $S=2^j$ in the infinite set; conclude as in (a). (c) Integrate the first form of Lemma 48(c) over
$I_j=[\sigma_1+j,\sigma_1+j+1]$ and use $\hat h\ge h_1(\sigma_1+j)$ there: $h_1(\sigma_1+j)\int_{I_j}\hat e^{-1}\le9\int_{I_j}\hat v1_{\mathcal G}+\log\hat h(\sigma_1+j)-\log\hat h(\sigma_1+j+1)$. Summing over $j<N$, the
logarithms telescope, and $\hat h(\sigma_1+N)\ge h_1(\sigma_1)$. With $\hat e_j=\int_{I_j}\hat e$, Cauchy-Schwarz on $I_j$ gives $\int_{I_j}\hat e^{-1}\ge1/\hat e_j$, and over $j$,
$(\sum_jh_1(\sigma_1+j)^{1/2})^2\le\sum_jh_1(\sigma_1+j)\hat e_j^{-1}\cdot\sum_j\hat e_j$, with $\sum_j\hat e_j\le\int e\,dt\le E_0/2$. (d) By Lemma 48(a), $(1/H)'=-H'/H^2\ge1/e-9V^21_{\mathcal G}/H\ge1/e-9\kappa\tau$ on
$[t_1,T_*)$. For $t_1\le t<t_n$ this gives $\int_t^{t_n}ds/e\le H(t_n)^{-1}+\frac92\kappa\tau(t)^2$, and by Cauchy-Schwarz $(t_n-t)^2\le\epsilon(t)(H(t_n)^{-1}+\frac92\kappa\tau(t)^2)$. Letting
$n\to\infty$ gives $\epsilon(t)\ge2/(9\kappa)$ for every $t\ge t_1$, while $\epsilon(t)\to0$. (e) By Lemma 48(a), $H'\le9V^2H-H^2/e\le\frac{81}4V^4e$ on $\mathcal G$ (maximize in $H$) and $H'\le0$ off $\mathcal G$.
In the notation of Lemma 48(c) put $f=\hat hL^{-4k}$; since $L_\sigma=1$ and $\hat h\ge0$, $f_\sigma\le L^{-4k}\hat h_\sigma=L^{-4k}(\tau^3H'-2\hat h)$, so $f_\sigma+2f\le\frac{81}4c^4\hat e$. Hence
$f(\sigma)\le\exp(-2(\sigma-\sigma_1))f(\sigma_1)+\frac{81}4c^4\int_{\sigma_1}^\sigma\exp(-2(\sigma-s))\hat e(s)\,ds$. Splitting the integral at $\sigma-A$ bounds it by $\exp(-2A)E_0/2+\int_{\sigma-A}^\infty\hat e\,ds$, because
$\int\hat e\,d\sigma=\int e\,dt\le E_0/2$; letting $\sigma\to\infty$ and then $A\to\infty$ gives $f\to0$. Integrating the bound for $f$ over $\sigma\ge\sigma_1$ gives the integral bound, since $\int f\,d\sigma=\int\tau HL^{-4k}dt$. (f) Put $y=1/H$ and $\beta=9V^21_{\mathcal G}\le9K$ on $[s,t]$. By Lemma 48(a),
$y'=-H'/H^2\ge1/e-\beta y$, so $(y\exp(\int_s^\cdot\beta))'\ge\exp(\int_s^\cdot\beta)/e$; integrating over $[s,t]$ and dropping $y(s)\ge0$ gives $y(t)\ge\int_s^t\exp(-9K(t-r))e(r)^{-1}dr$. By Cauchy-Schwarz,
$(\int_s^t\exp(-9K(t-r)/2)dr)^2\le\int_s^te\,dr\cdot\int_s^t\exp(-9K(t-r))e(r)^{-1}dr$, and $\int_s^t\exp(-9K(t-r)/2)dr=(1-\exp(-9K(t-s)/2))/(9K/2)$ (equal to $t-s$ if $K=0$). For the second statement take $s=t-\tau$:
for $r\in[t-\tau,t]$, $T_*-r\ge\tau$ and $L(r)\le L(t)$, so $K\le c^2\tau^{-1}L^{2k}$; since $x\mapsto x/(1-\exp(-x\tau))$ is nondecreasing on $x>0$ and $L\ge1$,
$H(t)\le\tau^{-2}L^{4k}\big((9c^2/2)/(1-\exp(-9c^2L^{2k}/2))\big)^2\int_{t-\tau}^te\le\tau^{-2}L^{4k}C(c)\int_{t-\tau}^te$. Finally $r\in[t-\tau(t),t]$ if and only if $r\le t\le\frac12(T_*+r)$, and
$\int_{\max(r,t_2)}^{(T_*+r)/2}dt/(T_*-t)\le\log2$, while only $r\ge2t_2-T_*\ge t_1$ occur, so Tonelli gives the last bound. (g) With $\beta=9V^21_{\mathcal G}$ (locally integrable, since $\mathcal G$ is relatively open and $V$ is continuous) and $B(r)=\int_s^r\beta$, the
function $y\exp(B)$ is absolutely continuous and $(y\exp(B))'\ge\exp(B)/e$ almost everywhere, as in (f); integrating over $[s,t]$ and dropping $y(s)\ge0$ gives
$y(t)\ge\int_s^t\exp(-(B(t)-B(r)))e(r)^{-1}dr=\int_s^t\exp(-2\Phi_t(r))e(r)^{-1}dr$, and Cauchy-Schwarz gives $(\int_s^t\exp(-\Phi_t))^2\le\int_s^te\,dr\cdot\int_s^t\exp(-2\Phi_t)e^{-1}dr$.
If $\Phi_t(t-\lambda)\le\log2$, then $\Phi_t\le\log2$ on $[t-\lambda,t]$ because $\Phi_t$ is nonincreasing, so $\int_{t-\lambda}^t\exp(-\Phi_t)\ge\lambda/2$. $\square$

Remark. (i) The thresholds are sharp for the scalar inputs used (Proposition 41(a), (d) and the energy inequality). For $k>\frac12$:
$H=h_0\tau^{-2}$, $V=c\tau^{-1/2}L^k$, $e=(h_0/c^2)\tau^{-1}L^{-2k}$, $J=h_0c^2\tau^{-3}L^{2k}$ satisfy $eJ=H^2$, $\frac12H'+J\le3V(HJ)^{1/2}$ once $2c^2L^{2k}\ge1$, lie in $\mathcal G$, and have
$\int e\,dt<\infty$. For (d): $H=\tau^{-2-a}$, $V^2=\tau^{-1-aq}$, $e=\tau^{-1+a(q-1)}$, $J=HV^2$ with $a>0$, $q>1$ satisfy $eJ=H^2$, $\frac12H'+J\le3V(HJ)^{1/2}$ once
$2\tau^{-aq}\ge1+\frac a2$, lie in $\mathcal G$, and have $\int e\,dt<\infty$, while $\tau V^2=\hat h^q$; so in (d) the bound $V^2\le\kappa\tau^{-1}\hat h$ cannot be weakened to $V^2\le\kappa\tau^{-1}\hat h^q$ with
$q>1$. (ii) With constant $h_1=h_0$, (c) gives $\int_{[t_1,t]\cap \mathcal G}V^2\ge\frac{2h_0}{9E_0}\lfloor\sigma(t)-\sigma_1\rfloor^2-C$, quadratic in $\log(1/\tau)$; this excludes swirl
$c\tau^{-1/2}L^k$ on $\mathcal G$ for $k<\frac12$ by a different route; (a) also covers $k=\frac12$. (iii) Compared with Corollary 45, Theorem 49(a) needs no gradient
hypothesis and allows the swirl to exceed Type I by $L^{1/2}$, but its near-axis condition concerns ball means centred on the axis (or the lower bound on $H$
directly), not points within $C\tau^{1/2}$ of the axis, and it is required at all late times (or, in the variable $\sigma$, on a subset of measure at least $\delta2^j$ of $[2^j,2^{j+1}]$ for a fixed $\delta>0$ and arbitrarily large $j$), not on a set of
infinite $dt/(\tau L)$ measure. (iv) No a priori bound of the kind excluded here is known in regime (II): there is no known lower bound $M\ge\gamma\tau^{-3/4}$,
and the swirl is not known to be bounded by $c\tau^{-1/2}L^{1/2}$. (v) Theorem 49(e) is pointwise in time and assumes nothing about $H$ at other times. For $k=0$ it contains
the cases $k\le0$ of (a) and (b), since it excludes $\hat h\ge h_0$ on any set of times accumulating at $T_*$; for $0<k\le\frac12$ the pointwise conclusion alone does not contain (a), since it allows $\hat h=o(L^{4k})$, for instance $\hat h\equiv h_0$. The integral conclusion contains (a) and (b) for $0<k\le\frac14$, since $\int(1+\sigma)^{-4k}d\sigma=\infty$ (and a block of (b) contributes at least $\delta h_02^j(1+2^{j+1})^{-4k}$); for $\frac14<k\le\frac12$, $\hat h\equiv h_0$ is compatible with both conclusions of (e) when $t_1$ is late enough.
Part (f) is a local form of the first conclusion of (e), without an initial value: the window integral $\int_{t-\tau}^te$ tends to $0$ and is integrable against $dt/\tau$. Part (g) keeps the time distribution of the swirl that (f) discards: the first statement of (f) follows from (g) by replacing $V^21_{\mathcal G}$ by its constant upper bound $K$ (then $\Phi_t(r)\le\frac92K(t-r)$ and $\int_s^t\exp(-\Phi_t)\ge(1-\exp(-\frac92K(t-s)))/(\frac92K)$). At a single time, (g) converts a lower bound on $H$, together with a bound on the dissipation over an admissible backward window $[t-\lambda,t]\subset[0,t]$, into a lower bound on the scale-invariant swirl integral $\int V^21_{\mathcal G}\,dt$ over that window; a large supremum of $V$ alone does not control this integral.
Parts (e), (f) and Corollary 52(d) were added after the initial audits, re-derived here by hand, and audited afterwards as described in the Verification section.

**Corollary 50 (axis-centred means in integrated form).** Let $a\in[\max\{0,T_*-1\},T_*)$, $\tau_a=T_*-a$, $L_a=L(a)$, $q\ge0$ and $U$ as in Lemma 47. Then
$$13E_0^{-1/3}\int_a^{T_*}\tau L^{-q}U^{8/3}dt\le\frac{\tau_a^2H(a)}{2L_a^q}+\frac{2187}{256}\int_a^{T_*}\tau^2L^{-q}V^4e\,dt,$$
and for $\gamma>0$, with $\mathcal T_\gamma=\{t\in[a,T_*):U(t)\ge\gamma\tau^{-3/4}\}$ and $h_0=13E_0^{-1/3}\gamma^{8/3}$,
$$\Big(\int_{\mathcal T_\gamma}\frac{dt}\tau\Big)^2\le\frac{E_0}{2h_0^2}\Big[\tau_a^2H(a)+\frac{2187}{16}\int_a^{T_*}\tau^2V^4e\,dt\Big].$$
In particular $\int_{\mathcal T_\gamma}dt/\tau<\infty$ if $\int^{T_*}\tau^2V^4e\,dt<\infty$, and $\int_{\mathcal T_\gamma}dt/(\tau L^{4k})<\infty$ if $V\le c\tau^{-1/2}L^k$ near $T_*$.

Proof. $U$ is a supremum of functions continuous in $t$, so it is lower semicontinuous and $\mathcal T_\gamma$ is a Borel set. For the first bound, $H(t)\le H(s)+\frac{2187}{128}\int_s^tV^4e$
(Corollary 46), $H\ge13E_0^{-1/3}U^{8/3}$ (Lemma 47), and Tonelli with $\int_s^{T_*}(T_*-t)L(t)^{-q}dt\le\frac12\tau(s)^2L(s)^{-q}$ ($L$ increases with $t$). For the second, Proposition 41(a), (d) give
$H'\le-2J+6Ve^{1/4}J^{3/4}\le-J+\frac{2187}{16}V^4e$, because $\max_{y\ge0}(6Ve^{1/4}y^3-y^4)=\frac{27}{256}(6Ve^{1/4})^4=\frac{2187}{16}V^4e$, and $J\ge H^2/e$. In the notation of Lemma 48(c),
$\hat h_\sigma=-2\hat h+\tau^3H'\le-2\hat h-\hat h^2/\hat e+\frac{2187}{16}\tau^3V^4e$. Integrating in $\sigma$ from $\sigma(a)$ and dropping $-2\int\hat h$ and the final value of $\hat h$:
$\int\hat h^2/\hat e\,d\sigma\le\tau_a^2H(a)+\frac{2187}{16}\int_a^{T_*}\tau^2V^4e\,dt$. On $\mathcal T_\gamma$, $\hat h\ge h_0$ (Lemma 47), and $(\int_{\mathcal T_\gamma}d\sigma)^2\le\int\hat e\,d\sigma\int_{\mathcal T_\gamma}\hat e^{-1}d\sigma$ with
$\int\hat e\,d\sigma\le E_0/2$. The last sentence follows from the first bound with $q=0$, and for $k\ge0$ with $q=4k$ (then $\tau^2L^{-4k}V^4e\le c^4e$). For $k<0$ put $p=-4k$; with $\tau(t)=\tau(s)e^{-z}$ one has $L(t)=L(s)+z\le L(s)(1+z)$, so $\int_s^{T_*}\tau L^pdt\le C_p\tau(s)^2L(s)^p$ with $C_p=\int_0^\infty e^{-2z}(1+z)^pdz$, and the same Tonelli argument gives $13E_0^{-1/3}\int_a^{T_*}\tau L^pU^{8/3}dt\le C_p(\tau_a^2L_a^pH(a)+\frac{2187}{128}\int_a^{T_*}\tau^2L^pV^4e\,dt)$, whose right side is finite when $V\le c\tau^{-1/2}L^k$. $\square$

Corollary 50 is the analogue of Corollary 45 without a gradient hypothesis and with the logarithmic measure $dt/\tau$ in place of $dt/(\tau L)$, for ball
means centred on the axis instead of point values near it.

**Proposition 51 (the coupled energy at a flat record near the axis).** Let $t$ be a velocity first crossing with $|b(x_t,t)|\ge\frac78M$, and let
$C_0,C_2$, $\rho_0=M/(16A)$ and $\rho_k=2^k\rho_0$ be as in Proposition 33 with $\rho_*=C_2L_E(M)$. Put $r_t=r(x_t)$, $h_b=M/\theta_b(t)$,
$$q=0\ \text{ if }r_t\le\rho_0,\qquad q=\lceil\log_2(r_t/\rho_0)\rceil\ \text{ if }r_t>\rho_0,\qquad p_q=\Big\lfloor\frac{5h_b}{8C_0}\Big\rfloor-q .$$
If $p_q\ge1$, then
$$H(t)\ge\|\eta(t)\|_2^2\ge\Lambda(t):=c_5\,\frac{\theta_b(t)^2\,2^{p_q}}{L_E(M)},\qquad c_5=\frac{2\pi C_0^2}{11\,C_2}.$$

Proof. Use the exact decomposition of the proof of Proposition 33 at $x=x_t$: $b(x_t)=B_{core}+\sum_{k=0}^JB_k+T$ with $|B_{core}|\le M/8$, $|T|\le M/8$, $|B_k|\le C_0\theta_b$ and
$\rho_J<\rho_*$. The kernel of $B_k$ is bounded by $(4\pi)^{-1}|x_t-y|^{-2}$ and supported in the shell $S_k=\{\rho_k\le|x_t-y|\le4\rho_k\}$, and almost every point lies in at most
two shells. Hence $\sum_k|B_k|\ge\frac58M$, and, with $|\omega^\theta|=r|\eta|$ and Cauchy-Schwarz, $|B_k|\le(4\pi)^{-1}\|\eta\|_{L^2(S_k)}(\int_{S_k}r(y)^2|x_t-y|^{-4}dy)^{1/2}$. Writing $y=x_t+z$,
$r(y)^2=r_t^2+2x_t'\cdot z'+|z'|^2$ with $x_t'$, $z'$ the horizontal parts; the middle term is odd in $z$, the spherical mean of $|z'|^2$ is $\frac23|z|^2$, and so
$\int_{S_k}r(y)^2|x_t-y|^{-4}dy=4\pi r_t^2\int_{\rho_k}^{4\rho_k}s^{-2}ds+\frac{8\pi}3\int_{\rho_k}^{4\rho_k}ds=\frac{3\pi r_t^2}{\rho_k}+8\pi\rho_k$. For $k\ge q$, $\rho_k\ge r_t$, so
$|B_k|^2\le\frac{3r_t^2+8\rho_k^2}{16\pi\rho_k}\|\eta\|_{L^2(S_k)}^2\le\frac{11\rho_k}{16\pi}\|\eta\|_{L^2(S_k)}^2$. The shells $k<q$ contribute at most $qC_0\theta_b$, so with $m=C_0\theta_b$,
$\sum_{q\le k\le J}|B_k|\ge(\frac{5h_b}{8C_0}-q)m\ge p_qm$; as each term is at most $m$, there are at least $p_q$ such shells. Index them from the top, $j=J-k$, $a_j=|B_{J-j}|$. The
$p_q-1$ shells $j\le p_q-2$ carry at most $(p_q-1)m$, so $\sum_{j\ge p_q-1}a_j\ge m$, and by Cauchy-Schwarz $m^2\le\sum_{j\ge p_q-1}a_j^22^j\cdot\sum_{j\ge p_q-1}2^{-j}\le2^{2-p_q}\sum_ja_j^22^j$.
Therefore $\sum_{k\ge q}|B_k|^2/\rho_k=\rho_J^{-1}\sum_ja_j^22^j\ge m^22^{p_q-2}/\rho_J$, and
$\|\eta\|_2^2\ge\frac12\sum_{k\ge q}\|\eta\|_{L^2(S_k)}^2\ge\frac{8\pi}{11}\sum_{k\ge q}\frac{|B_k|^2}{\rho_k}\ge\frac{2\pi C_0^2\theta_b^22^{p_q}}{11\rho_J}>c_5\frac{\theta_b^22^{p_q}}{L_E(M)}$. $\square$

Within the inner radius ($r_t\le\rho_0$) $q=0$ and the exponent is $\lfloor5h_b/(8C_0)\rfloor$; each octave of distance of the record point from the axis above $\rho_0$ costs one unit of the
exponent, and $p_q\ge1$ holds exactly when $5h_b\ge8C_0$ and $r_t\le\rho_02^{\lfloor5h_b/(8C_0)\rfloor-1}$. Compared with Lemma 47 at a record point on the axis, which gives $H\ge cM^{8/3}E_0^{-1/3}$, that is
$c\,\theta_b^2h_b^2/L_E(M)$, the gain is the factor $2^{p_q}/h_b^2$, which is exponential in $h_b$ when $p_q\ge\delta h_b$ for a fixed $\delta>0$ (for instance when $r_t$ is at most a fixed multiple of $\rho_0$). Proposition 41(c) with Proposition 33 (Corollary 42(c), (d)) already gives an $L^2$
lower bound with the factor $2^{2k_*}$; the shell-by-shell weighted optimization removes the explicit factor $A^{-2}$ from it (the dependence on $A$ remains through $q$).

**Corollary 52 (consequences at flat records near the axis).** Let $t$ be as in Proposition 51 with $p_q\ge1$ and $\Lambda=\Lambda(t)$.
(a) For $0\le s<t$, $36\sup_{[s,t]}V^2e\ge\Lambda$ or $\int_s^te\ge(t-s)^2\Lambda$. If $t\ge T_*/2$, then with $s=t-\tau$ either $36\sup_{[t-\tau,t]}V^2e\ge\Lambda$ or
$$M(t)^{8/3}\tau^2\le\frac{(6/\pi)^{1/3}}{2c_5}\,E_0^{4/3}\,h_b^2\,2^{-p_q}.$$
(b) For $s_0\le t$, $S_{s_0}(t)^4\epsilon(s_0)\ge\frac{128}{2187}(\Lambda-H(s_0))$.
(c) If $\Lambda(t_n)\to\infty$ along a sequence of such times $t_n\uparrow T_*$, then Theorem 49(d) applies: there is no $\kappa$ with $V^2\le\kappa\tau H$ on $\mathcal G$ near $T_*$.
(d) If $V\le c\tau^{-1/2}L^k$ on $\mathcal G$ near $T_*$ for some $c>0$, $k\ge0$, then along every sequence of such times $t_n\uparrow T_*$,
$(M(t_n)\tau_n^{3/4})^{8/3}\,2^{p_q(t_n)}h_b(t_n)^{-2}L(t_n)^{-4k}\to0$. In particular a swirl bound $V\le c\tau^{-1/2}$ on $\mathcal G$ near $T_*$ excludes a sequence of such crossings $t_n\uparrow T_*$ with
$M\tau^{3/4}\ge m_0>0$ and $2^{p_q}\ge f_0h_b^2$, $f_0>0$; and for every fixed $k$ the bound $V\le c\tau^{-1/2}L^k$ on $\mathcal G$ near $T_*$ fails, for every $c$, if there is a sequence of such crossings $t_n\uparrow T_*$ with
$M\tau^{3/4}\ge m_0$, $p_q\ge\delta h_b$ ($\delta>0$ fixed) and $h_b/\log L\to\infty$ along it.
Quantitatively, let $t_1\in[\max\{0,T_*-1\},T_*)$ and assume the same swirl bound on $\mathcal G\cap[t_1,T_*)$; then every such crossing time $t$ with $2t-T_*\ge t_1$ satisfies
$(M\tau^{3/4})^{8/3}2^{p_q}h_b^{-2}\le c_5^{-1}(6E_0/\pi)^{1/3}C(c)L^{4k}\int_{t-\tau}^te\,dr$, with $C(c)$ as in Theorem 49(f).
(e) For every $\lambda\in(0,t]$ with $\lambda^2\Lambda>4\int_{t-\lambda}^te\,dr$, in particular for every $\lambda\in((2E_0/\Lambda)^{1/2},t]$, one has $\int_{[t-\lambda,t]\cap \mathcal G}V^2dr>\frac29\log2$, hence
$\sup_{[t-\lambda,t]\cap \mathcal G}V^2>\frac{2\log2}{9\lambda}$. Here $(2E_0/\Lambda)^{1/2}=\tau\,(2E_0/(\tau^2\Lambda))^{1/2}$ with $\tau^2\Lambda$ as in the proof of (d), so if $M\tau^{3/4}\ge m_0>0$ the threshold length $(2E_0/\Lambda)^{1/2}$ is at most
$C\,h_b2^{-p_q/2}\tau$ with $C=((2/c_5)(6/\pi)^{1/3}E_0^{4/3}m_0^{-8/3})^{1/2}$. The explicit interval $((2E_0/\Lambda)^{1/2},t]$ is nonempty exactly when $(2E_0/\Lambda)^{1/2}<t$; the condition $\lambda^2\Lambda>4\int_{t-\lambda}^te\,dr$ may admit further window lengths.

Proof. (a) Lemma 48(b) with Proposition 51; for the second form use $\int_s^te\le E_0/2$, $\theta_b=M/h_b$ and $L_E(M)=(6E_0/(\pi M^2))^{1/3}$. (b) Corollary 46 with
Proposition 51. (c) $H(t_n)\ge\Lambda(t_n)$. (d) Theorem 49(e) with $H(t_n)\ge\Lambda(t_n)$ and $\tau^2\Lambda=c_5(\pi/(6E_0))^{1/3}(M\tau^{3/4})^{8/3}2^{p_q}h_b^{-2}$; in the last case
$\log_2(2^{p_q}h_b^{-2}L^{-4k})\ge\delta h_b-1-2\log_2h_b-4k\log_2L\to\infty$. The quantitative form is Theorem 49(f) with $H(t)\ge\Lambda(t)$. (e) Theorem 49(g) with $H(t)\ge\Lambda(t)$; for the second range use $\int_{t-\lambda}^te\le E_0/2$, and
$\int_{[t-\lambda,t]\cap \mathcal G}V^2\le\lambda\sup_{[t-\lambda,t]\cap \mathcal G}V^2$ (the set is nonempty because the integral is positive). $\square$

**Remark (the two branches of (FLAT$_0$)).** In regime (II) under (FLAT$_0$), $h_b\ge M/\Theta_0\to\infty$ along velocity first crossings, and $\theta_b^2/L_E(M)=M^{8/3}h_b^{-2}((\pi/6)/E_0)^{1/3}$.
The crossings split according to the octave distance $q$ of the record point from the axis.
(i) Near-axis branch ($p_q\ge1$). By Corollary 52(b), the swirl record satisfies $S_{s_0}(t)^4\epsilon(s_0)\ge\frac{128}{2187}\Lambda-C(s_0)$; at the energy-critical
rate $M\ge m_0\tau^{-3/4}$ this says that $S$ exceeds the Type I size $\tau^{-1/2}$ by the factor $(2^{p_q}/h_b^2)^{1/4}$, up to constants and the factor $\epsilon(s_0)^{-1/4}$; this factor diverges if
$p_q-2\log_2h_b\to\infty$, and is exponential in $h_b$ if $p_q\ge\delta h_b$ for a fixed $\delta>0$. If a lower bound $H\ge h_1(\sigma)\tau^{-2}$ with $h_1$ nondecreasing and unbounded persisted at all late
times, Theorem 49(c) (keep the last term of the sum) would give $\int_{[t_1,t^{(N)}]\cap \mathcal G}V^2dt\ge\frac2{9E_0}h_1(\sigma_1+N-1)-C$, so the critical norm $\int\|u^\theta\|_\infty^2dt$ would carry $h_1$ itself.
With no hypothesis on $H$ between the crossings, Corollary 52(d) shows that if there is a sequence of such crossings $t_n\uparrow T_*$ at the energy-critical rate with $p_q\ge\delta h_b$ and $h_b/\log L\to\infty$ along it,
then for every $c$ and $k$ the swirl exceeds $c\tau^{-1/2}L^k$ at points of $\mathcal G$ arbitrarily close to $T_*$ (not necessarily crossing times); this is again a necessary condition, since no such upper bound on the swirl is known.
By Corollary 52(a), either $M\tau^{3/4}\le CE_0^{1/2}h_b^{3/4}2^{-3p_q/8}$, or $\sup_{[t-\tau,t]}V^2e\ge c\,\tau^{-2}(M\tau^{3/4})^{8/3}E_0^{-1/3}2^{p_q}h_b^{-2}$; if $p_q\ge\delta h_b$ and $M\ge m_0\tau^{-3/4}$,
the first alternative eventually fails, and the second makes the scale-invariant quantity $\tau^2V^2e/E_0$ exponentially large in $h_b$ at some time of the last interval of length $\tau$. None of these
is a contradiction, because no a priori upper bound on $\int V^2dt$ or on $S^4\epsilon(s_0)$ is known: the available bounds are $|u^\theta|\le G/r$ and, for large $S$, $S^2\le G^2(1+G^2)^{-1/2}D$
(Limitations 1'), while $\int D\,dt=\infty$. A bound $S(t)^4\epsilon(s_0)=o(\Lambda(t))$ along these crossings would contradict Corollary 52(b) and exclude this branch. For $\int^tV^2ds$ the
record-time bound alone gives only $\frac92\int_{s_0}^tV^2ds\ge\log(\Lambda(t)/H(s_0))$ (Proposition 41(a)), which is linear in $p_q$; a stronger conclusion needs the persistent lower bound of Theorem 49(c).
(ii) Toroidal branch ($p_q\le0$, that is $5h_b<8C_0$ or $r_t>\rho_02^{\lfloor5h_b/(8C_0)\rfloor-1}$). Here the shell count of Proposition 33 does not force one full unit $C_0\theta_b$ of contribution
from the shells $k\ge q$, so Proposition 51 as stated does not apply (Corollary 54 below gives an exponential lower bound for $\|\nabla u\|_2^2$ in this branch instead). The following single-time family is a heuristic test, of which only orders of magnitude were checked. It suggests that a lower bound
$H\ge c\,\theta_b^2\,2^{c'h_b}/L_E(M)$ with fixed $c,c'>0$ cannot be obtained in this branch from single-time information: if its stated energy, BMO, gradient, vorticity and record-point
estimates were established uniformly, it would satisfy the single-time hypotheses of Proposition 33 with $HL_E(M)/\theta_b^2$ of order $h_b^{-2/3}$. Those uniform estimates are not proved here.
Let $h\to\infty$ and put $N_h=h^2$, $r_0=e^{-4h^2}$, $B=r_0^{-3/2}$, $a_h=B/h$, $R=r_0/10$ and $\ell=Re^{-N_h}$. Around a circle of radius $r_0$ about the
axis take, in meridional coordinates $(s,z)=(r-r_0,z)$ with $d=(s^2+z^2)^{1/2}$, the Stokes stream function $a_hr_0sf(d)$, with $f$ a smoothed truncated logarithm ($N_h$ for $d<\ell$,
$\log(R/d)$ for $\ell<d<R$, $0$ for $d>R$), and add a meridional bump of amplitude $B=a_hh$ and scale comparable to $r_0$ with disjoint support. Then, in orders of magnitude, $M\asymp Bh$, $\theta_b\asymp B$, $h_b\asymp h$, the energy is bounded, $H\asymp B^2/r_0$, $M/A\asymp h^2r_0e^{-h^2}$, $D/M^2\asymp e^{-h^2}h^{-3}$, $L_E(M)\asymp r_0h^{-2/3}$, so $4C_2L_E(M)<r_0$ for large $h$, and $q$ is of order $h^2$. This does not rule out other single-time lower bounds on $H$,
which is large in absolute terms here. Moreover, among positive radial weights $\chi(r)$ smooth on $r>0$, only $\chi=Cr^{-2}$ cancels transport and stretching for every smooth meridional velocity: these contribute
$\int u^r(\frac12\chi'+\frac\chi r)(\omega^\theta)^2dx$ to $\frac12\frac d{dt}\int\chi(\omega^\theta)^2dx$, which vanishes for all meridional velocities only if $\chi=Cr^{-2}$. Necessary conditions do hold in this branch, because Proposition 33 places no restriction
on the position of the record point: as in the proof of Lemma 34(b), Proposition 33 with $W_{s_0}\le W^+_{s_0}\le\frac12S\epsilon(s_0)$ (Lemma 34(a)) and $A/M^2\to0$ gives
$S(t)A(t)/M(t)^2\ge\frac{4\pi c_1}{\sqrt3\,\epsilon(s_0)}2^{k_*}-o(1)$ along velocity first crossings, which is exponential in $h_b$, and Corollary 42(c), (d) bound $2^{k_*}$ by the coupled energy
and the swirl. These involve $A$ or $r(x_t)$, and the known bounds $S\le(GD)^{1/2}$ and $A\le\sqrt2D$ turn the first one only into $h_b\le C(1+\log M)$, the strength of Proposition 14C.
Keeping the shell estimates of Proposition 51 (only the lower bound for the outer sum and the cap $|B_k|\le C_0\theta_b$ enter its dependence on $p_q$), its conclusion holds with $p_q$ replaced by
$p_{out}=\lfloor\sum_{q\le k\le J}|B_k|/(C_0\theta_b)\rfloor\ge p_q$, whenever $p_{out}\ge1$. Fix $0<\delta<1/(4C_0)$ and take sufficiently late crossings, so that $h_b$ is large, and put
$\varepsilon=C_0\delta+4C_0/h_b$. Either $p_{out}\ge\delta h_b$ and the exponential bound of Proposition 51 holds with $p_{out}$, or the shells $k\ge q$ carry less than $(\delta h_b+1)C_0\theta_b$.
In the second case, since all shells carry at least $\frac58M$ and the shells $k<q$ at most $qC_0\theta_b$, $q\ge\frac{5h_b}{8C_0}-\delta h_b-1$, so $q\ge4$. Put $j=\min(J,q-4)$ and
$b_{in}=B_{core}+\sum_{k=0}^{j}B_k$. This is the velocity at $x_t$ produced through the kernel $\phi(\cdot/\rho_{j+1})K$, which is supported in $B_{2\rho_{j+1}}(0)$, and
$2\rho_{j+1}\le2\rho_{q-3}=4\rho_{q-4}=2^{q-2}\rho_0<r_t/2$ because $r_t>2^{q-1}\rho_0$. The shells $j<k\le J$ are the shells $k\ge q$ and at most the three shells $q-3\le k\le q-1$, so
$\sum_{k=0}^{j}|B_k|\ge(\frac58-\varepsilon)M$ and $|b(x_t)-b_{in}|\le(\frac18+\varepsilon)M$: the meridional velocity at the record point is produced, up to that error, by the meridional vorticity in the ball
$B_{r_t/2}(x_t)$, on which $\frac12r_t<r<\frac32r_t$ and $|u^\theta|\le2G/r_t$, and at least $\frac{5h_b}{8C_0}-\delta h_b-4$ of the shells $k\le j$ contribute. The mass bound of the proof
of Proposition 33 then localizes: with $k''=k_*-\lceil\delta h_b\rceil-4$, which satisfies $k''\ge(\frac1{4C_0}-\delta)h_b-6\to\infty$, the shells $k<k''$ carry at most
$k''C_0\theta_b\le(\frac14-\varepsilon)M$, so $\sum_{k''\le k\le j}2Q_{4\rho_k}(x_t)/\rho_k\ge\frac38M$ (in particular $k''\le j\le q-4$), and as in that proof
$Q_{r_t/2}(x_t)\ge Q_{4\rho_j}(x_t)\ge\frac3{32}M\rho_{k''}=c_1(M^2/A)2^{k''}$, an exponential lower bound for the meridional vorticity mass of a disc that stays at distance at least $r_t/2$ from the axis. The core estimate localizes as well. Let $A_{\rm loc}=\sup_{B_{r_t/2}(x_t)}|\omega^\theta(t)|$; then $A_{\rm loc}>0$, since otherwise $b_{in}=0$, and we put $m=\lfloor\log_2(A/A_{\rm loc})\rfloor\ge0$, so that $2A_{\rm loc}\rho_m\le\frac18M$ and $\rho_m>M/(32A_{\rm loc})$. If $m>j$, then $|b_{in}|\le2A_{\rm loc}\rho_{j+1}\le\frac18M$, which contradicts $|b_{in}|\ge(\frac34-\varepsilon)M$; so $m\le j$, the part of $b_{in}$ produced through $\phi(\cdot/\rho_m)K$ (supported in $B_{2\rho_m}(x_t)\subset B_{r_t/2}(x_t)$) is at most $2A_{\rm loc}\rho_m\le\frac18M$, and $\sum_{m\le k\le j}|B_k|\ge(\frac58-\varepsilon)M$. Hence $j-m+1\ge(\frac58-\varepsilon)h_b/C_0$, and with $j\le q-4$ and $\rho_02^q<2r_t$ this gives $M/(32A_{\rm loc})<\rho_m<r_t2^{-2-(5/8-\varepsilon)h_b/C_0}$, that is,
$$r_t\sup_{B_{r_t/2}(x_t)}|\omega^\theta(t)|>M\,2^{(\frac5{8C_0}-\delta)h_b-7};$$
the shell count of the mass bound, started at $m$ instead of $0$, gives in the same way $Q_{r_t/2}(x_t)\ge\frac3{1024}(M^2/A_{\rm loc})2^{\lfloor(1/4-\varepsilon)h_b/C_0\rfloor}$. So in this case the exponentially large vorticity is located in the axis-free disc itself, and the scale-invariant quantity $r|\omega^\theta|/M$ exceeds $2^{(\frac5{8C_0}-\delta)h_b-8}$ somewhere in it.
The branch also contains record points much closer to the axis than $L_E(M)$, for which the disc of Proposition 33 contains the axis: the scalar histories of
Limitations 1' have $r_t/\rho_*\to0$ and $p_q\le0$. Away from the axis circulation does control the swirl source $F=\partial_z((u^\theta/r)^2)$ of Proposition 41: if $\mathcal D$ is a meridional disc of radius $R$ in $\{r\ge\varrho\}$, $\varrho>0$, with
solid of revolution $\operatorname{rev}\mathcal D$, then $r|F|=2|u^\theta\partial_zu^\theta|/r\le2Gr^{-2}|\nabla u|$ and Cauchy-Schwarz give
$\iint_{\mathcal D}r|F|\,dr\,dz\le\sqrt2\,GR\varrho^{-5/2}\|\nabla u\|_{L^2(\operatorname{rev}\mathcal D)}$. The local source estimate alone does not bound the total mass of $\mathcal D$. For the part of $\eta$ inherited from a fixed time $s_0$,
that is, the solution $\eta^{old}$ of $D_t\eta^{old}=\Delta_5\eta^{old}$ with $\eta^{old}(s_0)=\eta(s_0)$, the maximum principle gives
$Q_{\mathcal D}^{old}(t)=\iint_{\mathcal D}r|\eta^{old}(t)|\,dr\,dz\le\|\eta(s_0)\|_\infty\iint_{\mathcal D}r\,dr\,dz$; the estimates proved here do not give the required record-scale upper bound for vorticity generated elsewhere and
subsequently carried into the disc by transport and diffusion. Transport and stretching do not create this mass: since $\operatorname{div}_2b=\partial_ru^r+\partial_zu^z=-u^r/r$
in the meridional variables, the equation of $\omega^\theta$ takes the conservative form $\partial_t\omega^\theta+\operatorname{div}_2(b\,\omega^\theta)=\Delta_2\omega^\theta+\partial_r(\omega^\theta/r)+rF$, with
$\operatorname{div}_2$, $\Delta_2$ the operators in $(r,z)$; without viscosity and source the planar Jacobian $J_2$ of the meridional flow satisfies $rJ_2=\text{const}$ along trajectories, and
$\iint_E|\omega^\theta|\,dr\,dz$ is conserved for every material meridional set $E$, while the viscous term $\partial_r(\omega^\theta/r)$ is a drift toward the axis. So, up to the inherited part, the
mass of $\mathcal D$ is produced by the swirl source after $s_0$ and carried into $\mathcal D$. In the scalar histories of Limitations 1' the local source bound, applied to the disc of radius
$\frac34r_t$ about $x_t$ over the last transit time $r_t/(8M)$ and with the dissipation rate of those histories, tends to zero while $c_1(M^2/A)2^{k''}\to\infty$; so production inside that disc during that interval cannot alone account for the mass, which was present earlier or was
imported during the interval (Corollary 56 below gives a precise pointwise alternative in the second case of the localization). The local mass
proxy of those histories changes by $o(1)$ over that time, which is compatible with these possibilities. The global budgets give bounds, but not this one:
the scalar histories of Limitations 1' (both choices of $D$) remain consistent when one adds the proxies $\tau^{-1/2}/(\pi L)$ for the total meridional mass
$Q=\iint|\omega^\theta|\,dr\,dz=(2\pi)^{-1}\|\eta\|_1$, $w/(2\pi)$, with the compression work rate $w=\tau^{-3/2}L^{-1}$ of Limitations 1', for $(2\pi)^{-1}\|F\|_1$, and $2c_1(M^2/A)e^{c_{33}h}$ for $Q_{4\rho_*}(x_t,t)$. Indeed $Q'=(1-2/L)w/(2\pi)$, so the
budget $\|\eta(t_2)\|_1\le\|\eta(t_1)\|_1+\int_{t_1}^{t_2}\|F\|_1$ from the proof of Lemma 9 holds; $Q=o(D^{1/2})$; $\int_{t_a}^{T_*}Q^2dt=1/(\pi^2L(t_a))$ is finite, and with the further proxies
$K/2$ for $\|\omega^\theta\|_1$ and $8/(K\tau L^2)$ for $\int|\omega^\theta|r^{-2}dx$ the identity $(2\pi Q)^2=\frac K2\cdot\frac8{K\tau L^2}$ and the bound of Lemma 9' started at $t_a$ hold when $L(t_a)\ge16/K^2$;
the local proxy is $o(Q)$, below the bound of Proposition 41(c), and at least $c_1(M^2/A)2^{k_*}$; and $Q_{4\rho_*}A/M^2=2c_1e^{c_{33}h}$ grows exponentially in $h$. These identities and
limits were checked by a symbolic computation; no field is constructed. One possible approach is to control the survival and the
transport of vorticity produced by the swirl source; in the second case of the localization above it would suffice to prove an upper bound subexponential in $h_b$ for the total normalized mass $Q_{r_t/2}(x_t)A_{\rm loc}/M^2$
of the axis-free disc (all of its vorticity at time $t$, whatever its origin); since $Q_{r_t/2}(x_t)\le\frac\pi4r_t^2A_{\rm loc}$ and $A_{\rm loc}\le A$, such a bound for $Q_{r_t/2}(x_t)A/M^2$, or for $r_tA_{\rm loc}/M$, would suffice. By Corollary 58 below a bound $2^{o(h_b)}M^2/A$, from a fixed time $s_0$ on, for the inward work weighted by the dual solution of Lemma 40 with datum on the torus about the disc would also suffice.
By Corollary 56 below it would also suffice to prove, at every sufficiently late crossing in the second case of the localization, $|\eta(y,s)|\le N$ for $r(y)\ge r_t/4$ and
$|\eta(y,t')|\le N$ for $r(y)=r_t/4$ and $t'\in[s,t]$, where $N=\frac13Mr_t^{-2}2^{ah_b-7}$, $a=\frac5{8C_0}-\delta$, and $s=\max\{0,t-\lambda_1\}$ with $\lambda_1$ as there. When $s=0$ the first
bound holds at sufficiently late crossings. When $s=t-\lambda_1>0$ it is a bound at the scale-invariant level, $(T_*-s)^{3/2}|\eta(y,s)|\le(T_*-s)^{3/2}N=\frac13\,2^{-41.5}(Mr_t)^{5/2}2^{5ah_b/2}G^{-3}(1+\tau/\lambda_1)^{3/2}$
with $\tau=T_*-t$ (the last factor cannot be dropped without a comparison of $\tau$ and $\lambda_1$); the second is a bound nearer the axis.
Neither the orientation nor the nesting of the vorticity produced by the swirl source limits the number of octaves of a stack. Fix $g(x)=\gamma(|x|)$ in $C_c^\infty(\mathbb R^2)$, independent of $N$, with $0\le\gamma\le1$, $\gamma'\le0$, $\gamma=1$ on $[0,\frac12]$ and $\gamma=0$ on $[1,\infty)$, and put $\beta=\pi^{-1}\int g^2$, so that $\frac14<\beta<1$. Take $R>0$, $A>0$, an integer $N\ge1$ and pure-swirl data $b_0=0$, $u^\theta_0=rq_0$ with $q_0\ge0$,
$q_0^2=\sum_{k=1}^N(A^2/N)\,g(\rho_k^{-1}(r-R,z))^2$, $\rho_k=\rho_{\max}2^{k-N}$ and $\rho_{\max}<R/100$. Then $q_0$ is smooth (on the support of each term with $k<N$ the term $k=N$ equals $A^2/N$, and outside the disc of radius $\rho_{\max}/2$ only the term $k=N$ survives), $q_0\le A$, $|\Gamma_0|\le(R+\rho_{\max})^2A<4R^2A$ and $\|u_0\|_2^2\le CR^3\rho_{\max}^2A^2/N$.
Since $u_0\cdot\nabla u_0=-rq_0^2e_r$ and $\Delta u_0$ is azimuthal, $\partial_tb(0)=\mathbb P(rq_0^2e_r)$ and $\partial_t\omega^\theta(0)=r\partial_z(q_0^2)$: a sum of $N$ concentric dipolar shells, each nonpositive for $z>0$ and nonnegative for $z<0$ (because $\gamma'\le0$), with strict sign where $\gamma\gamma'\ne0$, the $k$th supported in the annulus $\rho_k/2\le|(r-R,z)|\le\rho_k$.
Write $c=RA^2/(2N)$, $a_k$ for the $k$th term of $q_0^2$ and $W_k=\partial_z^2\Delta_2^{-1}(Ra_k)$ for the planar field, $\Delta_2$ the Laplacian in the meridional plane. With $x=(r-R,z)$, on the disc of radius $\rho_k/2$ about $(R,0)$, $\Delta_2^{-1}(Ra_k)$ is $c|x|^2/2$ plus a constant, so $W_k=c$ there; by rotational symmetry the mean of $W_k$ over a disc of radius $\rho$ about $(R,0)$ is half the mean of $Ra_k$, which is $c\beta(\rho_k/\rho)^2$ for $\rho\ge\rho_k$.
The axisymmetric and planar Biot-Savart kernels are compared as follows. Put $h=g^2$. For $x=(r,z)$, $y=(s,\zeta)$, $d=|x-y|$ and $\ell=\sqrt{rs}$, the radial velocity of $\omega^\theta e_\theta$ is $\int H(x,y)\omega^\theta(y)\,ds\,d\zeta$ with $H(x,y)=\frac{s(z-\zeta)}{4\pi}J$, $J=\int_0^{2\pi}\cos\phi\,(d^2+4rs\sin^2(\phi/2))^{-3/2}d\phi$, and the planar kernel is $K_2(x-y)=(z-\zeta)/(2\pi d^2)$. The substitution $v=2\ell\sin(\phi/2)$ on $[0,\pi]$ and symmetry about $\phi=\pi$ give $J=\frac2\ell\int_0^{2\ell}B(v)(d^2+v^2)^{-3/2}dv$ with $B(v)=(1-v^2/(2\ell^2))(1-v^2/(4\ell^2))^{-1/2}$. Since $|B(v)-1|\le v^2/\ell^2$ for $v\le\ell$, $|B(v)-1|\le(1-v^2/(4\ell^2))^{-1/2}+1$ for $\ell\le v<2\ell$ and $\int_0^\infty(d^2+v^2)^{-3/2}dv=d^{-2}$, splitting at $v=\ell$ gives $|J-2/(\ell d^2)|\le2\ell^{-3}(\log(3\ell/d)+\frac{2\pi}3+\frac12)$ when $d\le\ell$. For $r,s\in[0.99R,1.01R]$ and $0<d<R/50$, which covers observation and source points in the disc of radius $\rho_{\max}$ about $(R,0)$, this and $|\sqrt{s/r}-1|\le|s-r|/(1.98R)$ give $|H-K_2|\le1/R$, since $H$ is the sum of $\sqrt{s/r}K_2$ and $\frac{s(z-\zeta)}{4\pi}(J-2/(\ell d^2))$. The field $\mathbb P(ra_ke_r)$ has azimuthal vorticity $r\partial_za_k$, so $[\mathbb P(ra_ke_r)]^r=\int H(x,y)\,s\,\partial_\zeta a_k(y)\,dy$, while $W_k=\int K_2(x-y)\,R\,\partial_\zeta a_k(y)\,dy$; with $|s-R|\le\rho_k$ on the support of $a_k$ and $\int_{B_{\rho_k}}|K_2(x-y)|\,dy\le\rho_k$ this gives $|[\mathbb P(ra_ke_r)]^r-W_k|\le C_gA^2\rho_k/N$ on the disc of radius $\rho_{\max}$, with $C_g=\frac{101}{100}\|\partial_2h\|_1+\|\partial_2h\|_\infty$, and the summed error is at most $4C_gc\rho_{\max}/R$, uniformly in $N$. Hence the mean $U_j$ of $\partial_tu^r(0)$ over the disc of radius $2^{-j}\rho_{\max}$ about $(R,0)$ is
$c\,[\,j+\frac43\beta(1-4^{-(N-j)})\,]+O(c\rho_{\max}/R)$ for $0\le j\le N$: a nested stack of one orientation with $N$ octaves, the planar leading term of the mean rising by $c(1-\beta4^{-(N-j-1)})$, between $c(1-\beta)$ and $c$, from the $j$th to the $(j+1)$st octave, all present in the initial source without transport or rearrangement of meridional vorticity. If moreover $\rho_{\max}/R<(1-\beta)/(16C_g)$, a condition independent of $N$, the means $U_j$ themselves, and not only their planar leading terms, increase: $U_{j+1}-U_j\ge c(1-\beta)-8C_gc\rho_{\max}/R>c(1-\beta)/2$ for $0\le j<N$.
This bears only on the structure of the initial acceleration: for each fixed $N$, $b(t)=t\,\partial_tb(0)+o(t)$, so the meridional velocity is small at small positive times, though the acceleration need not be; $t=0$ is not a velocity first crossing; nothing is said about reaching or sustaining a large-amplitude velocity stack, and the construction decides the toroidal branch in neither direction.
Independently of this construction, the toroidal branch is open.

**Lemma 53 (enstrophy at a speed point off the axis).** Let $u$ be a smooth decaying axisymmetric field with $e=\|\nabla u\|_2^2<\infty$ and $D'=\|\nabla u\|_\infty<\infty$ (Frobenius norm).
At every point $x$ with $r=r(x)>0$ and $U_x=|u(x)|>0$,
$$e\ \ge\ \frac{U_x^2\,r}{64\,[1+\log_+(D'r/U_x)]}.$$

Proof. Let $f=|u|$, so $|\nabla f|\le|\nabla u|$, and let $F(s)$ be the mean of $f$ over the circle $C_s$ of radius $s$ about $(r,z)$ in the meridional half-plane. By the Sobolev inequality,
$\|f\|_6\le4e^{1/2}$. On the meridional annulus $r/4<s<r/2$ the distance to the axis is at least $r/2$, so $\int_{r/4}^{r/2}\oint_{C_s}f^6\,dl\,ds\le\|f\|_6^6/(\pi r)\le4^6e^3/(\pi r)$, and some
$\rho\in[r/4,r/2]$ has $\oint_{C_\rho}f^6dl\le4^7e^3/(\pi r^2)$; by Jensen, $F(\rho)\le(4^8e^3/(2\pi^2r^3))^{1/6}<4(e/r)^{1/2}$. With $\delta=\min(r/8,U_x/(4D'))$, the Lipschitz bound gives $F(\delta)\ge\frac34U_x$. Since
$|F'(s)|\le(2\pi s)^{-1}\oint_{C_s}|\nabla f|\,dl\le(2\pi s)^{-1/2}(\oint_{C_s}|\nabla f|^2dl)^{1/2}$, Cauchy-Schwarz in $s$ and $\iint_{\{d<\rho\}}|\nabla f|^2dr\,dz\le e/(\pi r)$ give
$|F(\rho)-F(\delta)|\le(\log(\rho/\delta)/(2\pi))^{1/2}(e/(\pi r))^{1/2}$. As $\rho/\delta\le\max(4,2D'r/U_x)$,
$\frac34U_x\le(e/r)^{1/2}[4+(\log\max(4,2D'r/U_x)/(2\pi^2))^{1/2}]$, and the square of the bracket is at most $2(16+(\log4+\log_+(D'r/U_x))/(2\pi^2))\le36(1+\log_+(D'r/U_x))$. $\square$

**Corollary 54 (an exponential lower bound in each branch).** In regime (II), let $t$ be a velocity first crossing with $|b(x_t,t)|\ge\frac78M$, and let $C_0$, $\rho_0$, $r_t$, $h_b$, $q$, $p_q$, $c_5$ be as in
Proposition 51. If $h_b>16C_0/5$, then
$$\text{either}\quad H(t)\ge c_5\,\frac{\theta_b^2\,2^{5h_b/(16C_0)}}{L_E(M)}\qquad\text{or}\qquad\|\nabla u(t)\|_2^2\ge\frac{M^3}{6080\,A}\cdot\frac{2^{5h_b/(16C_0)}}{1+\log_+(Dr_t/M)} .$$

Proof. Put $m=\lceil5h_b/(16C_0)\rceil\ge1$. If $p_q\ge m$, Proposition 51 gives the first alternative. Otherwise $\frac{5h_b}{8C_0}-q<m\le\frac{5h_b}{16C_0}+1$, so $q>\frac{5h_b}{16C_0}-1>0$, hence
$q\ge1$ and $r_t>2^{q-1}\rho_0>2^{5h_b/(8C_0)-m-1}\rho_0\ge2^{5h_b/(16C_0)-2}\rho_0=\frac M{64A}2^{5h_b/(16C_0)}$. Apply Lemma 53 at $x_t$: $U_x=|u(x_t,t)|\in[\frac78M,M]$ and $\|\nabla u(t)\|_\infty\le D$, so
$1+\log_+(\|\nabla u(t)\|_\infty r_t/U_x)\le1+\log\frac87+\log_+(Dr_t/M)\le(1+\log\frac87)(1+\log_+(Dr_t/M))$, and
$\|\nabla u(t)\|_2^2\ge\frac{49M^2r_t}{4096(1+\log\frac87)(1+\log_+(Dr_t/M))}\ge\frac{M^2r_t}{95(1+\log_+(Dr_t/M))}$; insert the bound on $r_t$. $\square$

Lemma 53 was re-derived here step by step. Its Lipschitz step uses only the operator norm of $\nabla u$, and incompressibility is not used. Corollary 54 combines it with Proposition 51. The second case of the proof contains the toroidal branch $p_q\le0$ and also the crossings with $1\le p_q<m$. In that case, with $\lambda=5h_b/(16C_0)$, the intermediate bound $\|\nabla u(t)\|_2^2\ge M^2r_t/(95(1+\log_+(Dr_t/M)))$, the monotonicity of $z\mapsto z/(1+\log_+z)$ and $Dr_t/M>(D/(64A))2^\lambda\ge2^\lambda/(64\sqrt2)$ (from $A\le\sqrt2D$) give
$$\|\nabla u(t)\|_2^2\ge\frac{M^3}{D}\cdot\frac{2^\lambda}{6080\sqrt2\,(1+\lambda\log2)},$$
an exponential improvement of the elementary bound $\|\nabla u(t)\|_2^2\ge cM^3/D$ (Sobolev on the ball of radius $M/(2D)$ about $x_t$, on which $|u|\ge\frac38M$). An exponential lower bound at every velocity first crossing already follows from Proposition 14D(a) and the persistence of dyadic means (Limitations 1'): $\|\nabla u(t)\|_2^2\ge c(\Theta_0^3/D)2^{h/\sqrt8}$ with $h=M/\Theta_0$. Corollary 54 differs from it in using the meridional BMO norm $\theta_b\le\Theta_0$, in having $A$ in place of $D$ in its displayed form, and in exhibiting the dependence on the distance $r_t$ of the record point from the axis; its exponent constant $5/(16C_0)$ is not compared with $1/\sqrt8$. These estimates do not yield a contradiction. A single-time lower bound on $\|\nabla u\|_2^2$ is compatible with the energy inequality (the Leray bound $\|\nabla u(t)\|_2^2\ge c(T_*-t)^{-1/2}$ already holds), and the scalar histories of Limitations 1' satisfy the second alternative together with $\int\|\nabla u\|_2^2dt<\infty$: there $\|\nabla u\|_2^2A/M^3$ is of order $\tau^{-1/4}L^{-11/2}$ for the first choice of $D$ and $\tau^{-3/16}L^{-11/2}$ for the second, while $2^{5h_b/(16C_0)}$ is of order $\tau^{-5\alpha\log2/(16C_0)}$, which is smaller under the smallness conditions on $\alpha$ imposed there. This shows only that the disjunction is consistent with the scalar constraints tested; it does not show that either case is realized.

**Lemma 55 (a drift-free pointwise budget for $\eta$ off the axis).** Let $0\le s<t<T_*$, $\lambda=t-s$ and $\rho>0$. For every point $x$ with $r(x)>\rho$,
$$|\eta(x,t)|\le\max\Big\{\sup_{r(y)\ge\rho}|\eta(y,s)|,\ \sup_{s\le t'\le t,\ r(y)=\rho}|\eta(y,t')|\Big\}+2G^2\rho^{-4}\big(\lambda^{1/2}+4\lambda/\rho\big).$$

Proof. Regard functions of $(r,z)$ as functions on $\mathbb R^5=\mathbb R^4\times\mathbb R$ through $r=|y'|$, $z=y_5$, and the meridional velocity as the field $\tilde b(y)=u^r\,y'/|y'|+u^ze_5$. On such functions
$\Delta_5=\partial_r^2+\frac3r\partial_r+\partial_z^2=\Delta+\frac2r\partial_r$, $\tilde b\cdot\nabla_5f=b\cdot\nabla f$ and $|\nabla_5f|^2=f_r^2+f_z^2$. Since $t<T_*$, $u$, $\nabla u$ and $\omega$ are bounded on $\mathbb R^3\times[0,t]$. On $[0,t]$ the lifted fields are smooth off $\{y'=0\}$, and $\tilde b$ is bounded and
globally Lipschitz in $y$, since $|\nabla_5\tilde b|\le C(|\nabla b|+|u^r/r|)$ and $|u^r/r|\le\|\nabla u\|_\infty$. The function $\eta$ solves $\partial_t\eta+b\cdot\nabla\eta=\Delta_5\eta+\partial_z(q^2)$ with $q=u^\theta/r$, and
$\Gamma=ru^\theta$ solves $\partial_t\Gamma+b\cdot\nabla\Gamma=\Delta\Gamma-\frac2r\Gamma_r=\Delta_5\Gamma-\frac4r\Gamma_r$, with $|\Gamma|\le G$ by the circulation maximum principle. Fix $x$, let $W$ be a Brownian motion in $\mathbb R^5$ and let
$dX_u=-\tilde b(X_u,t-u)\,du+\sqrt2\,dW_u$, $X_0=x$. Let $T_\rho$ be the first $u$ with $r(X_u)\le\rho$, $\sigma_R$ the first exit time from the ball of radius $R$ about the origin, $T=\min(\lambda,T_\rho)$ and
$T_R=\min(T,\sigma_R)$. On $[0,T_R]$ the path stays in the compact set $\{\rho\le r,\ |y|\le R\}$, on which all fields are smooth and bounded on $[s,t]$, so Ito's formula applies and the stochastic integrals have mean zero.
For $F_u=\Gamma(X_u,t-u)$ it gives $dF=\frac4r\Gamma_r\,du+\sqrt2\,\nabla_5\Gamma\cdot dW$ (the transport and the diffusion $\Delta_5$ cancel against the equation of $\Gamma$), hence
$$\mathbb E\,F_{T_R}^2-F_0^2=\mathbb E\int_0^{T_R}\Big(2|\nabla\Gamma|^2+\frac{8\Gamma\Gamma_r}r\Big)(X_u,t-u)\,du .$$
With $Y_R=\mathbb E\int_0^{T_R}|\nabla\Gamma|^2du$, $|\Gamma|\le G$, $r\ge\rho$ on $[0,T_R]$ and Cauchy-Schwarz, $2Y_R\le G^2+8G\rho^{-1}\lambda^{1/2}Y_R^{1/2}$, so $Y_R\le G^2+16G^2\lambda/\rho^2$. For $\eta$ the same
formula gives $\eta(x,t)=\mathbb E\,\eta(X_{T_R},t-T_R)+\mathbb E\int_0^{T_R}\partial_z(q^2)(X_u,t-u)\,du$, and since $\partial_z(q^2)=2\Gamma\Gamma_z/r^4$,
$$\Big|\mathbb E\int_0^{T_R}\partial_z(q^2)\,du\Big|\le2G\rho^{-4}\lambda^{1/2}Y_R^{1/2}\le2G^2\rho^{-4}\big(\lambda^{1/2}+4\lambda/\rho\big).$$
As $R\to\infty$, $T_R\to T$ almost surely, and $\eta$ is bounded and continuous on $\{r\ge\rho\}\times[s,t]$ (there $|\eta|\le|\omega|/\rho$), so $\mathbb E\,\eta(X_{T_R},t-T_R)\to\mathbb E\,\eta(X_T,t-T)$. If $T=T_\rho<\lambda$, then
$r(X_T)=\rho$ and $t-T\in(s,t]$; otherwise $T=\lambda$, $r(X_T)\ge\rho$ and $t-T=s$. $\square$

Remark. The following argument, pointed out by an independent referee of this report, gives the bound of Lemma 55 with the smaller source term
$\sqrt2\,G^2\rho^{-4}\lambda^{1/2}(1+8\lambda/\rho^2)^{1/2}\le\sqrt2\,G^2\rho^{-4}\lambda^{1/2}+4G^2\rho^{-5}\lambda$. With $\mathcal P=\partial_t+b\cdot\nabla-\Delta_5$ and $c>0$, the two equations give
$\mathcal P(\pm\eta+c\Gamma^2)=\pm2\Gamma\Gamma_z/r^4-8c\Gamma\Gamma_r/r-2c|\nabla\Gamma|^2$, and completing the squares in $\Gamma_z$ and in $\Gamma_r$ separately gives
$\mathcal P(\pm\eta+c\Gamma^2)\le\Gamma^2(\frac1{2cr^8}+\frac{8c}{r^2})\le K_c:=G^2(\frac1{2c\rho^8}+\frac{8c}{\rho^2})$ on $\{r\ge\rho\}$. Applying Ito's formula to $\pm\eta+c\Gamma^2$ along the stopped paths of the proof (or the maximum principle
on $\{r>\rho\}\times(s,t]$, where all these functions and the drift are bounded) gives $\pm\eta(x,t)\le\pm\eta(x,t)+c\Gamma(x,t)^2\le\max\{\ldots\}+cG^2+K_c\lambda$, with the same maximum as in Lemma 55;
the minimum over $c$ of $cG^2(1+8\lambda/\rho^2)+\lambda G^2/(2c\rho^8)$ is the stated term. In its maximum-principle form this proof needs no stochastic calculus; like the proof above, it uses the
equation of $\Gamma$ and only the bound $|\Gamma|\le G$.

The point of Lemma 55 is that the swirl source enters only through the circulation bound $G$: its integral along the backward paths is controlled by the identity for $\Gamma^2$ along the
same paths, whatever the drift. The pointwise bound $|\partial_z(q^2)|=2|u^\theta\partial_zu^\theta|r^{-2}\le2GDr^{-3}$, inserted in the maximum principle for $\eta$, gives instead the source term
$2G\rho^{-3}\int_s^tD\,dt'\le2GD_*\rho^{-3}\lambda$ with $D_*=\sup_{[s,t]}D$; its coefficient of $\lambda$ exceeds the coefficient $8G^2\rho^{-5}$ of Lemma 55 by the factor $D_*\rho^2/(4G)$, while for
$\lambda$ small compared with $\rho^2$ the pointwise term can be the smaller one. At the radius $\rho=r_t/4$ used below, $D\rho^2/G>M^2/(128GD)\to\infty$ in regime (II), because
$r_t>M/(2A)\ge M/(2\sqrt2D)$ there. Specializing Lemma 55 to $\rho=r_t/4$ gives the backward window used in Corollary 56.

**Corollary 56 (earlier or nearer-axis large values of $\eta$ for the axis-free disc).** In regime (II), let $t$ be a velocity first crossing in the second case of the localization in Remark (ii) after
Corollary 52 (so $0<\delta<1/(4C_0)$, the crossing is late enough, and $r_t\sup_{B_{r_t/2}(x_t)}|\omega^\theta(t)|>M2^{ah_b-7}$ with $a=\frac5{8C_0}-\delta$). Put
$$N=\tfrac13\,Mr_t^{-2}\,2^{ah_b-7},\qquad\lambda_1=2^{-23}\,\frac{Mr_t^3\,2^{ah_b}}{G^2},\qquad s=\max(0,t-\lambda_1),$$
where $0<G<\infty$ ($G=0$ would make the solution swirl-free, hence regular), and assume $Mr_t2^{ah_b}\ge2^{15}G^2$ (true at sufficiently late such crossings, since $r_t>8\rho_0$ there and $M\rho_0=M^2/(16A)\ge M^2/(16\sqrt2D)\to\infty$). Then at least one of the following holds:
(i) (a large earlier value) $|\eta(y,s)|>N$ for some $y$ with $r(y)\ge r_t/4$;
(ii) (large values nearer the axis) $|\eta(y,t')|>N$ for some $t'\in[s,t]$ and some $y$ with $r(y)=r_t/4$; then $r|\omega^\theta|(y,t')>\frac1{16}r_t^2N=\frac1{48}M2^{ah_b-7}$.
At sufficiently late such crossings $N>\|\eta_0\|_\infty$, since $N\ge\frac13\,2^{-7}Mr_t^{-2}$, $M\to\infty$ and $r_t\to0$ (Lemma 28); then (i) forces $s=t-\lambda_1>0$, and
$(T_*-s)^{3/2}|\eta(y,s)|>N\lambda_1^{3/2}=\frac13\,2^{-41.5}(Mr_t)^{5/2}2^{5ah_b/2}G^{-3}$, which tends to infinity.

Proof. On $B_{r_t/2}(x_t)$ one has $r<\frac32r_t$, so $|\eta(t)|=|\omega^\theta(t)|/r$ exceeds $\frac2{3r_t}\cdot\frac M{r_t}2^{ah_b-7}=2N$ at some point $x\in B_{r_t/2}(x_t)$; and $r(x)>r_t/2>\rho:=r_t/4$. Lemma 55 with $\lambda=t-s\le\lambda_1$ bounds
the source term by $512G^2r_t^{-4}(\lambda_1^{1/2}+16\lambda_1/r_t)$. Here $8192\,G^2r_t^{-5}\lambda_1=2^{-10}Mr_t^{-2}2^{ah_b}=\frac38N$, and
$512G^2r_t^{-4}\lambda_1^{1/2}=N\cdot3\cdot2^{4.5}G(Mr_t2^{ah_b})^{-1/2}\le\frac38N$ under the assumption. So the maximum in Lemma 55 exceeds $2N-\frac34N>N$. If $s=0$, (i) would give $\|\eta_0\|_\infty>N$. The last statement uses $T_*-s>\lambda_1$
when $s=t-\lambda_1$. $\square$

So Corollary 56 gives either a value of $|\eta|$ above $N$ at the earlier time $s=t-\lambda_1$ at distance at least $r_t/4$ from the axis, where then
$(T_*-s)^{3/2}|\eta|>N\lambda_1^{3/2}\to\infty$ (a super-self-similar value; the quantity $(T_*-t)^{3/2}\eta$ is invariant under $u\mapsto\kappa u(\kappa x,\kappa^2t)$), or a value of $|\eta|$ above $N$ on the
cylinder $r=r_t/4$ during $[s,t]$, where $r|\omega^\theta|/M$ is then again exponentially large in $h_b$. The parameter $\lambda_1$ is $2^{-23}M^2r_t^22^{ah_b}G^{-2}$ transit times $r_t/M$ and
$2^{-23}Mr_t2^{ah_b}G^{-2}$ diffusion times $r_t^2$, both tending to infinity; the backward window $[s,t]$ has length $\min\{t,\lambda_1\}$, which is $\lambda_1$ whenever $t\ge\lambda_1$, in particular when alternative (i) holds at the late crossings of the last statement of Corollary 56. Both alternatives are statements about values, not about material trajectories: neither identifies the origin
of the vorticity present in the disc at time $t$, diffusion is included, and the axial position of the other point is not restricted. The alternative itself follows from the maximum principle for $\eta$; the content of
Corollary 56 is the length of the window. With the pointwise source bound and the coarser estimate $\int_s^tD\,dt'\le D_*(t-s)$, the maximum principle certifies the alternative for windows of length up to $Nr_t^3/(128GD_*)$, shorter than $\lambda_1$
by the factor $\frac3{512}D_*r_t^2/G>\frac3{4096}M^2/(GD(t))\to\infty$. This sharpens the transit-time statement of Remark (ii). Lemma 55 can be applied again at the point found in (ii): if
$|\eta(x,v)|>2K$ and $r(x)>R/4$, then with cutoff $\rho=R/4$ and window $\min\{3\cdot2^{-16}KR^5G^{-2},\,9\cdot2^{-24}K^2R^8G^{-4}\}$ each part of the source term is at most $\frac38K$, and the same
alternative holds at threshold $K$. Passing from $(R,K)$ to $(R/4,K/2)$ multiplies the guaranteed lower bound for $r|\omega^\theta|/M$ by $2^{-5}$ and the window by at most $2^{-11}$ in each step,
so the iteration does not close the branch: no estimate proved here bounds $(T_*-s)^{3/2}\|\eta(s)\|_\infty$ or $\eta$ at a smaller distance from the axis. In the scalar histories of Limitations 1'
(where $\|\eta\|_\infty$ is not specified) $\lambda_1/\tau=2^{-49/2}G^{-2}\tau^{-1/4}L^{-9/4}2^{ah_b}\to\infty$. Both alternatives need a large gradient at the time $v$ of the value found: since $|\eta|=|\omega^\theta|/r\le\sqrt2\|\nabla u(v)\|_\infty/r$ and $r(y)\ge r_t/4$, they require $\|\nabla u(v)\|_\infty>B:=Nr_t/(4\sqrt2)$, with $v=s$ in (i) and $v=t'$ in (ii), and hence $D(v)>B$ for the historical supremum $D$. In those histories, with $h_b=\alpha L$ and $\gamma=a\alpha\log2$, one has $\lambda_1=2^{-49/2}e^\gamma G^{-2}\tau^{3/4-\gamma}L^{-9/4}\to0$, so at sufficiently late crossings $s=t-\lambda_1>0$ and $T_*-s=\tau+\lambda_1\sim\lambda_1$, and the prescribed bound $D(s)\le(T_*-s)^{-3/2}\log(e/(T_*-s))^{-4}$ applies at $s$ (the histories prescribe $D$ at every sufficiently late time, not only at crossings). Hence $D(s)/B$ is of order $G^3\tau^{1/8+5\gamma/2}L^{-11/8}\to0$ (for the second choice of $D$ with the additional factor $(T_*-s)^{1/16}$). There alternative (i) cannot hold, and alternative (ii) can hold only at times $t'$ with $T_*-t'\le C\tau^{5/6+2\gamma/3}L^{-19/6}$ (with a stronger restriction for the second choice of $D$), a vanishing fraction of the window $\lambda_1$, which is of order $\tau^{3/4-\gamma}$ up to logarithmic factors. These are necessary timing restrictions in the prescribed scalar histories, not statements that (ii) occurs. In general the requirement on $D(s)$ is not a contradiction, since nothing proved here prevents the gradient from being large well before $t$.

**Lemma 57 (the velocity induced by recent production off the axis).** Let $0\le s<t<T_*$, $\lambda=t-s$, $M_*=\sup_{[s,t]}\|u\|_\infty$ and $D_*=\sup_{[s,t]}\|\nabla u\|_\infty$; let $x_t$ be a point with
$r_t=r(x_t)>0$ and axial coordinate $z_t$, and let $0<\varrho\le r_t/2$ and $Z>2M_*\lambda$. Let $\chi=\chi(r)$ be smooth with $0\le\chi\le1$, $\chi=1$ on $\{r\ge\varrho\}$ and $\chi=0$ on $\{r\le\varrho/2\}$, let
$\eta^{out}$ solve $\partial_{t'}\eta^{out}+b\cdot\nabla\eta^{out}=\Delta_5\eta^{out}+\chi F$ on $[s,t]$ with $\eta^{out}(s)=0$, where $F=\partial_z(q^2)$ and $b$ is the meridional velocity of the solution, and let $b^{out}$ be the
Biot-Savart velocity of $\omega^{out}e_\theta$, $\omega^{out}=r\eta^{out}(t)$. Then, with an absolute constant $C$,
$$|b^{out}(x_t)|\le CG\Big[\Big(\frac{D_*\lambda r_t}{\varrho^3}\Big)^{1/2}\Big(\frac{(Z\lambda E_0)^{1/2}}{\varrho^2}\Big)^{1/2}+\frac{(Z\lambda E_0)^{1/2}}{\varrho^2r_t}+\frac{(\lambda E_0)^{1/2}}{\varrho^2Z^{1/2}}\Big]+\mathcal L,$$
$$\mathcal L=CG\,D_*^{1/2}\lambda^{3/4}E_0^{1/4}\varrho^{-5/2}\sum_{j\ge2}(r_t+2^{j-2}Z)^{1/2}(2^jZ)^{1/4}\exp\Big(-\frac{(2^{j-2}Z-M_*\lambda)^2}{8\lambda}\Big).$$
The same bound holds for $\mathcal T[\omega^{out}](x_t)$ whenever $\mathcal T$ is a linear operator whose kernel is the Biot-Savart kernel multiplied by a function with values in $[-1,1]$.

Proof. (a) A kernel bound. The Biot-Savart field $v$ of $\omega e_\theta$, $\omega=\omega(r,z)$, satisfies $|v(x)|\le(4\pi)^{-1}\int|\omega(y)||x-y|^{-2}dy$. For $x=(r,z)$ and $y=(\bar r,\bar z)$ at angle $\phi$,
$|x-y|^2=d^2+4r\bar r\sin^2(\phi/2)$ with $d$ the meridional distance, and $\int_0^{2\pi}(d^2+4r\bar r\sin^2(\phi/2))^{-1}d\phi=2\pi d^{-1}(d^2+4r\bar r)^{-1/2}\le2\pi/(d\bar r)$, because $d^2+4r\bar r\ge(r+\bar r)^2$. Hence
$|v(x)|\le\frac12\iint|\omega|\,d^{-1}d\bar r\,d\bar z$, and the part of $\omega$ in a region $E$ contributes at most $\frac12(2\pi\delta\sup_{E\cap\{d<\delta\}}|\omega|+\delta^{-1}\iint_E|\omega|)$, for every $\delta>0$; the same holds for $\mathcal T$.
(b) Two budgets. Let $I_0=\{|z-z_t|\le Z\}$ and $I_j=\{2^{j-1}Z<|z-z_t|\le2^jZ\}$ for $j\ge1$, let $\eta_j$ be the solution with source $\chi F1_{\{z\in I_j\}}$ and zero data, $\omega_j=r\eta_j(t)$ and
$Q_j=\iint|\omega_j|\,dr\,dz$, so that $\omega^{out}=\sum_j\omega_j$. In the five-dimensional lift of the proof of Lemma 55, $\eta_j(y,t)=\mathbb E\int_0^\lambda(\chi F1_{\{z\in I_j\}})(X^y_u,t-u)\,du$ (the source vanishes near the axis).
Since $|F|=2|u^\theta\partial_zu^\theta|r^{-2}\le2GD_*r^{-3}$, $|\chi F|\le16GD_*\varrho^{-3}$, and every partial sum of the $\eta_j$ is bounded by $16GD_*\lambda\varrho^{-3}$. The $L^1(dx)$ budget of the proof of Lemma 9,
$r|F|\le2Gr^{-2}|\nabla u|$, Cauchy-Schwarz and $\iint_{\{z\in I_j,\,r\ge\varrho/2\}}r^{-5}dr\,dz\le8\cdot2^jZ\varrho^{-4}$ give
$Q_j\le\int_s^t\iint_{\{z\in I_j,\,r\ge\varrho/2\}}r|F|\,dr\,dz\,dt'\le2G(8\cdot2^jZ\varrho^{-4})^{1/2}(\lambda\mathcal E_j/(2\pi))^{1/2}$, where $\mathcal E_j=\int_s^t\int_{\{z\in I_j\}}|\nabla u|^2dx\,dt'$ and $\sum_j\mathcal E_j\le E_0/2$.
(c) The near strips. On the meridional disc of radius $r_t/2$ about $x_t$, $r\le\frac32r_t$, so $|\omega_0+\omega_1|\le a:=24GD_*\lambda r_t\varrho^{-3}$ there. By (a) with $Q=Q_0+Q_1$ and
$\delta=\min\{r_t/2,(Q/(2\pi a))^{1/2}\}$, the field of $\omega_0+\omega_1$ at $x_t$ is at most $(2\pi aQ)^{1/2}+2Q/r_t$; with (b) these are the first two terms.
(d) The far strips, far part. For $j\ge2$ let $E_j=\{|z-z_t|\le2^{j-2}Z,\ r\le r_t+2^{j-2}Z\}$. Outside $E_j$ the meridional distance to $x_t$ exceeds $2^{j-2}Z$, so by (a) that part of $\omega_j$ contributes at most
$2Q_j/(2^jZ)$, and $\sum_{j\ge2}2Q_j/(2^jZ)\le CG\varrho^{-2}(\lambda/Z)^{1/2}\sum_j2^{-j/2}\mathcal E_j^{1/2}\le CG\varrho^{-2}(\lambda E_0/Z)^{1/2}$: the third term.
(e) The far strips, near part. For $y\in E_j$ a backward path contributes to $\eta_j(y,t)$ only if its axial coordinate changes by more than $2^{j-2}Z$ within time $\lambda$; the axial component of the drift of the backward lifted process is $-u^z$, bounded in absolute value
by $M_*$, so this needs $\sup_{u\le\lambda}\sqrt2|W^5_u|\ge2^{j-2}Z-M_*\lambda>0$ ($W^5$ the axial component of $W$), an event of probability at most $4\exp(-(2^{j-2}Z-M_*\lambda)^2/(4\lambda))$. Hence
$|\omega_j|\le(r_t+2^{j-2}Z)L_j$ on $E_j$, with $L_j=64GD_*\lambda\varrho^{-3}\exp(-(2^{j-2}Z-M_*\lambda)^2/(4\lambda))$, and (a) with the optimal $\delta$ bounds this part by $(2\pi(r_t+2^{j-2}Z)L_jQ_j)^{1/2}$; inserting $L_j$ and the
bound for $Q_j$ gives $\mathcal L$. In (c)-(e) only the bound of (a) on the kernel was used, so they apply to $\mathcal T$. $\square$

At a velocity first crossing in the second case of the localization in Remark (ii) after Corollary 52, the meridional velocity at the record point satisfies $|b(x_t,t)-b_{in}|\le(\frac18+\varepsilon)M$, with $\varepsilon=C_0\delta+4C_0/h_b$ as there, where $b_{in}=\mathcal T[\omega^\theta(t)](x_t)$ for an operator of this
kind (its kernel is the Biot-Savart kernel multiplied by $\phi(\cdot/\rho_{j+1})$, and it is supported in $B_{r_t/2}(x_t)$), and $|b_{in}|\ge(\frac34-\varepsilon)M$. Fix $K\ge1$ with $Kr_t/M\le t$ and put $\lambda=Kr_t/M$, $s=t-\lambda$, $\varrho=r_t/4$ and $Z=4Kr_t$, so that $Z>2M_*\lambda$
