($M_*\le M$, $D_*\le D$), and split $\eta=\eta^{old}+\eta^{in}+\eta^{out}$ on $[s,t]$ into the solutions with data $\eta(s)$ and no source, with source $(1-\chi)F$ and zero data, and with source $\chi F$ and zero data. Lemma 57 gives
$|\mathcal T[\omega^{out}](x_t)|\le\beta_K:=CKGE_0^{1/4}D^{1/2}M^{-3/4}r_t^{-1}+CKGE_0^{1/2}M^{-1/2}r_t^{-2}+\mathcal L$, where $\mathcal L\le CK^{3/2}GE_0^{1/4}D^{1/2}M^{-3/4}r_t^{-1}\sum_{j\ge2}2^{3j/4}e^{-4^jKMr_t/32}$
(since $(2^{j-2}Z-M\lambda)^2/(8\lambda)\ge4^jKMr_t/32$) is small compared with the first term, uniformly in $K\ge1$, because $Mr_t\to\infty$ in this case ($r_t>8\rho_0$, $M\rho_0\ge M^2/(16\sqrt2D)$ and $D/M^2\to0$). Hence
$|\mathcal T[r(\eta^{old}+\eta^{in})(t)](x_t)|\ge(\frac34-\varepsilon)M-\beta_K$: up to $\beta_K$, the localized record velocity is carried by the vorticity present at time $t-Kr_t/M$, then transported, stretched and diffused,
and by the vorticity produced in $\{r<r_t/4\}$ during the window. In the scalar histories of Limitations 1' ($r_t=2^{-1/2}\tau^{1/2}L^{-3/4}$, $\theta_b=\Theta_0$) the ratio $\beta_K/\theta_b$ is at most of order
$K(\tau^{1/16}L^{-1/4}+\tau^{1/8}L^{5/2})$ for both choices of $D$ (for the second choice $\tau^{1/16}$ improves to $\tau^{3/32}$), which tends to zero for fixed $K$ and uniformly for $1\le K\le\tau^{-1/16}$ (these windows are admissible, since
$\lambda\le2^{-1/2}\tau^{19/16}L^{-3/4}=o(\tau)$): there the production off the axis during the last $K$ transit times contributes $o(\theta_b)$ to $b_{in}$. This is a pointwise statement about velocity and gives no bound on
vorticity mass; the histories are prescribed scalar functions, and no solution realizing them is constructed. It uses, besides the swirl bound and the localization, only the energy inequality and the records $M$ and $D$, whereas the corresponding mass statement in Remark (ii) uses the dissipation rate of those histories: with
$\int\|\nabla u\|_2^2dt\le E_0/2$ alone its bound is of order $\tau^{-1/8}L^{3/4}$ there, which eventually exceeds the mass $c_1(M^2/A)2^{k''}$ to be accounted for (at most of order $\tau^{-3/32}L^4$ for both choices),
so that this bound alone does not give the mass statement. In general, in this case $r_t\ge(M/(16A))2^{ah_b-2}$ with $a=\frac5{8C_0}-\delta$, and
$A\le\sqrt2D\le\sqrt2M^2$ eventually, so that for fixed $K$ and $a>0$, with $X=h_bM^{1/4}2^{-ah_b}$, $\beta_K/\theta_b\le C_K(X+X^2/h_b)$, and $\beta_K/\theta_b\to0$ provided $X\to0$ (recall $h_b\to\infty$), that is
$a(\log2)h_b-\frac14\log M-\log h_b\to+\infty$; for instance $h_b\ge((4a\log2)^{-1}+\epsilon)\log M$ eventually, for a fixed $\epsilon>0$. No such lower bound for $h_b$ is established here, and the scalar histories, where
$h_b=\alpha L$, do not satisfy it: there $X=\alpha e^{-\gamma}L\tau^{\gamma-3/16}\to\infty$ with $\gamma=a\alpha\log2\le\frac5{32}$, and the conclusion holds because $r_t$ is much larger than its lower bound. Neither part of the decomposition is excluded: the earlier vorticity may have been rearranged, and import from $\{r<r_t/4\}$ within $K$
transit times is not ruled out. The imported mass is, however, bounded by the production near the axis in an axial strip, up to a Gaussian tail. Let $\mathcal R$ and $\mathcal R'$ be the solid tori swept by $B_{r_t/2}(x_t)$ and $B_{3r_t/4}(x_t)$, let $\chi_{\mathcal R}$ be smooth and axisymmetric with $1_{\mathcal R}\le\chi_{\mathcal R}\le1_{\mathcal R'}$ (rotational averaging preserves these bounds; its support stays at distance at least $r_t/4$ from the axis), and let $\psi$ be the dual solution of Lemma 40 on $[s,t]$ with $\psi(t)=\chi_{\mathcal R}$. Then $0\le\psi\le1$, and $\psi(y,t')$ is the expectation of $\chi_{\mathcal R}$ at time $t$ along the diffusion $dR=(u^r-R^{-1})\,dt''+\sqrt2\,dB$, $dZ=u^z\,dt''+\sqrt2\,dW$ started at $y$ at time $t'$ and killed at the axis. Since $(1-\chi)F$ is supported in $\{r<\varrho\}$ with $|(1-\chi)F|\le|F|$, positivity of the propagator and duality give, for every $a>0$,
$$2\pi\iint_{B_{r_t/2}(x_t)}|r\eta^{in}(t)|\,dr\,dz\le\int_s^t\!\!\int_{\{r<\varrho\}}|F|\,\psi\,dx\,dt'\le\int_s^t\!\!\int_{\{r<\varrho,\ |z-z_t|\le Z'\}}|F|\,dx\,dt'+4e^{-a^2/(4\lambda)}\int_s^t\|F\|_1\,dt',\qquad Z'=\tfrac34r_t+M_*\lambda+a,$$
because a path started at $|z-z_t|>Z'$ reaches $\mathcal R'$ within time $\lambda$ only if $\sqrt2W$ moves by more than $a$. No separate flux term through the cylinder $\{r=\varrho\}$ is needed: crossing of that cylinder, by diffusion included, is contained in the propagator. In the scalar histories of Limitations 1', where the production enters only through the proxy $w$ for $\|F\|_1$, which fixes neither its radial nor its axial distribution, take $a=r_t/4$, so that $a^2/(4\lambda)=Mr_t/(64K)$; this tends to infinity for fixed $K\ge1$ and uniformly for $1\le K\le\tau^{-1/16}$, though not for every admissible window ($K=Mr_t$ gives $a^2/(4\lambda)=\frac1{64}$). Evaluating the estimate with the scalar budget $\int_s^t\|F\|_1\le\int_s^tw$ then gives $2\pi Q^{in}\le(1+o(1))\lambda w(t)$, with $\lambda w(t)=2^{-1/2}K\tau^{-1/4}L^{-7/4}$ and $Q^{in}=\iint_{B_{r_t/2}(x_t)}|r\eta^{in}(t)|\,dr\,dz$. This numerical upper bound exceeds the mass $m=c_1(M^2/A)2^{k''}$ to be accounted for (at most of order $\tau^{-3/32}L^4$) already for $K=1$; so it gives no contradiction even if the whole mass of the disc is imported within one transit time, and it gives no lower bound on the actual imported mass. With $S_{str}$ the strip integral and $E_a$ the Gaussian term on the right side, the unweighted estimate excludes $Q^{in}\ge m$ when $S_{str}+E_a<2\pi m$. In the scalar comparison this requires the time-averaged strip production $S_{str}/\lambda$ to be smaller than $w$ by the factor $2\pi m/(\lambda w)$, which is $O(K^{-1}\tau^{5/32}L^{23/4})$; no estimate here gives such a reduction. Small strip production is only one way to obtain small arrival: the $\psi$-weighted bound retains the attenuation along the adjoint diffusion, including killing at the axis, which the unweighted strip estimate discards, and signed cancellation, lost when $F$ is replaced by $|F|$, could reduce the actual contribution further. The pointwise bound $|F|\le2GDr^{-3}$ is an upper envelope, largest near the axis; it does not locate the actual production. The imported vorticity is also bounded pointwise without a Hessian bound. Since $u^\theta$ vanishes on the axis, $|u^\theta|\le rD_*$ and $|\partial_zu^\theta|\le D_*$, so $|F|=2|u^\theta||\partial_zu^\theta|r^{-2}\le2D_*^2r^{-1}$. Let $X_v=(V_v,Z_v)\in\mathbb R^4\times\mathbb R$ solve $dX_v=-\tilde b(X_v,t-v)\,dv+\sqrt2\,dW_v$, $0\le v\le\lambda$, started at a lift of the evaluation point, where $\tilde b(V,z,t')=(u^r(|V|,z,t')V/|V|,\,u^z(|V|,z,t'))$ is the lifted meridional velocity (smooth through $V=0$); the generator is $\Delta_{\mathbb R^5}-\tilde b\cdot\nabla$, and $\eta^{in}(y,t)=\mathbb E_y\int_0^\lambda((1-\chi)F)(|V_v|,Z_v,t-v)\,dv$. For $R=|V|$ one has $dR=(-u^r+3R^{-1})\,dv+\sqrt2\,dB$. Applying Itô's formula to $\sqrt{|V|^2+\epsilon^2}$, which is $1$-Lipschitz with four-dimensional Laplacian at least $3(|V|^2+\epsilon^2)^{-1/2}$, and letting $\epsilon\downarrow0$ gives, also for starting points on the axis, $3\,\mathbb E\int_0^\lambda R^{-1}dv\le\mathbb E|V_\lambda-V_0|+M_*\lambda\le2M_*\lambda+2\sqrt{2\lambda}$, and therefore
$$\|\eta^{in}(t)\|_\infty\le\tfrac43D_*^2\big(M_*\lambda+\sqrt{2\lambda}\big).$$
By the kernel bound in part (a) of the proof of Lemma 57, splitting at meridional distance $\delta$ and optimizing, and since $r<\frac32r_t$ on the disc, $|\mathcal T[r\eta^{in}(t)](x_t)|\le(3\pi r_t\|\eta^{in}(t)\|_\infty Q^{in})^{1/2}$. In the scalar histories, for fixed $K\ge1$, evaluating this with the two numerical upper bounds above gives the leading scale $K\tau^{-9/8}L^{-45/8}$ (with an additional factor $\tau^{1/16}$ for the second choice of $D$); divided by $M$ it diverges, so this test does not exclude import either, and it gives no lower bound on the actual induced velocity.

The following direct consequence of Lemma 40A and the localization records the inward-work budget for the whole disc. The mass upper bound does not require the second case of the localization; in that case the localization supplies a necessary lower bound on the weighted work.

**Corollary 58 (the mass of the disc and the reach-weighted inward work).** Fix $0<\delta<1/(4C_0)$. In regime (II), let $t$ be a sufficiently late velocity first crossing for which the second case of the localization in Remark (ii) after Corollary 52 applies, and let $\mathcal R$, $\mathcal R'$ and $\chi_{\mathcal R}$ be as in the paragraph after Lemma 57. For $0\le s_0<t$ let $\psi$ be the dual solution of Lemma 40 on $[s_0,t]$ with $\psi(t)=\chi_{\mathcal R}$, and put
$$W_\psi(s_0,t)=\int_{s_0}^t\!\!\int\psi\,q^2(u^r)_-\,dx\,dt',\qquad C(s_0)=\|\eta(s_0)\|_\infty+2^{-1/2}\max\{\|\nabla u(s_0)\|_\infty^2,G^2\}.$$
Then
$$2\pi\iint_{B_{r_t/2}(x_t)}|\omega^\theta(t)|\,dr\,dz\le\int\psi(s_0)|\eta(s_0)|\,dx+\frac1{\sqrt2}\Big[\int\psi(s_0)\frac{\Gamma(s_0)^2}{r^3}\,dx+3W_\psi(s_0,t)\Big],$$
the two terms with $s_0$ are together at most $C(s_0)|\mathcal R'|$ with $|\mathcal R'|=\frac98\pi^2r_t^3$, and the same holds with $\frac32\int_{s_0}^t\int\psi|u^\theta||\nabla u|^2$ in place of $3W_\psi(s_0,t)$. Consequently, for fixed $s_0$,
$$W_\psi(s_0,t)\ge\frac{2\sqrt2\pi c_1}3\,\frac{M^2}A\,2^{k''}-\frac{\sqrt2}3\,C(s_0)|\mathcal R'|$$
at every such crossing. Let $t_n\uparrow T_*$ be a sequence of such crossings with $h_b(t_n)\to\infty$ (as holds under (FLAT$_0$) in the setting of that Remark). If one had $W_\psi(s_0,t_n)\le2^{o(h_b)}M_n^2/A_n$, then $Q_{r_t/2}(x_t)A/M^2\le\frac3{2\sqrt2\pi}2^{o(h_b)}+o(1)$ along it, which contradicts the lower bound $c_12^{k''}$ of the localization, since $k''\ge(\frac1{4C_0}-\delta)h_b-6$ and $h_b\to\infty$; so the second case of the localization could not occur along that sequence. The weaker hypothesis $W_\psi(s_0,t_n)=o\big((M_n^2/A_n)2^{k''_n}\big)$ gives the same conclusion.

Proof. Since $|\eta|=|\omega^\theta|/r$ and $dx=2\pi r\,dr\,dz$ for axisymmetric functions, the left side is $\int1_{\mathcal R}|\eta(t)|\,dx\le\int\chi_{\mathcal R}|\eta(t)|\,dx$. Write $\eta(t)=P(t,s_0)\eta(s_0)+Z(t)$, where $Z(t)=\int_{s_0}^tP(t,v)F(v)\,dv$ is the source contribution from $s_0$ (Remark after Theorem 6'). Positivity of $P$ and duality give $\int\chi_{\mathcal R}|P(t,s_0)\eta(s_0)|\,dx\le\int\psi(s_0)|\eta(s_0)|\,dx$, and Lemma 40A with $\chi=\chi_{\mathcal R}$ bounds $\int\chi_{\mathcal R}|Z(t)|\,dx$ by the bracket (and by the bracket with the swirl dissipation term). Since $0\le\psi\le1$ and $\int\psi(s_0)\,dx=\int\chi_{\mathcal R}\,dx\le|\mathcal R'|$, the first term is at most $\|\eta(s_0)\|_\infty|\mathcal R'|$; since $u^\theta$ vanishes on the axis, $\Gamma^2r^{-3}=(u^\theta)^2r^{-1}\le\|\nabla u(s_0)\|_\infty^2r$, which is at most $\|\nabla u(s_0)\|_\infty^2$ for $r\le1$, and $\Gamma^2r^{-3}\le G^2$ for $r\ge1$. The lower bound $Q_{r_t/2}(x_t)\ge c_1(M^2/A)2^{k''}$ is the localization in Remark (ii). For the last statement, $r_t\to0$ (Lemma 28) and $A/M^2\le\sqrt2D/M^2\to0$ in regime (II), so the data terms are $o(1)$ and $o(M^2/A)$; if $W_\psi(s_0,t_n)A_n/M_n^2\le2^{\epsilon_nh_b(t_n)}$ with $\epsilon_n\to0$, then $2^{\epsilon_nh_b-k''}\le2^{6-(1/(4C_0)-\delta-\epsilon_n)h_b}\to0$ because $h_b(t_n)\to\infty$. $\square$

**Remark (signed form of Corollary 58).** In this Remark and the next one, $v=u^\theta$, $H(s)=\int\psi(s)(u^\theta(s))^2r^{-1}dx$, $X'$ and $Y'$ are as in Lemma 40A (not the $v=u^r/r$ and the coupled energy $H$ of this section). The proof of Lemma 40A gives its second inequality with the signed work $S_\psi(s_0,t)=\int_{s_0}^t\!\int\psi q^2(-u^r)\,dx\,dt'$ in place of $\int_{s_0}^t\!\int\psi q^2(u^r)_-$. Indeed, the identity of Lemma 40A integrated over $[s_0,t-\delta]$ gives, after dropping $H(t-\delta)\ge0$ and the radial part of $|\nabla v|^2$, $\int_{s_0}^{t-\delta}(2X'+Y')\,ds\le H(s_0)+3\int_{s_0}^{t-\delta}\!\int\psi q^2(-u^r)$. As $\delta\to0$ the left side increases to its value on $[s_0,t]$, and the right side converges, since $|\psi q^2u^r|\le\psi\|\nabla u(t')\|_\infty^2\|u(t')\|_\infty$ (because $|q|\le\|\nabla u\|_\infty$), $\int\psi(t')\,dx\le|\mathcal R'|$, and $\|u\|_\infty$ and $\|\nabla u\|_\infty$ are bounded on $[s_0,t]$ because $t<T_*$. Hence, with $\chi=\psi(t)$ the final datum as in Lemma 40, $\int\chi|Z(t)|\,dx\le\frac1{\sqrt2}[H(s_0)+3S_\psi(s_0,t)]$, so $S_\psi(s_0,t)\ge-H(s_0)/3$, and Corollary 58, both the lower bound and the conclusion, holds with $S_\psi$ in place of $W_\psi$. Since $S_\psi=W_\psi-\int_{s_0}^t\!\int\psi q^2(u^r)_+$, the outward work is subtracted, and $S_\psi\le W_\psi$; the signed form is a weaker sufficient condition. By the same identity the limit $H(t^-)=\lim_{s\uparrow t}H(s)$ exists and $\int_{s_0}^t\big(2\int\psi r^{-1}|\nabla v|^2\,dx+Y'\big)\,ds=H(s_0)+3S_\psi(s_0,t)-H(t^-)$, so an upper bound for $S_\psi$ bounds the $\psi$-weighted critical swirl dissipation. Conversely, $H(t^-)=\int\chi_{\mathcal R}(u^\theta(t))^2r^{-1}dx\le64G^2r_t^{-3}|\mathcal R'|=72\pi^2G^2$: as $s\uparrow t$, $\psi(s)\to\chi_{\mathcal R}$ pointwise off the axis and $\int\psi(s)\,dx=\int\chi_{\mathcal R}\,dx$, so $\psi(s)\to\chi_{\mathcal R}$ in $L^1$ by Scheffe's lemma, while $(u^\theta)^2r^{-1}\le\min\{\|\nabla u\|_\infty^2r,G^2r^{-3}\}\le\|\nabla u\|_\infty^{3/2}G^{1/2}$ is bounded on $[s_0,t]\times\mathbb R^3$ and converges to its value at time $t$ uniformly in $x$ as $s\uparrow t$ (within distance $\rho$ of the axis both values are at most $\rho\sup_{[s_0,t]}\|\nabla u\|_\infty^2$, and at distance at least $\rho$ the difference is at most $2\rho^{-1}\sup_{[s_0,t]}\|u\|_\infty\,\|u(s)-u(t)\|_\infty$, which tends to $0$ because $u$ is continuous on $[s_0,t]$ with values in $H^2\subset L^\infty$), so that $|H(s)-\int\chi_{\mathcal R}(u^\theta(t))^2r^{-1}dx|$ is at most $|\mathcal R'|$ times the supremum of the difference plus the bound times $\|\psi(s)-\chi_{\mathcal R}\|_{L^1}$; and $(u^\theta)^2r^{-1}=\Gamma^2r^{-3}\le64G^2r_t^{-3}$ on the support of $\chi_{\mathcal R}$. Hence $3S_\psi(s_0,t)$ and the $\psi$-weighted critical swirl dissipation differ by at most $H(s_0)+72\pi^2G^2$, which is bounded for fixed $s_0$ (by the proof of Corollary 58, $H(s_0)\le\max\{\|\nabla u(s_0)\|_\infty^2,G^2\}|\mathcal R'|$), while $M^2/A\ge M^2/(\sqrt2D)\to\infty$ in regime (II); so a bound $2^{o(h_b)}M^2/A$ for either gives a bound of the same form for the other. An illustration of the difference between the two works: the linear fields $u^r=-a(s)r$, $u^z=2a(s)z$, $u^\theta=\Omega(s)r$ with $\Omega'=2a\Omega$ solve the Navier-Stokes equations with the pressure $\frac12(a'-a^2+\Omega^2)r^2-(a'+2a^2)z^2$ and have $\omega^\theta=0$ and $F=0$. If $a\not\equiv0$ is periodic with mean zero and $\Omega(0)\ne0$, then $\Omega$ is periodic and, at each fixed point with $r>0$, $\int q^2(-u^r)\,ds=\frac r4[\Omega^2]$ vanishes over each period while $\int q^2(u^r)_-\,ds$ grows linearly with the number of periods. These identities are exact, but the fields have infinite energy, and they give no bound for the adjoint-weighted works ($S_\psi$ carries the evolving factor $\int\psi r\,dx$); their relevance to the second case of the localization is unproved.

By the probabilistic representation in the paragraph after Lemma 57, $\psi(y,t')=E[\chi_{\mathcal R}(Y_t)1_{\{\zeta>t\}}]$, where $Y=(R,Z)$ is the diffusion of that paragraph started at $y$ at time $t'$ and $\zeta$ is its killing time at the axis: the expected terminal value of the cutoff along the adjoint diffusion; it measures terminal arrival at the torus with survival, not the probability of visiting the torus before $t$, and $W_\psi$ discounts the inward compression work $q^2(u^r)_-$ by this weight. The paths of this diffusion are not fluid trajectories (it has the extra drift $-R^{-1}$ and diffusion), and inward work inside the torus shortly before $t$ has weight close to $1$ although $u^r<0$ there. The vorticity inherited from time $s_0$ contributes through the separate term $\int\psi(s_0)|\eta(s_0)|$; Lemma 40A bounds the contribution of all production after $s_0$, including production later transported into the torus, by the initial swirl term and $3W_\psi/\sqrt2$, and for fixed $s_0$ both initial terms are $O(r_t^3)$. This whole-history budget is not the finite-window alternative between old and imported vorticity used above. Corollary 58 is another application of the adjoint-weighted inward-work estimate of Lemma 40A. Under its additional hypotheses (integrability of $\omega_0^\theta$ and record windows), part (d) of the Remark (the windowed conditions concern a thin cylinder about the axis) obtains GOAL from a bound $O(M_n^2/D_n)$ for the weighted work with a datum in a cylinder of radius $O(D_n/M_n^2)$ about the axis and the window start $s_n$ as initial time; here a bound $2^{o(h_b)}M^2/A$, integrated from a fixed $s_0$ with the datum on the torus, excludes the second case of the localization when $h_b\to\infty$. The normalizing scales differ by the factor $2^{o(h_b)}D/A$; since the weights and the time intervals differ, this numerical comparison does not order the two hypotheses. Corollary 58 gives no bound of this kind for $W_\psi$, and its lower bound for $W_\psi$ is a necessary condition for the second case, not a contradiction. Substituting the inward work rate $(1+L^{-1})w$ of the scalar histories of Limitations 1' into the bound $W_\psi(s_0,t)\le\int_{s_0}^t\int q^2(u^r)_-$, which follows from $\psi\le1$, gives a numerical ceiling asymptotic to $2\tau^{-1/2}L^{-1}$. This ceiling exceeds the required lower bound $(M^2/A)2^{k''}$, which is $O(\tau^{-3/32}L^4)$, by a factor tending to infinity, so it gives no contradiction. The weight does not improve this ceiling by size alone. The integral $\int\psi(\sigma)\,dx=\int\chi_{\mathcal R}\,dx\le|\mathcal R'|$ is conserved for $s_0\le\sigma\le t$ (since $P(t,\sigma)1=1$), but $\psi(\sigma)$ need not vanish for $r<G/S$ when $\sigma<t$, and there only $|q|\le\min\{D,S/r\}$ is available ($|q|\le S^2/G$ holds only for $r\ge G/S$; $|q|\le D$ because $u^\theta$ vanishes on the axis). With $(u^r)_-\le M$ this gives $W_\psi(s_0,t)\le|\mathcal R'|\int_{s_0}^tD(\sigma)^2M(\sigma)\,d\sigma$, which in those histories is of order $\tau^{-5/4}L^{-41/4}$ for $D=M^2/L^4$ and $\tau^{-9/8}L^{-41/4}$ for $D=\tau^{1/16}M^2/L^4$, larger than the unweighted ceiling. Away from the axis the circulation bound gives a smaller bound, which still fails by a power in those histories. For $r\ge r_t/4$ one has $|q|=|\Gamma|r^{-2}\le16G/r_t^2$; with $(u^r)_-\le\|u(\sigma)\|_\infty\le M$ for $\sigma\le t$ and $\int\psi(\sigma)\,dx\le|\mathcal R'|$, the part of $W_\psi(s_0,t)$ over $\{r\ge r_t/4\}$ is at most $288\pi^2G^2r_t^{-1}\int_{s_0}^t\|u(\sigma)\|_\infty\,d\sigma$, and this off-axis contribution over the last window $[\max\{s_0,t-r_t^2\},t]$ is at most $288\pi^2G^2Mr_t$. Nothing here bounds the contribution of $\{r<r_t/4\}$ in general; item (v) of the next Remark bounds it over a window on which the radial velocity is nonpositive in the cylinder about the axis specified there. In the scalar histories $Mr_t=2^{-1/2}\tau^{-1/4}L^{-3/4}$, which exceeds the required lower-bound threshold, a fixed positive multiple of $(M^2/A)2^{k''}$, by a factor tending to infinity, since that threshold is $O(\tau^{-3/32}L^4)$. In general the scale $Mr_t$ lies above both thresholds of Corollary 58: in the second case of the localization, with $\bar q$ the octave index denoted $q$ in Proposition 51 (here $q=u^\theta/r$), $r_t>2^{\bar q-1}\rho_0$ with $\rho_0=M/(16A)$, $\bar q\ge\frac{5h_b}{8C_0}-\delta h_b-1$ and $k''\le\bar q-4$, so $c_1(M^2/A)2^{k''}=\frac3{32}M\rho_{k''}<\frac3{256}Mr_t$ and $M^2/A<2^{5-\bar q}Mr_t\le2^{6-(5/(8C_0)-\delta)h_b}Mr_t$. Thus a bound $W_\psi\le2^{o(h_b)}M^2/A$ is smaller than $Mr_t$ by a factor $2^{-ch_b}$ for every $c<\frac5{8C_0}-\delta$ once $h_b$ is large, and the off-axis estimate above does not give such a factor. By Green's theorem in the meridional half-plane, $|\iint_{B_{r_t/2}(x_t)}\omega^\theta\,dr\,dz|$ is the modulus of the circulation of $(u^r,u^z)$ around the boundary circle, at most $\pi r_tM$; this bounds the signed planar mass of the disc, not $Q_{r_t/2}(x_t)$, so $Mr_t$ is an upper scale for the signed mass and not a lower bound for the work. Finally, an estimate of $\int_{\{r\ge r_t/4\}}\psi(\sigma)r^{-4}\,dx$ that uses only a spatially uniform bound for $\psi(\sigma)$ and the value of $\int\psi(\sigma)\,dx$ cannot improve on $(r_t/4)^{-4}\int\psi(\sigma)\,dx$, because $\{r_t/4\le r\le r_t/4+\epsilon\}$ has infinite volume for every $\epsilon>0$; a spatially informative bound for $\psi$ would be needed. In the scalar histories also $r_tS/G=2^{-1/2}c_SL^{1/4}/G$, so the radius $G/S$ (the largest at which $|u^\theta|$ can reach $S$) and the record radius differ only by a power of $L$. The scalar histories do not determine $W_\psi$: they specify neither the spatial distribution of the inward work nor its weight of adjoint arrival at the torus.

**Remark (what the scalar histories leave open for the signed form).** Nine elementary facts restrict the geometry in which $S_\psi$ could exceed $2^{o(h_b)}M^2/A$; none of them decides it. (i) Since $|u^\theta|\le\min\{Dr,S,G/r\}$ with $D=\|\nabla u\|_\infty$, $S=\|u^\theta\|_\infty$ and $S^2\le GD$, integration in $r$ with splits at $S/D$ and $G/S$ gives, for every axial interval $I$ of length $\ell$, $\int_{\{z\in I\}}(u^\theta)^2r^{-1}dx\le4\pi GS\ell$, $\int_{\{z\in I\}}(u^\theta)^2r^{-3}dx\le4\pi DS\ell$, $\int_{\{z\in I\}}q^2|u^r|\,dx\le2\pi MS^2(1+\log(GD/S^2))\ell$, and $\int_{\{r\ge\rho,\,z\in I\}}(u^\theta)^2r^{-3}dx\le\frac{2\pi}3G^2\ell\rho^{-3}$. In the scalar histories of Limitations 1' the swirl energy $\Sigma'\ge\tau^{-1/2}L^{-1}$, the quantity $Y=\int(u^\theta)^2r^{-3}dx$ and the inward work rate $(1+L^{-1})w$ are therefore spread over axial intervals of total length at least $(4\pi Gc_SL^2)^{-1}$, $\tau^{1/2}L^2/(8\pi c_S)$ and $\tau^{1/4}/(2\pi c_S^2L^3(1+\log(GD/S^2)))$ respectively, all much longer than $\tau^{1/2}$; and a region $\{r\ge c\tau^{1/2},\ |z-z_t|\le C\tau^{1/2}/2\}$ carries at most the fraction $\frac{4\pi}3CG^2c^{-3}\tau^{1/2}L$ of $Y$ and, since $q^2|u^r|\le G^2Mr^{-4}$, at most the fraction $\pi CG^2c^{-2}\tau^{1/4}L$ of $w$. So, for fixed $c,C>0$, at each time only a vanishing fraction of the inward work is done in such a region, that is, within parabolic distance of the record at radii at least comparable to $(\Sigma'/Y)^{1/2}$; this radius is the root of the $Y$-weighted mean of $r^2$ and lies between $\sqrt2\,\tau^{1/2}$ and $\sqrt{10}\,\tau^{1/2}$, and the $Y$-weighted mean of $r(-u^r)$ is $w/Y=2$. (ii) By incompressibility and regularity at the axis, $ru^r(r,z)=-\int_0^r\varrho\,\partial_zu^z(\varrho,z)\,d\varrho$, so $|\int_Iu^r(r,z)\,dz|\le Mr$ for every interval $I$; hence an interval $I$ with $|\rho u^r(\rho,z)|\ge c>0$ for all $z\in I$ (on which $u^r(\rho,\cdot)$ is one-signed by continuity) has length at most $M\rho^2/c$. This limits the axial extent of a radial barrier at radius $\rho$; it does not by itself show that the adjoint paths pass around one. (iii) For all constants $(a,g)\ne(0,0)$ the fields $u^r=-a/r$, $u^\theta=g/r$, $u^z=0$ with pressure $-(a^2+g^2)/(2r^2)$ solve the stationary Navier-Stokes equations off the axis, with $\partial_z(q^2)=0$ (the velocity is the gradient of the multivalued harmonic function $-a\log r+g\theta$, so it is divergence free, its vector Laplacian vanishes, and $(u\cdot\nabla)u=\nabla\frac12|u|^2=-(a^2+g^2)r^{-3}e_r$ is balanced by the pressure). These fields have infinite energy and are singular on the axis, so they realize nothing in the present class; they show only that the stationary equations alone, on an annulus, permit inflow at any radial Reynolds number with any circulation and $F=0$. They do not exclude arguments that use further properties of the solution. (iv) With $\psi=r^2g$ as in Lemma 40 and $\psi(t)=\chi_{\mathcal R}$, for $s_0\le s<t$, $\frac d{ds}\int\psi r^{-2}dx=-2\int\psi u^rr^{-3}dx+4\pi\int_{\mathbb R}g(0,z,s)\,dz$ (the second term is the flux at the axis: the radial part of the operator integrates against $r^{-2}dx$ to $-2\pi\int\lim_{r\to0}r^{-1}\partial_r\psi\,dz=-4\pi\int g(0,z,s)\,dz$), and $g\ge0$. Since the support of $\chi_{\mathcal R}$ lies in $\{r\ge r_t/4\}$, integration from $s$ to $t$ gives $\int_{\{r<\rho\}}\psi(s)\,dx\le\rho^2\int\psi(s)r^{-2}dx\le\big[16(\rho/r_t)^2+2\rho^2(\int\chi_{\mathcal R}dx)^{-1}\int_s^t\!\int\psi u^rr^{-3}\,dx\,d\sigma\big]\int\chi_{\mathcal R}dx$: dual mass in $\{r<\rho\}$ exceeding $16(\rho/r_t)^2\int\chi_{\mathcal R}dx$ requires $\int_s^t\!\int\psi u^rr^{-3}\,dx\,d\sigma>0$. (v) Let $M=M(t)$ and $0<\lambda\le t-s_0$ with $M\lambda\ge\frac32r_t$, let $z_t$ be the height of the record point, and suppose that $u^r\le0$ on $\mathcal C=\{r<r_t/4,\ |z-z_t|<4M\lambda\}\times[t-\lambda,t]$. Then, on $\{r<r_t/4\}\times[t-\lambda,t]$, $\psi\le16(r/r_t)^2+2e^{-M^2\lambda/16}$, and $\psi\le2e^{-M^2\lambda/16}$ where $|z-z_t|\ge2M\lambda$. On functions of $(r,z,\sigma)$ the equation of $\psi$ is $\partial_\sigma\psi+u^r\partial_r\psi+u^z\partial_z\psi+\partial_{rr}\psi-r^{-1}\partial_r\psi+\partial_{zz}\psi=0$. Since $\partial_{rr}-r^{-1}\partial_r$ annihilates $r^2$, the operator applied to $h=16r^2/r_t^2$ gives $32ru^r/r_t^2\le0$ on $\mathcal C$; with $Z=4M\lambda$, $\kappa=M/2$ and $\beta=\kappa^2+\kappa M$, the operator applied to $w=e^{\beta(t-\sigma)-\kappa Z}(e^{\kappa(z-z_t)}+e^{-\kappa(z-z_t)})$ gives $-\kappa e^{\beta(t-\sigma)-\kappa Z}[(M-u^z)e^{\kappa(z-z_t)}+(M+u^z)e^{-\kappa(z-z_t)}]\le0$, because $|u^z|\le M$. For $\delta>0$ the function $h+w+\delta^2\sup_{[t-\lambda,t]}\|g\|_\infty$ ($g$ is bounded there, as in the Remark on compression at small radial Reynolds number) is at least $\psi$ on $\{r=r_t/4\}$, where $h=1$, on $\{|z-z_t|=Z\}$, where $w\ge1$, on $\{r=\delta\}$, where $\psi=r^2g$, and at $\sigma=t$, where $\psi=\chi_{\mathcal R}=0$ for $r<r_t/4$. The maximum principle on $\mathcal C\cap\{r>\delta\}$ and $\delta\to0$ give $\psi\le h+w$ on $\mathcal C$, and $w\le2e^{-M^2\lambda/4}$ there when $|z-z_t|\le2M\lambda$. When $|z-z_t|\ge2M\lambda$, a path of the diffusion of the paragraph after Lemma 57 that ends in the support of $\chi_{\mathcal R}$ at time $t$ needs an axial Brownian displacement $\sqrt2|W_t-W_\sigma|\ge2M\lambda-\frac34r_t-M\lambda\ge\frac12M\lambda$, so $\psi\le2e^{-M^2\lambda/16}$. Split $\{r<r_t/4\}$ at $|z-z_t|=4M\lambda$. Outside $I=(z_t-4M\lambda,z_t+4M\lambda)$ only the tail bound for $\psi$ is used; on $I$ one has $u^r\le0$, hence $\int_I|u^r|\,dz\le Mr$ by (ii). With $|u^\theta|\le G/r$, the part of $W_\psi(t-\lambda,t)$ over $\{r<r_t/4\}$ is therefore at most $16r_t^{-2}\lambda\int_0^{r_t/4}2\pi r\cdot G^2r^{-2}\cdot Mr\,dr+2e^{-M^2\lambda/16}\int_{t-\lambda}^t\!\int q^2|u^r|\,dx\,d\sigma=8\pi G^2M\lambda/r_t+2e^{-M^2\lambda/16}\int_{t-\lambda}^t\!\int q^2|u^r|\,dx\,d\sigma$. The part over $\{r\ge r_t/4\}$ is at most $288\pi^2G^2M\lambda/r_t$ by the paragraph before this Remark. So when the radial velocity is nonpositive on that cylinder, the work of the window is bounded above by $(8\pi+288\pi^2)G^2M\lambda/r_t$ plus the tail term; the work over $[s_0,t-\lambda]$ is not bounded by this argument, because $\psi(t-\lambda)$ need not vanish in the inner cylinder. In the scalar histories with $\lambda=\tau$ one has $M\tau=\tau^{1/4}$, much larger than $r_t$, and $M^2\tau=\tau^{-1/2}$, so the tail term is negligible; the numerical ceiling is of order $G^2\tau^{-1/4}L^{3/4}$, whereas the required lower-bound threshold is a fixed positive multiple of $(M^2/A)2^{k''}$ and is $O(\tau^{-3/32}L^4)$; the ceiling therefore exceeds that threshold by a factor tending to infinity and gives no contradiction. So if the contribution of $\{r<r_t/4\}$ to $W_\psi(t-\lambda,t)$ exceeds $8\pi G^2M\lambda/r_t+2e^{-M^2\lambda/16}\int_{t-\lambda}^t\!\int q^2|u^r|\,dx\,d\sigma$, then $u^r>0$ somewhere in $\mathcal C$, in agreement with (iv); in the scalar histories with $\lambda=\tau$ the tail term is negligible. (vi) Over the whole history, the part of the $\psi$-weighted critical swirl dissipation at distance at least $r_t/4$ from the axis is bounded by the circulation and the time integral of the squared swirl maximum. The circulation solves $\partial_s\Gamma+b\cdot\nabla\Gamma=L_0\Gamma$ with $L_0=\partial_{rr}-r^{-1}\partial_r+\partial_{zz}$, and $\psi$ solves $\partial_s\psi+b\cdot\nabla\psi+L_0\psi=0$. Hence $f=\Gamma^2$ satisfies $\partial_sf+b\cdot\nabla f=L_0f-2|\nabla\Gamma|^2$, and $\psi L_0f-fL_0\psi=\operatorname{div}(\psi\nabla f-f\nabla\psi+2f\psi r^{-1}e_r)-4\psi r^{-1}\partial_rf$, so that, pointwise for $s<t$ and $r>0$, $\partial_s(\psi\Gamma^2)+\operatorname{div}V=-2\psi|\nabla\Gamma|^2-8\psi\Gamma\partial_r\Gamma\,r^{-1}$ with $V=b\psi f-\psi\nabla f+f\nabla\psi-2f\psi r^{-1}e_r$. Since $\Gamma=O(r^2)$ and $\psi=r^2g$, $V=O(r^5)$ near the axis, and the integration in $x$ and over $[s_0,t-\delta]$ is justified as in the proof of Lemma 40A. Dropping $\int\psi(t-\delta)\Gamma(t-\delta)^2dx\ge0$, using $8|\Gamma\partial_r\Gamma|r^{-1}\le|\partial_r\Gamma|^2+16(u^\theta)^2$, $|\Gamma|\le G$ and $\int\psi(\sigma)\,dx=\int\chi_{\mathcal R}\,dx$, and letting $\delta\to0$, one obtains $\int_{s_0}^t\!\int\psi|\nabla\Gamma|^2\,dx\,d\sigma\le\big(G^2+16I\big)\int\chi_{\mathcal R}\,dx$ with $I=\int_{s_0}^t\|u^\theta(\sigma)\|_\infty^2\,d\sigma$. With $v=u^\theta=\Gamma/r$ one has $|\nabla v|^2\le2r^{-2}|\nabla\Gamma|^2+2v^2r^{-2}$, so on $\{r\ge r_t/4\}$ the integrand $2r^{-1}|\nabla v|^2+v^2r^{-3}$ of that dissipation is at most $64r_t^{-3}(4|\nabla\Gamma|^2+5v^2)$; since $\int\!\int\psi v^2\le I\int\chi_{\mathcal R}\,dx$ and $\int\chi_{\mathcal R}\,dx\le|\mathcal R'|=\frac98\pi^2r_t^3$, the part of the dissipation over $\{r\ge r_t/4\}$ is at most $288\pi^2G^2+4968\pi^2I$. By the identity in the Remark on the signed form of Corollary 58 and $H(s_0)\ge0$, $H(t^-)\le72\pi^2G^2$, therefore $3S_\psi(s_0,t)\le\mathcal D_{\rm in}+360\pi^2G^2+4968\pi^2I$, where $\mathcal D_{\rm in}=\int_{s_0}^t\!\int_{\{r<r_t/4\}}\psi(2r^{-1}|\nabla v|^2+v^2r^{-3})\,dx\,d\sigma$. The bound is invariant under the scaling of the equations, but nothing proved here bounds $I$ by $2^{o(h_b)}M^2/A$ in general. In the scalar histories $I=O(L^3)$ (it is at most $\frac13c_S^2L^3$ plus a fixed contribution of the times before the interval on which the histories are prescribed), so the part over $\{r\ge r_t/4\}$ is $O(G^2+c_S^2L^3)$, which is $o(M^2/A)$ there; so in those histories a bound $2^{o(h_b)}M^2/A$ for $\mathcal D_{\rm in}$ gives a bound of the same form for $S_\psi$, and the open part is the $\psi$-weighted swirl dissipation at distance less than $r_t/4$ from the axis. On any part of $\{r<r_t/4\}\times[s_0,t]$ where $\psi\le16(r/r_t)^2$ (by (v) this holds up to the tail $2e^{-M^2\lambda/16}$ on the window of (v) when its hypothesis holds), one has $\psi(2r^{-1}|\nabla v|^2+v^2r^{-3})\le8r_t^{-1}|\nabla u|^2$, so the energy inequality bounds the corresponding part of $\mathcal D_{\rm in}$ only by $4E_0/r_t$, which in the scalar histories is of order $\tau^{-1/2}L^{3/4}$ and does not decide it. The integration by parts $\int|\nabla v|^2r^{-1}dx=X-2Y$ (from $\partial_r\Gamma=v+r\partial_rv$, with the cutoff argument of the proof of Lemma 29 and $v=O(r)$ at the axis) shows that the unweighted density $2r^{-1}|\nabla v|^2+v^2r^{-3}$ integrates over all of space to $2X-3Y=2\mathcal K$, with $\mathcal K$ as in Lemma 29(a); with $0\le\psi\le1$ this gives $\mathcal D_{\rm in}\le2\int_{s_0}^t\mathcal K\,d\sigma$. In the scalar histories ($\mathcal K=w$) this ceiling is asymptotic to $4\tau^{-1/2}L^{-1}$ and exceeds the required threshold $O(\tau^{-3/32}L^4)$; it gives no lower bound for $\mathcal D_{\rm in}$, and it leaves open how much of the inner dissipation lies where $\psi$ is appreciable. (vii) The identity in (vi) also bounds the $\psi$-weighted source away from the axis. Since $-2|\nabla\Gamma|^2-8\Gamma\partial_r\Gamma\,r^{-1}=-2(\partial_z\Gamma)^2-2(\partial_r\Gamma+2u^\theta)^2+8(u^\theta)^2$, the same integration gives $\int_{s_0}^t\!\int\psi(\partial_z\Gamma)^2\,dx\,d\sigma\le(\frac12G^2+4I)\int\chi_{\mathcal R}\,dx$. Since $\partial_z(q^2)=2u^\theta\,\partial_z\Gamma\,r^{-3}$, the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and $\int_{s_0}^t\!\int\psi(u^\theta)^2\,dx\,d\sigma\le I\int\chi_{\mathcal R}\,dx$ give, for every $\rho>0$,
$$\int_{s_0}^t\!\!\int_{\{r\ge\rho\}}\psi|\partial_z(q^2)|\,dx\,d\sigma\le\sqrt2\,\rho^{-3}\big(I(G^2+8I)\big)^{1/2}\int\chi_{\mathcal R}\,dx\le\frac{9\sqrt2}8\pi^2\Big(\frac{r_t}\rho\Big)^3\big(I(G^2+8I)\big)^{1/2}.$$
In the scalar histories $I=O(L^3)$ and $M^2/A\ge L^4$ (with equality for the first choice $D=A=M^2/L^4$ in Limitations 1', and $M^2/A=\tau^{-1/16}L^4$ for the second choice $D=A=\tau^{1/16}M^2/L^4$), so for every fixed $c>0$ the part of the dual-weighted source $P_\psi(s_0,t)$ of How to resume (a) over $\{r\ge cr_tL^{-1/3}\}$ is $O(M^2/A)$, and there the source condition of How to resume (a) can fail only through the part at distance less than $cr_tL^{-1/3}$ from the axis. That part is not decided by the constraints used so far. Translate the axial coordinate so that $z_t=0$, and take $b=0$ and the corresponding dual solution $\psi$, put $\lambda=\kappa_1\tau^{5/8}L^{-3/16}$ and $a=\kappa_2(\lambda Gr_t/S)^{1/3}$ with a fixed large $\kappa_1$ and a fixed small $\kappa_2$, and take the swirl $u^\theta=S\,\phi_1(r/a)\,\zeta(z/r_t)\,\beta((t-s)/r_t^2)\,(2+\sin(z/\lambda))/3$, where $\phi_1$, $\zeta$, $\beta$ are fixed smooth cutoffs supported in $(1,2)$, $(-1,1)$ and $(1,2)$. Then $a$ is of order $\tau^{13/24}L^{-31/48}$, much smaller than $r_tL^{-1/3}$, and $\lambda/a\to0$. This kinematic history is not a solution of the circulation equation and does not realize the scalar histories (its meridional velocity vanishes), but it satisfies the conservation of the dual mass, the pointwise bounds $|u^\theta|\le\min\{Dr,S,G/r\}$ and $|\nabla u|\le D$, the inequality of (vi) with its own $I$, and the upper bounds $\tau^{-1}L^{-3/2}$, $\frac12\tau^{-2}$, $\frac12\tau^{-3}L^2$ and $\tau^{-1}L^{-7/4}$ given by the prescribed values of $\|\nabla u\|_2^2$, $\|\xi\|_2^2$, $\|\nabla\xi\|_2^2$ and $\|q\|_2^2$, with $\xi=\omega^r/r$ (after fixed factors are adjusted; the bound for $\|\nabla\xi\|_2^2$ fixes $\lambda$, the inequality of (vi) fixes $a$, and the other bounds hold with a power of $\tau$ to spare). Its $\psi$-weighted source, computed from the source density $S^2a^{-2}\lambda^{-1}$, the dual weight $a^2/r_t^2$, the volume $a^2r_t$ and the duration $r_t^2$, is of order $S^2a^2r_t/\lambda$, that is, of order $\tau^{-1/24}L^{7/48}$, much larger than $M^2/A$ for the first choice. For the second choice $D=A=\tau^{1/16}M^2/L^4$, where $M^2/A=\tau^{-1/16}L^4$, this example has source $o(M^2/A)$ and shows nothing. A solution cannot do this: with $b=0$ the work $S_\psi$ vanishes, and Lemma 40A bounds the $\psi$-weighted source by $2^{-1/2}H(s_0)$, which is zero for $s_0$ before the swirl is switched on. So a bound for the part nearer the axis has to use more than (vi), (vii) and these size bounds, for instance the swirl dynamics through the inward work, as Lemma 40A does. (viii) The identity of Lemma 40A localizes to the axis with errors controlled as in (vii). Let $\phi$ be a fixed smooth nonincreasing function on $[0,\infty)$ with $\phi=1$ on $[0,1]$ and $\phi=0$ on $[2,\infty)$, let $c_1=\sup|\phi'|$, $c_2=\sup|\phi''|$, and for $\rho>0$ put $\Phi=\phi(r/\rho)$ and $H_\Phi(s)=\int\psi\Phi(u^\theta)^2r^{-1}dx$. With $v=u^\theta$, the equations of $v$ and $\psi$ give, by the computation in the proof of Lemma 40A with the weight $\Phi$ (one more integration by parts in $r$; the axis flux vanishes as in Lemma 40A, since $v=rq$ and $\psi=r^2g$ make the diffusion boundary term on $\{r=\epsilon\}$ of order $\epsilon^3$ over bounded axial intervals, the additional integration is supported in $\{\rho\le r\le2\rho\}$, $\Phi$ removes radial infinity, and the axial cutoffs are removed as in Lemma 40A), $H_\Phi'(s)+\int\psi\Phi(2r^{-1}|\nabla v|^2+v^2r^{-3})\,dx=\int\psi q^2(-u^r)(3\Phi-r\Phi')\,dx+E_\Phi(s)$, where $E_\Phi=-\int\psi\,(\Phi''v^2+4\Phi'v\partial_rv+\Phi'v^2r^{-1})\,r^{-1}dx$; the drift contributes only the work term, the part $-r\Phi'\ge0$ of its weight coming from the radial transport of $v^2$ across $\{\rho<r<2\rho\}$. On the support of $\Phi'$, $\rho\le r\le2\rho$ and $|\partial_rv|\le\rho^{-1}(|\partial_r\Gamma|+|v|)$, so $|E_\Phi|\le\rho^{-3}\int\psi\big((c_2+5c_1)v^2+4c_1|v||\partial_r\Gamma|\big)dx$, and the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality with (vi) gives $\int_{s_0}^t|E_\Phi|\,d\sigma\le\rho^{-3}\big((c_2+5c_1)I+4c_1(I(G^2+16I))^{1/2}\big)\int\chi_{\mathcal R}\,dx$. Integrating over $[s_0,t-\delta]$, dropping $H_\Phi(t-\delta)\ge0$ and letting $\delta\to0$ as in the Remark on the signed form, and using $|\partial_z(q^2)|\le2^{-1/2}(2r^{-1}|\nabla v|^2+v^2r^{-3})$ as in Lemma 40A on $\{r<\rho\}$ and (vii) on $\{r\ge\rho\}$, one obtains $P_\psi(s_0,t)\le2^{-1/2}\big[H_\Phi(s_0)+W_\Phi(s_0,t)\big]+C_\phi\rho^{-3}\big(I+(I(G^2+16I))^{1/2}\big)\int\chi_{\mathcal R}\,dx$ with $W_\Phi(s_0,t)=\int_{s_0}^t\!\int\psi q^2(-u^r)(3\Phi-r\Phi')\,dx\,d\sigma$ and $C_\phi=2^{-1/2}\max\{c_2+5c_1,4c_1+2\}$ (the signed work converges absolutely as $\delta\to0$, since its integrand is bounded by $(3+2c_1)\psi\|\nabla u\|_\infty^2\|u\|_\infty$ and the dual mass is conserved). The weight $3\Phi-r\Phi'$ lies between $0$ and $3+2c_1$ and vanishes for $r\ge2\rho$, and $H_\Phi(s_0)\le\int\psi(s_0)\Gamma(s_0)^2r^{-3}dx$. The part of $W_\Phi$ with the weight $-r\Phi'$, the $\psi$-weighted radial flux of swirl energy through $\{\rho<r<2\rho\}$, is controlled in the same way: with $w(r)=\int_r^\infty(-\Phi'(a))a^{-3}da$, which is constant near the axis, vanishes for $r\ge2\rho$ and satisfies $0\le w\le\rho^{-3}$, $w'=\Phi'r^{-3}$ and $w''+3w'/r=\Phi''r^{-3}$, the equations of $\Gamma$ and $\psi$ give $\frac d{ds}\int\psi w\Gamma^2dx=\int\psi q^2u^rr\Phi'\,dx-\int\psi\big(2w|\nabla\Gamma|^2+(8w+4rw')v\,\partial_r\Gamma+\Phi''v^2r^{-1}\big)dx$, whose first term is the time density of that part; so by (vi), $|\Gamma|\le G$ and the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality that part is at most $\rho^{-3}\big(3G^2+(32+c_2)I+(8+4c_1)(I(G^2+16I))^{1/2}\big)\int\chi_{\mathcal R}dx$, and the weight $3\Phi-r\Phi'$ in $W_\Phi$ may be replaced by $3\Phi$ at that cost. In the scalar histories, with $\rho=cr_tL^{-1/3}$, the last term is $O(M^2/A)$, and the initial term is negligible, so the source condition of How to resume (a) follows from a bound $2^{o(h_b)}M^2/A$ for the signed inward work $W_\Phi$ inside the cylinder $\{r<2cr_tL^{-1/3}\}$. No signed work over larger radii appears explicitly, but the estimate still uses the global quantity $I$ and the dual weight generated by the whole drift. Subtracting the integrated localized identity from that of Lemma 40A gives the following comparison between the two signed works: $3S_\psi-W_\Phi=(H-H_\Phi)(t^-)-(H-H_\Phi)(s_0)+\int_{s_0}^t\!\int\psi(1-\Phi)(2r^{-1}|\nabla v|^2+v^2r^{-3})\,dx\,d\sigma+\int_{s_0}^tE_\Phi\,d\sigma$, and $2r^{-1}|\nabla v|^2+v^2r^{-3}\le r^{-3}(4|\nabla\Gamma|^2+5v^2)$ with (vi) bounds the dissipation term by $\rho^{-3}(4G^2+69I)\int\chi_{\mathcal R}dx$; with $H(t^-)\le72\pi^2G^2$ from the Remark on the signed form, $3S_\psi-W_\Phi=O(M^2/A)$ in the scalar histories. So there a bound $2^{o(h_b)}M^2/A$ for $W_\Phi$ is equivalent to a bound of the same form for $S_\psi$: the localization identifies the region that carries the condition and does not weaken it, and no bound for $W_\Phi$ is established. Incompressibility does not absorb this work either: with $B=r^{-1}\int_0^ra\,u^z(a,z)\,da$ one has $u^r=-\partial_zB$ and $|B|\le\frac12Mr$, so $W_\Phi=-\int_{s_0}^t\!\int(3\Phi-r\Phi')B(\psi\,\partial_z(q^2)+q^2\partial_z\psi)\,dx\,d\sigma$ and $|W_\Phi|\le(3+2c_1)M\rho\,(P_\psi(s_0,t)+\int_{s_0}^t\!\int_{\{r<2\rho\}}q^2|\partial_z\psi|\,dx\,d\sigma)$, which cannot be absorbed since $M\rho\to\infty$ at $\rho=cr_tL^{-1/3}$, and the conservation of the dual mass gives no bound for $\partial_z\psi$. For the kinematic history of (vii), $W_\Phi=H_\Phi(s_0)=0$, and eventually its swirl support lies inside $\{r<\rho\}$, so $E_\Phi=0$ as well; the localized identity would then force zero localized dissipation if that history satisfied the swirl equation, which recovers the exclusion by Lemma 40A. (ix) A one-sided barrier near the axis bounds the inner part of $P_\psi$ by the outflow rate. Let $k=\sup_{s_0\le s\le t}\|(u^r/r)_+(s)\|_\infty$, which is finite before $T_*$ and at most $\sup_{[s_0,t]}\|\nabla u\|_\infty$, and $N=1-e^{-kr_t^2/32}$ (for $k=0$ read $h=16r^2/r_t^2$ below). The function $h=(1-e^{-kr^2/2})/N$ satisfies $\partial_{rr}h-r^{-1}\partial_rh+u^r\partial_rh=N^{-1}kre^{-kr^2/2}(u^r-kr)\le0$, so it is a time-independent supersolution of the dual equation of Lemma 40 on $\{r<r_t/4\}\times[s_0,t]$; it vanishes on the axis and equals $1$ on $\{r=r_t/4\}$, while $\psi\le1$, $\psi\to0$ at the axis uniformly, and $\psi(t)=\chi_{\mathcal R}=0$ on $\{r<r_t/4\}$. Let $C_g=\sup_{[s_0,t]\times\{r<r_t/4\}}\psi/r^2$, which is finite because $g=\psi/r^2$ solves $\partial_sg+b\cdot\nabla g+\Delta_5g+2(u^r/r)g=0$, with $\Delta_5$ as below, which is the Laplacian in five space dimensions for functions of $(r,z)$; the drift and the potential $2u^r/r$ are bounded on $[s_0,t]$ and the terminal value $\chi_{\mathcal R}/r^2$ is smooth and bounded. On $\{\epsilon<r<r_t/4\}$, where the coefficients are bounded, compare $\psi$ with $h+C_g\epsilon^2$; the maximum principle for bounded functions on this cylinder, which is unbounded in $z$, follows by the usual penalization with $\delta e^{\lambda(t-s)}(1+z^2)$, $\lambda$ large compared with $\sup|u^z|^2$, and $\delta\to0$. Letting $\epsilon\to0$ gives $\psi\le h\le\frac k{2N}r^2\le(\frac k2+16r_t^{-2})r^2$ on $\{r<r_t/4\}\times[s_0,t]$, by $(1-e^{-x})^{-1}\le1+x^{-1}$ (for $k=0$, $\frac k{2N}$ means its limit $16r_t^{-2}$). Since $\partial_z(q^2)=-2q\xi$ with $\xi=-\partial_zu^\theta/r$, $|q|\le\|u^\theta\|_\infty/r$, $\psi^2r^{-2}\le(\frac k2+16r_t^{-2})\psi$ on $\{r<r_t/4\}$ and $\int\psi(s)\,dx=\int\chi_{\mathcal R}dx$, the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality gives $\int_{s_0}^t\!\int_{\{r<r_t/4\}}\psi|\partial_z(q^2)|\,dx\,ds\le2((\frac k2+16r_t^{-2})\int\chi_{\mathcal R}dx)^{1/2}\int_{s_0}^t\|u^\theta(s)\|_\infty\|\xi(s)\|_2\,ds$. In the scalar histories the outflow rate is bounded through the derivative proxies: $\alpha=u^r/r$ solves $\Delta_5\alpha=\partial_z\eta$ with $\Delta_5=\partial_{rr}+3r^{-1}\partial_r+\partial_{zz}$, and the identities $\int\alpha\Delta_5\alpha\,dx=-\|\nabla\alpha\|_2^2-2\pi\int\alpha(0,z)^2dz$ and $\|\Delta_5\alpha\|_2^2=\|\Delta\alpha\|_2^2+8\|r^{-1}\partial_r\alpha\|_2^2+4\pi\int(\partial_z\alpha(0,z))^2dz$ give $\|\nabla\alpha\|_2\le\|\eta\|_2$ and $\|\Delta\alpha\|_2\le\|\nabla\eta\|_2$; so by the inequality $\|f\|_\infty\le C_A\|\nabla f\|_2^{1/2}\|\Delta f\|_2^{1/2}$ for $f\in H^2(\mathbb R^3)$ (Agmon's inequality, $C_A$ absolute; it fails on classes containing nonzero constants), which applies to $\alpha$ because $\alpha(x_h,z)=-\int_0^1a\,\partial_zu^z(ax_h,z)\,da$ lies in $H^2$ for the smooth solution, $k\le C_A\sup_{s_0\le s\le t}(\|\eta(s)\|_2\|\nabla\eta(s)\|_2)^{1/2}=O(\tau^{-5/4}L^{1/2})$. With $\|\xi(s)\|_2=2^{-1/2}(T_*-s)^{-1}$, $\int\chi_{\mathcal R}dx\le\frac98\pi^2r_t^3$ and (vii) at $\rho=r_t/4$ this gives $P_\psi(s_0,t)=O(\tau^{-3/8}L^{1/8})$ on the scalar histories. Every threshold of the second case is a fixed multiple of $(M^2/A)2^{k''}=16M\rho_{k''}<2Mr_t$, and $Mr_t=2^{-1/2}\tau^{-1/4}L^{-3/4}$; so the upper bound $C\tau^{-3/8}L^{1/8}$ exceeds every such threshold by at least a constant multiple of $\tau^{-1/8}L^{7/8}$, and this estimate does not establish the required threshold bound there (it gives no lower bound for $P_\psi$). The barrier depends only on the outflow bound $k$, whereas the integrand defining the work $W_\Phi$ of (viii) is positive only where $u^r<0$, since $3\Phi-r\Phi'\ge0$. These observations alone give no further estimate for $W_\Phi$, because $\psi(s)$ depends on transport, diffusion and killing throughout $[s,t]$. The comparison has an exact form that keeps the actual drift: by It\^o's formula along the diffusion generated by the dual operator, started at $(r,z,s)$ with $r<r_t/4$ and stopped at the axis, at $\{r=r_t/4\}$ and at time $t$, $h(r)-\psi(r,z,s)$ equals the expected integral along the stopped paths of $\partial_rh\,(kr-u^r)\ge0$ plus the expectation of $h-\psi$ at the stopping point, which is nonnegative. A lower bound for this subtraction at the points that carry the inward work would be one route to an improvement; none is proved here. With these constraints the scalar histories still leave open whether the inward work and the swirl dissipation lie where $\psi$ is of the size of the kernel bound or where it is small, for instance, during the last $\tau$ units of time, at axial distance from the record much larger than $M\tau+\tau^{1/2}$, where $\psi$ is exponentially small by the Gaussian tail used in the paragraph after Lemma 57 (the axial displacement of the paths over a time $\tau$ is at most $M\tau$ plus that of $\sqrt2$ times a Brownian motion). For earlier times the relevant age is $t-s$, not $\tau$: for $s<t$ and $a>0$ the same estimate gives $\psi(r,z,s)\le4e^{-a^2/(4(t-s))}$ whenever $|z-z_t|\ge\frac34r_t+M(t)(t-s)+a$. No history of either kind is shown to be realizable, and these calculations neither establish nor refute the sufficient work bound $S_\psi(s_0,t)\le2^{o(h_b)}M^2/A$ proposed in How to resume (a). Possible further inputs include the location and timing of the swirl dissipation relative to the record and the radial velocity along the arrival paths; the estimates above do not control these quantities sufficiently.

**Remark (shell-tested form of Corollary 58).** The bound for $\int\chi_{\mathcal R}|Z(t)|\,dx$ used in Corollary 58 can be replaced by bounds for finitely many signed pairings of $Z(t)$ with smooth kernels. Let $t$, $\mathcal R$, $\chi_{\mathcal R}$, $s_0$, $\psi$ and $Z(t)$ be as in Corollary 58 and its proof, let $j$, $k''$, $\varepsilon$, $B_{core}$ and $b_{in}$ be as in Remark (ii) after Corollary 52, and put $e=b(x_t,t)/|b(x_t,t)|$. The decomposition in the proof of Proposition 33 is that of the Biot-Savart law for $b$, which is generated by $\omega^\theta e_\theta$; so for $k\le j$ there is a smooth vector field $m_k$, obtained from the kernel $\psi_kK$, with $B_k\cdot e=\int\omega^\theta(y)\,m_k(y)\cdot e_\theta(y)\,dy$, $|m_k|\le(4\pi)^{-1}\rho_k^{-2}$, and $m_k=0$ outside $\{\rho_k\le|y-x_t|\le4\rho_k\}$. Put
$$w_k(r,z)=\frac r{2\pi}\int_0^{2\pi}m_k\cdot e_\theta\,d\theta,\qquad\Psi_k(s)=P(t,s)^*w_k,\qquad X(s_0,t)=\max_{k''\le k\le j}\rho_k\Big(\int w_kZ(t)\,dx\Big)_+ .$$
Then: (a) $X(s_0,t)\ge\big(\frac3{256}-\frac\pi8\,r_t^2\|\eta(s_0)\|_\infty M^{-1}\big)(M^2/A)\,2^{k''}$; (b) $X(s_0,t)\le(2\pi)^{-1}\int\chi_{\mathcal R}|Z(t)|\,dx\le(2\pi)^{-1}P_\psi(s_0,t)$, with $P_\psi(s_0,t)=\int_{s_0}^t\!\int\psi|\partial_z(q^2)|\,dx\,ds$ as in How to resume (a); (c) for every bounded function $c=c(r,s)$, $\int w_kZ(t)\,dx=-\int_{s_0}^t\!\int(\partial_z\Psi_k)\,(q^2-c)\,dx\,ds$; (d) with $W=\sum_{k''\le k\le j}w_k$, $B=1+2(j-k'')\log2$ and $Q(s_0,t)=\int_{s_0}^t\|q(s)\|_4^4\,ds$, one has $\|W\|_2^2\le32r_tB$, $\int_{\mathcal R}\eta(t)^2\,dx\ge9M^2/(2048\,r_tB)$, $\frac38M-4\pi r_t^2\|\eta(s_0)\|_\infty\le4(r_tB\,Q(s_0,t))^{1/2}$ and $B\ge1+2\log2\,(\frac{3h_b}{8C_0}-1)$; (e) with $\sigma=\frac32r_t+\int_{s_0}^t\|u(s)\|_\infty\,ds+(10(t-s_0))^{1/2}$, $V_\sigma=(2\pi e\sigma^2/3)^{3/2}$, $\Lambda=\log(V_\sigma/(32\pi^2r_t\rho_{k''}^2))$ and the dual-weighted quartic $Q_\psi(s_0,t)=\int_{s_0}^t\!\int\psi q^4\,dx\,ds\le Q(s_0,t)$, one has $\Lambda>B$ and $\frac38M-4\pi r_t^2\|\eta(s_0)\|_\infty\le(32r_tB(1+8\Lambda)\,Q_\psi(s_0,t))^{1/2}$. Consequently, for fixed $s_0$ and a sequence $t_n$ of crossings as in Corollary 58 with $h_b(t_n)\to\infty$, a bound $X(s_0,t_n)\le2^{o(h_b)}M_n^2/A_n$ excludes the second case of the localization along that sequence: $r_t\to0$ (Lemma 28) and $M\to\infty$, so the correction in (a) tends to zero, and $k''\ge(\frac1{4C_0}-\delta)h_b-6$. By (b), the bounds for $P_\psi$, for $S_\psi$ and for the dual-weighted swirl dissipation in How to resume (a) each imply this bound. No reverse inequality holds for general prescribed sources: fix the propagator, the terminal time and the dual weights $\psi$ and $\Psi_k$, and choose a fixed smooth axisymmetric amplitude $a$ with compact support in $\{r>0\}$ and $C_a=\int_{s_0}^t\!\int\psi a^2\,dx\,ds>0$; for the source $\partial_z(q_N^2)=Na^2\sin(2Nz)+2aa_z\sin^2(Nz)$ of $q_N=a(r,z)\sin(Nz)$ one has $P_\psi/N\to(2/\pi)C_a$, while, writing $Z_N(t)=\int_{s_0}^tP(t,s)\partial_z(q_N^2)(s)\,ds$, (c) gives $\int w_kZ_N(t)\,dx\to-\frac12\int_{s_0}^t\!\int(\partial_z\Psi_k)a^2\,dx\,ds$, so the finitely many scaled pairings $\rho_k\int w_kZ_N(t)\,dx$, and their positive parts, stay bounded independently of $N$; whether this separation occurs along Navier-Stokes histories is not known. The pairings keep the cancellation of $Z(t)$ against the kernels $w_k$ in the single direction $e$; they do not discard every oscillation of the source on scales below $\rho_k$ (an envelope, growing amplitudes, or production at earlier times transported into a configuration seen by the kernels can contribute), and (c) still requires control of the axial derivative of the transported kernel $\Psi_k$. The dual $L^2$ identity of item (v) of the Remark on signed and cancellation-preserving forms of Lemma 40 gives $\int_{s_0}^t\|\partial_z\Psi_k(s)\|_2^2\,ds\le\frac12\|w_k\|_2^2\le4r_t$, which with $c=0$ in (c) is the Cauchy-Schwarz argument behind (d), but no estimate here yields $X(s_0,t_n)\le2^{o(h_b(t_n))}M_n^2/A_n$. The axial derivative also obeys an energy bound in which no Lipschitz constant of $b$ enters: the coefficients of the dual operator $\Delta-\frac2r\partial_r$ do not depend on $z$, so $v=\partial_z\Psi_k$ solves $\partial_sv+b\cdot\nabla v+\Delta v-\frac2r\partial_rv+(\partial_zb)\cdot\nabla\Psi_k=0$; on pairing with $v$ the transport term drops out ($\operatorname{div}b=0$), the radial term gives the axis trace $2\pi\int v(s,0,z)^2dz=0$ ($v=O(r^2)$ at the axis), and $(\partial_zb)\cdot\nabla\Psi_k=\operatorname{div}(\Psi_k\partial_zb)$ is moved onto $v$, so, with $|\Psi_k|\le\|w_k\|_\infty$ (positivity and $P(t,s)^*1\le1$) and the justification of (c), $\|\partial_z\Psi_k(s)\|_2^2+\int_s^t\|\nabla\partial_z\Psi_k\|_2^2\,d\sigma\le\|\partial_zw_k\|_2^2+\|w_k\|_\infty^2\int_s^t\|\partial_zb\|_2^2\,d\sigma\le\|\partial_zw_k\|_2^2+\|w_k\|_\infty^2E_0/2$; since $\int(\partial_z\Psi_k)^4dx=-3\int\Psi_k(\partial_z\Psi_k)^2\partial_z^2\Psi_k\,dx\le3\|w_k\|_\infty(\int(\partial_z\Psi_k)^4dx)^{1/2}\|\partial_z^2\Psi_k\|_2$, this gives $\int_{s_0}^t\!\int(\partial_z\Psi_k)^4\,dx\,ds\le9\|w_k\|_\infty^2(\|\partial_zw_k\|_2^2+\|w_k\|_\infty^2E_0/2)$, and with $c=0$ in (c), Hölder's inequality and $\int_{s_0}^t\!\int q^2\,dx\,ds\le E_0/2$ (from $(u^\theta/r)^2\le|\nabla u|^2$), $|\int w_kZ(t)\,dx|\le[9\|w_k\|_\infty^2(\|\partial_zw_k\|_2^2+\|w_k\|_\infty^2E_0/2)]^{1/4}(E_0/2)^{1/2}Q(s_0,t)^{1/4}$. This is again a size bound, through $E_0$, the norms of the kernel $w_k$ (which depend on $k$) and the unweighted quartic $Q(s_0,t)$ of (d), and no estimate here makes it of the required order. By (d), for fixed $s_0$ a bound $r_{t_n}B_nQ(s_0,t_n)/M_n^2\to0$ would also exclude the second case along the sequence (the correction term is $o(M_n)$ because $r_t\to0$); the ratio $r_tB\,Q(s_0,t)/M^2$ is invariant under the scaling $u\mapsto\lambda u(\lambda x,\lambda^2t)$ (both $Q$ and $M^2/r_t$ scale like $\lambda^3$, and $j$, $k''$ do not change), so this asks a scale-invariant quantity to tend to zero, and no estimate here gives it. By (e) the same holds with $Q(s_0,t)$ replaced by $2(1+8\Lambda)Q_\psi(s_0,t)$, which localizes the quartic to the dual weight of the torus at the cost of one logarithmic factor ($\Lambda$ is the logarithm of a ratio of volumes, invariant under the same scaling, and $\Lambda>B$); no estimate here gives the resulting bound either.

Proof. Since $\omega^\theta=r\eta$, $dy=r\,d\theta\,dr\,dz$ and $dx=2\pi r\,dr\,dz$ for axisymmetric functions, $B_k\cdot e=\int w_k\eta(t)\,dx$. A horizontal circle of radius $r$ that meets $B_s(x_t)$ with $s<r_t$ does so in an arc of half-angle $\alpha<\pi/2$ (points with $\cos\theta\le0$ are at distance at least $r_t$ from $x_t$) with $r\sin\alpha\le s$, hence of length $2r\alpha\le\pi r\sin\alpha\le\pi s$; with $s=4\rho_k$ this gives $|w_k|\le(2\pi)^{-1}(4\pi\rho_k^2)^{-1}\cdot4\pi\rho_k=(2\pi\rho_k)^{-1}$. The circle through $(r,z)$ meets $B_{4\rho_k}(x_t)$ only if the meridional distance from $(r,z)$ to the record point is at most $4\rho_k$, and $4\rho_k\le4\rho_j\le2^{\bar q-2}\rho_0<r_t/2$ by Remark (ii), with $\bar q$ the octave index denoted $q$ in Proposition 51; so $w_k$ is smooth, vanishes near the axis, is supported in the solid torus swept by the meridional disc of radius $4\rho_k$ about the record point, whose volume is $32\pi^2r_t\rho_k^2$, and $|w_k|\le(2\pi\rho_k)^{-1}\chi_{\mathcal R}$. (a) By Remark (ii), $|b(x_t)-b_{in}|\le(\frac18+\varepsilon)M$ and $|b(x_t)|\ge\frac78M$, so $b_{in}\cdot e\ge(\frac34-\varepsilon)M$; since $|B_{core}|\le M/8$ and $\sum_{k<k''}|B_k|\le k''C_0\theta_b\le(\frac14-\varepsilon)M$, $\sum_{k''\le k\le j}B_k\cdot e\ge\frac38M$. Write $\eta(t)=P(t,s_0)\eta(s_0)+Z(t)$. Since $|P(t,s_0)\eta(s_0)|\le\|\eta(s_0)\|_\infty$, $|\int w_kP(t,s_0)\eta(s_0)\,dx|\le\|\eta(s_0)\|_\infty\|w_k\|_1\le16\pi r_t\rho_k\|\eta(s_0)\|_\infty$, and $\sum_{k''\le k\le j}\rho_k<2\rho_j<r_t/4$; hence $\sum_{k''\le k\le j}(\int w_kZ(t)\,dx)_+\ge\frac38M-4\pi r_t^2\|\eta(s_0)\|_\infty$. The left side is at most $X\sum_{k\ge k''}\rho_k^{-1}\le2X/\rho_{k''}$, and $\rho_{k''}=2^{k''}M/(16A)$. (b) By positivity of $P$, $|Z(t)|\le\int_{s_0}^tP(t,s)|F(s)|\,ds$ with $F=\partial_z(q^2)$, so $\rho_k|\int w_kZ(t)\,dx|\le(2\pi)^{-1}\int\chi_{\mathcal R}|Z(t)|\,dx\le(2\pi)^{-1}P_\psi(s_0,t)$ by duality. (c) By duality, $\int w_kZ(t)\,dx=\int_{s_0}^t\!\int\Psi_kF\,dx\,ds$. For fixed $t<T_*$ the coefficients are smooth and bounded with their derivatives on $[s_0,t]$, and $\Psi_k=r^2g_k$, where $g_k$ is the five-dimensional density of Lemma 40 with the smooth, compactly supported final datum $w_k/r^2$; so $\Psi_k$ and $\partial_z\Psi_k$ are $O(r^2)$ at the axis and $\int_{s_0}^t(\|\Psi_k\|_1+\|\partial_z\Psi_k\|_1)\,ds<\infty$ by standard parabolic estimates, and $q$ is bounded. Integration by parts in $z$ against an axial cutoff of length $\ell$, whose error is at most $C\ell^{-1}\|q\|_\infty^2\int_{s_0}^t\|\Psi_k\|_1\,ds$, gives the formula with $c=0$; since $\int_{\mathbb R}\partial_z\Psi_k\,dz=0$ for almost every $(r,s)$, it holds for every bounded $c(r,s)$. (d) Let $d$ be the meridional distance from $(r,z)$ to the record point. Then $w_k(r,z)\ne0$ only if $d\le4\rho_k$; if $d\le4\rho_j$ and $m$ is the least $k\ge k''$ with $4\rho_k\ge d$, then $|W|\le\sum_{k\ge m}(2\pi\rho_k)^{-1}\le(\pi\rho_m)^{-1}\le g(d)$ with $g(d)=\min\{(\pi\rho_{k''})^{-1},4/(\pi d)\}$, since $\rho_m\ge\rho_{k''}$ and $\rho_m\ge d/4$; and $W=0$ if $d>4\rho_j$, in particular outside $\mathcal R$. The disc $\{d\le4\rho_j\}$ lies in $\{r>r_t/2\}$ and is preserved by the reflection in $r=r_t$, which preserves $g(d)$, so $\|W\|_2^2\le2\pi r_t\iint_{\{d\le4\rho_j\}}g(d)^2\,dr\,dz$, and in polar coordinates of radius $y$ about the record point this is $4\pi^2r_t\big(\int_0^{4\rho_{k''}}\pi^{-2}\rho_{k''}^{-2}y\,dy+\int_{4\rho_{k''}}^{4\rho_j}16\pi^{-2}y^{-1}\,dy\big)=32r_tB$. The proof of (a) gives $\int W\eta(t)\,dx=\sum_{k''\le k\le j}B_k\cdot e\ge\frac38M$ and $|\int WP(t,s_0)\eta(s_0)\,dx|\le4\pi r_t^2\|\eta(s_0)\|_\infty$; Cauchy-Schwarz on $\mathcal R$ gives the second inequality, and Cauchy-Schwarz with $\int WZ(t)\,dx\ge\frac38M-4\pi r_t^2\|\eta(s_0)\|_\infty$ and $\|Z(t)\|_2^2\le\frac12Q(s_0,t)$ (item (v) of the Remark on signed and cancellation-preserving forms of Lemma 40) gives the third. Finally, every shell satisfies $|B_k|\le C_0\theta_b$ (proof of Proposition 33), so $(j-k''+1)C_0\theta_b\ge\frac38M$, that is, $j-k''\ge\frac{3h_b}{8C_0}-1$. (e) Put $U=P(t,\cdot)^*W$, $f=P(t,\cdot)^*(W^2)$, $E_W=\|W\|_2^2\le32r_tB$ and $K_W=(\pi\rho_{k''})^{-1}$, so that $|W|\le K_W$ by the proof of (d). Since $\chi_{\mathcal R}=1$ on the support of $W$, positivity of the dual propagator and the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality give $U^2\le\psi f$, and $f\le K_W^2\psi$. For $r>0$ and $s<t$ the duals $\psi$ and $f$ are positive, and $h_U=U/\psi$ satisfies $h_U^2\le f/\psi\le K_W^2$. For the pairs $(\phi_1,\phi_2)=(U,\psi)$ with $\Phi(a,c)=a^2/c$ and $(\phi_1,\phi_2)=(f,\psi)$ with $\Phi(a,c)=a\log(a/c)$, one has on $[s_0,t-\delta]$ $\frac d{ds}\int\Phi(\phi_1,\phi_2)\,dx=\int D^2\Phi(\phi_1,\phi_2)[\nabla\phi_1,\nabla\phi_2]\,dx$, where the integrand is the Hessian of $\Phi$ evaluated on the pair of gradients, equal to $2\psi|\nabla h_U|^2$ and to $f|\nabla\log(f/\psi)|^2$ respectively. To see this, apply the chain rule on $\{r>\epsilon\}$ with spatial cutoffs: the transport terms combine to $b\cdot\nabla\Phi$, whose integral vanishes; the first-order part of the dual operator $\Delta-\frac2r\partial_r$ obeys the chain rule; the dual operator applied to a function $\phi$ integrates to $4\pi\int\phi(0,z)\,dz$, which vanishes for $\phi=\Phi(\phi_1,\phi_2)=r^2\Phi(\phi_1/r^2,\phi_2/r^2)$ because $\phi_1/r^2$ and $\psi/r^2$ are smooth five-dimensional densities as in Lemma 40 and $\psi/r^2>0$ (strong maximum principle); and $|\Phi(\phi_1,\phi_2)|\le C\psi$, by $U^2\le\psi f$, $f\le K_W^2\psi$ and $x\log x\ge-e^{-1}$, which allows the cutoffs to be removed. Since $\int U^2/\psi\,dx\le\int f\,dx=E_W$ at $s=t-\delta$, the first pair gives $2\int_{s_0}^{t-\delta}\!\int\psi|\nabla h_U|^2\le E_W$; the second gives $\int_{s_0}^{t-\delta}\!\int f|\nabla\log(f/\psi)|^2=\mathcal H_{rel}(t-\delta)-\mathcal H_{rel}(s_0)$ for $\mathcal H_{rel}=\int f\log(f/\psi)\,dx$; and the entropy identity of item (vi) of the Remark on signed and cancellation-preserving forms of Lemma 40, applied to $W^2/K_W^2$, gives $\int_{s_0}^{t-\delta}\!\int f|\nabla\log f|^2=\mathcal H_f(t-\delta)-\mathcal H_f(s_0)$ for $\mathcal H_f=\int f\log f\,dx$. Since $f\le K_W^2\psi\le K_W^2$, $\mathcal H_{rel}(t-\delta)$ and $\mathcal H_f(t-\delta)$ are at most $E_W\log K_W^2$. Nonnegativity of relative entropy gives $\mathcal H_{rel}(s_0)\ge E_W\log(E_W/\int\chi_{\mathcal R}dx)$, and $\int\chi_{\mathcal R}dx\le\frac98\pi^2r_t^3<V_\sigma$. The support of $W^2$ lies in the ball of radius $r_t+4\rho_j<\frac32r_t$ about the axis point $x_0=(0,z_t)$, so the second-moment bound and the Gaussian bound of the same item give $\mathcal H_f(s_0)\ge E_W\log(E_W/V_\sigma)$. Writing $\nabla U=\psi\nabla h_U+h_U\nabla\psi$ and using $h_U^2\le f/\psi$ and $\log\psi=\log f-\log(f/\psi)$, $|\nabla U|^2/\psi\le2\psi|\nabla h_U|^2+4f|\nabla\log f|^2+4f|\nabla\log(f/\psi)|^2$; hence, letting $\delta\to0$, $\mathcal J_U=\int_{s_0}^t\!\int|\nabla U|^2/\psi\,dx\,ds\le E_W+8E_W\log(K_W^2V_\sigma/E_W)$. Here $K_W^2V_\sigma=32r_te^\Lambda$ and $\Lambda-B+1=\log(V_\sigma/(32\pi^2r_t\rho_j^2))>\log(2\pi^{-2}(3\pi e/2)^{3/2})>1$, because $\sigma\ge\frac32r_t$ and $\rho_j<r_t/8$; so $\Lambda>B\ge1$ and $32r_tB<32r_te^{\Lambda-1}$, and since $E\mapsto E\log(32r_te^\Lambda/E)$ increases on $(0,32r_te^{\Lambda-1}]$, $\mathcal J_U\le32r_tB(1+8(\Lambda-\log B))\le32r_tB(1+8\Lambda)$. Finally, by the proof of (d) and by (c) with $c=0$, summed over $k$, $\frac38M-4\pi r_t^2\|\eta(s_0)\|_\infty\le\int WZ(t)\,dx=-\int_{s_0}^t\!\int(\partial_zU)\,q^2\,dx\,ds\le(\mathcal J_U\,Q_\psi(s_0,t))^{1/2}$ by the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality with weight $\psi$. $\square$

**Remark (radial transport required by the dual-weighted quartic).** Let $t$, $\mathcal R$, $\chi_{\mathcal R}$, $s_0$, $\psi$ and $Q_\psi=Q_\psi(s_0,t)$ be as in the Remark on the shell-tested form of Corollary 58, put $m=\int\chi_{\mathcal R}\,dx$, $\alpha=u^r/r$, $\alpha_\pm=\max\{\pm\alpha,0\}$, $\Delta_5=\partial_{rr}+\frac3r\partial_r+\partial_{zz}$ and $\mathcal L=\partial_s+b\cdot\nabla-\Delta_5$, and let $S(s)$ and $D(s)$ be the historical records of $\|u^\theta\|_\infty$ and $\|\nabla u\|_\infty$ on $[s_0,s]$. (a) For $p=0$ or an integer $p\ge2$ and real $\beta$, pointwise for $r>0$, $$\mathcal L(\Gamma^pr^\beta)=-p(p-1)\Gamma^{p-2}r^\beta|\nabla\Gamma|^2-2p(\beta+2)\Gamma^{p-1}\partial_r\Gamma\,r^{\beta-1}-\beta(\beta+2)\Gamma^pr^{\beta-2}+\beta\Gamma^pr^\beta\alpha,$$ by the chain rule, $\partial_s\Gamma+b\cdot\nabla\Gamma=\Delta_5\Gamma-4r^{-1}\partial_r\Gamma$ and $\mathcal L(r^\beta)=\beta r^\beta\alpha-\beta(\beta+2)r^{\beta-2}$ (for $p=0$ the terms carrying a factor $p$ are omitted, here and in the axis expression below); for even $p\ge2$ the first three terms are $-\Gamma^{p-2}r^\beta$, with $\Gamma^{p-2}r^\beta\ge0$, times a quadratic form in $(\partial_r\Gamma,\partial_z\Gamma,\Gamma/r)$ that is positive definite exactly when $-2p<\beta<-2$. The case $p=2$, $\beta=-3$ is the identity of Lemma 40A and the case $p=4$, $\beta=-6$ is that of item (vii) of the Remark on signed and cancellation-preserving forms of Lemma 40. If $n=2p+\beta>-2$, then $\frac d{ds}\int\psi\,\Gamma^pr^\beta\,dx=\int\psi\,\mathcal L(\Gamma^pr^\beta)\,dx$ for $s_0\le s<t$ whenever $\Gamma^pr^\beta$ and $\mathcal L(\Gamma^pr^\beta)$ are bounded on $\{r\ge1\}\times[s_0,s]$: with $\psi=r^2g$ and $\Gamma=r^2\gamma$, $g$ and $\gamma$ smooth five-dimensional functions as in Lemma 40, each term of $\psi\,\mathcal L(\Gamma^pr^\beta)$ is $O(r^n)$ near the axis, the axis expression of the integration by parts, $r(\Phi\partial_r\psi-\psi\partial_r\Phi)-2\psi\Phi=-nr^{n+2}g\gamma^p+r^{n+3}(\gamma^p\partial_rg-pg\gamma^{p-1}\partial_r\gamma)$ for $\Phi=\Gamma^pr^\beta$, tends to zero, and these axis bounds, the standing regularity and the decay of $\psi$ and its derivatives generated by the compactly supported terminal datum give absolute convergence of the individual terms and allow the spatial cutoffs to be removed as in the proof of Lemma 40A. (b) Inward transport. Put $V(s)=\int\psi(s)(u^\theta)^4r^{-2}dx$, $W_4=\int_{s_0}^t\!\int\psi\,rq^4(-u^r)\,dx\,ds$ and $Y_-=\int_{s_0}^t\!\int\psi(u^\theta)^4\alpha_-^2\,dx\,ds$. For $p=4$, $\beta=-6$ one has $\Gamma^4r^{-6}=(u^\theta)^4r^{-2}\le S^2D^2$ and $\Gamma^4r^{-6}\alpha=rq^4u^r$, and the identity $12X^2-32XY+24Y^2=12(X-\frac43Y)^2+\frac83Y^2$ with $X=\partial_r\Gamma$, $Y=\Gamma/r$ shows that the first three terms of (a) equal $-\frac83q^4-12q^2[(\partial_ru^\theta-\frac q3)^2+(\partial_zu^\theta)^2]$. Integrating over $[s_0,t-\delta]$, dropping $V(t-\delta)\ge0$ and the square, and letting $\delta\to0$ (by $|rq^4u^r|\le S^2D^3$ and $\int\psi\,dx=m$) gives $\frac83Q_\psi\le V(s_0)+6W_4$. Hence $W_4\ge\frac49Q_\psi-\frac16V(s_0)$: if $Q_\psi>0$, the mean of the radial Reynolds number $r(-u^r)$ with respect to the measure $\psi q^4\,dx\,ds$ on $\mathbb R^3\times[s_0,t]$ is at least $\frac49-V(s_0)/(6Q_\psi)$. Since $rq^4(-u^r)\le(u^\theta)^2q^2\alpha_-$, the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality gives $W_4\le(Q_\psi Y_-)^{1/2}$, and $6ab\le\frac43a^2+\frac{27}4b^2$ gives $Q_\psi\le\frac34V(s_0)+\frac{81}{16}Y_-$, the form of item (vii) just cited in which only the inward part of the radial velocity enters. (c) Outward transport. Fix $0<\varepsilon<2$ and put $w(s)=S(s)^{4-\varepsilon}D(s)^\varepsilon$, which is continuous and nondecreasing; since $|q|\le\min\{D(s),S(s)/r\}$, $q^4\le w(s)r^{-4+\varepsilon}$. For $p=0$ and $\beta=-2+\varepsilon$, (a) gives for $R(s)=\int\psi(s)r^{-2+\varepsilon}dx$ the identity $R'=-(2-\varepsilon)\int\psi r^{-2+\varepsilon}\alpha\,dx+(2-\varepsilon)\varepsilon\int\psi r^{-4+\varepsilon}dx$. Put $X=\int_{s_0}^t\!\int w\psi r^{-4+\varepsilon}dx\,ds\ge Q_\psi$, $A_\varepsilon=\int\chi_{\mathcal R}r^{-2+\varepsilon}dx\le(r_t/4)^{-2+\varepsilon}m$ and $J_\varepsilon=\int_{s_0}^t\!\int w\psi r^\varepsilon\alpha_+^2\,dx\,ds$. Integrating $d(wR)=R\,dw+wR'\,ds$ over $[s_0,t-\delta]$, dropping $-w(s_0)R(s_0)-\int R\,dw\le0$ and the part of the work with $\alpha<0$, and using $w(t-\delta)\le w(t)$ and the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality gives, after $\delta\to0$, $(2-\varepsilon)\varepsilon X\le w(t)A_\varepsilon+(2-\varepsilon)(XJ_\varepsilon)^{1/2}$, hence $$Q_\psi\le\frac{2w(t)A_\varepsilon}{(2-\varepsilon)\varepsilon}+\varepsilon^{-2}J_\varepsilon .$$ Here $R(t-\delta)\to A_\varepsilon$: $\psi(s)\to\chi_{\mathcal R}$ in $L^1$ as $s\uparrow t$ (as in the Remark on the signed form of Corollary 58), and the identity of item (iv) of the Remark (what the scalar histories leave open for the signed form), with $g\ge0$ and $|u^r|\le D(t)r$, gives $\int\psi(s)r^{-2}dx\le e^{2D(t)(t-s)}\int\chi_{\mathcal R}r^{-2}dx$, so the part of $R(s)$ in $\{r<\rho\}$ is at most $\rho^\varepsilon$ times a bound independent of $s$. The exponent $\varepsilon$ may depend on $t$ but is held fixed during the integration over $[s_0,t]$; an exponent varying with $s$ adds terms without a sign. (d) Consequently, along a sequence of times on which $V(s_0)=o(Q_\psi)$ and $w(t)A_\varepsilon/((2-\varepsilon)\varepsilon)=o(Q_\psi)$ (for instance when item (e) of the Remark on the shell-tested form of Corollary 58 gives, in the second case of the localization, a lower bound for $Q_\psi$ of larger order than these two quantities), one necessarily has $Y_-\ge\frac{16}{81}(Q_\psi-\frac34V(s_0))$, $W_4\ge(\frac49-o(1))Q_\psi$ and $J_\varepsilon\ge\varepsilon^2Q_\psi(1-o(1))$. These are necessary conditions expressed through the dual-weighted integrals; they establish neither their compatibility nor their incompatibility with Navier-Stokes histories, and they give no lower bound for $\psi$ itself on any region. (e) Scope of the quartic majorant. Put $E^*(t)=\sup_{s_0\le s\le t}\|\nabla u(s)\|_2^2$. The smoothing bound $\|\psi(s)\|_\infty\le\min\{1,Cm(t-s)^{-3/2}\}$ of item (vi) of the Remark on signed and cancellation-preserving forms of Lemma 40 and $\int_0^\infty\min\{1,Cmv^{-3/2}\}\,dv=3(Cm)^{2/3}$ give $\varepsilon_\psi=\int_{s_0}^t\!\int\psi|\nabla u|^2dx\,ds\le3(Cm)^{2/3}E^*(t)$, so item (vii) of that Remark gives $Q_\psi\le\frac34\|r^2q(s_0)^4\|_\infty m+Q^\sharp$ with $Q^\sharp=\frac{243}{16}(Cm)^{2/3}S(t)^4E^*(t)$. Put $N_0=9M^2/(2048\,r_tB(1+8\Lambda))$, with $B$ and $\Lambda$ as in items (d) and (e) of the Remark on the shell-tested form of Corollary 58. For fixed $s_0$ (so that $4\pi r_t^2\|\eta(s_0)\|_\infty=o(M)$ along the crossings), item (e) of that Remark shows that the second case of the localization requires $Q_\psi\ge N_0(1-o(1))$; so a bound $\limsup(\frac34\|r^2q(s_0)^4\|_\infty m+Q^\sharp)/N_0<1$ along the crossings, in particular the scale-invariant scalar condition $\frac34\|r^2q(s_0)^4\|_\infty m+Q^\sharp=o(N_0)$, would exclude the second case. This comparison is limited by the position of the record point. Suppose $|b(x_t,t)|\ge M/2$ and $9S(t)^2E^*(t)\ge H(s_0)$, and put $\rho_n=E^*(t)^{1/2}/\|\eta(t)\|_2$, $x=r_t/\rho_n$, $\ell=1+\log_+(2\|\nabla u(t)\|_\infty\max\{r_t,\rho_n\}/M)$ and $y=\max\{x,\ell^{-1/2}\}$ (both norms are positive, since a decaying meridional velocity without vorticity vanishes). Proposition 41(d), with $H=\|\xi\|_2^2+\|\eta\|_2^2$, gives $\|\eta(t)\|_2^2\le H(t)\le9S(t)^2E^*(t)$, the supremum there being at most $S(t)^2E^*(t)$. Apply the first form of Lemma 43 at $x_t$ with $R=\rho_ny$ (so $r_t\le R\le\max\{r_t,\rho_n\}$), the upper bound $E^*(t)^{1/2}$ for $\|\nabla u(t)\|_2$ and $\delta=\min\{R,M/(4\sqrt2\pi\|\nabla u(t)\|_\infty)\}$: its first term is at most $\frac M4$, $\log(R/\delta)\le\log(2\sqrt2\pi)+\ell-1$, and since $\ell\ge1$ and $R\ge\rho_n\ell^{-1/2}$ the remaining terms give $M\le8\|\eta(t)\|_2(R\ell)^{1/2}+E^*(t)^{1/2}R^{-1/2}\le9\|\eta(t)\|_2(R\ell)^{1/2}$, that is $M^2\le81\|\eta(t)\|_2E^*(t)^{1/2}y\ell$. Together they give $r_t^3S(t)^4E^*(t)/M^2\ge x^3/(6561\,y\ell)$, and with $m\ge|\mathcal R|=\frac{\pi^2}2r_t^3$ and $1+8\Lambda>8B$, $$\frac{Q^\sharp}{N_0}\ge\frac{1024}{243}\Big(\frac{\pi^2C}2\Big)^{2/3}\frac{B^2x^3}{\ell\max\{x,\ell^{-1/2}\}}\ge\frac{1024}{243}\Big(\frac{\pi^2C}2\Big)^{2/3}\frac{B^2\min\{1,x\}^3}{\ell}.$$ Hence $Q^\sharp=o(N_0)$ requires $B^2x^3/(\ell\max\{x,\ell^{-1/2}\})\to0$, and along crossings with $r_t\ge\rho_n$ the quotient $Q^\sharp/N_0$ is at least a fixed multiple of $B^2/\ell$; where moreover $B^2/\ell\to\infty$, no comparison of $Q^\sharp$ with $N_0$ excludes the second case. This concerns the scalar quantity $Q^\sharp$ only: it gives no lower bound for $Q_\psi$, for $\varepsilon_\psi$, or for the majorant $\frac34\|r^2q(s_0)^4\|_\infty m+\frac{81}{16}S(t)^4\varepsilon_\psi$ that retains the actual $\varepsilon_\psi$. (f) Range of the family in (a). Let $p\ge2$ be even and put $b=-\beta$. The quadratic form of (a) is $p(p-1)(X^2+(\partial_z\Gamma)^2)+2p(\beta+2)XY+\beta(\beta+2)Y^2$ with $X=\partial_r\Gamma$, $Y=\Gamma/r$; its minimum over $X$ is $p(p-1)(\partial_z\Gamma)^2+\frac{(b-2)(2p-b)}{p-1}Y^2$. It is positive semidefinite exactly when $-2p\le\beta\le-2$ (for $\beta$ outside this interval it takes both signs), and $\beta\Gamma^pr^\beta\alpha=b\,\Gamma^pr^{\beta-2}\,r(-u^r)$. For $-2p<\beta<-2$, integrating (a) against $\psi$ over $[s_0,t-\delta]$, dropping the terminal term and letting $\delta\to0$ as in (b) (the integrands are bounded, since $|u^\theta|\le\min\{S,Dr\}$ and $|\Gamma|\le G$ give $\Gamma^pr^\beta\le G^p$ for $r\ge1$ and $\Gamma^pr^\beta\le S^p$ or $S^{2p-b}D^{b-p}$ for $r<1$, according as $b\le p$ or $b>p$) gives $$\frac{(b-2)(2p-b)}{p-1}\int_{s_0}^t\!\int\psi\,\Gamma^pr^{\beta-2}\,dx\,ds\le b\int_{s_0}^t\!\int\psi\,\Gamma^pr^{\beta-2}\,r(-u^r)\,dx\,ds+\int\psi(s_0)\Gamma(s_0)^pr^\beta\,dx .$$ So, when this weight is not zero, the mean of $r(-u^r)$ with respect to $\psi\Gamma^pr^{\beta-2}dx\,ds$ is at least $f(b)=\frac{(b-2)(2p-b)}{(p-1)b}$ minus the datum term divided by $b\int_{s_0}^t\!\int\psi\Gamma^pr^{\beta-2}$. For $p=4$ and $\beta=-6$ this is the bound $\frac49$ of (b). Over $2<b<2p$ the maximum of $f$ is $f(2\sqrt p)=\frac{2(\sqrt p-1)}{\sqrt p+1}$. It equals $\frac23$ for $p=4$, at $\beta=-4$, and it increases to $2$ as $p\to\infty$. So no member of the family certifies in this way a lower bound of $2$ or more for a weighted mean of the radial Reynolds number. Write $I=\int_{s_0}^t\!\int\psi\,\Gamma^pr^{\beta-2}\,r(-u^r)\,dx\,ds$ and $\mathcal Q$ for the quadratic form. Wherever (a) can be integrated against $\psi$ as above, it gives $bI=\int\chi_{\mathcal R}\Gamma(t)^pr^\beta dx-\int\psi(s_0)\Gamma(s_0)^pr^\beta dx+\int_{s_0}^t\!\int\psi\,\Gamma^{p-2}r^\beta\mathcal Q\,dx\,ds$. No member with even $p\ge2$ gives an upper bound for $I$ involving only the endpoint terms by discarding the entire quadratic-form term on the basis of its pointwise sign: for $b>0$ this would need $\mathcal Q\le0$, which fails at $Y=0\ne X$; for $b<0$ it would need $\mathcal Q\ge0$, and the form is indefinite there; for $b=0$ there is no transport term. This does not exclude upper bounds obtained by completing the square. Since $\mathcal Q=p(p-1)\big[(X+\frac{\beta+2}{p-1}Y)^2+(\partial_z\Gamma)^2\big]-\frac{(\beta+2)(2p+\beta)}{p-1}Y^2$, for $\beta>0$ the same identity, after the terminal term and the square term (both nonnegative) are discarded, gives $$I\le\frac1\beta\int\psi(s_0)\Gamma(s_0)^pr^\beta\,dx+\frac{(\beta+2)(2p+\beta)}{(p-1)\beta}\int_{s_0}^t\!\int\psi\,\Gamma^pr^{\beta-2}\,dx\,ds ,$$ so, when this weight is not zero, the mean of $r(-u^r)$ with respect to $\psi\Gamma^pr^{\beta-2}dx\,ds$ is at most $\frac{(\beta+2)(2p+\beta)}{(p-1)\beta}$ plus the datum term divided by $\beta\int_{s_0}^t\!\int\psi\Gamma^pr^{\beta-2}$. Over $\beta>0$ this constant is smallest at $\beta=2\sqrt p$, where it equals $\frac{2(\sqrt p+1)}{\sqrt p-1}>2$ (it is $6$ at $p=4$, $\beta=4$, and $12$ at $p=2$, $\beta=2$). For $\beta>0$ the hypothesis $|\Gamma|\le G$ does not make $\Gamma^pr^\beta$ or the other integrands of (a) bounded on $\{r\ge1\}$ (with $|\nabla\Gamma|\le G/r+rD$ the form term is only $O(r^{\beta+2})$ there), so the integration must then be justified separately (for instance by the decay at infinity of $\psi(s)$, $s<t$, the dual of the compactly supported datum $\chi_{\mathcal R}$ under a bounded drift). These are statements about this family of identities only. (g) The mean in (b) has no universal upper bound, and the datum term in (b) is needed. Define $W_4$, $Q_\psi$ and $V$ as in (b), but for an arbitrary terminal time $t$, $s_0=0$ and a smooth, compactly supported axisymmetric terminal cutoff $\chi$, $0\le\chi\le1$, vanishing near the axis, with $\psi$ the dual of Lemma 40 with $\psi(t)=\chi$. For the fixed cutoff $\chi$ below, no inequality $W_4\le cQ_\psi$ holds with a constant $c$ independent of the solution and of $t$. Put $d=1+r^2+z^2$ and $h=\sqrt{15}\,d^{-7/4}$, let $u^{(n)}$ be the smooth solution with the pure-swirl datum $u^{(n)}(0)=n\,rh\,e_\theta$ (energy $\|u^{(n)}(0)\|_2^2=8\pi n^2$, $\eta(0)=0$, and $|\Gamma|\le n\sqrt{15}\,\frac43(\frac37)^{7/4}$ by the maximum principle for $\Gamma$), let $\chi$ equal $1$ on $\{(r-1)^2+(z-2)^2\le\frac14\}$ and vanish outside $\{(r-1)^2+(z-2)^2<\frac9{16}\}$, fix $a>0$ and take $t=t_n=a/n^2$ (the local smooth solution exists on $[0,t_n]$ once $n$ is large, by the scaling below). At $s=0$ the meridional velocity vanishes and its time derivative is $n^2B$, with $B$ the Leray projection of $rh^2e_r$ (the centrifugal force $(u^\theta)^2r^{-1}e_r$ of the datum is $n^2rh^2e_r$); the $\eta$ of $B$ is $\partial_z(h^2)$, and since $-\Delta_5d^{-3/2}=15d^{-7/2}=h^2$ its stream function is $\partial_z(d^{-3/2})$, so $r(-B^r)=K:=r^2\partial_{zz}(d^{-3/2})=3r^2(4z^2-1-r^2)d^{-7/2}$. The scaling $u^{(n)}(x,s)=n\,v^{(1/n)}(x,ns)$, with $v^{(\nu)}$ the solution of viscosity $\nu$ from $rh\,e_\theta$, and local $H^k$ bounds ($k$ large) on an interval independent of $\nu\in(0,1]$ give, uniformly for $0\le\tau\le a$, $b^{(n)}(\tau/n^2)=\tau B+O(1/n)$ and $q^{(n)}(\tau/n^2)/n=h+O(1/n)$ (errors in $L^\infty$, for fixed $a$), with $|q^{(n)}|/n\le C\min\{1,r^{-1}\}$. The maximum principle applied to $\psi_n-\chi$ gives $\psi_n\to\chi$ uniformly, hence in $L^1$ since $\int\psi_n=\int\chi$. Therefore $Q_\psi/n^2\to a\int\chi h^4dx$ and $W_4/n^2\to\frac{a^2}2\int\chi h^4K\,dx$. On the support of $\chi$, $r\ge\frac14$, $4z^2-1-r^2\ge\frac{35}{16}$ and $d<12$, so $K\ge\frac{35}{589824}$ there and $\lim_nW_4/Q_\psi\ge\frac{35a}{1179648}$, which exceeds $\frac49$ for $a=2^{14}$ and is unbounded as $a\to\infty$; for small $a$ the same limit is below $\frac49$ (it is at most $\frac a2\max_{\operatorname{supp}\chi}K$), so the inequality of (b) fails when $V(s_0)$ is omitted. The inward flow comes from the secondary circulation driven by the swirl ($K<0$ near the plane $z=0$, where the flow is outward, and $K>0$ where $4z^2>1+r^2$). In this family $V(0)/Q_\psi\to\infty$ and the circulation bound grows like $n$, and the construction establishes no crossing of the second case; so the family excludes only bounds of $W_4$ by $Q_\psi$ alone with a constant independent of $t$, not bounds with a constant depending on $t$ and $\chi$, bounds that also contain $V(s_0)$, bounds under a fixed circulation bound, or bounds that use the second-case structure. (h) If the meridional velocity may contain a harmonic strain, a fixed circulation bound and a negligible datum term give no universal upper bound for the mean either. In (g) the meridional velocity is the decaying Biot-Savart velocity of $\eta$, while the circulation bound grows and the datum term dominates. Fix $G_0>0$ and take instead the Burgers vortex $u^r=-r$, $u^z=2z$, $u^\theta=G_0r^{-1}(1-e^{-r^2/2})$, an exact steady solution with $\eta=0$, $0\le\Gamma\le G_0$, $q\le G_0/2$, $q\le G_0r^{-2}$ and infinite energy, and, for $\lambda\ge16$, the cutoff $\chi_\lambda(x)=\chi(x/\lambda)$ with $\chi$ as in (g) (the scaling $x\mapsto x/\lambda$, $s\mapsto s/\lambda^2$ makes this equivalent to the vortex with $u^r=-\lambda^2r$, $u^z=2\lambda^2z$, the cutoff $\chi$ and rescaled endpoint times; $Q_\psi$, $W_4$ and $V$ all scale by the same factor, so $W_4/Q_\psi$ and $V/Q_\psi$ are invariant). Here $r(-u^r)=r^2$. In $\tau=t-s$ the dual equation reads $\partial_\tau\psi=\psi_{rr}-r^{-1}\psi_r-r\psi_r+\psi_{zz}+2z\psi_z$, and $\psi=r^2e^{-2\tau}\Phi$ turns it into $\partial_\tau\Phi=\Delta_y\Phi-y\cdot\nabla_y\Phi+\partial_z^2\Phi+2z\partial_z\Phi$, with $\Phi$ regarded as a function of $(y,z)\in\mathbb R^4\times\mathbb R$, $|y|=r$. We take for $\psi$ the solution given by $\Phi(\tau,y,z)=\mathbb E[(\chi_\lambda r^{-2})(Y_\tau,Z_\tau)]$ ($\chi_\lambda r^{-2}$ extended by zero near the axis), where $Y_\tau=e^{-\tau}y+(1-e^{-2\tau})^{1/2}N$, $Z_\tau=e^{2\tau}z+(\frac{e^{4\tau}-1}2)^{1/2}N'$ and $N$, $N'$ are independent standard Gaussians in $\mathbb R^4$ and $\mathbb R$. It is nonnegative, vanishes on the axis, and $\int\psi(\tau)\,dx=\int\chi_\lambda\,dx$ (the substitutions $y\mapsto e^{\tau}y$, $z\mapsto e^{-2\tau}z$ in the starting point have Jacobians $e^{4\tau}$ and $e^{-2\tau}$, and $2\pi r\,dr=d^4y/(\pi r^2)$). The support of $\chi_\lambda$ lies in $\{\frac\lambda4\le r\le\frac{7\lambda}4,\ \frac{5\lambda}4\le z\le\frac{11\lambda}4\}$, and $\int\mathbb P(Z_\tau\in I)\,dz=e^{-2\tau}|I|$ for an interval $I$. On $\{r\le\lambda/8\}$ this gives $\psi\le16r^2\lambda^{-2}e^{-2\tau}p_\lambda\,\mathbb P(Z_\tau\in[\frac{5\lambda}4,\frac{11\lambda}4])$ with $p_\lambda=\mathbb P(|N|\ge\frac\lambda8)=(1+\frac{\lambda^2}{128})e^{-\lambda^2/128}$, hence $\int_{\{r\le\lambda/8\}}\psi(\tau)\,dx\le\frac{3\pi}{1024}\lambda^3e^{-4\tau}p_\lambda$ and $\int_{s_0}^t\!\int_{\{r\le\lambda/8\}}\psi q^4\le\frac{3\pi}{65536}G_0^4\lambda^3p_\lambda$. For $0\le\tau\le\frac1{10}$ the same representation bounds the mass of $\psi(\tau)$ in $\{r\ge4\lambda\}$ by $C\lambda^{-1}\int_{4\lambda}^\infty r^3(1+\frac{r^2}{32})e^{-r^2/32}dr$ (there $e^{-\tau}r-\frac{7\lambda}4\ge\frac r4$), its mass in $\{r<2\}\subset\{r\le\lambda/8\}$ is bounded by the previous estimate, and $\int\chi_\lambda\ge\frac{\pi^2}2\lambda^3$; since $q\ge G_0/(2r^2)$ for $r\ge2$, this gives $Q_\psi\ge\frac{\pi^2}{20\cdot32^4}G_0^4\lambda^{-5}(1-o(1))$ whenever $t-s_0\ge\frac1{10}$, with $o(1)$ as $\lambda\to\infty$ uniformly in $s_0$. As $r(-u^r)\ge\lambda^2/64$ on $\{r>\lambda/8\}$, $W_4\ge\frac{\lambda^2}{64}\big(Q_\psi-\int_{s_0}^t\!\int_{\{r\le\lambda/8\}}\psi q^4\big)$, so $W_4/Q_\psi\ge\frac{\lambda^2}{64}(1-o(1))$ for all $s_0\le t-\frac1{10}$. For fixed $\lambda$, $r^2q^4\le G_0^4\min\{\frac{r^2}{16},r^{-6}\}$, the bound $\psi\le16r^2\lambda^{-2}e^{-2\tau}\mathbb P(Z_\tau\in[\frac{5\lambda}4,\frac{11\lambda}4])$ and the choice $R=e^{\tau/3}$ give $\int\psi(\tau)r^2q^4\le(C_\lambda R^6e^{-4\tau}+R^{-6}\int\chi_\lambda)G_0^4\le C'_\lambda G_0^4e^{-2\tau}$, and the same argument with $q^4\le G_0^4\min\{\frac1{16},r^{-8}\}$ gives $\int\psi(\tau)q^4\le C''_\lambda G_0^4e^{-8\tau/3}$; so $Q_\psi$ and $W_4$ stay bounded as $s_0\to-\infty$, while $Q_\psi$ increases and $V(s_0)\to0$. Thus $|\Gamma|\le G_0$ is fixed, $V(s_0)/Q_\psi\to0$, and the mean is unbounded as $\lambda\to\infty$. Since $\eta=0$, the inward flow here is a harmonic strain, which the decaying Biot-Savart law of a finite-energy solution excludes.
