So an upper bound of the mean in (b) below $\frac49$ under a fixed circulation bound and a negligible datum term needs hypotheses that exclude these vortices, for example the decaying Biot-Savart law recovering the meridional velocity from $\eta$; the example establishes nothing about the second-case structure. In (h) $q$ depends only on $r$, so $\partial_z(q^2)=0$; for every $s_0$ and every final cutoff the source $P_\psi(s_0,t)$ vanishes, the source part $Z(t)=\int_{s_0}^tP(t,s)\partial_z(q^2)(s)\,ds$ of $\eta(t)$ vanishes, and $\int wZ(t)\,dx=0$ for every bounded compactly supported kernel $w$. In (g), $q^{(n)}(x,s)=n\,q^{(n)}_v(x,ns)$ with $q^{(n)}_v=v^\theta/r$ for $v=v^{(1/n)}$; since $v^\theta$ and $\partial_zv^\theta$ vanish on the axis, $|q^{(n)}_v|\le\|\nabla v\|_\infty$ and $|\partial_zq^{(n)}_v|\le\|\nabla^2v\|_\infty$, and the $H^k(\mathbb R^3)$ bounds on an interval independent of $\nu$ ($k$ large) give $|\partial_z((q^{(n)}_v)^2)|\le C$ on $[0,a/n]$ for large $n$, with $C$ independent of $n$; with the conservation of the dual mass this gives $P_\psi\le Ca\int\chi\,dx$ for large $n$ (and, since the same bounds give $\partial_z((q^{(n)}_v)^2)\to\partial_z(h^2)=-105z\,d^{-9/2}$ uniformly on $[0,a/n]$ and $\psi_n\to\chi$ in $L^1$, $P_\psi\to a\int\chi|\partial_z(h^2)|\,dx>0$), while $Q_\psi/n^2\to a\int\chi h^4dx>0$. So for fixed $a$, (g) has $Q_\psi\to\infty$ with bounded $P_\psi$, and in (h) $W_4/Q_\psi$ is unbounded while $P_\psi=0$. These examples exclude the bounds of the mean described in (g) and (h); they give no lower bound for the source growing with $Q_\psi$ and no counterexample to the source condition of How to resume (a) at crossings of the second case. (i) Finite energy and a fixed circulation bound do not give the bound either. For every $G>0$ there are smooth axisymmetric finite-energy solutions with $0\le\Gamma\le G$, off-axis cutoffs that are axial translates of dilations of one profile, and finite intervals on which $V(s_0)/Q_\psi$ is arbitrarily small while $W_4/Q_\psi$ is arbitrarily large. The construction places the vortex of (h) at the rear stagnation point of a large vortex ring. Hill's spherical vortex of radius $1$ and speed $c$ has $\eta=\frac{15c}2$ in the ball and $\eta=0$ outside, and on the axis $\phi=\frac c4(5-3z^2)$ for $|z|\le1$ and $\phi=\frac c2|z|^{-3}$ for $|z|\ge1$; so $u^z=2\phi$ equals $c$ at $z=-1$, with $\partial_zu^z=3c$ there from both sides. Radu and Stevenson (2026) constructed smooth swirl-free travelling waves of the Euler equations with the speed of Hill's vortex, converging to it in $L^2\cap C^\alpha$, with relative vorticity between $0$ and $15$ and vorticity supports in $\{|x|<2\}$ (in their normalization). The lifted relative vorticities then converge weak-* in $L^\infty(\mathbb R^5)$, and since the Newton kernel of $\Delta_5$ and its gradient are $O(|Y|^{-3})$ and $O(|Y|^{-4})$, hence locally integrable, $\phi$ and $\partial_z\phi$ converge locally uniformly; so for one such wave $U(x-cse_z)$, with $U$ in every $H^k$, the axis function $u^z-c$ has a simple zero $p$ with positive slope. On the axis $\partial_ru^r=-\frac12\partial_zu^z$, so after multiplying velocity and speed by one constant (still called $c$), $U(p)=ce_z$ and $DU(p)=A:=\operatorname{diag}(-1,-1,2)$. Let $v^\nu$ be the Navier-Stokes solution of viscosity $\nu$ with datum $U$; on a fixed interval $[0,T]$ it is smooth for small $\nu$ and within $O(\nu)$ of the travelling wave in every $H^m$ (Constantin's inviscid limit of smooth solutions). Then $B_R(x,s)=Rv^{R^{-2}}(x/R,s)$ solves the equation with viscosity $1$ and has finite energy; its velocity $\beta_R$ in the frame moving with $R(p+cse_z)$ satisfies $|\beta_R(0,s)|\le C_TR^{-1}$, $\|D\beta_R\|_\infty\le C_T$, and $\beta_R\to Ay$, $D\beta_R\to A$ uniformly on $[0,T]\times\{|y|\le M\}$ for each $M$. The axial change of frame leaves $r$ and the weighted integrals unchanged. Add to the datum the swirl $\delta F$, $F=r\rho(y/L)q_B(r)e_\theta$, with $q_B=(1-e^{-r^2/2})r^{-2}$ the profile of (h) for $G_0=1$, $\rho$ smooth, radial and compactly supported with $0\le\rho\le1$ and $\rho=1$ near $0$, and $0<\delta\le G$. For fixed $T$, $R$ large enough for the preceding estimates on $[0,T]$, fixed $L$ and small $\delta$, the $H^m$ energy estimate for $w=u_\delta-B_R$ gives a smooth solution on $[0,T]$ with $\|w\|_{H^m}\le C\delta$. The reflection $u\mapsto Su(S\,\cdot)$, $S=\operatorname{diag}(1,-1,1)$, preserves the equation, fixes the meridional components and reverses the swirl, so by uniqueness $u_{-\delta}$ is the reflection of $u_\delta$, and the meridional difference $\frac12(w_++w_-)$, $w_\pm=u_{\pm\delta}-B_R$, solves the equation linearized at $B_R$ with zero datum and forcing $-\mathbb P\operatorname{div}\frac12(w_+\otimes w_++w_-\otimes w_-)$ of size $O(\delta^2)$; hence the meridional velocity differs from $B_R$ by $O(\delta^2)$ in $C([0,T];H^m)$, so in $W^{1,\infty}$. The circulation solves $\partial_s\Gamma+b\cdot\nabla\Gamma=\partial_{rr}\Gamma-r^{-1}\partial_r\Gamma+\partial_{zz}\Gamma$ with $0\le\Gamma(0)\le\delta$, and comparison with $\varepsilon e^{Cs}(1+r^2+z^2)$, a supersolution by the linear growth of the drift, gives $0\le\Gamma\le\delta\le G$ on $[0,T]$. In the moving frame write $Y=(\xi,z)\in\mathbb R^4\times\mathbb R$ with $r=|\xi|$, $a=\beta^r/r$ (so $|a|\le\|D\beta\|_\infty$) and $\tilde\beta=(a\xi,\beta^z)$, so that $\operatorname{div}_5\tilde\beta=2a$. Then $f=\psi/r^2$ satisfies $-\partial_sf=\Delta_5f+\operatorname{div}_5(\tilde\beta f)$, so $f(T-\tau)$ is the density of the diffusion with drift $-\tilde\beta(T-\tau)$ started from $\chi_\lambda/r^2$; $h=q/\delta$ solves $\partial_sh=\Delta_5h-\tilde\beta\cdot\nabla h-2ah$ and equals $\mathbb E_Y[h(0,X_s)\exp(-2\int_0^sa(s-\tau,X_\tau)\,d\tau)]$ with $dX_\tau=-\tilde\beta(s-\tau,X_\tau)\,d\tau+\sqrt2\,dB_\tau$; and $\int\psi H\,dx=\frac1\pi\int fH\,dY$ for axisymmetric $H$. For large $R$, all $L$ and small $\delta$ one has $|\tilde\beta|\le K_T(1+|Y|)$, $\|D\tilde\beta\|_\infty\le K_T$ and $|a|\le K_T$; Ito's formula for $1+|Y|^4$ gives uniform fourth moments of the dual densities and exit probabilities from $\{|Y|<M\}$ of order $M^{-4}$, and $0\le h\le\frac12e^{2K_TT}$. So the normalized integrands $r^2h^4$, $h^4$ and $-ar^2h^4$ of $\delta^{-4}V(s_0)$, $\delta^{-4}Q_\psi$ and $\delta^{-4}W_4$ are bounded by $C_T(1+|Y|^2)$ and their tails are $O(M^{-2})$ uniformly, while coupling the diffusions through a common Brownian motion, stopped on exit from $\{|Y|<M\}$, gives locally uniform convergence of $h$ and weak convergence of the dual densities whenever the drifts converge locally uniformly under these bounds. Letting $\delta\to0$ (convergence of the drift in $C^1$), then $R\to\infty$ (local convergence to $\tilde\beta=(-\xi,2z)$, $a=-1$), then $L\to\infty$ (the datum tends to $q_B$ locally uniformly, and $q_B$ is the bounded stationary solution, since $q_B''+\frac3rq_B'+rq_B'+2q_B=0$), the triple $\delta^{-4}(V(s_0),Q_\psi,W_4)$ on $[t-T,t]$ converges to the triple of (h) for $G_0=1$ on an interval of length $T$, the limiting dual density being $e^{-2\tau}$ times the Ornstein-Uhlenbeck expectation of (h). Choosing first $\lambda$ and then a finite $T$ by (h), and then $L$, $R$, $\delta$ in this order, gives the stated ratios. The meridional velocity of each of these solutions is an $L^2$ divergence-free field determined by its curl, so the decaying Biot-Savart law holds. Thus finite energy, the decaying Biot-Savart law and a fixed circulation bound do not imply an upper bound for $W_4/Q_\psi$, even when $V(s_0)/Q_\psi$ is arbitrarily small; an estimate restricted to the second case must use information established for that case, and the construction does not establish that its solutions enter the second case. In the same iterated limit the source is small: $\delta^{-2}P_\psi\to0$, while $\delta^{-4}Q_\psi$ tends to the positive quartic integral of (h) with $G_0=1$, for the fixed cutoff $\chi_\lambda$ and the fixed interval length $T$, so $P_\psi/Q_\psi^{1/2}\to0$. Indeed, put $\kappa=\sup_{[0,T]}\|D^2\beta\|_\infty$ (Cartesian derivatives); then $\kappa\le C_TR^{-1}+C_{T,R,L}\delta^2$, since $\|D^2\beta_R\|_\infty=R^{-1}\|D^2v^{R^{-2}}\|_\infty$ and the meridional correction is $O(\delta^2)$ in $C([0,T];H^m)$ for the large $m$ used above. Take $L\ge1$ and, for fixed $R$ and $L$, $\delta$ so small that $C_{T,R,L}\delta^2\le1$. From $a=\int_0^1\partial_r\beta^r(\sigma r,z)\,d\sigma$ one has $\|\nabla a\|_\infty\le C\kappa$ and $|r\partial_ra|=|\partial_r\beta^r-a|\le2K_T$. Differentiating the representation of $h$ along the flow of the diffusion, whose derivative is at most $e^{K_T\tau}$, gives $\|h\|_\infty+\|\nabla h\|_\infty\le C_T$ on $[0,T]$. By $2a+r\partial_ra+\partial_z\beta^z=0$, the function $\vartheta=\partial_zh$ solves $\partial_s\vartheta=\Delta_5\vartheta-\tilde\beta\cdot\nabla\vartheta+(r\partial_ra)\vartheta-\partial_za\,(\xi\cdot\nabla_\xi h+2h)$, with $|\vartheta(0)|\le C/L$; so the inhomogeneous Feynman-Kac representation and $\mathbb E_Y(1+|X_\tau|)\le C_T(1+|Y|)$ give $|\vartheta|\le C_T(L^{-1}+\kappa(1+|Y|))$. Since $\partial_z(q^2)=2\delta^2h\vartheta$ and the dual densities have bounded second moments, $\delta^{-2}P_\psi\le C_{\lambda,T}(L^{-1}+R^{-1}+C_{T,R,L}\delta^2)$, which tends to zero when $\delta\to0$, then $R\to\infty$, then $L\to\infty$. The inputs taken from the literature are the cited ring construction and the inviscid limit of smooth solutions on a fixed interval.

## Limitations / Open Questions

1. Within the strategy of admissible amplitude-$M$ isotropic normalizations, the first missing implication is the
   exclusion of (C) for the actual fixed-data trajectory, i.e. a uniform local
   $L^2$ translation modulus (L2M) with retained $L^2$ oscillation at $\tau=0$ at some admissible amplitude-$M$ scale.
   Sufficient conditions: the local vorticity bound (VL1) or its one-sided form (Corollary 6A), the sup-norm
   level-scale condition of Section 5, or (L2LS). Vanishing
   of a Reynolds defect alone is not the retained-oscillation, uniform-in-time compactness criterion required in (B); no
   equivalent defect criterion for the exclusion of (C) is proved here (Section 6, Remark (i)). Section 10 shows that the
   one-dimensional forms of the single-time conditions used here (amplitude and Lipschitz normalizations, the plateau
   modulus after a dilation, retained oscillation at the energy scale) do not give it, for a one-dimensional model
   profile and, by Proposition 10', for a family of three-dimensional divergence-free axisymmetric swirl-free fields of
   fixed energy on which every retained-oscillation window fails (L2M); so the exclusion of (C) has to use the
   evolution (or single-time information not used here). By Proposition 7(c), only
   velocity Type II singularities can fall into (C). Section 11 gives a sufficient condition stated at single times:
   in regime (II), along some sequence of velocity first crossings $t_n\uparrow T_*$, the meridional vorticity mass $N_n=\int_{T_{2\rho_n}(x_n)}|\eta(t_n)|\,dx$
   near the record, for some $0<\rho_n\le1$ with $\rho_n/L_E(M_n)\to\infty$, is at most $CM_n^2/D_n$ (or, sufficient for this, the signed compression work since a fixed time is at most
   $CM_n^2/D_n$). It excludes (UP) and so (C). The lower bound $(\sqrt{2/3}-o(1))M_n^2/D_n$ for the work is proved
   (Proposition 30), so the required bound is of the right order; the compression work done within any fixed number of
   Euler times and Lipschitz lengths of the record is $o(M_n^2/D_n)$ (Remark after Corollary 35), so the bound has to
   control work done over long histories or at scales much larger than $M_n/D_n$, and a crude memory estimate misses it
   by the factor $(D\epsilon(s_0)/M)^2$, where $\epsilon(s_0)=\int_{s_0}^{T_*}\|\nabla u\|_2^2\,dt$ (Remark after Corollary 35). The single-time family of the Remark after Theorem 32
   shows that the constraints of energy, amplitude, gradient, circulation, symmetry and the plateau modulus at one time do
   not give the bound, so the evolution (or further single-time information) has to be used. If $\|\omega_0^\theta\|_{L^1}<\infty$, it suffices to bound
   the swirl source or the inward compression work over the last $2\delta_n=2K^2D_n^2/M_n^4$ units of time before $t_n$, with $K=\|\omega_0^\theta\|_{L^1}+E_0/2$ the constant of Lemma 9' (or over a shorter window
   that depends on the trajectory), where both are at least of order $M_n^2/D_n$; the scalar time budgets (energy, Lemma 2, Lemma 9', Lemma 29(b),
   the $L^1$ contraction and Proposition 30) are consistent with a violation of this bound, so spatial structure or the coupled dynamics has to
   enter (Remark (a windowed source condition) after Theorem 32). Up to small errors the record mass condition and the windowed source condition concern only
   cylinders of radius $O(D_n/M_n^2)$ about the axis, which contain the record point, and on a selected final part of the window the source,
   integrated over all space, has source-weighted mean radius at least $(2/\sqrt3-o(1))/M_n$ (Remark (the windowed conditions concern a thin
   cylinder about the axis)). In that cylinder the produced vorticity is bounded by an adjoint-weighted inward work, up to terms of order at most $M_n^2/D_n$ (Lemma 40A, which involves no derivative of the adjoint weight); no $O(M_n^2/D_n)$ bound for this work is proved. Up to $O(M_n^2/D_n)$, only the work where the radial Reynolds number $r(u^r)_-$ exceeds $\frac16$ and the distance from the axis is below $G\varepsilon_nE_0D_n/(4M_n^2)=o(D_n/M_n^2)$ enters, and it lies at distance more than $(6D_n)^{-1/2}$ from the axis; a bound $\varepsilon_n\le CM_n^2/D_n^{3/2}$ on the tail parameter $\varepsilon_n=K^{-2}\int_{t_n-2\delta_n}^{t_n}\|\eta\|_{L^1}^2ds+(2/E_0)\int_{t_n-2\delta_n}^{t_n}\|\nabla u\|_2^2ds$ of the Remark (a windowed source condition) would suffice, but by Leray's lower bound it fails along every sequence with $M_n(T_*-t_n)^3\to0$ (Remark (compression at small radial Reynolds number)). The Biot-Savart law bounds the part of this work produced by the vorticity in the tube about the record point by $\frac12GD_n$ times the time integral of the tube mass, hence by $\frac12GD_n(t_n-s_n)$ times the largest tube mass on the window, a coefficient that tends to infinity for $G>0$; the part produced by the vorticity outside the tube has the analogous bound by the exterior mass, and no bound of it by the tube mass or of order $M_n^2/D_n$ is established. A signed pairing gives a bound under the condition $\sup|r\varphi|<\sqrt2/3$ on the meridional Reynolds number $r\varphi$, but on the selected windows $\sup|r\varphi|\ge1/\sqrt3-o(1)$, so that condition fails there (parts (f) and (g) of that Remark); in fact, by an elementary kinematic bound in (g), $\sup_x|r\varphi|\ge M_n^2/(128D_n)\to\infty$ along velocity first crossings in regime (II), and by the $BMO^{-1}$ regularity criterion of Lei and Zhang (J. Funct. Anal. 261 (2011)) $\sup|r\varphi|$ is unbounded near $T_*$ on every singular trajectory, so no hypothesis imposing a uniform finite bound on $\sup_x|r\varphi(x,t)|$ for all $t$ sufficiently close to $T_*$ can hold on a singular trajectory. If the transport of the produced vorticity by the meridional drift is dropped, or replaced by transport by a linear strain with arbitrary time dependence, a size bound does hold: in a smooth model class the memory part of the compression work is then at most $\frac3{16}G^2$ (respectively $\frac38G^2$) times the swirl source, uniformly in the length of the window (Remark (the memory part without transport)); the bound with constant $\frac3{16}$ for the true propagator, together with a bound $O(M_n^2/D_n)$ for the work of the inherited vorticity, would give GOAL on the record windows of the Remark (a windowed source condition), on which $\|\eta(s_n)\|_{L^1}\le M_n^2/D_n$, when $G^2<16/(3\sqrt3)$ (with the constant $\frac38$, when $G^2<8/(3\sqrt3)$), and neither required estimate is established. For general smooth axisymmetric divergence-free Lipschitz drifts a time-uniform bound of the absolute memory work by a multiple of $G^2\mathcal I$ is false: the same Remark gives a fixed drift (of infinite kinetic energy) and an independently prescribed steady swirl with $W_T<0$ and $|W_T|/(G^2\mathcal I_0(T))$ bounded below by a positive constant times $\log\log T$ for large $T$; the one-sided estimate relevant to Lemma 29(b) fails in the same generality: a second example there gives a family of drifts of Lipschitz constant $1$ with prescribed steady swirls (a single swirl before the rescaling to Lipschitz constant $1$) with $W\ge c\,G^2\mathcal I\log N/\log\log N$ on windows of length $N\to\infty$ in Lipschitz units, beating any bound $\gamma G^2\mathcal I+C+C\|b\|_\infty^2/\|\nabla b\|_\infty$ with $\gamma$ and $C$ independent of the drift, the swirl and the window length. Both examples survive truncation to compactly supported drifts that agree with the original ones on a ball whose radius grows with the window and have velocity and Lipschitz bounds comparable to those of the untruncated drifts (the sign $W_T\le0$ of the first example is kept exactly); the energies of the truncations are unbounded along the families, but after the parabolic rescaling they are compactly supported families of energy one (with Lipschitz constants tending to infinity), so a coefficient depending only on $G$ and the energy is also excluded; no example with one fixed finite-energy drift, or with simultaneous bounds on the energy and the Lipschitz constant, is given. So neither the absolute nor the one-sided memory-work estimate for the true propagator can be obtained from these general drift properties, finite energy included (the examples do not concern the estimate for the inherited vorticity); both would have to use properties that the examples lack (such as a bound on the scale-invariant energy $\|b\|_{L^2}^2\|\nabla b\|_\infty^{1/2}$, which for the full Navier-Stokes velocity at the velocity records is at least $\frac\pi{24}(M_n^2/D_n)^{5/2}$, transport of the swirl by the same drift, generation of the drift by the vorticity), and neither is established here. Under the cap $|u^\theta|\le Dr$ the same Remark proves partial bounds: for sources supported in $\{Dr^2\le G\}$ the inward memory work is at most $0.934\,G^2\mathcal I$ uniformly in the window length for drifts with $b^r\le0$ everywhere, and at most $1.867\,G^2\mathcal I$ if only the outflow is restricted to radial Reynolds number $r(b^r)_+\le(2(1+\log^+(\frac{28}3(G/D)^{1/2}/r)))^{-1}$ (the restriction on the support of the source is used to obtain these constants); for drifts with $b^r\ge0$ everywhere, with the swirl transported by the same drift, it is at most $\frac{G^2}8(1+\log^+(4N/G))\mathcal I$ on windows of length $N$ in units of $1/D$; for any drift, with the swirl transported, it is at most a constant times $G^2\Lambda\mathcal I$ with $\Lambda=\frac DG(\|\Gamma\|_{L^1}/G)^{2/3}$ at the start of the window; its finite-time form improves on the trivial bound $\frac12DGT\mathcal I$ on a window of length $T$ exactly when $T$ exceeds the diffusion time $t_*=(C\|\Gamma\|_{L^1}/G)^{2/3}$ of the circulation mass, and the window-uniform form only when $T>3t_*$. For an arbitrary drift, without a restriction on the support of the source, the inward memory work is at most $\int\!\!\int\mathcal H(r)|F|\,dx\,d\tau$ plus an occupation charge of the outflow (possibly infinite), with $\mathcal H$ the occupation potential of that Remark; under the weak-outflow condition it is at most $2\int\!\!\int\mathcal H(r)|F|\,dx\,d\tau$, and the constants $0.934$ and $1.867$ need in addition $F=0$ for $r>(G/D)^{1/2}$. A nonzero meridional drift of finite energy has neither global sign, so for the Navier-Stokes drift the available statements are the weak-outflow bound, the bound with the outflow charge and the circulation-mass bound; on the record windows neither the weak-outflow condition nor the support restriction is known to hold, the outflow charge is not known to be small compared with $G^2\mathcal I$, and the circulation-mass bound needs $\Gamma\in L^1(dx)$ at the start of the window, which finite energy does not give, and improves on the trivial bound only under the mass condition stated with it. The record mass condition of Section 11 is sufficient, but its necessity for GOAL is not established. Heuristically, if the velocity
   records are carried by a coherent structure of size $\rho_n\gg M_n/D_n$ (a jet or vortex of that size moving at speed
   about $M_n$, with the gradient record elsewhere), the Lipschitz-scale limit of Lemma 31 is constant, so
   $N_nD_n/M_n^2\to\infty$ by Lemma 31(b), while the normalization at the scale $\rho_n$ may retain oscillation and satisfy
   (L2M), in which case GOAL holds by Theorem 5. For normalizations at such larger scales the corresponding sufficient
   condition is the one-sided mass bound of Corollary 6A(b). Since $\iint_P|\omega^\theta|\,dr\,dz=(2\pi)^{-1}\int_{T_P}|\eta|\,dx$ for the
   solid of revolution $T_P$ of $P$, and the split in the proof of Lemma 31(c) gives $\int_{T}|\eta(t)|\le\|\eta(s_0)\|_\infty|T|+\mathcal I_{s_0}(t)$ for
   every bounded set $T$, that bound follows from $\mathcal I_{s_0}(s_n)\le CM_n\ell_n$ for an admissible normalization at the
   scale $\ell_n$ with (L2-OSC) and bounded $r(x_n)$. Under (FLAT$_0$), where no admissible normalization satisfies (L2-OSC)
   (Proposition 14A(c)), Proposition 33 and $A\le\sqrt2D$ give, at every sufficiently late velocity first crossing $t_n$, $W_{s_0}(t_n)\ge c(M_n^2/D_n)2^{cM_n/\Theta_0(t_n)}-C$, so a bound
   $W_{s_0}(t_n)\le(M_n^2/D_n)\Upsilon(M_n/\Theta_0(t_n))$ with $\Upsilon(x)2^{-cx}\to0$ already excludes (FLAT$_0$). In the toroidal branch of Section 12 (record points with $p_q\le0$, with $p_q$ as defined in Proposition 51) a localized bound suffices for the second case of the localization there: by Corollary 58, a bound $2^{o(h_b)}M_n^2/A_n$, where $h_b=M/\theta_b$ is the ratio of the record velocity to the meridional BMO norm (Proposition 51), from a fixed time on, for the inward work weighted by the dual solution of Lemma 40 with final datum the cutoff $\chi_{\mathcal R}$ of the paragraph after Lemma 57 (equal to $1$ on the torus swept by the disc of radius $r_t/2$ about the record point and supported in the torus swept by the disc of radius $3r_t/4$) excludes that case along crossings with $h_b\to\infty$, and so does the same bound for the signed work (Remark after Corollary 58), which is not larger; no such bound is proved, and the second Remark after Corollary 58 records what the scalar histories of Limitations 1' leave open for the signed form.
   Theorem 38(b) removes the restriction to bounded $r(x_n)$ in the source condition $\mathcal I_{s_0}(t_n)\le CM_n\ell_n$ (an energy
   argument bounds $|E_{n,R}|$, where $E_{n,R}$ is the solid of revolution of the meridional rectangle $P_{n,R}$ of Corollary 6A), and Theorem 38 locates (C): every normalization with retained oscillation has
   $M_n\ell_n=o(S(t_n))$; in (C) every such normalization also has $M_n\ell_n=o(\mathcal I_{s_0}(t_n))$, and in (C) the field at velocity records is a plateau at every fixed multiple of
   $M_n/D_n$ and $L^2$-negligible at every fixed multiple of $\mathcal I_{s_0}(t_n)/M_n$. So one sufficient way to exclude (C) outside (FLAT$_0$)
   (where (C) holds vacuously, Proposition 14A(c); outside it near-maximizers of $O_0$ give admissible normalizations with (L2-OSC), as in the proof of Theorem 38(a)) is to establish
   a bound $\int_{E_{n,R}}|\eta|\le C_{R,A}M_n\ell_n$, for all $R,A$ and uniformly in the window times, at one normalization with
   (L2-OSC); its necessity is not proved. In (C) with such a normalization, Corollary 6A(c) and the bound of the inherited
   mass force $\int_{s_0}^{v}\!\int\psi_n|F|/(M_n\ell_n)\to\infty$ along some window times $v$ ($\psi_n$ the adjoint weight of Lemma 40 with terminal datum at time $v$ a cutoff of $E_{n,R}$, as defined below), so the bound is exactly what fails there.
   Lemma 40A reduces it to a localized bound on the swirl-weighted dissipation $\int\!\int\psi_n|u^\theta||\nabla u|^2$ (Lemma 40 needs in addition a bound on the radial flux of the backward adjoint weight),
   which is not proved. Through the entropy and quartic forms (items (vi) and (vii) of the Remark after Lemma 40) a sufficient condition is a
   rate for the localized dissipation: fix $s_0\in(0,T_*)$, take axisymmetric cutoffs $1_{E_{n,R}}\le\chi_n\le1$ supported in $E_{n,2R}$, put
   $v_n=\int\chi_n$, let $\psi_n(s)=P(t,s)^*\chi_n$ be the absorbing adjoint weight of Lemma 40 with terminal datum $\chi_n$ at the window time $t$,
   and $S_n=S(t_n)$; if $S_n^4v_n\varepsilon_{\psi_n}\log M_n\le C_{R,A}M_n^2\ell_n^2$ for $\varepsilon_{\psi_n}=\int_{s_0}^t\!\int\psi_n|\nabla u|^2$, uniformly over the window times
   and for all $R,A$, at one normalization with (L2-OSC), then the bound above holds there and GOAL follows. The available localization
   gives $\varepsilon_{\psi_n}\to0$ without a rate, and no rate is established. The exact Jacobian form of the produced vorticity (Remark (Jacobian form of the produced vorticity) in Section 11) does not weaken
   the bound: its one-sided condition is equivalent to the bound together with an absolute bound on the commutator history. The swirl-scale condition $M_n\ell_n\ge\kappa S(t_n)$ is never met (Theorem 38(a)), so it gives no route.
1'. In the weaker sense of Section 6A the missing implication is the exclusion of (C*) = (FLAT) or (C*-D), by
   Proposition 14A equivalently of (FLAT$_0$) and of (C*)$\cap$(HD). A
   dimensional scenario was tested against the scalar bounds given by the energy, the energy ball, $M'\le24MD$, velocity
   Type II, the Beale-Kato-Majda criterion and the swirl bounds $S^2\le CGD$ (a scalar consistency check, not a
   construction and not a verification of compatibility with the full system): $M\sim\tau^{-3/5}$,
   $D\sim\tau^{-11/10}$ ($\tau=T_*-t$), a logarithmic jet with amplitude $o(M)$ per octave, a strain at the Lipschitz scale
   of the sub-amplitude structures and a swirl tube of core $D^{-1/2}$. Its exponent bookkeeping was checked
   symbolically; it postulates historical BMO $=o(M)$, which is (FLAT$_0$) of Proposition 14A and gives (FLAT) by
   Proposition 14(c), and it was not found to contradict the published
   regularity criteria for axisymmetric flows with swirl that were checked (as reported by the second-model
   consultation; the original statements were not all re-read). The meridional vorticity budget
   $\|\eta(t)\|_1\le\|\eta_0\|_1+\int_0^t\|\partial_z(q^2)\|_1$ requires an axial modulation of the swirl in that scenario. This
   is heuristic evidence that (FLAT) cannot be excluded by the a priori bounds used here; it is not a construction
   and not a counterexample. In such a flat scenario every weak limit of an admissible amplitude-$M$ isotropic normalization is constant; heuristically, sub-amplitude limits
   with a constant Galilean boost are logarithmically unbounded or carry a uniform far-field pressure force, and the
   boundedness requirement of GOAL, rather than compactness, appears to be the obstruction. Proposition 14B(c) makes part of this
   precise: if $D/\Theta_0^2$ does not tend to $0$, gradient records along a sequence with
   $D(t_n)\ge\delta\Theta_0(t_n)^2$ give, along a subsequence, a nonconstant ancient limit at the BMO amplitude whose
   pressure gradient is exactly the increment pressure (no uniform force survives); after a constant horizontal boost it
   is axisymmetric about a fixed line with nonzero swirl; it grows at most logarithmically on each finite time slab, and
   its global boundedness is not proved. Finite energy limits the flatness itself: by Proposition 14C, unconditionally in
   regime (II), $\Theta_0\ge cM/\log(E_0M)$ near $T_*$, so (FLAT$_0$) can hold only at a logarithmic rate. This estimate permits (FLAT$_0$), and the arguments given here
   do not exclude it; a dynamical argument could exclude it without sharpening this estimate, and none is given. The particular
   scalar constraints listed next, which are scalar forms of the main quantitative results proved in the body for (FLAT$_0$), admit explicit
   histories with $\Theta_0/M\to0$ at this logarithmic rate for a small flatness parameter and a swirl $c_S(T_*-t)^{-1/2}\log(e/(T_*-t))$ with $c_S\ge1$, on a sufficiently late interval. This is a feasibility
   check for that specified list; it does not verify every scalar consequence of the report and constructs no velocity field. Let $\tau=T_*-t$ be small, $L=\log(e/\tau)$,
   $c_S\ge1$, and fix one $\alpha>0$ with $\kappa\alpha\le\frac1{256}$ and $c_{33}\alpha\le\frac1{16}$, where $\kappa=(\log2)/64$ is the constant of Proposition 14D and $c_{33}=(\log2)/(4C_0)$, so that
   $2^{k_*}\le e^{c_{33}h}$ in Proposition 33, with $h=M/\Theta_0$ the flatness ratio, when $\theta_b=\Theta_0$ at the records. The functions $M=\tau^{-3/4}$, $\Theta_0=M/(\alpha L)$, $D=M^2/L^4$,
   $A=D$, $S=c_S\tau^{-1/2}L$, a dissipation rate $\|\nabla u\|_2^2=\tau^{-1}L^{-3/2}$ (integrable) and a compression work rate $w=\tau^{-3/2}L^{-1}$ are nondecreasing near $T_*$, lie in regime (II)
   with $\Theta_0/M\to0$ and $S^2/D\to0$, and satisfy: the swirl restriction $S^2\le G^2(1+G^2)^{-1/2}D$
   whenever $S>\sigma(0)=\|u_0^\theta\|_\infty$, which is sharper than Lemma 1(c) (at a first crossing of a swirl level $\lambda>\sigma(0)$ at $x_\lambda$, Lemma 1(a),
   $r_\lambda\lambda\le G$ and $(e_\theta\cdot\nabla)u=r^{-1}(u^re_\theta-u^\theta e_r)$ give $D\ge r_\lambda^{-1}(r_\lambda^{-2}+\lambda^2)^{1/2}\ge\lambda^2G^{-2}(1+G^2)^{1/2}$); the lower bounds $\mathcal K\ge\frac34Y\ge\pi^2S^6/(24G^2D^2)$
   of Lemma 29(a) and $\Sigma'\ge\pi^2S^4/(8D^2)$, where $\Sigma'=\int(u^\theta)^2r^{-1}dx$, $\mathcal K=X-\frac32Y$ with $X=\int|\nabla\Gamma|^2r^{-3}dx$, and $Y=\int\Gamma^2r^{-5}dx$ are the quantities of Lemma 29 (the Remark on the swirl disc after Lemma 34, with $v(t)=S(t)$ since $S$ is strictly increasing near $T_*$, and $d=\|\nabla u\|_\infty\le D$), here
   $S^6/D^2=c_S^6L^{14}$ and $S^4/D^2=c_S^4\tau L^{12}$ tend to zero relative to the work rate and to $\Sigma'$; the upper bound $\Sigma'\le2\|u^\theta\|_2\|\partial_ru^\theta\|_2\le2\|u_0\|_2\|\nabla u\|_2$
   (integrate $(u^\theta)^2=\partial_r(r(u^\theta)^2)-2ru^\theta\partial_ru^\theta$ in $r$); the bounds $\int q^2(-u^r)dx\le M(\Sigma'Y)^{1/2}$ and
   $\int q^2(-u^r)dx\le D\Sigma'$ on the work rate; $D/\Theta_0^2\to0$ and $M'/(\Theta_0D)\to0$ (the scalar gradient bound of
   Proposition 14B(a) and its growth consequence); Proposition 14C(b), whose controlling quantity $M^2(\Theta_0/D)^32^{3h/16}$ tends to $0$, and
   $M^5/D^3\to0$; both work ceilings $W_{s_0}(t)\le C\Theta_0(t)\epsilon(s_0)$ and $W_{s_0}(t)\le CS(t)\epsilon(s_0)$, which hold pointwise in the rates
   and hence for every late $s_0$; the lower bound of Proposition 33 for every fixed $s_0$ ($S/M\to0$ gives $|b|\ge\frac78M$ at the records
   eventually, and $W_{s_0}D/M^2$ grows like $\tau^{-1/2}L^{-5}$ while $e^{c_{33}h}\le e^{1/16}\tau^{-1/16}$); Proposition 14D(b) at the dyadic crossings
   (with $m_n=2^nm_0$, $t_n$ the first time with $\|u(t_n)\|_\infty=m_n$, $\tau_n=T_*-t_n$, $L_n=\log(e/\tau_n)$, $h_n=m_n/\Theta_0(t_n)$ and $R_n=2^{\lfloor h_n/64\rfloor}\Theta_0(t_n)/D(t_n)$ as there; $\tau_n=m_n^{-4/3}$ and $t_n-t_{n-1}=(2^{4/3}-1)\tau_n$, while the persistence time of Proposition 14D(b) (denoted $\delta_n$ there) is comparable to $\tau_n^{3/2-\kappa\alpha}L_n^5=o(\tau_n)$, so the intervals
   are disjoint; the certified costs, comparable to $\tau_n^{3/4-2\kappa\alpha}L_n^8$, form a convergent geometric-type series; the dissipation
   rate supplies each of them since $\tau_n^{-1}L_n^{-3/2}/(m_n^2R_n)\to\infty$; the floor in $R_n$ changes $R_n$ by a factor in $[\frac12,1]$);
   Proposition 14D(c) with the next-record index ($h_{n+1}-h(t)\le\frac83\alpha\log2$ for $t_{n-1}\le t<t_n$); the lower bound
   $\|\nabla u(t)\|_2^2\ge c(\Theta_0^3/D)2^{h/\sqrt8}$ at velocity first crossings, which follows from Proposition 14D(a) on the ball centred at the
   record point of radius $R=2^k\Theta_0/D$, $k=\lfloor(h-C)/\sqrt8\rfloor$, whose smooth mean is at least $\Theta_0$ because consecutive dyadic means
   differ by at most $\sqrt8\Theta_0$ (it requires $16\sqrt2\,\kappa\alpha<\frac14$, and $16\sqrt2\,\kappa\alpha<\frac3{16}$ for the second choice of $D$ below, and fails for larger $\alpha$), together with its weaker relatives (the
   large-mean ball after Proposition 14C with 14D(a), the inequality of Proposition 14(d) with $\delta\le\sqrt8/h$, and the energy
   $E_0\ge c(\Theta_0^5/D^3)2^{3h/\sqrt8}$ of that ball) and the consequence $1+\mathcal I_{s_0}\ge c(\Theta_0^2/D)2^{h/\sqrt8}$ of Theorem 38(d) on
   it; and the identity and inequalities of Lemma 29, satisfied jointly by
   the scalar proxies $\Sigma'(t)=\Sigma_0+\int_{t_a}^tw\,ds$ for fixed $t_a$ and $\Sigma_0$ (eventually between $\tau^{-1/2}L^{-1}$ and $5\tau^{-1/2}L^{-1}$, since
   $-\frac d{d\tau}(2\tau^{-1/2}L^{-1})=w(1-2/L)$), $\mathcal K=w$, $Y=w/2$, $X_z=9w/16$ and $X_r=19w/16$ (with $X_r=\int|\partial_r\Gamma|^2r^{-3}dx$ and $X_z=\int|\partial_z\Gamma|^2r^{-3}dx$, so $X=X_r+X_z$), the signed work rate and the source rate $\|F\|_1$ equal to $w$ and the
   inward work rate equal to $(1+L^{-1})w$, so that the outward work rate is $L^{-1}w$ (the signed and the inward rate cannot be equal: if the inward rate is positive, $u^r$ is positive on an open set, since testing $\operatorname{div}u=0$ with $\phi(r)\psi(z/R)$, $\phi$ nonincreasing, and letting $R\to\infty$ shows that a finite-energy field with $u^r\le0$ everywhere has $u^r\equiv0$, and there $\Gamma$, which is real analytic in space at positive times and not identically zero, does not vanish identically; the factor $1+L^{-1}$ changes none of the comparisons in this paragraph): then $\frac d{dt}\Sigma'+2\mathcal K=3w$, $X_r\ge\frac94Y$ (so
   $\mathcal K\ge X_z+\frac34Y$), $\|F\|_1\le\frac2{\sqrt3}\mathcal K$, $\|F\|_1\le2(YX_z)^{1/2}$ and $Y\ge\pi^2S^6/(18G^2D^2)$ hold at every late time;
   and the inequalities of Proposition 41 and Corollary 42, satisfied with the further proxies (notation of Section 12: $\xi=\omega^r/r$, $P=\|q\|_2^2$) $\|\xi\|_2^2=\frac12\tau^{-2}$,
   $\|\eta\|_2^2=2\tau^{-2}$, $\|\nabla\xi\|_2^2=\frac12\tau^{-3}L^2$, $\|\nabla\eta\|_2^2=2\tau^{-3}L^2$ (so $H=\frac52\tau^{-2}$, $J=\frac52\tau^{-3}L^2$), $P=\tau^{-1}L^{-7/4}$,
   $\|u^\theta\|_\infty=S$ and the record point at distance $r_t=\|\nabla u\|_2/\|\eta\|_2=2^{-1/2}\tau^{1/2}L^{-3/4}$ from the axis (within $4\rho_*$, and $r_t\le12E_0D^2/(\pi M^4)$ eventually, as Lemma 28 requires): then the separate energy inequalities of $\xi$ and $\eta$ in the proof of
   Proposition 41(a) hold with left side $(1+L^{-2})/(2c_S)$ times the right side, $\frac12H'+J\le3S(HJ)^{1/2}$ (ratio $(1+L^{-2})/(3c_S)$), $H'\le\frac92S^2H$
   (ratio $4/(9c_S^2L^2)$), eventually $H^2\le\|\nabla u\|_2^2J$ and $H\le9S^2\|\nabla u\|_2^2$ (Proposition 41(d); ratios $\frac52L^{-1/2}$ and $\frac5{18c_S^2}L^{-1/2}$),
   $\|\nabla u\|_2^2\le\|\nabla u_0\|_2^2+E_0D$, $P^2\le\Sigma'Y$ (ratio at most $2L^{-3/2}$), $\|F\|_1\le2P^{1/2}\|\xi\|_2$ (ratio $2^{-1/2}L^{-1/8}$), and eventually
   $\Sigma'^2\le E_0P$ and $P\le\|\nabla u\|_2^2$ (ratio $L^{-1/4}$; from
   $\Sigma'=\int rq^2\le P^{1/2}\|u^\theta\|_2$ and $|q|\le|\nabla(u^\theta e_\theta)|$), $\|\xi\|_2^6\le4X_z^2\|\nabla\xi\|_2^2$ (ratio $\frac{16}{81}$; from
   $\|\xi\|_2^2\le X_z^{1/2}(\int\xi^2r^{-1})^{1/2}$), $\tau^{1/2}S=c_SL\to\infty$ (Corollary 42(a)), the right side of Corollary 42(c), bounded below by a positive multiple of
   $\rho_*^{3/2}\|\eta\|_2$, which is comparable to $\tau^{-1/4}$, exceeds its left side, at most of order $\tau^{-c_{33}\alpha}L^4$, as does the first right side of
   Corollary 42(d), at least of order $\tau^{-1/4}L^{1/4}$ (its second bound does not apply, the record point being within $4\rho_*$ of the axis), the inequalities of item (d) of the Remark on the shell-tested form of Corollary 58 if the second case of the localization occurs at the records (when $\theta_b=\Theta_0$, $h_b=\alpha L$ and $B\ge3c_{33}\alpha L+1-2\log2$; hence the lower bound $9M^2/(2048r_tB)$ for $\int_{\mathcal R}\eta(t)^2\,dx$ is at most a multiple, depending on $\alpha$ and $C_0$, of $L^{-1/4}\|\eta(t)\|_2^2$, which is compatible with the total energy but does not determine its distribution in $\mathcal R$; and for fixed $s_0$ the $\eta$ energy inequality $\frac12\frac d{dt}\|\eta\|_2^2+\|\nabla\eta\|_2^2\le\|q\|_4^2\|\nabla\eta\|_2$ with the values above forces $Q(s_0,t)\ge\tau^{-2}L^2(1-o(1))$, while the right side of the bound for $Q$ is at most $\frac{9\sqrt2}{1024}\tau^{-2}L^{3/4}(1+o(1))$; by item (e) the second case also requires $Q_\psi(s_0,t)\ge9M^2(1-o(1))/(2048r_tB(1+8\Lambda))$, and here $B\le\frac12L+O(\log L)$ and $\Lambda\le2L+O(1)$ (for fixed $s_0$ the integral $\int_{s_0}^t\|u\|_\infty\,ds$ is bounded, and $\rho_{k''}\ge M/(16A)$), so the required size is at least $\frac{9\sqrt2}{16384}\tau^{-2}L^{-5/4}(1-o(1))$; the part of $Q_\psi$ in $\{r\ge r_t/4\}$ is at most $\frac98\pi^2r_t^3\int_{s_0}^t\min\{D(s),16Gr_t^{-2}\}^4ds=O(\tau^{-11/6}L^{1/12})$ (by $|q|\le\|\nabla u\|_\infty$, $|q|\le Gr^{-2}$ and the conserved mass of $\psi$), so if the second case occurs at the records of a smooth solution with these scalar values, then $\int_{s_0}^t\!\int_{\{r<r_t/4\}}\psi q^4\,dx\,ds\ge\frac{9\sqrt2}{16384}\tau^{-2}L^{-5/4}(1-o(1))$; the barrier of item (ix) of the Remark (what the scalar histories leave open for the signed form) gives for this integral the upper bound $(\frac k2+16r_t^{-2})\int_{s_0}^t\|u^\theta\|_\infty^2P\,ds=O(\tau^{-9/4}L^{3/4})$, which neither contradicts the requirement nor shows that it can be met, and its term $16r_t^{-2}$ alone gives $32c_S^2\tau^{-2}L^{7/4}(1+o(1))$, so even an outflow rate $k=O(\tau^{-1})$ would leave this estimate inconclusive; item (vii) of the Remark on signed and cancellation-preserving forms of Lemma 40 gives the smaller bound $Q_\psi(s_0,t)\le Cc_S^4\tau^{-2}L(1+o(1))$ for the whole of $Q_\psi$, with an absolute constant $C$, because $\|\nabla u\|_2^2$ is nondecreasing near $T_*$ and the smoothing bound of item (vi) of that Remark gives $\varepsilon_\psi\le\int_{s_0}^t\min\{1,Cm(t-s)^{-3/2}\}\|\nabla u(s)\|_2^2\,ds\le3(Cm)^{2/3}\|\nabla u(t)\|_2^2+O(m)$ with $m=\int\chi_{\mathcal R}\,dx\le\frac98\pi^2r_t^3$, so that $\varepsilon_\psi=O(L^{-3})$; this bound exceeds the requirement by a factor of order $c_S^4L^{9/4}$ and is inconclusive as well (by item (e) of the Remark (radial transport required by the dual-weighted quartic) the quotient of the scalar majorant $Q^\sharp$ defined there by the requirement is at least a fixed multiple of $B^2/\ell$, here of order $L$ since $\ell=\frac14L+O(\log L)$, because the record lies at distance $\|\nabla u\|_2/\|\eta\|_2$ from the axis; so changing the logarithmic exponents of these histories while keeping that radius, the powers of $\tau$ in $M$ and $D$ and the logarithmic flatness scale does not close the gap for this majorant); and by the Remark (radial transport required by the dual-weighted quartic), with $\varepsilon=2/\log(D/S)$ at time $t$ (asymptotic to $2/L$, and then $w(t)=e^2S^4$), since $V(s_0)=O(m)$ and $w(t)A_\varepsilon/\varepsilon=O(\tau^{-3/2}L^{17/4})$ are small compared with the requirement, the second case at the records also requires that the $\psi q^4$-weighted mean of $r(-u^r)$ over $[s_0,t]$ be at least $\frac49-o(1)$ and that $J_\varepsilon\ge\varepsilon^2\frac{9\sqrt2}{16384}\tau^{-2}L^{-5/4}(1-o(1))$, while $(u^r/r)_+\le k$ and the bound $\int\psi(s)r^2dx\le m\sigma^2$, with $\sigma$ as in item (e) of the Remark on the shell-tested form of Corollary 58, which follows from the second-moment comparison of item (vi) of the Remark on signed and cancellation-preserving forms of Lemma 40 and the terminal bound $\int\chi_{\mathcal R}(r^2+(z-z_t)^2)\,dx\le\frac94r_t^2m$ (the inner torus has mean square distance $\frac54r_t^2$ from $(0,z_t)$, the support in $\{r\le r_t\}$ lies within distance $\frac54r_t$ of that point, and the support outside $\mathcal R$ in $\{r>r_t\}$ is contained in the outer half-annulus $(\mathcal R'\setminus\mathcal R)\cap\{r>r_t\}$, which lies within distance $\frac74r_t$ of $(0,z_t)$ and has volume $(\frac58+\frac{19}{24\pi})|\mathcal R|<|\mathcal R|$), bound $J_\varepsilon$ only by $O(\tau^{-2}L^{11/4})$), and
   Lemma 43 holds at the record point in both forms: in the first, if $\delta\ge M/(\sqrt2\pi D)$ the first term alone is at least $M$, and otherwise
   $\log(R/\delta)\ge\frac14L+O(\log L)$ for every $R\ge r_t$, so the second term divided by $M$ is at least of order $L^{1/8}$; in the second,
   $\|\eta\|_2\|\nabla u\|_2=r_t\|\eta\|_2^2=\sqrt2\,\tau^{-3/2}L^{-3/4}$, $R_x=r_t$ and $\log(DR_x/M)=\frac14L+O(\log L)$, so its right side exceeds $M^2=\tau^{-3/2}$ by a factor of order
   $L^{1/4}$ (with $\frac3{16}L$ in place of $\frac14L$ for the second choice of $D$ below). The radius matters here: a record point at distance at most
   $\tau^{1/2}L^{-5/4}$ from the axis violates the first form (take $R=\tau^{1/2}L^{-5/4}$ and $\delta=M/(DL)$; the right side divided by $M$ tends to $0$),
   although the second form holds there; Corollary 46 holds, the ratio of $H$ to $S^4\int_t^{T_*}\|\nabla u\|_2^2$
   being of order $L^{-7/2}$;
   these proxies do not represent constructed fields.
   The placement of the record point matters also in another way: the particular radius $r_t=L^{-8}$ for $D=A=M^2/L^4$, or $r_t=\tau^{1/8}L^{-8}$ for the second choice $D=A=\tau^{1/16}M^2/L^4$ below, although compatible with Lemma 28 up to fixed
   constants, violates the second bound of Corollary 42(d) (the ratio of its left side to its right side grows like $\tau^{-c_{33}\alpha}L^{3/4}$). The asymptotic comparisons reduce, up to fixed constants and bounded dyadic
   factors, to powers $\tau^aL^\ell$ with $a$ affine in $\kappa\alpha$ and $c_{33}\alpha$, and their exponents were checked by a symbolic computation
   over the whole parameter range; the dyadic spacing, the summability of the cost envelope and the joint proxy identities were checked
   separately. These specified scalar constraints are therefore satisfied jointly with $\Theta_0/M\to0$; this is not a verification of every
   scalar consequence of the report or of the equations, and it constructs no velocity field and no Navier-Stokes trajectory. The same checks also pass, with $\kappa\alpha\le\frac1{512}$ and $c_{33}\alpha\le\frac1{32}$, when $D=A=M^2/L^4$ is replaced by $D=A=\tau^{1/16}M^2/L^4$ (with the same $S$ and the same further proxies; the left side of
   Corollary 42(c) is then at most of order $\tau^{-1/16-c_{33}\alpha}L^4$, which needs $c_{33}\alpha<\frac3{16}$); for both
   choices one also has $SD/M^2\to\infty$ (so the bound of Corollary 44 holds eventually) and $(A/M^2)S\|\nabla u\|_2^2\tau\to\infty$ (the scalar forms of Lemma 34(b) with this dissipation rate),
   $A\tau\to\infty$ (so $\int A\,dt=\infty$) and $M^2\tau\to\infty$. Four inequalities that are not in the list above also hold on these histories for both choices of $D$: the energy inequality $\frac12P'+\|\xi\|_2^2\le2DP$ of $q=u^\theta/r$ (test the equation of $q$ with $q$, use $\xi=-\partial_zq$ and $|u^r/r|\le D$; the right side exceeds the left by a factor of order $\tau^{-1/2}L^{-23/4}$, times $\tau^{1/16}$ for the second choice), the inequality $\sigma'/\sigma+\sigma^2/G^2\le D$ for $\sigma=\|u^\theta\|_\infty$ at times of differentiability (the equation of $u^\theta$ at a point of maximum at distance $r_\sigma$ from the axis, with $r_\sigma\sigma\le G$), whose left side is of order $\tau^{-1}L^2$, $\|\xi\|_2^4\le P\|\partial_z\xi\|_2^2$ (integrate $\|\xi\|_2^2=\int q\,\partial_z\xi\,dx$ by parts), which holds with a factor $L^{1/4}$ to spare if the unspecified axial part $\|\partial_z\xi\|_2^2$ is taken to be $\frac12\|\nabla\xi\|_2^2$, and the bound $\int q^2(u^r)_-\,dx\le SP^{1/2}\|\nabla u\|_2$ for the inward work rate (since $q^2|u^r|=|u^\theta||q||u^r/r|$ and $|u^r/r|\le|\nabla u|$ pointwise), whose right side $c_S\tau^{-3/2}L^{-5/8}$ exceeds the inward work rate $(1+L^{-1})w$ by a factor of order $c_SL^{3/8}$. On the second choice $\log(M^2/D)=\log(M^2/A)=\frac{L-1}{16}+4\log L$, so
   $h/\log(M^2/D)\to16\alpha$: for a given $C>0$ and $\alpha<C/16$ (in the range above) a bound $h\le C\log(M^2/D)$ or $h\le C\log(M^2/A)$ at the
   records holds eventually while $h\to\infty$, so such a bound would not exclude (FLAT$_0$) by itself. On the first choice
   $h/\log(M^2/D)=\alpha L/(4\log L)\to\infty$, so it fails there. The swirl is taken larger than $(T_*-t)^{-1/2}$ by the factor $L$ because exactly Type I swirl is incompatible with these rates
   (Corollary 45: $M=\tau^{-3/4}$ at a record point within a fixed multiple of $\tau^{1/2}$ of the axis, and $D$ polynomial in $\tau^{-1}$); with the
   factor $L$ the proof of Corollary 45 needs $\int\tau^2\|u^\theta\|_\infty^4\|\nabla u\|_2^2\,dt<\infty$, which here is $\int c_S^4L^4\|\nabla u\|_2^2\,dt=\infty$ (Remark (ii) after Corollary 45).
   The results after Corollary 46 leave this family, as specified, untouched. With $\theta_b=\Theta_0$ at the records, $r_t/\rho_0=8\sqrt2\,\tau^{-1/4}L^{-19/4}$ ($\rho_0=M/(16A)$;
   $8\sqrt2\,\tau^{-3/16}L^{-19/4}$ for the second choice of $D$ above) is eventually larger than $2^{5h/(8C_0)}=(e/\tau)^{5c_{33}\alpha/2}$, because $\frac52c_{33}\alpha\le\frac5{32}<\frac14$
   (respectively $\le\frac5{64}<\frac3{16}$); so $p_q\le0$ (here $q=\lceil\log_2(r_t/\rho_0)\rceil$ is the octave count of Proposition 51, not the swirl quotient) and Proposition 51 is empty, and the family lies in the toroidal branch of the Remark on the two branches in Section 12.
   Theorem 49 and Corollary 50 do not exclude these scalar assignments: the swirl exceeds $\tau^{-1/2}L^{1/2}$, $\|u^\theta\|_\infty^2/(\tau H)=\frac25c_S^2L^2\to\infty$, $\int\tau^2\|u^\theta\|_\infty^4\|\nabla u\|_2^2\,dt=\infty$,
   and $\int\|u^\theta\|_\infty^2dt$ grows like $c_S^2L^3/3$, while Theorem 49(c) with $\hat h=\frac52$ asks only for order $L^2$; Lemma 48 holds (every late time lies in $\mathcal G$, the ratio
   $H/(36S^2\|\nabla u\|_2^2)$ being $\frac5{72}c_S^{-2}L^{-1/2}$). Read instead with the record point on the axis (octave count $q=0$), the same rates are excluded by Corollary 52(a): the ratio of $\Lambda$
   to $S^2\|\nabla u\|_2^2$ grows like $\tau^{-5c_{33}\alpha/2}L^{-5/2}$, while $M^{8/3}\tau^2=1$ violates the other alternative; Corollary 52(b) fails as well. A family of this
   kind must therefore keep its record point many octaves of $\rho_0$ away from the axis.
   A sufficient, unproved estimate for
   excluding (FLAT$_0$) in regime (II) is $W_{s_0}(t)D(t)/M(t)^2\le C(1+h(t))^p$ at every sufficiently late velocity first crossing $t$, for fixed
   $s_0$, $C$ and $p$ (a special case of the bound with $\Upsilon$ in item 1). Indeed, since $S=o(M)$ the records eventually satisfy
   $|b(x_t,t)|\ge\frac78M(t)$, and Proposition 33 with $A\le\sqrt2D$ gives $W_{s_0}(t)D(t)/M(t)^2\ge c\,2^{cM(t)/\Theta_0(t)}-C$ there, so $h$ is bounded at
   those crossings; for an arbitrary late $t$ the earliest time $s(t)$ with $M(s(t))=M(t)$ is a velocity first crossing, and $h(t)\le h(s(t))$
   because $\Theta_0$ is nondecreasing. Hence $h=O(1)$ near $T_*$, contradicting (FLAT$_0$). The same argument needs only the weaker
   single-time hypothesis $Q_{4\rho_*}(x_t,t)A(t)/M(t)^2\le C(1+h(t))^p$ at those crossings, for the meridional mass within $4\rho_*$ of the
   record point with $\rho_*=C_2L_E(M(t))$ as in Proposition 33, since that proposition gives $Q_{4\rho_*}(x_t,t)A/M^2\ge c_1\,2^{k_*}$ with
   $k_*\ge\lfloor h/(4C_0)\rfloor$ at the same crossings. The three forms are ordered: the work form implies the source form
   $\mathcal I_{s_0}(t)A/M^2\le C'(1+h)^p$ by Lemma 29(b), $A\le\sqrt2D$ and $\Sigma'(s_0)A/M^2\to0$, and the source form implies the mass form
   (again with another constant) by the split in the proof of Proposition 30, since $2\pi Q_{4\rho_*}(x_t,t)\le\|\eta(s_0)\|_\infty|T|+\mathcal I_{s_0}(t)$ for the solid of revolution $T$ of that
   meridional disc, $|T|\to0$ (Lemma 28) and $M^2/A\to\infty$.
   Proposition 14D adds that a large mean persists along its own path for a time (the persistence time of Proposition 14D(b)) at least $ce^{\kappa h_n}/(h_nm_n^2)$ between
   dyadic records and, for $t_{n-1}\le t<t_n$, bounds $M(t)^2(T_*-t)$ below by $ce^{\kappa h_{n+1}}/h_{n+1}$ in terms of the flatness
   ratio $h_{n+1}=M(t_{n+1})/\Theta_0(t_{n+1})$ of the next dyadic record (whenever $h_{n+1}\ge h_*$); under (FLAT$_0$) this
   bound tends to infinity, but the dissipation certified by the proposition is summable. By the Remark on the pressure
   impulse after Proposition 14D: at every sufficiently large dyadic record with $h_n\ge h_*$, the signed pressure impulse
   $J_n$ along a backward-diffusing packet, over the age interval $\varsigma_n=KE_0^{2/3}m_n^{-4/3}$ (here $K$ is an absolute constant of that Remark, not the constant of Lemma 9'), is at least $m_n/2$, and the
   older averaged velocity has norm at most $m_n/4$. A bound $J_n\le C(u_0)\Theta_0(t_n)$ on an infinite subsequence would exclude
   (FLAT$_0$); no such bound is proved.
1''. For general bounded divergence-free families a time-integrated curl bound gives no compactness of the terminal
   slice at the original terminal time (an explicit family with a uniformly bounded time-integrated curl has terminal
   slices that converge only weakly, Section 8). For Navier-Stokes trajectories this gap is closed by Lemma 21: (EC), and
   hence (VC), implies (L2M), so with (OSC$_0$) GOAL holds in $C_tL^2_{loc}$ at the physical terminal time (Proposition 22);
   the rules of Theorem 17 and Proposition 20(b) remain correct but are not needed for this conclusion. Theorem 17 shows that this is not an obstruction to GOAL in the
   topology (ST): for an admissible normalization with (OSC$_0$) and a joint subsequence on which the Reynolds defect vanishes ($\mathcal R=0$), an
   explicit rule selects a slice within one Euler time before $s_n$; along a further subsequence the slices converge
   strongly there and the terminal limit is nonconstant. For a certified normalization what remains
   open is (EC) (or (VC)) itself on the physical records; the gap between (ST) and the $C_tL^2_{loc}$ convergence of
   Theorem 5 remains only for defect-free normalizations without such a vorticity certificate. Proposition 16(b)
   gives convergence at the original terminal time when the limit is classical near $\tau=0$ with bounded gradient and time derivative; a certificate for this on
   the physical records is missing. Proposition 15
   needs a terminal quiet zone and a far-tail bound at amplitude $o(M)$ that are not derived; the available far-field
   bound for the time-integrated tail is $16\pi A_nW_n^2/R_n$ with $W_n=M(t_n)/B_n$, and no estimate relates these quantities.
   Proposition 20 weakens (VC) to the exterior bound (EC). On the (C*-D) side a sufficient route (not a proved
   equivalent) is (EC) for one certified normalization, that is, a bound on the meridional vorticity at distance at least $h\ell_n$ from the axis that
   is inherited from earlier times or carried out of the swirl core; the swirl source itself is negligible there
   (Lemma 19), and nothing controls this inherited vorticity (Section 9B, Remark (iii)); by Lemma 26A it is carried, up to $o(1)$, by fluid
   that did not visit the tube $\{r_n\le a_n\}$, $a_n\to0$, during the last $A_n\to\infty$ Euler times, which neither locates its
   generation (diffusion moves vorticity between particles) nor bounds it; Lemma 21 propagates such a bound
   forward over bounded Euler times but does not create one. By Proposition 25, for single-time fields the failure of
   the exterior mass bound at every certified scale is compatible with compactness of the certified rescalings, so (EC)
   may be strictly stronger than the exclusion of (C) (a strict separation for trajectories is not proved), and (EC)
   cannot follow from the single-time constraints tested there alone. Bounds of order $M_n\ell_n^2$ on the dissipation of
   every fixed rescaled window of one certified normalization would give (EC) (Section 9D, Remark (ii)); they are not proved.
2. Approaches that were tried and fail, with the reason:
   the global bound on $\|\omega^\theta\|_1+\int\!\!\int|\omega^\theta|/r^2$ off the axis (it gives the vorticity
   certificate only at axis distances $r_0\gtrsim K/(M\ell)$, while a size-$M$ structure of scale $\ell$ at distance $r_0$
   has energy $\sim M^2\ell^2r_0$, forcing $r_0\lesssim E_0/(M^2\ell^2)\ll K/(M\ell)$);
   the centrifugal reformulation (the meridional field solves swirl-free Navier-Stokes with a forcing
   $(u^\theta)^2/r\,e_r$ whose rescaled local $L^1$ norm is $O(GD/M^2)\to0$; vanishing forcing gives no compactness) and
   backward inheritance of fine-scale vorticity along the meridional flow;
   the signed comparison with the swirl-free evolution of the same meridional datum (for a fixed $s_0<T_*$ let $b^0$ be
   the axisymmetric swirl-free Navier-Stokes solution, with pressure $p^0$ and $b^0(s_0)=b(s_0)$, global and smooth by the classical theorem for data without swirl, and
   for $s_0<t<T_*$ and a smooth, divergence-free, axisymmetric terminal field $\mathfrak g$ without swirl, with $\mathfrak g\in\bigcap_kH^k$, let $\mathfrak a$ solve
   $-\partial_s\mathfrak a-\Delta\mathfrak a-(b\cdot\nabla)\mathfrak a+(\nabla b^0)^T\mathfrak a+\nabla\mathfrak p=0$, $\nabla\cdot\mathfrak a=0$, $\mathfrak a(t)=\mathfrak g$ on $[s_0,t]$, where the $j$-th component of $(\nabla b^0)^T\mathfrak a$ is $\sum_i\mathfrak a_i\partial_jb^0_i$;
   pairing $\mathfrak a$ with the equation $\partial_s(b-b^0)-\Delta(b-b^0)+(b\cdot\nabla)(b-b^0)+((b-b^0)\cdot\nabla)b^0+\nabla(p-p^0)=(u^\theta)^2r^{-1}e_r$
   gives the exact formula $\int(b-b^0)(t)\cdot\mathfrak g\,dx=\int_{s_0}^t\!\int(u^\theta)^2r^{-1}\mathfrak a^r\,dx\,ds$, and since the transport by the
   divergence-free field $b$ drops out of the energy identity of $\mathfrak a$, $\|\mathfrak a(s)\|_2^2+2\int_s^t\|\nabla\mathfrak a\|_2^2\,d\sigma\le e^{2K}\|\mathfrak g\|_2^2$
   with $K=\int_{s_0}^{T_*}\|\nabla b^0\|_\infty\,ds<\infty$, a finite constant determined by $b(s_0)$ and $T_*-s_0$ that requires no control of later gradients of $b$; with the pointwise bounds
   $|\mathfrak a^r|/r\le|\nabla\mathfrak a|$ and $(u^\theta)^4\le G^2(u^\theta/r)^2\le G^2|\nabla u|^2$ (gradients in $\mathbb R^3$ with the Frobenius norm, of which $\mathfrak a^r/r$ and $-u^\theta/r$ are entries in cylindrical components), and $\int_0^{T_*}\|\nabla u\|_2^2\le E_0/2$, the
   Cauchy-Schwarz inequality bounds the response by $\frac12GE_0^{1/2}e^K\|\mathfrak g\|_2$, so $|\int b(t)\cdot\mathfrak g\,dx|\le(1+\frac12Ge^K)E_0^{1/2}\|\mathfrak g\|_2$,
   which is weaker than $\|b(t)\|_2\|\mathfrak g\|_2\le E_0^{1/2}\|\mathfrak g\|_2$; tested at sufficiently late velocity first crossings in regime (II), where $|b|\ge(M^2-S^2)^{1/2}=(1-o(1))M$ at the record point, on the Leray projection of the meridional part of the ring average of a
   mollified evaluation of $b$ at scale $cM/D$ with a small fixed $c>0$ (which pairs with $b$ as the evaluation does) and with the bound of its $L^2$ norm by that of the mollifier, it gives an inequality of the form $M^5\le CE_0D^3$ of Lemma 2, which is compatible
   with regime (II); no adjoint estimate that improves this record bound uniformly as $t\uparrow T_*$, with constants controlled by fixed-past data, is established here,
   and this calculation supplies no counterpart of the maximum principle of the dual of Lemma 40; nor is an $L^1$ estimate for $\mathfrak a$ established here: the projected evaluation tests can be nonintegrable, and the same linear adjoint system with general smooth coefficients need not preserve $L^1$, since the Fourier transform of an integrable field is continuous while that of the Leray projection $\mathbb Pf$ of an integrable field $f$ with $\mathbb Pf\in L^2$ tends to $(I-e\otimes e)\int f\,dx$ along each unit direction $e$, so $\mathbb Pf$ is integrable only if $\int f\,dx=0$: the test field above is not integrable when the evaluated direction has a nonzero axial component, and for the same system with $b=0$, $b^0$ replaced by $\varepsilon w$ ($w$ nonzero, smooth, compactly supported, divergence-free and axisymmetric without swirl, $\varepsilon>0$) and $\mathfrak g=\partial_zw$, the forcing $\varepsilon(\nabla w)^T\mathfrak a$ is compactly supported with $z$-mean $\varepsilon\int|\partial_zw|^2dx>0$ at time $t$, so by Duhamel's formula $\|\mathfrak a(s)\|_1=\infty$ for $s<t$ close to $t$, however small $\varepsilon$ is (this example does not have $b^0$ evolving by the swirl-free Navier-Stokes equations from $b(s_0)$, so it concerns the linear system only); worked out with a second-model strategy answer and checked by hand);
   Kolmogorov-scale normalizations (local limits such as a Burgers vortex in a strain are unbounded);
   amplitude-$M$ gradient-record normalization at length $M/D$ in regime (II) (only constant limits under (UP));
   energy and temporal certificates (both fail under (UP): the first by Lemma 2 and the definition of (UP), the second by Proposition 7);
   the energy-scale normalization (bounded energy and total dissipation do not exclude a Reynolds defect; frozen
   laminated shear layers are stationary for Euler);
   local-amplitude rescalings (the far-field pressure is not controlled; without a sufficient tail estimate such as a
   quiet zone a spatially uniform force may survive);
   the BMO-amplitude extraction in a constant Galilean frame and the BMO-class Liouville route (Proposition 14B:
   it proves only $D\le C_a+C\Theta_0^2$ and, along sequences with $D(t_n)\ge\delta\Theta_0(t_n)^2$, subsequential nonconstant ancient limits,
   axisymmetric with nonzero swirl after a constant boost, that are not shown to be bounded; the axisymmetric Liouville
   case with swirl remains and contains the bounded-circulation case of the KNSS conjecture, open in the literature we
   read (Remark (iii) after Proposition 14B), and its positive resolution would force $D/\Theta_0^2\to0$, a consequence compatible with scalar rates that satisfy (FLAT$_0$), so no exclusion of (FLAT$_0$) is derived from it);
   backward uniqueness for the limits of Proposition 14B(c) (if such a limit $v$ had $\operatorname{curl}v(\cdot,0)=0$ outside a ball,
   then $v(\cdot,0)$ would be the Biot-Savart field of a bounded compactly supported vorticity plus an entire harmonic field of
   logarithmic growth, hence bounded; the bound would extend to every finite backward slab (the common facts of
   Proposition 14B(b) bound $\nabla^2v$ and part (a) bounds $\Pi[v]$, and integrating $dv/d\sigma=\Delta v-\Pi[v]$ along the
   material curves of the globally Lipschitz field $v$ gives $\|v(\sigma)\|_\infty\le\|v(0)\|_\infty+C|\sigma|$), and the exterior backward
   uniqueness and unique continuation theorems of Escauriaza, Seregin and Sverak, in versions reported by the second model
   and not read by us, would make $v$ constant; but nothing gives exterior vanishing of the terminal normalized vorticity
   $\Theta_n^{-2}\omega(x_n+\Theta_n^{-1}y,s_n)$ on the physical records, and
   such a rigidity conclusion for every sequence covered by 14B(c) would give $D/\Theta_0^2\to0$, and hence
   $\kappa_n\to\infty$ for the sequences of Remark (ii) after Proposition 14B, which still leaves (FLAT$_0$) open);
   a Gaussian frequency for $q=u^\theta/r$ (with $q$ lifted radially to $\mathbb R^4\times\mathbb R$, $X_0$ fixed on the axis,
   $F(\xi,s)=q(X_0+\sqrt{T_*-t}\,\xi,t)$, $s=-\log(T_*-t)$ and $d\mu=(4\pi)^{-5/2}e^{-|\xi|^2/4}d\xi$ on $\mathbb R^5$, the quotient
   $N=\|\nabla F\|_{L^2(\mu)}^2/\|F\|_{L^2(\mu)}^2$, defined where $\|F\|_{L^2(\mu)}>0$, satisfies
   $dN/ds\le(T_*-t)M^2N+4(T_*-t)^2D^2$, and at the scalar rates of Remark (ii) after Proposition 14B neither coefficient is
   integrable in $s$; centring on an axial material curve bounds the remaining drift by $C(T_*-t)D|\xi|$, an available
   bound whose prefactor also diverges at those rates, so the inequality gives no uniform frequency control there; and a
   bound on $N$ alone would not give oscillation of the velocity at amplitude $M$);
   the Hessian at gradient records (controls a point, not a neighbourhood);
   the global $L^1$ bound for $\eta$ (deficit $(E_0M)^{1/2}$) and the a priori $L^1$ bound for $\omega^\theta$ with
   swirl (Lemma 9', supercritical);
   Euler Liouville arguments (false: the steady jet $e^{-r^2}e_z$, whose canonical pressure is constant, and, in the
   laboratory frame, the uniformly translating spherical vortex of M. J. M. Hill (Phil. Trans. R. Soc. Lond. A 185
   (1894)), a Lipschitz weak solution with velocity $O(|x|^{-3})$ at infinity, are nonconstant bounded ancient swirl-free
   Euler solutions with canonical pressure; the second is classical and was not re-derived here);
   dissipation budgets (they exclude only cascades whose required cumulative dissipation exceeds the energy budget; dissipating a fixed fraction of each window energy does not, when the window energies are summable over the levels, as in the bookkeeping of Proposition 24, Remark (i), and frozen layers evade the argument as well);
   viscous cancellation of the two signed masses of Corollary 6A (the cancellation rate $2\nu\int_{\{w=0\}}|\nabla w|\,d\mathcal H^1$
   at the sign changes of the normalized meridional vorticity $w$ admits no lower bound of the alternating-layer form
   $c\nu m^3$, $m$ the smaller signed mass on a fixed rectangle: exact swirl-free pipe flows $f(r,t)e_z$ (independent of $z$, hence of infinite energy, and changing with $k$), with $f$ solving
   the radial heat equation from $f(r,0)=\chi(r)F(\pi kr^2)$, where $F(\theta)=\frac98\cos\theta-\frac18\cos3\theta$ has flat extrema
   ($F'=-\frac32\sin^3\theta$) and the cutoff $\chi$ equals $1$ near $[1,\sqrt2]$ and vanishes near $r=0$, keep both signed masses of
   order $k$ on $[1,\sqrt2]\times[0,1]$ for $k^{1/4}$ Euler times at normalized viscosity $k^{-13/4}$, while their total, the
   meridional mass $\int_1^{\sqrt2}|\partial_rf|\,dr=2k$ of that rectangle, decreases only by $O(k^{-1})$: at heat time $\nu t=k^{-3}$
   the exact radial heat flow multiplies the modes $e^{\pm i\pi kr^2}$ and $e^{\pm3i\pi kr^2}$ by $e^{-x}$ and $e^{-9x}$, $x=4\pi^2r^2/k$,
   up to perturbations $O(k^{-2})$ in sup norm, the cutoff contributes $O(e^{-ck^3})$ in $C^1$ on $[1,\sqrt2]$, and at the $k+1$
   points where $\pi kr^2\in\pi\mathbb Z$ the values $\pm1$ change by $\frac98(1-e^{-x})-\frac18(1-e^{-9x})+O(k^{-2})=\frac92x^2+O(k^{-2})$,
   so the variation through these points, which bounds the mass from below, is $2k-O(k^{-1})$; the flat extrema matter,
   since for $F=\cos$ the values change by $1-e^{-x}\approx x$ and the loss tends to $12\pi^2$);
   concentration-cancellation with record freezing (window weak limits solve the Euler-Reynolds system with a nonnegative
   defect, which vanishes exactly when the convergence is strong; with $\epsilon^p_n=\sup_{s\le t_n}\|\nabla p(s)\|_\infty/(M_nD_n)$,
   which tends to $0$ under (UP) by Proposition 7(a), Proposition 7(b) bounds the relative record increase over $A$ window
   Euler times $\ell_n/M_n$ only by $A\epsilon^p_n\sigma_n$, $\sigma_n=\ell_nD_n/M_n$, and $\sigma_n\to\infty$ in (C) for
   normalizations with (OSC$_0$) (Proposition 22(d)), so this bound does not show that the record is frozen; and even a frozen record does
   not force a window mean to be saturated: stationary Euler pipe flows $f_n(r)e_z$ with a plateau at the axis and an
   oscillating annulus have frozen records, vanishing subscale flux and a positive defect);
   a near-axis cancellation functional (with $D_t=\partial_t+u^r\partial_r+u^z\partial_z$, $\Delta_5=\partial_r^2+3r^{-1}\partial_r+\partial_z^2$,
   gradients taken in $(r,z)$ and expressions on the axis read through the smooth axisymmetric extension, $q=u^\theta/r$, $Q=\partial_zq$ and $\phi=\psi/r$, where $b=\operatorname{curl}(\psi e_\theta)$, so that $-\Delta_5\phi=\eta$, $H=\frac12(Q^2+\eta^2)$ satisfies
   $(D_t-\Delta_5)H=-|\nabla Q|^2-|\nabla\eta|^2+Q\mathcal E$ with $\mathcal E=-2q(\phi_{rr}+3\phi_r/r)+rq_r\phi_{zz}-r\phi_{rz}Q$, and on
   the axis $Q\mathcal E=-8qQ\phi_{rr}$, which has no sign; for data with $\phi=A+cr^2$, $q=q_0+q_1z$ near a point of the axis, $c<0<q_0,q_1$,
   one gets $D_tH=-16cq_1q>0$ there at the initial time while $\nabla Q=\nabla\eta=0$);
   selection by production events (the source records $\max_{s\le t}\|\partial_z(q^2)(s)\|_\infty$ are physical events and, in (C),
   grow faster than $D$ along the times of Corollary 6A, but a source maximum does not force retained velocity oscillation:
   in a smooth compactly supported single-time family, a jet $n^{24}e_z$ on a ball of radius $n^{-16}$ contains a swirl packet
   of amplitude $n^{18}$ and radius $n^{-24}$ that carries the source maximum $x_*$, and a remote packet of amplitude $n^{21}$
   and radius $n^{-21}$ fixes $S\asymp n^{21}$, with $M\asymp n^{24}$, $D\asymp n^{42}$; for every $B\in[S,M]$ the rescaling
   $(u(x_*+(B/D)y)-n^{24}e_z)/B$ is $O(n^{-3})$ on bounded sets; the budget of the Remark after Lemma 9' is compatible with
   the available upper bound and is not assigned to windows);
   finite-energy closure of pipes and an energy charge for record increases (proposed constructions, not established
   here: for smooth compactly supported radial $f_0$ with $\int_0^\infty rf_0\,dr=0$, the solution $f$ of
   $f_t=\nu(f_{rr}+r^{-1}f_r)$, $f(\cdot,0)=f_0$, $a=r^{-1}\int_0^r\rho f\,d\rho$ and an axial cutoff $\chi\in C_c^\infty(\mathbb R)$ equal to
   $1$ near $0$, the field $V_L=(-L^{-1}a\chi'(z/L),0,f\chi(z/L))$ in cylindrical components $(r,\theta,z)$ is divergence
   free, has finite energy and solves Navier-Stokes with zero pressure up to a residual of order $L^{-1/2}$ in $H^s$ on a
   fixed time interval; applied to the profiles of the item on viscous cancellation this suggests exact finite-energy
   solutions with changing data, $D/M^2\to0$ and windows with (L2-OSC) but without (L2M), but a stability estimate with
   its dependence on $L$, on $k$ and on the divergent number of Euler times, the diagonal choice of the parameters and
   the verification of the historical record properties after rescaling are not carried out here; separately, a suitably
   positioned remote packet can have a canonical-pressure contribution that accelerates a constant core $e_z$ carrying the
   speed maximum (the sign depends on the position of the packet as well as on the distribution of its kinetic energy),
   and for a fixed smooth profile at amplitude $M$ and scale $\ell$ the dissipation over one Euler time is of order $(M\ell)^{-1}$
   times the energy, the ratio of the viscous cost $M\ell^2$ of an Euler window to its energy $M^2\ell^3$; using this for an
   exact-solution family needs control of its normalized enstrophy over that time; these observations suggest that
   record increases need not be charged by dissipation, but neither this nor a fixed-factor increase of the global
   record of an actual solution within one Euler time is established; neither proposal is used as a counterexample, and
   neither involves swirl);
   pigeonhole over certified windows with the local budget of Lemma 9' (the total weight of certified windows with boundedly
   overlapping persistence cylinders is finite by energy, Proposition 24, and absolute continuity of the budget gives no rate);
   choice of scale at a certified point using the bound $\int_{B_R}|w|\le\sqrt2|B_R|\sigma$ (single-time obstruction,
   Proposition 25);
   the Lagrangian form of the swirl source (for Euler, with labels $a=(r_0,z_0)$, $r_0>0$, at time $t_1$ and the meridional flow
   map $X$, Cauchy's formula gives $(\det D_aX)\,\omega^\theta(X(a,t),t)=\omega^\theta(a,t_1)+\frac12\{\Gamma(t_1)^2,\rho\}_a$, where
   $\{f,g\}=f_rg_z-f_zg_r$ and $\rho=\int_{t_1}^tr(X(a,s),s)^{-2}ds$, so for a compact label set $E\subset\{r_0>0\}$, with
   $\Phi=(\Gamma(t_1)^2,\rho)$ and $N_E(\xi)$ the number of points of $E$ mapped to $\xi$, the area formula gives the unweighted
   meridional mass of the net source contribution $\omega^\theta(X(a,t),t)-(\det D_aX)^{-1}\omega^\theta(a,t_1)$ on $X(E,t)$ as
   $\frac12\int_{\mathbb R^2}N_E(\xi)\,d\xi\le\frac12|\Phi(E)|\operatorname{ess\,sup}N_E$ (this does not control the time integral of the
   absolute source); no bound on $N_E$, and none on the viscous remainders of the Navier-Stokes version, which contain
   brackets with $\rho$, is proved, and the kinematic information alone gives none: if the meridional velocity vanishes
   on an annular region, then $\rho=(t-t_1)/r_0^2$ there, and a swirl whose square runs $k$ times through the same range in
   $z_0$ gives $N_E=k$ almost everywhere on the image; such prescribed fields are not Euler solutions, so this does not
   exclude a bound that uses the dynamics; iterating over blocks of Euler times returns to the history problem of Section 9B);
   quantitative Liouville theorems across scales and backward propagation of concentration (in viscous units
   $v_n(X,t)=M_n^{-1}u(x_n+X/M_n,s_n+t/M_n^2)$ the Euler window has radius $L_n=M_n\ell_n\to\infty$ and, on windows with retained
   oscillation, the history has depth $s_nM_n^2$, much larger than $L_n^2$, so time depth is not the obstruction; but the maximum-principle lemma behind the
   Liouville theorem of Koch, Nadirashvili, Seregin and Sverak has constants that depend on the domain and the drift
   bound and degenerate with the radius: $w=\tanh(Y_5/2)$ solves $w_t-w\,\partial_5w-\Delta w=0$ in $\mathbb R^5$ with drift of size
   at most $1$, its supremum on $B_{2R}$ is $\tanh R$, its value at $(R/2)e_5$ is smaller only by the factor
   $1-\tanh(R/4)/\tanh R=2e^{-R/2}(1+o(1))$, and it vanishes at the centre; this drift is not a Navier-Stokes velocity, so the
   example only shows that a scale-uniform version needs further structure; the swirl source, of size $O(\epsilon)$ when
   the normalized swirl is $\epsilon$ and three derivatives are bounded, costs an error $O(\epsilon R^2)$ over viscous time $R^2$; the
   Holder estimate of Lei and Zhang (arXiv 1011.5066) for $\Gamma$ at the axis depends on a scale-invariant drift quantity
   which, at radii comparable to $R$, is at least of order $R$ on the exact pipe flows
   $\frac{R^2}{2R^2+t}e^{-r^2/(4(2R^2+t))}e_z$, $-R^2\le t\le0$, which are bounded by $1$; and the Liouville theorem of Lei, Zhang and
   Zhao (arXiv 1701.00868) for solutions with swirl assumes $\Gamma\in L^\infty_tL^p_x$, a norm multiplied by $M_n^{3/p}$ under
   this rescaling; on the other hand, failure of (L2M) at shifts $\rho_n$ with $M_n^2/D_n\ll\rho_n\ll L_n$ in these units gives
   $\int_{B_{(R+2)L_n}}|\omega_n|^2\ge c_RL_n^3/\rho_n^2$ at the failing time, but the coefficients of the vorticity equation
   rescaled to that shift, of sizes $\rho_n$ and $\rho_n^2D_n/M_n^2$, diverge, and the backward enstrophy charge this gives
   over the last $M_n^2/D_n$ units of viscous time is $o(M_nE_0)$, below the available dissipation budget; the critical $L^3$
   bound used in Tao's quantitative argument for the Escauriaza-Seregin-Sverak theorem is not supplied by energy);
   normalization at records of $r|u^{mer}|$, with $u^{mer}=u^re_r+u^ze_z$ the meridional velocity and $r$ the distance to the
   axis (a singular solution cannot satisfy $|u|\le C/r$ on $[0,T_*)$, by results of Koch, Nadirashvili, Seregin and Sverak
   and of Chen, Strain, Tsai and Yau, not re-checked here, and $r|u^\theta|\le G$, so $\sup_{0\le s<T_*}\|r\,u^{mer}(s)\|_\infty=\infty$;
   this may hold already at $s=0$, since the data class does not bound $\|r\,u^{mer}(0)\|_\infty$, so finite attained records
   are not established; if a finite positive record $\sup_{0\le s\le s_A}\|r\,u^{mer}(s)\|_\infty=r_AA\in(0,\infty)$ is attained at a point $x_A$ at time
   $s_A$, with $r_A=r(x_A)>0$ and $A=|u^{mer}(x_A,s_A)|>0$, the rescaling $U(y,\tau)=A^{-1}u^{mer}(x_A+r_Ay,s_A+r_A\tau/A)$ satisfies
   $|U(0,0)|=1$ and $|U(y,\tau)|\le r_A/r(x_A+r_Ay)$ for $-s_AA/r_A\le\tau\le0$ and $r(x_A+r_Ay)>0$, so it is bounded away from the rescaled axis,
   but neither the existence of such records with $s_A\uparrow T_*$ nor the divergence of the backward depth $s_AA/r_A$ is
   proved, $U$ is not shown to be bounded near the rescaled axis, its gradient $r_AA^{-1}\nabla u^{mer}$ is not shown to be
   bounded, and with amplitude $M(s_A)$ in place of $A$ the retained value $A/M(s_A)$ is not shown to stay away from $0$;
   for the two fields $f$ of the Remark after Proposition 14C, which are single-time fields and not solutions, with
   $m=\|f\|_\infty$ and the parameters $a$, $L$ of that Remark, a point $x_A$ where $r|f|$ is maximal lies, for large $L$, at
   distance $r_A=r(x_A)$ comparable to the outer radius $ae^L$, $|f(x_A)|$ is comparable to $m/L$, and
   $r_A\|\nabla f\|_\infty/|f(x_A)|$ is of order $e^L$, since $\|\nabla f\|_\infty$ is of order $m/(La)$);
   Holder regularity near the axis as possible input for the Euler-length bounds of Constantin, Ignatova and Vicol
   (Limitations 6) (fix $0<t<T_*$, put $D=D(t)>0$ and $q=u^\theta/r$, and choose $\rho>0$ with $\rho^2\le t$; smoothness at
   the axis and the gradient bound give $|q|\le D$, hence $|\Gamma|\le Dr^2$ for $\Gamma=ru^\theta$, throughout the preceding
   history; the radial lift of $\eta=\omega^\theta/r$ to $\mathbb R^4\times\mathbb R$ solves
   $(\partial_\sigma+\tilde b\cdot\nabla-\Delta_5)\eta=\partial_z(q^2)$ with $\tilde b=(u^rY/r,u^z)$ and
   $\Delta_5=\partial_r^2+3r^{-1}\partial_r+\partial_z^2$; with $a'(\sigma)=u^z(0,a(\sigma),\sigma)$, $r_y=|y'|$ and
   $\sigma_s=t+\rho^2s$, the function $\tilde\eta(y,s)=(\rho/D)\eta(\rho r_y,a(\sigma_s)+\rho y_5,\sigma_s)$ on $B^5_1\times[-1,0]$ solves
   $\tilde\eta_s+\mathcal B\cdot\nabla\tilde\eta-\Delta_5\tilde\eta=\operatorname{div}_y\tilde F$, where $\mathcal B$ is $\rho$ times the lifted velocity increment from the
   moving axis point and $\tilde F=(\rho^2/D)q^2e_5$, so that $\|\mathcal B\|_\infty+\|\operatorname{div}_y\mathcal B\|_\infty+\|\tilde F\|_\infty\le CD\rho^2$ (the
   lifted drift is not divergence free, $\operatorname{div}_y\mathcal B=2\rho^2u^r/r$), while $|\tilde\eta|\le2/r_y$ from $|\omega^\theta|\le2D$ is
   locally square integrable in five dimensions; for $D\rho^2\le c_0$ interior De Giorgi-Nash-Moser estimates bound $\tilde\eta$
   and its parabolic Holder seminorm on the half cylinder, and multiplication by $r\le\rho/2$ gives
   $\sup_{t-\rho^2/4\le\sigma\le t}\rho^\alpha[\omega^\theta(\cdot,\sigma)]_{C^\alpha(B_{\rho/2}((0,0,a(\sigma))))}\le CD$ with $0<\alpha<1$
   and $C$ independent of $t$, $\rho$ and the axis point; this argument gives uniform normalized control only at lengths
   $\rho\lesssim D^{-1/2}$, which at the scalar rates of Remark (ii) after Proposition 14B, with $\tau=T_*-t$ and
   $L=\log(e/\tau)$, is $D^{-1/2}=\tau^{3/4}L=o(\sqrt\tau)$, whereas the Euler length $\ell(t)$ of that criterion satisfies
   $\sqrt\tau=o(\ell(t))$ by definition and, if its velocity bound holds on the whole space, $\ell(t)\ge c\tau\|u(t)\|_\infty$,
   hence $\ell(t)\ge c\tau^{1/4}$ at global velocity records at those rates; these comparisons concern scalar rates, not a
   constructed solution, and the argument does not establish the hypotheses of that criterion at its Euler length;
   worked out with a second-model research answer and checked by hand, in the form requested by a second-model audit);
   spatial power bounds $|u|\le Cr^{-\alpha}$, $|\omega^\theta|\le Cr^{-\gamma}$ with $\alpha>1$, $\gamma>2$ used as single-time bounds (Proposition 25(d)), and
   qualitative off-axis regularity (certified windows approach the axis, Corollary 26).
3. In alternative (B) the selection is an existence statement for a scale; Theorem 5' gives an explicit sufficient
   rule defined from an observable, and whether (B) implies (L2LS) is open. The same holds in (B*): Corollary 18
   replaces the retrospective end-time shift of Theorem 12 and the anchor by rules computed from the trajectory, but it
   starts from a (B*) normalization that is itself only shown to exist. So the exclusion of (C), or of (C*), would give
   the analytic requirements of GOAL for an existentially selected normalization; a selection rule from records for
   every trajectory needs in addition a criterion such as (L2LS), or (EC) for the outputs of a prescribed rule producing (OSC$_0$) normalizations
   (the Remark at the end of Section 6 states what is known about rules in (B)). The regime split is an asymptotic predicate of the trajectory.
   Normalization classes other than those of Sections 5 to 9 (componentwise amplitudes,
   non-ball windows) are not exhausted.
4. Off-axis singular points are excluded by the Caffarelli-Kohn-Nirenberg partial regularity theorem together with
   axisymmetry. This qualitative fact is discussed above (Corollary 26 and Limitations 2), but no proof here depends on
   it; no epsilon-regularity criterion and no local energy bound of partial regularity theory is used either (Proposition 14B(a) is proved by a blow-up at the gradient scale; the localized energy inequality in the proof of Proposition 14A(a) is a direct computation for the smooth solution), except in the following
   remark, on which no other result depends. A quantitative form of the off-axis fact, obtained by averaging over rotated copies, gives only a weak necessary
   condition at velocity records. For $z=(x,t)$ and $Q_\rho(z)=B_\rho(x)\times(t-\rho^2,t)$ let $\mathcal A(z,\rho)=\sup_{t-\rho^2\le s\le t}\rho^{-1}\int_{B_\rho(x)}|u(s)|^2$ and
   $\mathcal H(z,\rho)=\rho^{-3/2}\int_{t-\rho^2}^t(\|u(s)\|_{L^4(B_\rho(x))}^2+\|p(s)\|_{L^2(B_\rho(x))})\,ds$. By the local energy bound of C. Guevara and N. C. Phuc (arXiv:1702.00449,
   Proposition 3.2 with $\alpha=2$, $\beta=1$), $\mathcal A(z,\rho/2)\le C(\mathcal H(z,\rho)+\mathcal H(z,\rho)^2)$ for suitable weak solutions, in particular for the present smooth solution. If
   $0<\rho\le r(x)$ and $\rho^2\le t$, the ball $B_\rho(x)$ has $N=\lfloor2r(x)/\rho\rfloor\ge r(x)/\rho$ pairwise disjoint rotated copies about the axis (consecutive centres are at distance
   $2r(x)\sin(\pi/N)\ge4r(x)/N\ge2\rho$), on which $|u|$ and $|p|$ take the same values ($p=R_iR_j(u_iu_j)$ is axisymmetric), so $\int_{B_\rho(x)}f\le(\rho/r(x))\int_{\mathbb R^3}f$ for
   $f=|u|^6$ and $f=|p|^3$. With Hölder's inequality on $B_\rho(x)$, $\|u\|_6\le C\|\nabla u\|_2$ and $\|p\|_3\le C\|u\|_6^2$ this gives
   $\mathcal H(z,\rho)\le C\rho^{-2/3}r(x)^{-1/3}\int_{t-\rho^2}^t\|\nabla u\|_2^2dt'$. At a velocity record with $D\le M^2$ (true eventually in regime (II)), $|u(t)|\ge M/2$ on $B_{1/(2M)}(x_t)$, so
   $\rho=1/M$ gives $\mathcal A(z,\rho/2)\ge\pi/12$; hence, if $Mr_t\ge1$ and $t\ge M^{-2}$,
   $$M^2\int_{t-M^{-2}}^t\|\nabla u\|_2^2\,dt'\ge c\,M^{4/3}r_t^{1/3}$$
   with an absolute constant $c>0$: the mean enstrophy over the last $M^{-2}$ units of time before a velocity record is at least $cM^{4/3}r_t^{1/3}$. (The cubic epsilon-regularity
   criterion, in the form of G. Seregin, arXiv:2006.04140, Proposition 1.4(1), with $\|u\|_3^3\le CE_0^{3/4}\|\nabla u\|_2^{3/2}$, gives instead the lower bound $cE_0^{-1}(Mr_t)^{4/3}$, which is the
   above multiplied by $r_t/E_0$ up to constants.) In the toroidal branch of the Remark on the two branches, $r_t>(M/(64A))2^{ah_b}$, so the right side exceeds
