   $\frac c4M(M^2/A)^{1/3}2^{ah_b/3}$, with $M^2/A\to\infty$ in regime (II): it is exponentially large in $h_b$. On the scalar histories of Limitations 1', however,
   $M^{4/3}r_t^{1/3}=2^{-1/6}\tau^{-5/6}L^{-1/4}$ while the enstrophy is $\tau^{-1}L^{-3/2}$ (and $M^{-2}=\tau^{3/2}=o(\tau)$), so the condition holds there for both choices of $D$, with the factor
   $\tau^{-1/6}L^{-5/4}$ to spare. An estimate of the local velocity from the local gradient alone, without control of a local mean, cannot replace this one: the local solution $u=Ve_3$, $p=0$ has zero gradient and arbitrary velocity.
5. The results are proved for the Sobolev class $u_0\in\bigcap_kH^k$; Lemma 9 needs the extra integrability stated.
6. Possible routes not yet completed: a local, scale-critical bound on the vorticity produced by the swirl source
   $\partial_z(q^2)$ of $\eta$ (after rescaling, $q_n(y,\tau)=(\ell_n/M_n)q(x_n+\ell_ny,s_n+\tau\ell_n/M_n)$ with $q=u^\theta/r$ satisfies $q_n^2\to0$ in $L^1_{loc}$ and the source tends to $0$ in $W^{-1,1}_{loc}$ and
   uniformly away from every fixed normalized tube around the axis, Lemma 19; $(G/D)^{1/2}=o(M/D)$ is the crossover radius
   of the pointwise envelope of $q$, and no concentration of the absolute source within a multiple of that radius is
   proved), whose accumulated effect over the history is not controlled; concentration-cancellation methods for
   swirl-free axisymmetric flows (the vanishing-viscosity compactness results of Jiu, Wu and Yang (Math. Methods Appl.
   Sci., DOI 10.1002/mma.3087) and Nobili and Seis (Math. Ann. 382 (2022)) assume uniform $L^1\cap L^p$ control of the
   relative vorticity $\omega^\theta/r$, with $p>1$, and the compactness result for smooth Euler approximations of Chae
   and Imanuvilov (EJDE 1998, no. 26) uses uniform $L\log^\alpha L$ control with $\alpha>1/2$; the required uniform
   bounds have not been established here; Corollary 6A(c) rules out uniform local $L^1$ control of the normalized relative vorticity on its
   retained-oscillation witness windows in (C) when the normalized axis distance is bounded, and does not establish this
   obstruction for axis-escaping windows or for time-integrated bounds);
   time-integrated or Lagrangian arguments using that the rescaled limit must be an Euler-Reynolds solution with a
   nonnegative defect; and the heat depth of the history: on every admissible normalization with retained $L^2$ oscillation
   (L2-OSC) the normalized heat time of the history, $\nu_ns_nM_n/\ell_n=s_n/\ell_n^2$, tends to infinity, since retained
   oscillation gives $\ell_n^3\le CE_0/M_n^2$ and $s_n\to T_*>0$ (without (L2-OSC) this fails: $\ell_n=1$ is admissible in
   regime (II) and has heat depth $s_n$); a whole-space solution of the heat equation bounded by $1$ over heat time $T$ has
   gradient at most $CT^{-1/2}$ at its end, so the exact Navier-Stokes shear and pipe comparisons of this report that reduce to
   heat flow can fail (L2M) only along subsequences whose heat age at the failing times tends to zero (they model recently
   created structure, which a changing datum can supply); heuristically, on a fixed trajectory the fine structure of a window in (C) at relative
   scale $\delta$ must be produced or maintained by the nonlinear terms against diffusion, which alone would damp it within about
   $\delta^2M_n\ell_n$ Euler times. Two quantitative forms of this, the stationary example below and the heat-depth
   bound for the avoidance interval of Lemma 26A were checked by hand and then audited (the audit added the
   specification of the cutoff in (i) and corrected the alternative in (ii)); none of them gives a contradiction or
   excludes (C). (i) If a failing increment of (L2M) has physical shift $\rho_n=\alpha_n\ell_n$ with $\alpha_n\to0$,
   then with $H_a=I-\chi(aD_x)$, $D_x=-i\nabla$, where $\chi\in C_c^\infty(\mathbb R^3;\mathbb R)$ is even,
   $0\le\chi\le1$, $\chi=1$ on $B_1$ and $\chi=0$ outside $B_2$, and $a_n=L\rho_n$ for a large fixed $L$, one has
   $\|H_{a_n}u\|_2^2\ge cM_n^2\ell_n^3$ at the failing time; the identity
   $\frac12\frac d{dt}\|H_au\|_2^2+\|\nabla H_au\|_2^2=-\int H_a^2u\cdot(u\cdot\nabla u)\,dx$, the bound
   $M_na\|\nabla u\|_2^2$ for its right side and $\|\nabla H_au\|_2\ge a^{-1}\|H_au\|_2$ then force, during the
   preceding physical time $K\ell_n^2+O(\ell_n^2\log M_n)\to0$ (for a fixed window multiplier $K>0$, restricted in (ii); not the constant of Lemma 9'), a dissipation
   $\int\|\nabla u\|_2^2\,dt\ge cM_n\ell_n^2/\alpha_n$; but (UP) gives $\rho_nD_n/M_n\to\infty$, so this charge is
   $o(D_n\ell_n^3)$ and tends to zero, and energy gives no rate contradicting it. (ii) Let $P$ be a meridional
   rectangle of side at most $2R\ell_n$ in $\{r\ge h\ell_n\}$ and
   $N_n=(M_n\ell_n)^{-1}\int\!\!\int_P|\omega^\theta|\,dr\,dz$ at a time $b_n=s_n+\tau_n\ell_n/M_n$, with
   $-A\le\tau_n\le0$ for a fixed $A$, of the retained-oscillation normalization. If this normalized mass stays at
   least $N_n/2$ during the preceding physical time $K\ell_n^2$, the pointwise bound
   $|\nabla u|^2\ge\frac12|\omega^\theta|^2$ and Cauchy-Schwarz on $P$ give
   $\int\|\nabla u\|_2^2\,dt\ge\frac{\pi h}{16R^2}KM_n^2\ell_n^3N_n^2$ over that time; since $b_n\to T_*$ and
   $K\ell_n^2\to0$, the dissipation over that time tends to zero, so along any sequence on which this persistence
   holds, $KM_n^2\ell_n^3N_n^2\to0$. If instead the displayed dissipation lower bound fails, persistence fails at some
   time of the interval. Writing $\mathfrak Z_n$ for the physical dissipation on the interval and $E_n=M_n^2\ell_n^3$,
   the accumulated normalized absolute swirl source then satisfies
   $\mathfrak S_n\le\frac{4RG}{\sqrt{2\pi}h^{5/2}}\sqrt{K\mathfrak Z_n/E_n}<\frac{GKN_n}{\sqrt2h^2}$, by Cauchy-Schwarz and the
   bound of Lemma 19 on the swirl source, and for fixed $0<K\le h^2/(2\sqrt2G)$ Kato's inequality for the conservative
   equation of $\omega^\theta$ in the meridional plane forces an integrated positive inward boundary flux, by
   transport or diffusion, greater than $N_n/4$ in these units during the interval. Its physical length $K\ell_n^2$ is
   $KM_n\ell_n\to\infty$ Euler times, and no upper bound on this influx is proved. A local inequality bounding the Kato cancellation
   of interleaved signed masses below by $c\nu\min\{N^+,N^-\}^3$ per unit Euler time fails, whatever the elapsed heat depth of the observation interval, for the exact
   stationary Navier-Stokes flows $(0,f_k(x,z),\frac12)$ on the half-space $z>0$ with $p=0$,
   $f_k=\frac a8[9e^{\lambda_1z}\cos kx-e^{\lambda_3z}\cos3kx]$, $a=\frac14$, $\nu=k^{-4}$, where $\lambda_j<0$ solves
   $\nu\lambda^2-\frac12\lambda-\nu j^2k^2=0$: the vorticity component $\partial_xf_k$ has sign changes at $x\in\pi\mathbb Z/k$, masses of order
   $k$ on $(0,2\pi)\times(1,2)$, circulations bounded by $\frac{5a}4$ times the perimeter, and gradient $O(\nu k^4)$ at its zero
   lines, so the ratio of cancellation to the cubic quantity is $O(k^{-2})$; the layers are renewed by inflow, and the flow is
   neither axisymmetric nor defined on the whole space, so this excludes only a local scalar closure (particles reach $1<z<2$ from the inflow boundary within a few Euler times, so it does not test an estimate requiring old incoming material). The avoidance
   interval of Lemma 26A has heat depth $\nu_nA_n\le C\nu_n^{7/8}\to0$, so it does not certify thermal age. What is missing
   is a bound on the high-frequency transfer in (i), or on the influx in (ii), that is small compared with the displayed
   charges. The Euler-scaling Type II scenarios of Seregin (arXiv 2402.13229 v3, 2507.08733 v2,
   2606.29468 v1) extract ancient Euler limits under scale-wise energy bounds with an exponent $m<1$ (or a slowly
   varying function in place of a power) and a concentration hypothesis; these are additional rate hypotheses (for $m=1$
   they are scale-invariant Type I bounds), not consequences of finite energy and bounded circulation, and the amplitude
   is a prescribed power of the length rather than $M$. Under those energy bounds the parabolic concentration condition
   $r^{-2m_0}\int_{Q_r}|v|^3\ge c_1$, $m_0=2m/(3-m)$, is impossible (after the rescaling it equals the $L^3$ norm of the rescaled
   field over a cylinder of radius tending to $0$, which the uniform $L^{10/3}$ bound makes small), and under the physical
   bound on $\partial_tv$ assumed in the axisymmetric Liouville statements the rescaled time derivative tends to $0$, so every
   extracted limit is stationary and the all-scale bounds make it zero (both calculations were checked by hand under the
   hypotheses as printed in the parts of the preprints listed in Verification; they are given here only in outline). The partial
   Type I theorem of Q. S. Zhang (arXiv 2604.07785, Theorem 1.1: an axisymmetric Leray-Hopf solution with bounded
   initial circulation and $u^r\ge-c/\sqrt{T-t}$ stays regular through $T$) gives at a singular time only
   $\limsup_{t\uparrow T_*}\sqrt{T_*-t}\,\|(u^r)_-(t)\|_\infty=\infty$. With $\tau=T_*-t$, the functions $M=\tau^{-1}$, $D=\tau^{-9/5}$,
   $\|(u^r)_-\|_\infty=\tau^{-3/5}$ satisfy $M^5\le CE_0D^3$, $M'\le24MD$, $D/M^2\to0$ and this conclusion while
   $\|(u^r)_-\|_\infty/M\to0$ (exponent bookkeeping, not a solution). A radial velocity of size $cM$ within $KM/D$ of the axis
   would contradict (C) (Section 9D, Remark after Corollary 26); Zhang's theorem does not supply one. The September
   2026 revision of a preprint of Shahmurov (arXiv 2604.21213 v3, 3 September 2026; only the abstract was read here)
   announces, for smooth axisymmetric flows with swirl, an exact $L^p$ balance for $r^du^\theta$, $-1\le d<1$, with a
   signed radial-compression action, continuation under finite $L^p$ mass and bounded signed action for $p\ge3/(1-d)$,
   the needed mass on the critical diagonal $d=1-3/p$ for every finite $p\ge2$ from $H^1$ data and bounded
   circulation, and a sliding Petrovskii displacement criterion with endpoint coefficient $2$ and an iterated-log
   correction. The precise statements, in particular the form of the sliding-interval inequality, have not been
   verified against the text, and the second-model consultation that had reported them withdrew its claim to have read
   the text. For $p=3$ the following calculation is independent of that attribution: with $Z=\|u^\theta\|_3^3>0$ and
   $\chi_{0,3}=Z^{-1}\int(u^r/r)|u^\theta|^3\,dx$, multiplying the swirl equation by $3u^\theta|u^\theta|$ gives
   $Z'+\frac83\|\nabla|u^\theta|^{3/2}\|_2^2+3\int|u^\theta|^3r^{-2}\,dx=-3\chi_{0,3}Z$, so an upper bound on the
   signed action $A(t)=-\int_{t_0}^t\chi_{0,3}\,ds$ bounds $|u^\theta|^{3/2}$ in
   $L^\infty_tL^2_x\cap L^2_t\dot H^1_x$, hence $u^\theta$ in $L^4_tL^6_x$, and the criterion of Chen, Fang and Zhang
   (arXiv:1505.00905, Theorem 1.1: for an axisymmetric weak solution with $H^2$ data, $r^du^\theta\in L^a_tL^b_x$ with
   $0\le d<1$, $\frac2a+\frac3b\le1-d$, $b>\frac3{1-d}$, $a\ge\frac2{1-d}$ implies smoothness; this statement was read in the
   arXiv version, the proof was not checked) then gives continuation. Thus, conditional on that criterion,
   $\limsup_{t\uparrow T_*}A(t)=+\infty$ at a first singular time (if $Z$ vanishes at some earlier time the solution
   has no swirl afterwards and stays regular). This does not locate the compression in the windows of (C) or give a
   radial velocity comparable with $M$, so it does not exclude (C), and the unverified sliding criterion supplies no
   established further conclusion here. The other recent items are literature leads reported by the consultation, not
   checked against the sources, and their applicability has not been established; the following remarks only explain
   why none of them is expected to apply directly. A stationary Liouville theorem, such as those attributed to Wang
   and Yang (arXiv 2608.06040), cannot be invoked without proving stationarity and its spatial hypotheses for the
   limits here. Nonzero purely azimuthal axisymmetric vorticity on a complete azimuthal orbit cannot satisfy
   $|\omega\cdot e|\ge c|\omega|$ for a fixed unit vector $e$ and $c>0$, since $e_\theta\cdot e$ vanishes somewhere on
   the orbit; this does not exclude a cone condition restricted to a small off-axis window, and the exact geometric
   hypothesis of Lei, Ren and Tian (arXiv 2501.08976) is unverified here. The quantitative weak $L^3$ bounds of
   Ozanski and Palasek (Ann. PDE 9 (2023)) need a bound that energy and $M$ give only as
   $\|u\|_{L^{3,\infty}}\le CE_0^{1/3}M^{1/3}$. The abstract of Bae, Chen and Kim (arXiv 2610.06385, 5 October 2026,
   abstract read here) states global energy compactness in $C_tL^2_x$ of regular approximate swirl-free axisymmetric
   Euler flows with relative vorticity in $L^1\cap L^p$ ($p\ge4/3$, and below $4/3$ under control of the absolute
   impulse); its exact hypotheses are unverified. Corollary 6A excludes bounds on
   $\eta_n=(\ell_n^2/M_n)\,\omega^\theta/r$ that are uniform in $n$ in $L^\infty_\tau(L^1_{loc}\cap L^p_{loc})$,
   $p\ge1$, on its witness windows at bounded normalized distance from the axis, since the normalized $L^1$ norm of
   $\eta_n$ over the solid of revolution of its rectangle is $2\pi(N_n^++N_n^-)\to\infty$; it does not exclude
   membership of each $\eta_n$, membership of a weak limit, time-integrated bounds, or bounds on windows whose
   normalized distance from the axis tends to infinity. The abstract of a preprint of Constantin, Ignatova and Vicol
   (arXiv 2609.20762 v2, 29 September 2026; the abstract, Definition 1.1, Theorem 1.2, Remark 1.3 and the statement and outline of proof of Proposition 3.1 were read here, the proofs were not checked) states local regularity for axisymmetric
   suitable weak solutions, smooth before the terminal time $0$, with $|u(\cdot,t)|\le C\ell(t)/(-t)$ and
   $|\nabla^2u(\cdot,t)|\le C/((-t)\ell(t))$ for an Euler length $\ell$ (non-increasing, doubling, $\ell(t)\to0$ and
   $(-t)/\ell(t)^2\to0$), the Hessian bound being replaceable by a Holder bound on the azimuthal vorticity and a bound
   on its potential vorticity. These are a priori pointwise bounds at a prescribed scale, which the problem does not
   allow one to assume. If they held on the whole space with $M$ comparable
   with $\ell(t)/(-t)$, interpolation would give $D\le C/(-t)$ and hence $D/M^2\le C(-t)/\ell(t)^2\to0$, so that class
   lies in regime (II). Neither of these bounds at a prescribed Euler length is derived here from fixed data, from
   $D/M^2\to0$ or from (FLAT$_0$). As outlined in that preprint, the proof rescales at the Euler length in space and
   at $|t|$ in time, uses that a bounded circulation is subcritical for this rescaling so that the swirl and its source
   vanish in every limit, uses transport of the limiting potential vorticity along backward characteristics together
   with the bound $C/|\tau|$ inherited from the hypotheses to make the limiting azimuthal vorticity vanish, and
   concludes by Liouville's theorem for the bounded, divergence free and curl free limiting meridional velocity,
   whose gradient at a selected point is bounded away from zero; the convergence of that gradient uses the second
   order bound. The normalizations at historical records used in this report give neither a bound on the
   potential vorticity of the form inherited there nor second order control at a length of that kind, so this
   mechanism does not yield exclusion of (FLAT$_0$) or of alternative (C) here.
   Two further necessary conditions on the meridional mass were derived by hand and audited once (the audit found two
   defects, repaired below: the weighted identity had been stated for all $0<k<4$, and the compatibility of the two
   bounds in (i) had been given a wrong reason). Put
   $Q(t)=\int\!\!\int|\omega^\theta|\,dr\,dz=(2\pi)^{-1}\|\eta(t)\|_{L^1}$, which is invariant under the Navier-Stokes
   scaling, and $S_Q=(2\pi)^{-1}\|\partial_z(q^2)\|_{L^1}$. The proof of Lemma 9 gives
   $Q(t_2)\le Q(t_1)+\int_{t_1}^{t_2}S_Q$ for $t_1\le t_2<T_*$ whenever $Q(t_1)<\infty$ ($Q(0)<\infty$ is an
   assumption on the datum, not implied by $u_0\in\bigcap_kH^k$), and at the witness times $t'_n$ of Corollary 6A(c),
   at any axis distance, $Q(t'_n)\ge N_nM_n\ell_n$ with $N_n=(N^+_{n,R}+N^-_{n,R})(t'_n)\to\infty$. (i) For
   $\Gamma=ru^\theta$ and $2\le k<4$ (for $0<k<2$ the same identity holds under additional weighted integrability at
   infinity, which finite energy does not give),
   $\frac d{dt}\int\Gamma^2r^{-k}=-2\int|\nabla\Gamma|^2r^{-k}+k(k-2)\int\Gamma^2r^{-k-2}-k\int\Gamma^2u^rr^{-k-1}$;
   with the radial Hardy inequality $\int\Gamma^2r^{-5}\le\frac49\int(\partial_r\Gamma)^2r^{-3}$ the case $k=3$ gives
   $\frac d{dt}\int\Gamma^2r^{-3}+\frac23\int|\nabla\Gamma|^2r^{-3}\le3\int(u^\theta)^2(u^r)_-r^{-2}$, and
   Cauchy-Schwarz with the same Hardy inequality gives $S_Q\le(3\pi)^{-1}\int|\nabla\Gamma|^2r^{-3}$. Hence, if
   $Q(0)<\infty$ ($\int(u_0^\theta)^2r^{-1}<\infty$ holds for the data considered), in (C) the compression action
   $\int_0^{t'_n}\!\int(u^\theta)^2(u^r)_-r^{-2}$ is at least $\frac{2\pi}3N_nM_n\ell_n-C(u_0)$,
   $C(u_0)=\frac13\int(u_0^\theta)^2r^{-1}+\frac{2\pi}3Q(0)$. It is also at most $M(t'_n)E_0/2$, since
   $\int(u^\theta)^2r^{-2}\le\|\nabla u\|_2^2$. The two bounds are compatible (with $P_{n,R}$ the meridional rectangle of Corollary 6A: the points $(r,z)$, $r\ge0$, within $R\ell_n$ of $(r(x_n),x_{n,3})$ in each coordinate):
   $N_nM_n\ell_n\le\int\!\!\int_{P_{n,R}}|\omega^\theta(t'_n)|\,dr\,dz\le4\sqrt2R^2D(t'_n)\ell_n^2$, so the ratio of
   the lower bound to the upper one is at most $C_R(D(t'_n)/M(t'_n)^2)M_n\ell_n^2/E_0\to0$, by regime (II) and
   $M_n^2\ell_n^3\le CE_0$. (ii) If $\|\omega_0^\theta\|_1<\infty$, Lemma 9' and
   $\|\eta\|_1\le(\int|\omega^\theta|r^{-2})^{1/2}\|\omega^\theta\|_1^{1/2}$ give
   $\int_0^{T_*}Q^2\,dt\le K^2/(4\pi^2)$ with $K=\|\omega_0^\theta\|_1+E_0/2$ (so $Q<\infty$ for almost every time);
   hence at every time $t$ with $0<Q(t)<\infty$ and $\Delta(t)=2K^2/(\pi^2Q(t)^2)\le t$ one has
   $\int_{t-\Delta(t)}^tS_Q>Q(t)/2$: the accumulated absolute swirl source over the preceding physical time
   $\Delta(t)$ exceeds half the current meridional mass (this does not locate the source or identify which of it
   survives). At the witness times of (C) this time is at most $2K^2/(\pi^2N_n^2M_n^2\ell_n^2)$, which may still
   contain many Euler times of the window since $M_n\ell_n^3\le CE_0/M_n$; the production may take place anywhere
   along the axis. The envelopes $|\partial_z\Gamma|\le Dr$ and $|\Gamma|\le\min\{G,Dr^2\}$ give only
   $\int_0^\infty|\partial_z(q^2)|\,r\,dr\le4G^{1/2}D^{3/2}$ at each height, a bound per unit axial length of the
   swirling core whose axial extent is not controlled, and the global bounds of $S_Q$ by
   $\pi^{-1}\|\nabla u\|_2\|\nabla\omega\|_2$ or by the weighted dissipation above are not controlled at a single
   time. (iii) Two second-model consultations, whose derivations were checked by hand, add the following. The equation
   $\partial_tq+b\cdot\nabla q=(\Delta+2r^{-1}\partial_r)q-2(u^r/r)q$, tested against $q$ with a time cutoff, gives
   $\int_{t-d}^tS_Q\le\pi^{-1}e(t,d)\,(\frac1{2d}+2D(t))^{1/2}$ for $2d\le t$, where $e(t,d)=\int_{t-2d}^t\|q\|_2^2$;
   under the additional hypothesis $\|\omega^\theta_0\|_1<\infty$ of (ii), combining this estimate with (ii) and
   $\int_0^{T_*}\|q\|_2^2\le E_0/2$ gives $Q(t_k)/D(t_k)^{1/2}\to0$ along every sequence $t_k\to T_*$
   with $Q(t_k)\to\infty$. This does not conflict with the witness bound of (C), since by (i)
   $N_nM_n\ell_n\le4\sqrt2R^2D(t'_n)\ell_n^2\le C_RD(t'_n)(E_0/M_n^2)^{2/3}=o(D(t'_n)^{1/2})$, where
   $C_R$ depends also on the fixed (L2-OSC) constant, using $D(t'_n)\le D(t_n)\le M_n^2$ for large $n$. Energy bounds the
   axial length of strong swirl, $|\{z:\sup_r|u^\theta(r,z,t)|\ge\kappa(GD(t))^{1/2}\}|\le64E_0/(\pi\kappa^4G^2)$ for
   $0<\kappa\le1$ (at such a height $|u^\theta|\ge v_0/2$ on a radial interval of length $v_0/D$ in $r\ge v_0/(2D)$,
   $v_0\ge\kappa(GD)^{1/2}/2$), but not that of weak swirl, and a smooth swirling field supported in $r\le2a$,
   $|z|\le2a^{-2}$ with bounded Lipschitz constant and enstrophy, circulation $O(a^2)$, energy $O(a^2)$ and no
   meridional vorticity has $S_Q\ge c/a$, so $S_Q$ is not bounded at one time by these quantities. Since
   $|\omega^\theta|\le\sqrt2D$, the part of the witness rectangle $P_{n,R}$ with $r\le cN_nM_n/D(t'_n)$ carries at
   most half of its mass for a small $c=c(R)>0$, so the cancelling mass lies beyond the Lipschitz length by an
   unbounded factor, and the region $r\le C(G/D)^{1/2}$ carries $o(M_n\ell_n)$ of it. The envelope rate
   $4G^{1/2}D^{3/2}$ per unit axial length is attained up to a constant factor by smooth profiles at radius
   $(G/D)^{1/2}$, and at this rate an axial length $O(\ell_n)$ would supply an absolute source budget equal to the
   witness mass in a time much shorter than $\ell_n^2$, while the deadline of (ii) computed from the witness mass
   alone is much longer than $\ell_n^2$. Scalar bookkeeping (exponents only, not a field) is consistent with all the
   inequalities in (i)-(iii), with the energy, enstrophy and Lemma 9' budgets and with regime (II), while $Q\to\infty$
   and $Q/(M\ell)\to\infty$. None of these conditions excludes (C); what is missing is control of the export and
   survival of the produced signed vorticity. (iv) A third second-model consultation, whose derivations were checked
   by hand, gives a necessary condition for regime (II) itself. Split $\eta=\eta^d+\eta^s$ with the same drift,
   $\eta^d(0)=\eta_0$, $\eta^s(0)=0$, and $\eta^s$ forced by $\partial_z(q^2)$. Testing with $\eta^s$ and integrating the
   source by parts in $z$ gives $\|\eta^s(t)\|_2^2\le\int_0^t\|q(s)\|_4^4ds\le\int_0^tD(s)^2\|q(s)\|_2^2ds=o(D(t)^2)$ (norms in $L^p(dx)$; there is no
   positive axis boundary term, and the little-o follows from $\int_0^{T_*}\|q\|_2^2\le E_0/2$ and $D(t)\to\infty$). The
   meridional velocity $b$ satisfies $b=e^{\rho^2\Delta}b+k_\rho*\times(\omega^\theta e_\theta)$ with
   $k_\rho=\int_0^{\rho^2}\nabla g_s\,ds$, $g_s$ the heat kernel, $|k_\rho(z)|\le C|z|^{-2}e^{-c|z|^2/\rho^2}$; splitting
   $\omega^\theta=r\eta^d+r\eta^s$ only on $|x-y|\ge a$, using $|\omega^\theta|\le\sqrt2D$ for the total vorticity on $|x-y|<a$,
   $\|\eta^d\|_\infty\le\|\eta_0\|_\infty$, $r(y)\le r(x)+|x-y|$ and Cauchy-Schwarz, and choosing $a=(r(x)\|\eta^s\|_2/D)^{2/3}$,
   one gets
   $|b(x)|\le C[E_0^{1/2}\rho^{-3/2}+\|\eta_0\|_\infty(r(x)\rho+\rho^2)+D^{1/3}(r(x)\|\eta^s\|_2)^{2/3}+\|\eta^s\|_2\rho^{1/2}]$.
   At a velocity record $|u(x_n,t_n)|=M_n$ one has $|b(x_n)|\ge M_n-S(t_n)$ and, from the energy of a solid torus around
   the axis, $r(x_n)\le CE_0D_n^2M_n^{-4}$; with $\rho=LE_0^{1/3}M_n^{-2/3}$ this gives, at all large velocity records in
   regime (II), $\|\eta^s(t_n)\|_2^2\ge cE_0^{-2}M_n^{11}D_n^{-5}$ (and $\ge cE_0^{-1/3}M_n^{8/3}$ for a record on the axis). The two
   bounds are compatible with the upper bound $o(D^2)$, since $M^{11}D^{-7}\le CE_0^{7/3}M^{-2/3}$ by the energy-ball
   inequality; for $M=\tau^{-3/5}$, $D=\tau^{-11/10}$ ($\tau=T_*-t$) the general lower scale is $\tau^{-11/10}$, the axis scale $\tau^{-8/5}$ and
   $D^2=\tau^{-11/5}$ (scalar bookkeeping, not a field). A rate
   $\int_0^{t_n}\|q\|_4^4=o(E_0^{-2}M_n^{11}D_n^{-5})$ along the velocity records would exclude regime (II); it is not available.
7. Context from the literature (as reported by the second-model literature check; not re-read): the classical
   parabolic blow-up rescaling of Koch, Nadirashvili, Seregin and Sverak gives a bounded nonzero ancient mild solution
   that may be constant, and in regime (II) it is constant since its gradient is at most $D/M^2$; a structure theorem
   of Lei and Zhang (Pacific J. Math. 254 (2011)), under the stronger hypothesis $\|ru_0\|_\infty<\infty$, says that the
   parabolic rescaling at an almost historical maximum $Q_0$ of $|u|$, attained at distance $r_0$ from the axis, with
   $r_0Q_0$ large is close to a nonzero constant (their $Q_0$ is a velocity, not the meridional mass $Q$ of
   Limitations 6). Where the
   published numerical and computer-assisted singularity scenarios (Hou and Huang; Hou; Luo and Hou; Chen and Hou;
   Elgindi; Elgindi, Ghoul and Masmoudi, all for Euler, with a boundary, or non-rigorous) allow a ratio $D/M^2$ to be
   read off, it does not tend to zero, i.e. they would fall in regime (I), where Theorem 2 applies to Navier-Stokes.
   No scenario in regime (II) is known to us beyond dimensional ones. Logarithmic criticality results for the circulation
   give further necessary conditions at the axis (statements read in the arXiv abstracts, proofs not checked): a smooth
   solution stays regular if $\sup_t|ru^\theta(r,z,t)|\le C_*|\ln r|^{-2}$ for small $r$ (Lei and Zhang, arXiv 1505.02628), or if
   $|ru^\theta(r,z,t)|\le|\ln r|^{-3/2}$ for $0<r\le\delta_0<\frac12$ (Wei, arXiv 1508.03318). So $\Gamma$ cannot satisfy these bounds near
   the axis on $[0,T_*)$; this restricts only the circulation and gives no bound on the meridional quantities on which (C),
   (FLAT$_0$) and the toroidal branch of Section 12 depend. A scale-critical criterion for the meridional vorticity is
   Corollary 1.1 of Li and Pan (arXiv 1805.10752; statement read in the paper, proof not checked; they attribute the result
   first to Lei and Zhang): a bound $|\omega^\theta(r,z,t)|\le Cr^{-2}$ gives regularity. Hence the scale-invariant quantity
   $r^2|\omega^\theta|=r^3|\eta|$ is unbounded on $\mathbb R^3\times[0,T_*)$. This is again a necessary condition: it bounds nothing
   from above, and the conditions that would close (C), (FLAT$_0$) or the toroidal branch are upper bounds.

## Verification

Proofs. Every numbered statement in the Findings is accompanied by a proof in this report; no input is granted
(an earlier version granted a quantitative $\epsilon$-regularity step in Proposition 14B(a), now replaced by a proof). Some proofs refer to
standard arguments (Arzela-Ascoli, Riesz-Frechet-Kolmogorov, Hardy space and BMO duality, Calderon-Zygmund estimates)
or reuse steps of an earlier proof instead of repeating them (the symmetry and pressure identifications in Theorems 2,
4(a) and 6', and the steps reused in Theorem 17(d) and Proposition 20(b)). Several proofs are abbreviated, among them the localized div-curl and kernel translation
estimate in Section 8, the passage from the pressure tail to time equicontinuity in Proposition 15 and the dyadic
weighted pressure estimates of Lemma 16; this list is not exhaustive. Passages labelled heuristic, dimensional or
exponent bookkeeping are not proofs and are not used as such. The proofs were written and checked by hand; no proof
assistant was used. The statements and displayed proofs of Theorems 3 and 4 (the mollified time-derivative identity, the time modulus, the rigidity
step, the reduction to (UP) and the two cited Liouville cases) were also read by an independent reader, who found no defect at that level. The constant $24$ of Lemma 3 and the exponent bookkeeping of the dimensional scenario in
Limitations 1' were also checked by symbolic computation; the other exponent bookkeeping (after Proposition 24 and in
Limitations 6) and the constants of Proposition 10 were checked by the author by hand (the audits of Limitations 6(iii)
and 6(iv) additionally report exact rational arithmetic). The profile of Proposition 10 was in
addition evaluated numerically on a grid for $3\le N\le8$; this gives consistency checks on sampled amplitudes,
finite-difference derivative estimates, increments and window variances, and does not prove the statements for these $N$ or verify the
constants of the proof. Throughout this report, "the second model" (also "second-model audit", "second-model consultation") means an independent AI language model consulted for refereeing, alternative derivations and literature pointers; its outputs were treated as claims, derivations incorporated as proofs were checked by hand, and literature reports retain the source-verification qualifications stated alongside them.

Section 11. Its proofs were written and checked by hand. Lemmas 27 to 29, Proposition 30, Proposition 33 and Corollary 35 were also checked line by line by a second, independent reader of the same kind as the author (that check used the constant $2\pi$ in Lemma 27(a); the constant $\pi$ used here comes from the split in the proof and was checked by hand afterwards). A further independent reader later checked Lemma 27(a), Lemma 28, Lemma 29, Proposition 30, Lemma 31, Theorem 32, Lemma 34 and the sufficient condition after Theorem 32, at the level of the statements and of the elementary steps (Lemma 27(b) and Theorem 4 taken as stated), and found no defect. Lemma 31, Theorem 32, Lemma 34 and the reflection step of Proposition 33 were sent to the second model as a hostile referee. It found two gaps, both repaired in the text: the layer-cake step of Lemma 31(b) first used vorticity outside the meridional tube (the measure is now restricted to the tube), and the Duhamel step of Lemma 31(c) needs bounded radii (now $\rho_n\le1$). The single-time family in the Remark after Theorem 32 was proposed in that consultation and its orders (records, energy, gradient, swirl, circulation, mass) were checked by hand. Proposition 36 and Theorem 37 were developed separately and each was sent to the second model as a hostile referee; the defects it found (the enclosing ball in the dyadic chain of Proposition 36, and the Galilean subtraction in the symmetry step of Theorem 37, which must use the unsubtracted field) are repaired in the proofs given here, which are condensed. Proposition 37A was developed in a parallel line of this work, sent there to the second model as a hostile referee (which found that the anchoring must hold uniformly in $n$, since a fixed octave for infinitely many $n$ does not control other subsequences; the stated form is the repair), and checked here by hand step by step, including the constants in (a), the pigeonhole over anchoring octaves and the limit of means over fixed balls; the dynamic remark after it (the drop kernel bounds and the packing constant $21/(32\pi\rho^3)$) was also checked by hand. The text as given here was audited once more by the second model as a hostile referee, which confirmed the proof and found two defects in the explanatory prose (the description of the uncovered cases and a pointwise growth description), both repaired. The sharp axis constant $3\pi/16$ after Lemma 27 and the window bound in the Remark after Corollary 35 were checked by hand. Afterwards the whole of Section 11 was audited once more by the second model as a hostile referee, item by item. It confirmed the constants of Lemmas 27 to 29, Propositions 30 and 33 and Lemma 34, and the trichotomy of Theorem 32, and found the following defects, each checked by hand and repaired in the text: the integrations by parts in Lemma 29 need the weighted finiteness from the standing regularity, not only bounded circulation; the single-time family gives the stated mass only for radii much larger than $L_E$; the window bound must use the distance to the displaced axis, and the work is $\nu_n^{-2}$ times the rescaled work, not invariant; the comparison with $D$ in the memory remark needs the weighted maximum principle for $r\eta^d$; Proposition 36(b) needs a convention at $\tau=0$; the constant $c_0$ of Theorem 37 depends on $c$ and its time modulus is obtained by mollification; and the summary used the instantaneous swirl norm where Lemma 34 gives the historical record $S$. The sharpness assertion in the memory remark was replaced by the inner Biot-Savart argument given there, which was also checked by hand. Theorem 38, Proposition 39 and Lemma 40 were written and checked by hand (the circle bound, the rotation argument for the volume of $E_{n,R}$, the layer-cake tail $2N/L$, the constants $\frac3{2\pi}$ and $\frac1\pi$ of the mean estimate, the recentering in (e), the angular integration by parts and the constant $3+3/(2\sqrt\pi)$ of Proposition 39, the energy identity with its axis term, and the square completion and the factor $1/\sqrt3$ of Lemma 40). A first draft of Theorem 38(a) claimed GOAL at the swirl scale; it was withdrawn because vanishing normalized vorticity mass forces a constant limit. The block was then audited by the second model as a hostile referee, item by item. It confirmed the constants and the logic and found the following, each checked by hand and repaired in the text: the vanishing in (a) needs the circle bound $2\pi R(N^++N^-)$ of Corollary 6A(a), now stated there; the elimination after Proposition 39 needs $\alpha\ge0$ (negative $\alpha$ is covered directly); Lemma 40 needs an axisymmetric cutoff and the absorbing adjoint; the sufficient condition after it needs a cutoff of volume $O(|E_{n,R}|)$ and control of the inherited terms, which (L2-OSC) supplies; the weighted Hardy counterexample must use a cutoff away from the axis and small positive ages; the cutoff-strain assertion needed the exit estimate now written out; and the scale window and the summary must be stated for normalizations with retained oscillation and as a sufficient condition, not an equivalent. The Remark after Lemma 40 (signed form, the cancellation-preserving duality identity, the failure of the localized instantaneous sign, and the bounded adjoint-weighted work on the Burgers column) was checked in a second consultation of the same referee, which confirmed the signed identity and the backward local energy identity used in our attempt and found no closure; its Burgers potential and the exponent bookkeeping were checked by hand. Item (v) of that remark (the $L^2$ form) was checked by hand: the energy inequality of the produced vorticity, the dual energy identity of the absorbing adjoint, the lower bound on the volume of the window solid and the lower bound on oscillating scales. The Jacobian form after it was derived in a third consultation of the same referee and checked by us: the expansion of the commutator $\mathcal C$, the source identity $J(\Gamma,q)=F$, the commutation of $J$ with divergence-free transport and the value $-8pz$ were verified symbolically, and the coarea and circulation steps by hand. A hostile referee pass by the same second model found one omission, now repaired:
the class of $a$ must be specified (bounded solutions, where uniqueness holds; smoothness alone does not give uniqueness for the
heat equation on all of space). It also showed that the one-sided condition is equivalent to the mass bound together with an
absolute commutator bound, and gave the material-level example; the two exact identities for $B_\pm$, the example and its
constant $1/90$ were checked by hand. An independent recomputation, symbolic and numerical, of item (v) of the Remark after Lemma 40 and of the Remark on the Jacobian form found no defect (for the scalar example the actual constant is about $1/46$). The localized barrier after Lemma 40 was taken from an independently refereed derivation and rechecked by hand (supersolution computation, equation of the five-dimensional density, boundary comparison). The Remark on the meridional gradient record was checked by a further hostile consultation of the same referee, which also supplied the short proof of (b) given there (our first proof used two blow-up cases near and away from the axis); we checked the swirl-vector equation, the rescaling and the coefficient bounds by hand. A direct check that the record reduction holds with the meridional length (the residual of Theorem 32 then stated through the source $\mathcal I_{s_0}$) was also made, and is superseded by (b). The strain example in item (iv) of the Remark (signed and cancellation-preserving forms of Lemma 40) comes from an independently refereed computation; we verified symbolically the adjoint formula and the equation of $f$, and by hand the radial integral $\pi G^2\gamma\log2$ of $q^2$ for $f=1$ and the time integral of the adjoint weight. Item (vi) of the Remark after Lemma 40 was proposed by the same referee in a hostile consultation on a five-dimensional variant of ours, whose support-free form it showed to be false for general tests (separated patches lose entropy of order $v\log N$); we checked by hand the entropy identity and its axis terms, the second-moment equation with the constant $10$, the comparison argument, the binary entropy split, the closed budget and the exponents. A hostile audit of item (vi) by the same referee confirmed these steps and found three defects, each checked by hand and repaired: the sign partition must assign the zero set of $Z(t)$ to one part; the entropy bound at the windows needs a support condition on the cutoff, not only a mass bound; and the statement that no localized dissipation bound is known was false, since the drift-independent smoothing of the adjoint (whose Nash argument we checked) localizes $\varepsilon_\psi$, though not at a useful rate. The quartic identity of item (vii) was proposed by a referee; we verified the pointwise identity symbolically for arbitrary smooth $q,u^r,u^z$ and checked by hand the duality step, the axis terms, and the constants $\frac34$ and $\frac{81}{16}$ in Young's inequality. A hostile audit of item (vii) confirmed the identity, the duality (the axis terms are in fact $O(r^4)$), the constants, the conditional consequence and the absorption of the inherited strain, found that the swirl bound must be the historical record, and asked for the wording on sufficiency adopted above; it found the dissipation rate and the work bound unproved, and showed by a prescribed dissipation history with summable pulses at the windows that the rate does not follow from the scalar energy budget and the adjoint smoothing alone. A further hostile audit pass of the same referee on the radial form of the work ceiling, item (v) and the repaired Jacobian form found no error in their estimates and identities, and three defects, each checked by hand and repaired: the necessary rate in item (v) must be stated at the terminal window time and only as a requirement of the chosen majorant; the scalar example needs an integer frequency $N$ (otherwise one sign of the variation can fail) and a compactly supported initial circulation fixed independently of $N$; and statements that the sufficient conditions are not necessary for GOAL were unproved and now say that their necessity is not established. The Remark on the BMO form of the work ceiling was checked by hand and by an independent hostile audit by a second model, which confirmed the lemma, the divergence-free extension across the axis, the duality step for bounded $b$ and consequences (i) and (ii), suggested taking the minimum of the two ceilings before the time integration, and showed that the naive cut-off BMO bound on an off-axis torus is false; item (iii) states the repaired form, which we checked by hand. The bound on $D$ in Proposition 14B(a) is proved by a blow-up at the gradient scale; this replaced an argument through the one-scale $\epsilon$-regularity theorem whose quantitative form had been granted. The new proof was checked by hand and by an independent hostile audit by a second model, which confirmed every step (the choice of the time, the scaling and Galilean covariance of the increment pressure, the Gronwall bound for the weighted mean, the interior parabolic estimate and embedding with absolute constants, the oscillation lower bound) and noted that the conclusion $\Theta_0\to\infty$ uses that $T_*$ is a singular time. The Remark on the shell-tested form of Corollary 58 was derived by hand and audited by the same referee, which confirmed the signs of the localization, the duality and the integration by parts, sharpened the angular bound to $\pi s$, corrected a radius inequality to $\rho_j<r_t/8$ (with the resulting constants), and asked for the qualification on cancellation; all corrections were checked by hand. Item (e) of that Remark was supplied by an independent hostile referee asked to check a weaker form of ours (with a factor quadratic in the number of shells, from a weighted Cauchy-Schwarz inequality shell by shell); we checked by hand the pointwise bound $U^2\le\psi f$ for the propagated square, the identities for convex one-homogeneous functions of two dual solutions with their axis terms, the entropy, relative-entropy and Gaussian bounds, the inequality $\Lambda>B$, the monotonicity step and the orders in the scalar histories, and replaced the indicator of the largest shell torus used by the referee with the cutoff $\chi_{\mathcal R}$. A hostile referee of the entered item, its proof and the dependent sentences confirmed every step and the orders, and asked for three precision repairs, which were made: the identity is stated for the two pairs used, with the cutoff argument, and the consequences for the scalar histories are stated conditionally in the Limitations and in How to resume (a). The Remark (radial transport required by the dual-weighted quartic) was derived by hand. Its pointwise identity, axis expression, square completion and radial-moment identity were checked symbolically as formal identities in $p$ and $\beta$; they are used in the stated range $p=0$ or integer $p\ge2$, with the terms carrying a factor $p$ omitted when $p=0$. The integrated identities use $2p+\beta>-2$, the standing regularity and the decay of the dual solution, and an exponent $\varepsilon$ held fixed throughout each time integration; the general outward comparison keeps the factor $(2-\varepsilon)^{-1}$. Item (b) reproduces the signed quartic identity underlying item (vii) of the Remark on signed and cancellation-preserving forms of Lemma 40 and sharpens its estimate by keeping only the inward part of the radial strain. A hostile referee of the second model confirmed all constants of (a) to (c) and the orders in the scalar histories, asked for the repairs just listed, supplied the bound for $\varepsilon_\psi$ through the smoothing bound, the terminal second-moment bound used in Limitations 1' and, for power-logarithmic histories with the record at distance $\|\nabla u\|_2/\|\eta\|_2$, the observation that the quartic majorant stays at least a factor of order $L$ above the requirement; we checked these by hand and proved the general form, item (e), from Proposition 41(d) and Lemma 43. A second hostile referee confirmed item (e) in every constant, asked that it be stated for the scalar comparison only (a bound with $\limsup<1$ would also exclude the second case) and supplied the sharper form through the first form of Lemma 43 now stated, with the constants $6561$ and $\frac{1024}{243}$, which we checked by hand.

Independent audits. A second model acted as a hostile referee on frozen copies of
parts of the report and as a research consultant. Its output was treated as a list of claims: every reported defect and
proof was checked by hand before it was used, and no result of this report rests on its assertion alone; literature
statements reported by it have the verification status stated below (checked against the original texts only where so
stated, otherwise reported as context or used conditionally). The criterion of Proposition 15, the
relative-energy argument of Proposition 16, Lemma 26A with its application, the Remark on the accumulated swirl source
after Lemma 9' and parts of the Remark at the end of Section 6 were proposed in these consultations; the proof of Proposition 10' was developed in a consultation from an embedding
proposed by the author of this report. Each of these was then checked by hand line by line. The audited parts were Sections 1 to 5; Sections 6 to 10; Theorem 5', Proposition 7(c), the pressure Hessian
bound, Lemma 9' and Section 9; Lemma 11, Theorem 12, Corollary 13 and Proposition 14; Theorem 6' and Proposition 15;
Lemma 15', Lemma 16, Proposition 16 and Section 9A; Sections 9B, 9C and 9D separately; the whole report, four times as listed here (two later whole-report audits are
described below)
(internal consistency, the selection requirement, forbidden assumptions, presentation; the second time with spot checks
of Lemma 26A, Corollary 6A, Lemma 21 and Proposition 22, the third with spot checks of Theorem 6, Proposition 24 and
Theorem 17(d), the fourth with spot checks of Lemma 11, Theorem 12, Corollary 13, Proposition 14(a) to (e), Proposition
7(c), Lemma 26A and Proposition 14A including the paragraph after its proof, and found no failing step in these proofs); and, five times, the repairs made after the preceding audit (the third of these also covered the
written-out proof of Proposition 16(c); the fourth re-audited the repairs made after the second whole-report audit; the
fifth also covered the Remark on the accumulated swirl source after Lemma 9' and four items of Limitations 2); and Proposition 10' together with the passages of the Result, the
Limitations and this section that describe it. The defects found were of these kinds, and the text was revised in response to each: implications stated as equivalences (failure of (L2M) versus a nonzero
Reynolds defect; several "if and only if" statements in Section 9); missing quantifiers or hypotheses (the time
modulus in Theorem 4(a), joint subsequences in Corollary 13, index ranges and subsequence scopes in Sections 9A to 9D,
the hypotheses of Propositions 15 and 16(a) in the Result); constants computed from the whole sequence instead of each
record (Theorem 17(d)); an invalid step in an auxiliary example after Lemma 15' (replaced by a second-difference
argument); a wrong zero-limit statement for large-amplitude Galilean rescalings (the limit is a constant); incomplete
displayed proofs (Proposition 8, Theorem 5(b), Proposition 10(v)); a criterion stated as a selection rule although it
is a condition on a given normalization (Proposition 22); an unrestricted index range (Proposition 10(v), now $j\ge0$); a dilation statement without its amplitude factor
(Proposition 10'(vi')); a conclusion stated more strongly than proved (that an exclusion of (C) must use the
evolution); a misreported numerical tolerance (Section 10); a hypothesis of Lemma 21 omitted in the Result, and later its collar width; a transfer of Lemma 26A to
rectangles meeting the axis (now restricted to exterior rectangles); the scope of the temporal
certificate and the description of (B*) overstated in the Result; the degenerate cases $\sigma=0$, $G=0$ of Lemma 26A; a localization of the swirl source in the swirl core
not implied by Lemma 19; two constructions described as exact solutions without the needed stability and record-growth
arguments; undefined operators in a displayed identity; and statements of GOAL for normalizations whose selection is retrospective
(Theorem 12) or existential (alternatives (B), (B*)), now qualified as such; in the fourth whole-report audit, a
functional (non Navier-Stokes) example used to assert a non-equivalence for the trajectory class (Section 6, Remark (i),
now restricted), a blanket claim that the rescaled sequences lack the vorticity integrability assumed in the no-swirl
compactness literature (Limitations 6, now restricted to what Corollary 6A(c) proves), and missing scope qualifiers in
Result 6, the rigidity remark after Theorem 12, Theorem 5(a) and Limitations 6(iii). These repairs were made after
the fourth whole-report audit; the later whole-report audits and their stated coverage are described below, and they
did not individually recheck every earlier repair. No audit found a use of a forbidden
assumption (the shape inequality $MH\le CD^2$, Type I bounds, $H^2$ compactness, a bounded stream potential, BMO
smallness, self-similarity, affine background subtraction, time-dependent Galilean frames, ancient finite energy, a free
pressure gradient).

Final referee pass of the reductions. After the last additions, the second model refereed the Setting and Sections 1 to 10 again, line
by line, on frozen copies and in seven parts (Sections 1 to 6; the two halves of Section 6A; Sections 7 and 8; Sections 9 and 9A; Sections
9B to 9D; Section 10), with instructions to redo every constant, scaling relation and limit extraction and to check the hypotheses of the
cited theorems. It reported no defect in any numbered proof. It reported four precision points, each checked by hand and repaired: the
standing assertion on first crossings needs continuity of the underlying function, not only of its running maximum (a discontinuous
example shows this); the comparison after Lemma 9 used the bound $\ell\le L_E$, which admissibility does not give, and is now stated for
normalizations with retained oscillation, where the energy gives $M_n^2\ell_n^3\le2E_0/c$; the consequence in Proposition 16(b) now assumes
a bounded gradient and time derivative of the limit (the bounded stationary Euler solution $\cos(y_1^2+y_2^2)e_3$ has an unbounded gradient,
so classical regularity alone does not give this); and one sentence of Remark (iii) in Section 9B on the earlier location of exterior
vorticity, which Lemma 21 does not support once the enlarged balls reach the axis, was replaced. Section 11 was then refereed in the
same way in four parts. No defect was found in its proofs; Lemma 31 now states that the crossings converge to $T_*$, which its
admissibility claims use, and the closing clause of Proposition 36(b), which asserted that the terminal value is spatially constant
although (b) assumes convergence only for almost every time, now assumes weak convergence at $\tau=0$. Section 12 was refereed in three
parts. No defect was found in its proofs; Corollary 42(c) and the exclusion criterion in the Remark after it now require the crossings to
converge to $T_*$ (a repeated fixed crossing would make that criterion vacuous), a convention to this effect was added to the notation of
Section 11, and the quantity $\tau^2V^2e$ in the Remark on the two branches of (FLAT$_0$), which scales like the energy, was replaced by
the invariant $\tau^2V^2e/E_0$. The same model also checked the
Limitations and this section against the Findings; it found a stale sentence on the localization of the inward work and a wrong
cross-reference, both corrected. The front matter was then checked against the repaired body, and citations and quantifiers were
corrected. The Limitations were refereed in three parts: in items 1 and 1' the radius used in a comparison is now stated for both
parameter choices; in item 2 an overstatement of what dissipation budgets exclude was corrected; in item 6 the description of the cited
compactness theorems for swirl-free axisymmetric flows was corrected (the vanishing-viscosity results assume $L^1\cap L^p$ control of
$\omega^\theta/r$ with $p>1$, and the result for smooth Euler approximations assumes $L\log^\alpha L$ control with $\alpha>1/2$, which was
checked against the published abstract). Parts (f) and (g) of the Remark (compression at small radial Reynolds number) were refereed twice
and checked independently by hand. All constants were confirmed: the five-dimensional kernel, the normalization of a circle, the
four-dimensional Riesz composition, the total $\frac12$ and its sharpness, the two coefficients, the pairing identity and the bound on
$r\varphi$. The second referee showed that the smallness hypothesis of (g) is incompatible with the selected record windows (Lemma 29(b),(c)
force $\sup|r\varphi|\ge1/\sqrt3-o(1)$ there); this is now stated and proved in (g) and recorded in the closing sentence of that Remark and in
Limitations item 1, whose description of (f) was also corrected. The source-weighted form of this bound and the elementary
kinematic bound $\sup_x|r\varphi|\ge M^2/(128D)$ at velocity records satisfying $4GD\le M^2$ were then refereed separately; every step and constant was
confirmed, the case $G=0$ of the converse was written out, and the referee pointed to the $BMO^{-1}$ regularity criterion of Lei and
Zhang, which makes a uniform finite bound on $\sup_x|r\varphi(x,t)|$ throughout a terminal time interval incompatible with a singularity; this is stated in (g), in the front
matter and in Limitations item 1. Theorem 1.4 of that paper and its hypotheses (suitable weak solutions, initial data in $L^2$ with
bounded initial circulation, a $BMO$ potential of the full velocity) were read in the original, and its use is confined to the contextual regularity observation.

Literature. Re-read in the original texts for this report: arXiv 2402.13229 (pages 1 to 4), 2507.08733 (Theorems 2.1
and 3.1, and the step of the proof of Theorem 2.1 where the threshold is used), 2606.29468 (Theorem 3.1) and 2604.07785
(Theorem 1.1). Cited as reported by the second model's literature checks, without re-reading: the
Koch-Nadirashvili-Seregin-Sverak statements used (Liouville Theorems 5.1 and 5.2, Remark 6.1, Proposition 4.1); the
no-swirl compactness theorems of Limitations 6; the theorem of Lei and Zhang and the numerical and computer-assisted
scenarios of Limitations 7; the regularity criteria compared with the dimensional scenario of Limitations 1'; and the
remaining statements of the four preprints above (Theorem 5.1 of 2507.08733, the Liouville propositions, the boundary
Holder estimate used by Zhang). Of the preprint of Constantin, Ignatova and Vicol (v2) the abstract, Definition 1.1, Theorem 1.2, Remark 1.3 and the statement and outline of proof of Proposition 3.1 were read here; its proofs were not checked.
The October 2026 literature additions in Limitations 6 (Shahmurov, Wang-Yang, Lei-Ren-Tian,
Ozanski-Palasek, Bae-Chen-Kim) require primary-source verification; for Chen-Fang-Zhang the statement of Theorem 1.1
and Remark 1.3 was later read in the arXiv version, but not the proof. The second-model literature consultation that
reported them states in its final form that it retrieved no original paper or abstract and withdraws its earlier
claims of verification, and the auditor of that passage could not retrieve the sources either. Here the abstracts
of arXiv 2604.21213 v3 and 2610.06385 were read (twice, the second time to check the wording of Limitations 6
against them); the $p=3$ identity, the signed integrating-factor argument, the interpolation and the weak-$L^3$
estimate were checked by hand and then audited, and continuation uses the Chen-Fang-Zhang criterion conditionally
on its proof, which was not checked. The conditional divergence of the work in the Remark on the relation to the literature after Proposition 37A and the scalar consistency
check in Limitations 1' were audited by the second model as a hostile referee. It confirmed the interpolation, the exponents and the restart,
and found four defects, each checked by hand and repaired: the divergence must be derived for $\int\mathcal K$ first and then transferred to the
work through $\Sigma'\ge0$ (which gives the full limit); the scalar rates need $\sigma>0$; the separate limits must hold for one common $\alpha$
(they were first checked with different values, one of which violates Proposition 14C(b)), and the floor, the next-record index, the dyadic
disjointness and the summability need checks of their own; and the boundedness of $h$ requires the work bound at every late first crossing.
The symbolic computation was redone with one common $\alpha$ and these additional checks. It was repeated for the second choice in
Limitations 1' ($D=A=\tau^{b}M^2/L^4$, at that stage with $S=\sigma D^{1/2}$) at $b=0$ and $b=\frac1{16}$, with the scalar forms of Lemma 34(b), $A\tau\to\infty$ and
$M^2\tau\to\infty$ added. A second hostile recomputation by the second model confirmed these exponents and found that combinations of
proved bounds which the computation had not included fail on part of the parameter range then stated ($\kappa\alpha\le\frac1{32}$, $\sigma\le\sqrt G$):
the dissipation lower bound from dyadic means and Proposition 14D(a), and the swirl restriction from Lemma 1(a) with the angular
derivative, both now in Limitations 1' and both checked by hand. The ranges were reduced to $\kappa\alpha\le\frac1{256}$ ($b=0$),
$\kappa\alpha\le\frac1{512}$ ($b=\frac1{16}$) and $\sigma^2\le G^2(1+G^2)^{-1/2}$, these combinations and their weaker relatives were added, the summability
exponent of the second choice was corrected to $\frac34-2b-2\kappa\alpha$, and all checks pass at both values. At $b=\frac18$ some fail for every $\alpha>0$ (among them
the supply condition of Proposition 14D(b)), so the variant is asserted only for $b=\frac1{16}$. The audit also corrected the wording of the
logarithmic conclusion, now stated with the ratio $h/\log(M^2/D)$. A third hostile recomputation by the second model confirmed these
derivations and checks and found that both choices then used for the swirl ($S=\sigma M/L^2$ and $S=\sigma D^{1/2}$) violate, for every $\sigma>0$,
the lower bound $\mathcal K\ge\frac34Y\ge\pi^2S^6/(24G^2D^2)$ obtained from Lemma 29(a) and the swirl disc, through the work lower bound it
implies; this was checked by hand and is now the Remark on the swirl disc after Lemma 34. The swirl was replaced by $S=c_S\tau^{-1/2}$ for both choices, the two swirl-disc bounds, the upper bound
of $\Sigma'$ by the energy and the dissipation, two upper bounds on the work rate, and comparisons of the lower bound of Proposition 33 with the swirl and with the
accumulated work were added to the symbolic computation. Both runs of this computation pass at $b=0$ and $b=\frac1{16}$ on the stated
parameter ranges. A fourth hostile audit by the second model confirmed the swirl-disc constants and these exponents, supplied the joint proxy
tuple now stated in Limitations 1' (satisfying Lemma 29 at all times), and tested stronger coupled work, source, strain and produced-vorticity
comparisons (among them the strain needed at an instantaneous swirl maximum, the maximum principle for $u^\theta/r$ and the estimate
$|\int q^2u^r|\le S\|q\|_2\|u^r/r\|_2$); none of the inequalities examined contradicts the repaired rates, and its wording corrections were
adopted. A consultation of the second model then found an inequality outside that list, Proposition 41, which excludes the
swirl constants $c_S^2<\frac7{18}$ that the list had allowed. Proposition 41, Corollary 42 and this exclusion were checked by hand step by
step (both vorticity equations, the cancellation in the stretching term, the five-dimensional Dirichlet identity, the weighted bound,
the constants $\frac92$ and $3^{-1/2}$, and the energy identity for $(u^\theta)^2/r$, whose dissipative part was also confirmed by numerical quadrature
for an explicit profile); the family was restricted to $c_S\ge1$. A hostile audit of Section 12 by the second model confirmed the equations, the
regularity argument, the cancellation, the constants, parts (b) and (c), the identity for $(u^\theta)^2/r$, the logarithmic consequence and the
remark, and asked for an explicit account of the axis values (adopted), for the equation number of the coupled system and a qualified
literature statement (adopted). It also showed that the energy proxies first used in Limitations 1' ($H=\tau^{-2}$, $J=\tau^{-3}$) violated two
elementary consequences of the equations, the Hardy-type inequality of Proposition 41(d) and the separate energy inequality of $\eta$; both
were checked by hand and the proxies were replaced by those now stated, which satisfy them. Its suggestion to place the record point at a
radius $\tau^{2b}L^{-8}$ was rejected: the enstrophy bound in Proposition 41(c), which we derived in checking it, excludes such a radius on the
family (second bound of Corollary 42(d)); the record point is placed within $4\rho_*$ of the axis instead, and the range of $c_{33}\alpha$ was
reduced to $\frac1{16}$ and $\frac1{32}$ for the two values of $b$. The consequence of Proposition 41(d) for $H$ and Corollary 42(d) were checked by hand.
The new comparisons were added to the symbolic computation, which passes at both values of $b$. This verifies the specified scalar
comparisons, not every scalar consequence of the equations or their realization by fields.
A further hostile re-audit by the second model confirmed Proposition 41(c), (d), Corollary 42(d) and the energy proxies then in use, and
found one error: a remark after Corollary 42 said that the enstrophy is not bounded by the historical records, whereas
$\|\nabla u(t)\|_2^2\le\|\nabla u_0\|_2^2+E_0D(t)$ (from $\frac12\frac d{dt}\|\omega\|_2^2\le\|\nabla u\|_\infty\|\omega\|_2^2$ and the energy inequality); this was checked by
hand and the remark corrected, together with the wording on the record radius. A further consultation proved Lemma 43; its kernel
formula, the sharp constant $\frac12$, the four regions of the integral and the final constant were checked by hand. Lemma 43 excluded the
rates then assigned in Limitations 1' (enstrophy $\tau^{-7/8}$, $\|\eta\|_2^2=2\tau^{-7/4}$, swirl exactly $c_S\tau^{-1/2}$): the right side of Lemma 43
was of order $\tau^{-21/16}L$ against $M^2=\tau^{-3/2}$. The family was redesigned with the rates now stated, and all comparisons, old and new,
pass the symbolic computation at both values of $b$. Corollary 44 and the Remark after it were derived from Lemma 43, Proposition 41(d),
the enstrophy bound and Lemma 28 and checked by hand. A hostile audit by the second model then confirmed Lemma 43 (including the monotonicity
of its second form in the three upper bounds) and Corollary 44 with all its constants and its dichotomy, and found the following, each checked by hand
and repaired: two equalities in the proof of Lemma 43 are inequalities on the half-plane; the Remark after Corollary 44 must keep the exceptional
alternative and note that Lemma 34(b) already forces faster swirl growth; a threshold in Limitations 1' ($16\sqrt2\kappa\alpha<\frac18$) was left over from the
earlier rates and is $\frac14$ (or $\frac3{16}$); and Limitations 1' asserted Lemma 43 at the record point wherever it lies, having checked only its second form.
The first form, with $R$ and $\delta$ free, excludes a record point within distance $\tau^{1/2}L^{-5/4}$ of the axis on that family, so the record radius is now
specified as $\|\nabla u\|_2/\|\eta\|_2$, where both forms hold; this was checked by hand and added to the symbolic computation, which passes at both values of $b$.
Corollary 45 (Type I swirl against a near-axis meridional velocity of order $(T_*-t)^{-3/4}$) was derived from Proposition 41(a), (d), Lemma 43 and the
enstrophy bound and checked by hand step by step; it replaces an earlier conditional remark that assumed a nondecreasing enstrophy. A separate hostile
audit by the second model of an earlier four-step proof confirmed each step, the constants $\kappa_1$ and $a$, the absence of a circular choice of $\kappa$ and the counting in Step 4,
and asked for two clarifications, now made (the set $\mathcal G$ is relatively open in $[t_1,T_*)$; the pulse history in the Remark after Corollary 44 needs a stated
background enstrophy and gradient bound). It found no published statement identical to Corollary 45 and none is claimed.
Corollary 46 was first derived in its last-passage form, checked by hand, and its comparison on the scalar family was added to the symbolic
computation (which passes at both values of $b$). A hostile audit by the second model confirmed it, the repaired record radius in Limitations 1'
(both forms of Lemma 43, uniformly in $R$ and $\delta$, and the exclusion of an axial record), the comparison with Lemma 34(b) and the thresholds,
asked for the late-time qualification now stated in Remark (i) after Corollary 46, and pointed out the stronger additive form
$H'\le\frac{2187}{128}V^4e$, which was checked by hand and is now the statement of Corollary 46. A further consultation of the same referee
confirmed the additive form and a dyadic-block proof of a stronger Corollary 45 (near-axis condition only on a set of infinite measure $dt/(\tau L)$), noted that
this measure must diverge near $T_*$ (the restriction $T_*-a_0\le1$ ensures that any divergence occurs near $T_*$), and proposed the shorter integrated proof now given, which was checked by hand step by step
(the pointwise bound $1/(\tau L)\le e+\tau H/\kappa_1$, the Tonelli step and the constant $C_1$).
Lemma 47 through Corollary 52 were developed in a parallel line of work on this problem and refereed there by the second model, which found the exact kernel
of Lemma 47 (replacing a cruder kernel bound), the threshold $k\le\frac12$ and the dyadic-block proof of Theorem 49(a), the power in Theorem 49(d) with its
scalar example, and the shell integral of Proposition 51 (replacing an earlier version that assumed the record point within the inner plateau radius, with a
weaker exponent), and withdrew several claims of that work (a switching configuration, a threshold of order $h_1^{1/2}$, and the exclusion of the scalar family of
Limitations 1' as specified, whereas its on-axis reading is excluded). A separate hostile audit by the second model of those sections and of the
combination in Corollary 50 confirmed them with these repairs. Every proof was then re-derived here by hand (Newton's potential, the integrals
$\int_{|y|=s}r^4=\frac{32\pi}{15}s^6$ and $\int_0^1(1-x^3)^2=\frac9{14}$, the optimization giving $0.3801209$, the Riccati ceiling, the dyadic and unit-block summations,
the shell integral $3\pi r_t^2/\rho_k+8\pi\rho_k$ and the top-shell Cauchy-Schwarz bound), and the two sharpness examples after Theorem 49, the emptiness of Proposition 51
on the scalar family, the failure there of the hypothesis of Theorem 49(d), the inequalities of Lemma 48 on it, and the exclusion of its on-axis reading were
added to the symbolic computation, which passes at both values of $b$. The single-time family in the Remark on the two branches was checked only to orders of magnitude. The construction of a nested stack from pure-swirl data at the end of the toroidal part of that Remark was proposed by the second model in a consultation of the parallel line of work; the identities $\partial_tb(0)=\mathbb P(rq_0^2e_r)$, $\partial_t\omega^\theta(0)=r\partial_z(q_0^2)$, the central value of each shell and the bound on its means were re-derived here by hand at leading order in $\rho_{\max}/R$. A hostile referee of that paragraph by the second model required a monotone bump with $0\le g\le1$ (for the circulation bound, the orientation of the shells and the exterior means), the exact planar dyadic means (the increment per octave is not exactly $c$), a quantified planar approximation, and a narrower scope sentence; all were checked and adopted. The radial velocity of a single shell, computed from the axisymmetric Biot-Savart law by quadrature with $R=1$ and $\rho_k=0.01,0.02,0.04$, differs from the planar value by at most $1.4\cdot10^{-3}c$ at the centre and by about $0.07\,c\rho_k/R$ at distance $4\rho_k$ along $e_r$, consistent with the stated error bound. A later hostile referee by the second model of the stack paragraph, of Lemma 57 with its decomposition, of the duality paragraphs and of the corresponding summary supplied an analytic proof of the kernel comparison, which replaces an earlier sketch and was checked here step by step (the two elementary inequalities for $B$ and the bound $|H-K_2|\le1/R$ were also checked numerically), the condition on $\rho_{\max}/R$ under which the exact dyadic means increase, and wording repairs (weak signs of the shells, the sign of the backward axial drift, the localization stated for the meridional velocity at the record point, the fixed-$K$ qualification and the radial cutoff in the summary, and a separate symbol for the strip integral); it confirmed the constants $\frac12$, $\frac43$ and $3\pi$ and the orders in the scalar histories, including the use of the stronger restriction on $\alpha$ for the second choice of $D$. Lemma 57 and the decomposition after it were first obtained in the parallel line of work and refereed there by the second model (the repairs concerned the powers of $K$, the form of the leak term, the use of the cutoff operator $\mathcal T$ instead of a restriction of the vorticity to the disc, and the scope); they were re-derived here step by step. The kernel bound (a) with the constant $\frac12$ was derived here by the angular integration (the parallel work cited the corresponding bound of Gallay and Sverak, with an unspecified constant), and the orders on the scalar histories, the lower bound for $r_t$ in the general statement and the comparison with the mass statement were recomputed by hand. A hostile referee of Lemma 57 and its decomposition paragraph by the second model confirmed every step of the proof and led to four precisions in the paragraph (the admissibility of the window, the orders for each choice of $D$, the exact form of the sufficient condition on $h_b$, and the statement that the conclusion is a pointwise bound on velocity, not on vorticity mass). The quantitative off-axis remark in Limitations 4 was derived here, refereed by the second model (which proposed the form with the local energy bound in place of the cubic criterion), and its two cited propositions were read in the arXiv versions. The duality bound for the imported mass after Lemma 57 was first obtained in the parallel line of work; the use of the dual solution of Lemma 40, its probabilistic representation, the strip estimate and the orders in the scalar histories were re-derived here. A hostile referee pass of the second model on the parallel derivation found it correct after repairs (the axis term of the adjoint pairing, which vanishes only on the dual domain of Lemma 40; killing of the diffusion at the axis; the distinction between the two comparison masses; sufficiency, not necessity, of small near-axis production), and these are incorporated in the text; the same pass supplied the pointwise bound for the imported vorticity through the occupation estimate for $R^{-1}$, which was re-derived here. A hostile referee of the paragraph as entered here by the second model confirmed the duality step, the constants $4$ and $\frac43$, the velocity bound and the orders, and led to repairs (the range of $K$ for the Gaussian factor, the generator of the five-dimensional process and the regularization of the occupation estimate at the axis, the explicit exclusion criterion $S_{str}+E_a<2\pi m$ in place of an unspecified power of $\tau$, the attenuation retained by the weighted bound, and the statement that the numerical bounds give no lower bound for the actual import), which are applied. The remark after Corollary 56 that in the scalar histories only alternative (ii) can hold, late in the window, was derived here by hand; a hostile referee by the second model confirmed its exponents and led to two clarifications (the instantaneous gradient against its historical supremum, and $s=t-\lambda_1>0$ with $T_*-s\sim\lambda_1$ at late crossings), which were made. Corollary 58 was first obtained in the parallel line of work as a reduction of the mass target of the toroidal branch to Lemma 40A; the proof and the bound of the data terms by $C(s_0)|\mathcal R'|$, which needs no integrability of $\eta(s_0)$, were derived here by hand; a ceiling $|\mathcal R'|G^{-2}\int S^4M$ for $W_\psi$, obtained in the parallel line of work and first accepted here, rests on the pointwise bound $|q|\le S^2/G$, which holds only for $r\ge G/S$; a follow-up hostile referee of the repaired passages by the second model found this, and the ceiling was replaced by the bound with $|q|\le D$, checked here by hand together with its orders. The same follow-up pass confirmed the conservation of $\int\psi\,dx$, Corollary 58 with its constants and the exponents of the remark after Corollary 56, and led to wording repairs in the summary and in the sentence introducing Corollary 58. A hostile referee of the parallel derivation by the second model found the reduction correct as a sufficient condition and corrected its interpretation (the paths of the adjoint diffusion are not fluid trajectories, the whole-history budget is not the finite-window alternative, and only the integrand is shared with the condition near the axis); these corrections are incorporated. A hostile referee of Corollary 58 as entered here by the second model confirmed the proof and its constants ($|\mathcal R'|=\frac98\pi^2r_t^3$, the coefficient $3/\sqrt2$ and $2\sqrt2\pi c_1/3=\sqrt2\pi/256$) and led to repairs (the fixed $\delta$, late crossings and $h_b\to\infty$ as explicit hypotheses, the source contribution taken from $s_0$, the separate accounting of the inherited vorticity, the statement that the two weighted-work conditions are not ordered, and the reading of the scalar orders as a numerical ceiling, not as the size of $W_\psi$); these were checked by hand and applied. The clarification of the term velocity first crossing (attainment of the running maximum), the statement of the planar leading term in the dyadic means of the pure-swirl stack and two notation remarks were made after a cross-report consistency audit. The constants of Lemma 47 (the sphere integrals, the matching of Newton's potential at $s=\rho$, the minimum $0.3801209$ and $0.3802^{-8/3}=13.18$) were also checked by a separate symbolic and numerical computation. A further hostile audit of the entered text by the second model confirmed every constant and found a quantifier gap in Theorem 49(b) (the blocks must be arbitrarily far out), the undefined axial value of $q$ in Proposition 51, the case $k<0$ of the last sentence of Corollary 50, a wrong decimal in Lemma 47, and overstated scope claims (the exponential gain needs $p_q\ge\delta h_b$, and the single-time family bears only on a normalized exponential lower bound); all were checked by hand and repaired. The meridional-mass proxies added to the scalar family in the toroidal part of that Remark were proposed by the second model as an obstruction; their budget identities and the integral of $Q^2$ were checked by hand and by a separate symbolic computation, and the limits over the whole parameter range of Limitations 1' follow from the positive exponents of $\tau$ in the ratios (for instance the local proxy divided by $Q$ is of order $\tau^{1/2-d-c_{33}\alpha}L^5$ when $D=\tau^{-3/2+d}L^{-4}$); the local source bound in that Remark was re-derived by hand. The localization of the exponential mass bound to a disc away from the axis in the same Remark (the variant of Proposition 51 with the outer shell count and the bound on the vorticity mass of the disc of radius $r_t/2$) was derived by hand from the decomposition in the proof of Proposition 33. The whole toroidal part of that Remark was then audited by the second model as a hostile referee, which confirmed the constants ($c_1$ and the shell counts of the localization, the source bound, the orders of the single-time family) and found that the localization needs $\delta<1/(4C_0)$, late crossings and the cutoff $j=\min(J,q-4)$ (the decomposition stops at $J$), that the shell count of the toroidal case still forces a positive but less than one unit of outer contribution, and that the inherited vorticity from a fixed time is controlled by the maximum principle; all were checked by hand and repaired. The conservative form of the meridional vorticity equation and the conservation of material meridional mass without viscosity and source, stated there, were checked by hand and by a symbolic computation; a second consultation of the same model on local mechanisms for the axis-free disc (transit times, circulation, import from the axis, persistence of means, rearrangement) found no estimate that closes this branch. Lemma 53 (with its constants: the Sobolev constant, the choice of the circle by averaging over the annulus, the Lipschitz radius and the logarithmic factor) and Corollary 54 (the split at $m=\lceil5h_b/(16C_0)\rceil$ and the resulting lower bound for $r_t$) were re-derived by hand. An independent hostile audit by a second model confirmed both, with the constants $64$, $95$ and $6080$; following it, the remark after Corollary 54 now states that the second case also contains crossings with $1\le p_q<m$, gives the improvement over $cM^3/D$ in a form without $r_t$, gives the orders for both scalar histories, and states the consistency claim in its limited form. The comparison with the earlier bound from Proposition 14D(a) was added here after that audit. The localization of the core estimate (the supremum $A_{\rm loc}$ of $|\omega^\theta|$ on the disc of radius $r_t/2$ and the index $m$) was derived by hand; an independent hostile audit by a second model confirmed every step and constant, asked for the justification of $A_{\rm loc}>0$, and found that the closing target had been glossed as a statement about the origin of the vorticity; the target is now stated for the total vorticity of the disc. Theorem 49(e), (f) and Corollary 52(d) (the completed square $9V^2H-H^2/e\le\frac{81}4V^4e$, the Duhamel bound and its splitting, the window bound for $1/H$ by Cauchy-Schwarz with the monotonicity of $x/(1-\exp(-x\tau))$, the identity $\int_r^{(T_*+r)/2}dt/(T_*-t)=\log2$, and the formula for $\tau^2\Lambda$) were re-derived by hand; an independent audit of (e) and of Corollary 52(d) by a second model, made in the parallel line of work, found both correct; a hostile audit of the entered text of (e), (f), Corollary 52(d) and the near-axis Remark by a second model confirmed every constant and found that the comparison of (e) with (a) in Remark (v) was wrong for $0<k\le\frac14$ (the integral conclusion does contain (a) and (b) there), that "infinitely many crossings" must be a sequence $t_n\uparrow T_*$, and that $K$, the domain of the window integral and the range of $t_1$ needed explicit statements; all were checked by hand and repaired. Theorem 49(g) and Corollary 52(e) (the integrating factor $\exp(9\int V^21_{\mathcal G})$, the factor $2$ between its exponent and $\Phi_t$, the Cauchy-Schwarz step, the monotonicity of $\Phi_t$ and the constant of the threshold length) were derived by hand; a hostile audit by a second model confirmed both statements and every constant, and asked for three wording repairs (how (f) follows from (g), the dependence of the window on the dissipation bound, and threshold length instead of window length, with a possibly empty range), which were made. Lemma 55 and Corollary 56 (the five-dimensional lift, the operator identities for $\eta$, $\Gamma$ and the drift of $\Gamma^2$ along the backward paths, the quadratic step and the constants $3/8$, $3\cdot2^{4.5}$ and $2^{-41.5}/3$) were derived by hand, and the operator identities and constants were checked by a symbolic computation. An independent hostile audit of these two statements confirmed the lift, the Ito computations, the localization and limit arguments, the maximum form, all constants and the scaling reading of the conclusion; it asked for the explicit slab bounds on $u$ and $\nabla u$, a corrected comparison with the pointwise source bound (coefficients of $\lambda$, with the factor $D_*\rho^2/(4G)$), the exclusion of the case $s=0$ in (i) at late crossings, the description of (ii) as a statement about values on a cylinder, and an iteration through Lemma 55 with explicit windows instead of Corollary 56; it also supplied the maximum-principle proof of the Remark after Lemma 55. These repairs were made, and the new constants (the optimization over $c$, the completing of squares, the windows of the iteration and the factor $\frac3{512}D_*r_t^2/G$) were checked by hand and by a symbolic computation.
