A further consistency audit of the summary against the body found an incorrect description of $A$ in the opening paragraph and four omitted hypotheses in the summary items 8 and 9 and in Limitations 1 (the radii in Lemma 31, the hypotheses of Proposition 37A(c), $b\ne0$ in Lemma 43, regime (II) for $|b|\ge\frac78M$); all were repaired. Theorem 49(e), (f), Corollary 52(d) and Lemma 53 were also first obtained in the parallel line of work mentioned above (part (f) suggested by the second model refereeing it, Lemma 53 in a second-model consultation there), and the identity for $\Gamma^2$ along backward paths used in Lemma 55 was found there for another case of this problem (in a rescaled limit, with stopping at a moving parabolic cylinder); the version with stopping at a fixed radius, the Remark after Lemma 55 and Corollary 56 were obtained here. A consistency audit of Section 12 against its summary by the second model found no gap in the numbered proofs; it found that the single-time family of the two-branch Remark had been described as a proof of impossibility, that Corollary 56 had been glossed as a statement about the age of the vorticity, that the source bound over one transit time had been read as excluding recent production while ignoring import, and that a normalized threshold in that Remark had dropped the factor $(1+\tau/\lambda_1)^{3/2}$; it also found notation collisions (the time set and the circulation bound both written $G$, the swirl quotient and the octave count both written $q$, a disc and $\|q\|_2^2$ both written $P$, a weight, a field and a rate all written $w$, the material derivative and $\|\nabla u\|_\infty$ both written $D_t$, the enstrophy and the exponential both written with $e$), omitted constants and time domains in the summary, an ambiguous reference in the proof of Corollary 42(c), an imprecise paraphrase of Theorem 49(b) and an overstated range statement in Corollary 52(e). All of these were checked against the text and repaired as stated now; the time set is $\mathcal G$ throughout. A later outside reading of the Overview and the Result in the parallel line of work, done as by a reader who has not seen the body, found no numerical inconsistency between the Overview, the Result and the cited statements, and listed symbols used before their definition and five wording points. I checked each point against the cited statements and added the missing definitions (the cylindrical components, the first crossings of gradient levels, the Euler-scale normalization and the plateau alternative, the Reynolds defect, the constant of Lemma 9', the transit time, the meridional BMO norm, the energy length, the exterior certificate and the alternatives (B) and (B*)), the quantifiers in the statement about (C), the precise form of Corollary 54 on the toroidal branch, the reference for $\mathcal I_{s_0}(t)=o(S(t))$ and the admissible radii of Lemma 31 in the Overview; one suggested qualification was not adopted because the sentence that follows it already states it. The second model then compared each added definition with the body statement it summarizes and found all of them correct; it also listed further symbols used before their definition in the Overview and the Result (the residual alternative (R), the oscillation $O_\epsilon$ and the certificate (OSC$_0$), the conditions (VL1), (FLAT), (C*-D), (L2LS), (A) and (VC), the defect energy $e_1$, the memory work $W_T$ and the part $\eta^{in}$), a second use of the letter $q$ in the toroidal paragraph of the Result, a missing qualification that $\delta>0$ is fixed in the statement taken from Corollary 52(d), and an ambiguous item reference for $H$. I checked each against the body and repaired all of them. A further outside read of the section Limitations / Open Questions recomputed its constants and exponents (they were consistent) and reported notation used there before or without a definition: the letter $h$ used both for $T_*-t$ in three scalar histories and for the flatness ratio $M/\Theta_0$, the letter $\delta_n$ used both for the window length and for the persistence time of Proposition 14D(b), and undefined symbols ($\epsilon(s_0)$, $\varepsilon_n$, $K$, $E_{n,R}$, $P_{n,R}$, $\psi_n$, $\sigma(0)$, the quantities of Lemma 29, $\xi$ and $P$ of Section 12, $\mathcal R$); it also reported a sentence that attributed to every normalization with retained oscillation a bound that Theorem 38 gives only in (C), a missing quantifier on the crossing times in a consequence of Proposition 33, and an unsimplified constant. I checked each item against the body, renamed $T_*-t$ to $\tau$ in those histories, added the definitions, and repaired the sentence and the quantifier. The second model then refereed these repairs against the body. It confirmed the recomputed exponents and the glosses, corrected one description (the region of Corollary 6A is a rectangle of the half-plane, a square only away from the axis), and found further reuses of letters inside that section ($F$, $C_0$, $q$, $\delta_n$, $K$, a rescaled field called $h$, $S_n$, $\varphi$) and two quantities without a local definition (the dyadic data of Proposition 14D(b) and the rescaled swirl quotient $q_n$). I checked these and repaired them by renaming or by local definitions. The letter $\tau$ still denotes $T_*-t$ in the scalar histories, where it is always defined, and rescaled time elsewhere; the letter $h$ still denotes both the flatness ratio and the fixed distance multiple $h\ell_n$ from the axis of the exterior conditions, as in the body. The paragraph after Corollary 58 on the inward work away from the axis (the bound $288\pi^2G^2Mr_t$ over the last window and the comparison of $Mr_t$ with the thresholds of Corollary 58) was derived here by hand and refereed by the second model, which confirmed the bounds, the constants, the orders in the scalar histories and the use of Green's theorem, and asked that the paragraph say that only the off-axis contribution is bounded, that Green's theorem controls the signed and not the absolute planar mass, and that the closing remark concerns spatially uniform bounds for $\psi$ only. I made these repairs and replaced the scalar-history comparison of the second model by the general inequalities $c_1(M^2/A)2^{k''}<\frac3{256}Mr_t$ and $M^2/A<2^{5-q}Mr_t$, which I derived by hand from the localization (they use $c_1=\frac3{512}$, $r_t>2^{q-1}\rho_0$, $\rho_0=M/(16A)$ and $k''\le q-4$); these two inequalities were not sent to a referee, but they were rechecked independently by hand from the same facts. The Remark on the signed form of Corollary 58 was derived here from the proof of Lemma 40A and refereed by the second model, which confirmed the passage to the limit, the signed form of the corollary, the existence of the terminal limit of $H$ and the linear fields with their pressure, and asked that the illustration by the linear fields not be read as a statement about the adjoint-weighted works; the Remark says so. The Remark on what the scalar histories leave open for the signed form was derived here by hand; its radial integrals, the radius-restricted bound, the minimal axial lengths and fractions in the scalar histories and the stationary rotation-suction fields were also checked by a symbolic computation, and the identity for $\int\psi r^{-2}dx$ with its axis flux was rechecked by hand. Parts of it (the spreading lengths, the incompressibility bound and the identity for $\int\psi r^{-2}dx$) were also obtained in the parallel line of work, with a second-model referee there. A hostile referee of the entered text by the second model confirmed all integrals, lengths and fractions and the stationary fields, and asked that the fractions be stated for the instantaneous inward work, that the second-moment radius be given as a range ($\Sigma'$ is only bounded between two multiples of $\tau^{-1/2}L^{-1}$), that the barrier statement claim only a length bound for intervals with a uniform lower bound, that the stationary fields be described as a statement about the stationary equations alone, and that the advective displacement $M\tau$ not be called a reach of the diffusive adjoint (it is replaced by the Gaussian tail of the paragraph after Lemma 57); these repairs were made, and the conclusion is stated as neither establishing nor refuting the signed form. The converse bound $H(t^-)\le72\pi^2G^2$ in the Remark on the signed form and the paragraph How to resume in the Overview were derived here by hand and refereed by the second model, which confirmed the bound, the limit argument with Scheffe's lemma and the uniform convergence, and the transfer of bounds between the signed work and the dual-weighted dissipation, confirmed that Corollary 52(b) holds in the first case of the localization with $p_{out}$ in place of $p_q$ (its proof uses only the lower bound for the coupled energy) and the description of what Corollary 52(d) excludes, and asked for local definitions of the octave counts and of the window mass condition, for the explicit time window of the off-axis estimate, and for a narrower reading of the sharpness remark in the paragraph after Corollary 58; these repairs were made. Item (vi) of the Remark on what the scalar histories leave open was derived here by hand and refereed by the second model, which confirmed the identity with its axis flux, the Young step, the conservation of the dual mass and the constants, and asked for four repairs, which were made and checked by hand: the energy comparison is stated only where $\psi\le16(r/r_t)^2$, upper bounds are written with $O(\cdot)$, the time integral in the scalar histories is $O(L^3)$ up to a fixed contribution from times before the prescribed interval, and the transfer to the signed work is stated in asymptotic form. Item (vii) was derived here by hand with the cruder constant $2\rho^{-3}(I(G^2+16I))^{1/2}$ and refereed by the second model, which confirmed it, supplied the completed square that gives the constant stated, and noted that the bound on the cutoff mass is that of the outer torus; we checked the identity, the square and the Cauchy-Schwarz step by hand. The kinematic swirl history in (vii) is a modification of one supplied by the same referee as an obstruction for the relaxed constraints, with the remark that it violates the circulation equation; the referee's version violated the prescribed values of $\|\xi\|_2^2$ and $\|\nabla\xi\|_2^2$ by powers of $\tau$ (a point raised by a referee of the parallel line of work for a related core model), and the parameters were changed so that these bounds hold; we checked every exponent against the ceilings of the scalar histories by exact rational arithmetic, together with the pointwise, dissipation and (vi) checks, and that Lemma 40A excludes it for solutions; these checks concern upper bounds only and do not show that the example realizes the scalar histories. The same referee confirmed the identities of the shell-tested Remark and found no estimate of the required strength for the near-axis part. A later hostile referee by the second model of item (vii), its example, the shell-tested Remark and the corresponding sentences of How to resume (a) confirmed the completed square, the constants $\sqrt2$ and $\frac98\pi^2$, the exponents of the example (including its weighted source, which is of two-sided order $\tau^{-1/24}L^{7/48}$), and the kernel, angular and volume constants and the constant $\frac3{256}$ of the shell-tested Remark; it asked for a compactly supported amplitude and the exact limit $(2/\pi)C_a$ in the oscillatory example of that Remark, for the positive parts and the uniformity in the sentence of How to resume (a), and for upper-bound language for the example of (vii); these repairs were checked by hand and made. Item (d) of the shell-tested Remark was derived here by hand; a hostile referee by the second model confirmed the kernel bound, the symmetry argument and the inequalities of (d), corrected two statements of the draft (the two sides of the bound for $Q$ have the same scaling degree, they are not separately invariant; and on the scalar history of Limitations 1' the right side is only bounded above, so the comparison there is made through the forced lower bound $Q\ge\tau^{-2}L^2(1-o(1))$), and asked that the closing sentence of that Remark acknowledge the $L^2$ control of $\partial_z\Psi_k$; I checked each repair by hand, and added the lower bound for $B$ from the shell cap. A second referee of the entered text confirmed every constant, the scaling statement (with $A$ of the dimension of a velocity gradient, so $j$ and $k''$ do not change) and the sign of the dropped axis term, and asked that the scalar-history clause separate the lower bound for $B$ from $B$ and compatibility with the total energy from its distribution; this was done. Item (viii) was derived here by hand from the proof of Lemma 40A; the localized identity was also checked numerically by quadrature for a smooth test configuration (agreement to about $5\cdot10^{-16}$), and the error bound and the orders in the scalar histories were checked by hand. A hostile referee of item (viii) by the second model re-derived the identity independently, confirmed the constants $c_2+5c_1$ and $4c_1$, the passage $t-\delta\to t$, the pointwise source inequality and the orders, supplied the explicit $C_\phi$, the comparison $3S_\psi-W_\Phi=O(M^2/A)$ with its proof and the formula with the primitive $B$, and asked for a complete account of the boundary terms, a qualified form of the sentence on larger radii and a corrected sentence on the kinematic history (the inequality alone keeps a remainder; the identity itself excludes that history); these were checked by hand and made. The bound for the flux part of $W_\Phi$ with the weight $w$ was first obtained in the parallel line of work, where a second-model referee gave it in this form; the identity for $\int\psi w\Gamma^2dx$ was re-derived here by hand and checked numerically by quadrature for a smooth test configuration (agreement to about $3\cdot10^{-16}$), and the constants were recomputed for the cutoff used here. Item (ix) sharpens a comparison made here earlier with $\|\nabla u\|_\infty$ in place of $k$; the sharpening by the one-sided outflow rate and Agmon's inequality was found in the parallel line of work, where a second-model referee supplied it, and the barrier, the two identities with axis terms, the Cauchy-Schwarz step and the orders were re-derived here by hand. A further independent referee checked the barrier computation, the comparison on the truncated cylinder (it supplied the boundary correction $C_g\epsilon^2$ and the penalization in $z$), the source identity, both identities with axis terms including the coefficient $8$, the function class for Agmon's inequality, the exponents and the threshold algebra, and the It\^o identity; it also asked that the threshold comparison be stated as a comparison of upper bounds and that the remark on the sign of $u^r$ claim no necessity, and both changes were made. A consistency referee of the whole block (How to resume (a), the Remarks after Corollary 58 and the shell-tested Remark) then rechecked items (i)-(ix), the constants quoted in How to resume and the parts (a)-(c) of the shell-tested Remark. It found one wrong sign, in the integrated expression for $3S_\psi-W_\Phi$ (the error term enters with a plus sign; the absolute estimate is unaffected), and several overstatements: the necessity wording after (iv) and (v), the scope of the kinematic example of (vii) (it concerns only the first choice of $D$ in Limitations 1'), the diffusion age in the Gaussian tail remark, the normalization of the scaled shell pairings, and the closing sentence on what decides the case. All were corrected, and I checked each correction. A follow-up referee confirmed these corrections and asked for five further wording changes, all of which were made: the threshold language in (v) and in the paragraph before the Remark, the window work in (v) stated as an upper bound, the unresolved statement after (ix) named as the work bound of How to resume (a), and one transition in (viii). A further hostile referee compared the Overview with the body, statement by statement. It confirmed the status line, the description of the first missing implication and items 1 to 5. It asked for eleven precision repairs, and I checked each against the cited body statement before making it. The repairs restore omitted hypotheses: the drift class and the strain form in item 6; the fixed $s_0$, the sequence and the support of the final cutoff in the sentence on Corollary 58; the cylinder and window of item (v) in How to resume (a); the restriction to admissible isotropic normalizations; and the record hypotheses of the extractions at the BMO amplitude. They also replace "small" by $o(\theta_b)$ for Lemma 57, state that the outer shell count sums magnitudes, and separate two symbols from other uses: the function $q$ from the octave index, and the weighted swirl energy $H$ from the coupled vorticity energy. A follow-up referee confirmed these repairs; at its request the source $\partial_zf$ and the absolute constant were named in item 6, and the sentence on Lemma 57 now says that the bound, evaluated on the scalar histories, is $o(\theta_b)$. A hostile referee of the Result section against the body found no mismatch of substance (items 1 to 9, the status and the first missing implication); its one editorial correction, a cross-reference to Result item 4', was made. A hostile referee of the Limitations against the body found no wrong claim, exponent mismatch or unsupported equivalence; its two wording corrections (the toroidal branch is characterized by $p_q\le0$, not by distance from the axis, and one phrase on what a mechanism does not exclude) were made, and its request to replace the references to the second model was met instead by defining that term in the paragraph on proofs above. A research consultation of the second model on inequalities outside the list of Limitations 1' found that the exact equality of the signed and the inward work rates in those histories is impossible (repaired there, with the argument checked by hand) and proposed four further inequalities; three of them are new relative to the list and are recorded there with their evaluations, which were checked by hand and by symbolic computation; none is violated. A further hostile referee of these last edits (the confinement remark in How to resume (a), the definition of the second model, the description of the toroidal branch, the repaired work rates and the three added inequalities) confirmed the last three and required precision repairs of the first two (the confinement of the paths does not by itself give residence in a cell or repeated inflow and outflow, and the literature reports keep their own qualifications); these were made, together with a clearer reference to the definition of $p_q$. A second research consultation of the second model, on weighted and localized energy identities for $\eta$ and $\xi$ (with cutoffs and with the adjoint weight of Lemma 40), the swirl budget together with the maximum principle for the circulation, and the integrals of these balances over windows of length comparable to $T_*-t$, found no violated inequality among those it could evaluate: the global forms of its production bounds are exactly the estimates used to derive the separate energy inequalities in the proof of Proposition 41(a), which are already checked in the list, and the localized forms involve quantities the histories do not specify, so they were neither verified nor found violated. Its sharper bound for the inward work rate was checked by hand and is the fourth added inequality; a hostile referee of these two additions confirmed the bound, its evaluation and its absence from the list, and required this precision in the identification with Proposition 41(a). A third consultation, on the dissipation of the swirl inside the cylinder $\{r<r_t/4\}$, derived exact cutoff identities for it in terms of $q$ and explicit smooth spatial profiles showing that $P$ and $\|\xi\|_2$ alone do not bound the instantaneous unweighted inner swirl dissipation; these profiles do not realize the full scalar histories or the coupled evolution. The only consequence incorporated is the ceiling $\mathcal D_{\rm in}\le2\int_{s_0}^t\mathcal K$ in item (vi) of the Remark after Corollary 58, checked by hand; a hostile referee of that sentence confirmed it and required precision repairs (the unweighted density, the cutoff justification, no lower bound), which were made.
Standard facts (the Leray-Hopf energy inequality, the circulation maximum principle, the Beale-Kato-Majda criterion, the
Caffarelli-Kohn-Nirenberg theorem, global regularity without swirl) are used as known.

Further checks and remaining verification limits. That the Gaussian lacunary profile of
Section 10 satisfies the single-time form of (FLAT) uniformly over all windows is not proved. The proof of Proposition 10' (the
three-dimensional transfer) was developed by the second model from an embedding proposed by the author, checked by hand
and then audited; the repairs made in
response to that audit (the normalized form of (vi'), the case $K>N$ in the scalar lemma, the wording of the closing
paragraph of Section 10 and two numerical descriptions) were audited again together with Corollary 6A, and the only
defect found (the description of the decay rate in (ii)) was corrected. Lemma 26A and its application were checked by hand and spot-checked
in the second whole-report audit, which found no mathematical defect beyond the degenerate cases now treated; the
re-audit of those repairs confirmed the degenerate cases and led to the restriction of the summary in the Result to
exterior rectangles. The Remark on the accumulated swirl source after Lemma 9' and the four items of Limitations 2 on viscous
cancellation, concentration-cancellation with record freezing, the near-axis cancellation functional and selection by
production events were checked by hand and then audited; the audit confirmed every formula and power and found three
defects of wording (the time localization of the source bound in the Result, a stale description of the $L^{3/2}$
contraction, and the reference in this sentence) and one missing specification (the flat-extremum profile of the pipe
flow example), all repaired. The two proposals in the Limitations 2 item on finite-energy closure of pipes and an
energy charge for record increases arose in a consultation; the divergence identity and the residual were checked by hand,
while the sign of the pressure force depends on the packet geometry and the dissipation scaling is a fixed-profile
calculation; neither supplies the missing exact-solution or record-growth argument. An audit found two omissions in an earlier wording
(the decay hypotheses on $f_0$ and $\chi$, without which $V_L$ can have infinite energy, and the diagonal choice of the
cutoff length), and the third whole-report audit found that the stability, parameter-selection and record-growth
arguments needed to establish exact-solution families are not supplied; the item now states them as provisional. That
audit also led to the repairs in Limitations 6 (no localization of the source in the swirl core), in the items of
Limitations 2 on near-axis cancellation (definitions of $D_t$ and $\Delta_5$), on the Lagrangian form of the swirl source
(the multiplicity) and on viscous cancellation (the energy class and the estimate of the loss), and to clarifications
in Section 5 (level scales), Section 9A, Result 5 and Limitations 1'; these repairs were audited once, and the two
further defects found (the packet geometry in the closure item and an overstatement in the numerical sentence below)
were repaired. The remark on the heat depth of the history in Limitations 6 and the item of Limitations 2 on
quantitative Liouville theorems and backward propagation were checked by hand and audited once; the audit confirmed the
scalings, the examples and the enstrophy bound of the second (and the statements of Lei and Zhang, Theorem 1.1, and of Lei,
Zhang and Zhao, Theorem 1.3, in the auditor's copies of the primary texts; only the abstracts were read here), and found a
quantifier error in the first, which had claimed divergence of the heat depth on every admissible normalization; it is
now restricted to normalizations with retained oscillation (the hand check had missed this error). The two
quantitative forms (i) and (ii) of the age argument added to that remark, the stationary half-space example there
and the heat-depth bound for the interval of Lemma 26A were proposed in a consultation, checked by hand (the
threshold on $K$ that removes the source term in (ii) was added here) and then audited. The audit confirmed the
scaling powers, the persistence constant, the source threshold, the stationary inflow example and the heat-depth
exponent; it required an explicit real even cutoff with $0\le\chi\le1$ in (i), and a correction in (ii): the
quarter-mass influx conclusion follows when the displayed dissipation bound fails, not merely when half-mass
persistence fails, and the vanishing of the persistence charge is conditional on persistence. Both repairs were
made, and a second audit found the repaired text correct. A numerical evaluation of the exact heat flow of the uncut profiles of the viscous-cancellation item
was consistent with its $O(k^{-1})$ estimate for the flat profile and with the limit for $F=\cos$: $k$ times the
flat-profile loss approached $336\pi^4\approx3.27\cdot10^4$, the sum of the changes $\frac92x_j^2$ of the values at the points
$\pi kr^2\in\pi\mathbb Z$ with interior weights $2$ and endpoint weights $1$, and the cosine loss approached $12\pi^2$. Identifying $336\pi^4$ as the
exact asymptotic constant would also need control of the shifted extrema, and the numerical evaluation is a
consistency check, not that proof.
The paragraph of Limitations 6 on two necessary conditions for the meridional mass $Q$ (the weighted identity for
$\Gamma$ with $k=3$, the bound of $S_Q$ by the weighted dissipation, and the $L^2$-in-time bound for $Q$ with its
recent-production consequence) was derived and checked by hand; the weighted identity for
$k=1,\frac52,3$, the Hardy constant and the bound of the production by the weighted dissipation were also checked by
numerical quadrature on one test field with a divergence-free drift (smooth in the meridional variables with the
vanishing at the axis used in the integrations, but without the parity of a smooth Cartesian field; a consistency check
of the integrations only). An audit confirmed the constants, the $L^2$-in-time bound and its consequence, and found
the two defects stated in that paragraph, which were repaired after that audit; the repaired paragraph was not the
subject of a further dedicated audit.
Item (iii) of that paragraph summarizes two second-model research answers; I rederived the $q$ identity and the
time-cutoff bound, the strong-swirl length bound, the functional swirl example, the witness-mass localization, the
comparison of the three times and the exponent arithmetic of the scalar bookkeeping by hand. One second-model audit of
item (iii) confirmed each estimate and the scalar arithmetic (the auditor reports exact rational arithmetic), with the
qualifications stated there: the consequence $Q/D^{1/2}\to0$ and the deadline of (ii) assume
$\|\omega_0^\theta\|_1<\infty$, the comparison of times gives a necessary duration for an absolute source budget and
says nothing about actual production, export or survival, and the bookkeeping is a scalar consistency test, not a
field. It asked that the dependence of $C_R$ on the oscillation constant be stated; this was done.
Item (iv) of that paragraph summarizes a third second-model research answer; I rederived the energy inequality for
$\eta^s$, the kernel bounds, the solid-torus bound and the exponent chain by hand. One second-model audit of item (iv)
confirmed the energy inequality, the kernel bound, the velocity bound (it supplied the near and far split now stated),
the solid-torus bound, both lower bounds and the exponents (the auditor reports exact rational arithmetic), as a necessary
condition and scalar compatibility only.
Proposition 14A (Section 6A) was obtained from a second-model research answer (re-zooming at a Lebesgue point of the
weak limit and its defect, and the one-sided local energy bound for the terminal slice) and rewritten here; I
rederived each step by hand. One second-model audit confirmed (a), (b) and (c) step by step, including the extension
of the tangent to positive rescaled times on which the energy limit at $\tau=0$ rests; it found that the paragraph after
the proof claimed more than was proved (that (HD) does not characterize (C*-D), and that the vorticity at the tangent is
carried in), and that paragraph was rewritten to state only what is proved; the fourth whole-report audit checked the
rewritten paragraph and Proposition 14A(a) to (c) again and found them correct.
Proposition 14B was derived by hand (part (b), and the extraction of (d) in a form that controlled only the azimuthal
part of the boost) and in a second-model research answer, which proved the Liouville statements of (b) independently
by the same maximum-principle arguments, pointed out that with the record mean subtracted the axis moves with the
horizontal part of the boost (now handled by the comparison with the axis-centred mean in (c)), and proposed the
unit-viscosity gradient-record extraction of (c) and the scalar rates of Remark (ii); I checked each step by hand. Part
(a) was first derived using the cited $\epsilon$-regularity theorem and interior derivative estimates without re-reading
their sources. I later read the statement and attributions of the restatement as Theorem 1.1 of Guevara and Phuc,
arXiv 1702.00449; I did not re-read the original regularity proofs or the interior derivative estimates, and the
quantitative regularity step was explicitly granted in that version of Proposition 14B(a); it has since been replaced by the proof at the gradient scale, which uses no epsilon-regularity theorem. Of the literature statements in Remark (iii),
I read Theorem 1.3 and Remark 1.4 of Lei, Zhang and Zhao (arXiv 1701.00868) and Section 3 of Zhang's survey (arXiv
2101.04905v2) in the original; the statements attributed to Koch, Nadirashvili, Seregin and Sverak and to Lei and Zhang
are as reported in the second-model research answers. The constructions in the first of these answers of nonconstant
bounded solutions of scalar transport-diffusion equations with smooth bounded divergence-free drifts are not used.
A second-model audit of the frozen text checked the BMO increment and pressure estimates, the Galilean drift
calculation, the applicability of the one-cylinder $L^3$-$L^{3/2}$ criterion (through that restatement), the two Liouville arguments, the
convergence of the increment pressure and both record extractions, and found that the limiting pressure gradient is
exactly $\Pi[v]$. It identified the constant-field case of (a), the curl-freeness of $\Pi[v]$ for unbounded members (now
part of the definition of the class), a sign in the vorticity equation, and qualifications concerning subsequences,
the constant horizontal boost, the uniformity of the decay of $\Gamma$ in Remark (iii) and the scope of the logarithmic
example; I checked these by hand and repaired the text. A later whole-report audit of the repaired text spot-checked parts
(a) to (d) again and found no failing step, subject to the regularity input granted in (a); it could not retrieve primary
sources. It also found two stale or undefined passages elsewhere (a remark after Theorem 17 that predated Proposition 22,
and an undefined symbol in the record-freezing item of Limitations 2) and two places where the granted input was not
carried into a summary; these were repaired by hand.

Proposition 14C was derived by hand from Lemma 2's argument, the dyadic comparison of ball means and the
Cauchy-Schwarz inequality; the first form in part (c) uses only $D\le M^2$ near $T_*$ (regime (II)), and its improved form the bound
of Proposition 14B(a), now proved. The constants were checked by hand, as were the
monotonicity step in (b), the logarithmic example of the Remark and the consistency with the scalar rates. A
second-model audit of a frozen text, in which (c) was still stated under the granted bound, found (a), (b), the conditional
(c), the Remark and the plateau radius correct, supplied the admissibility check for the plateau balls now noted in the
Remark, and independently derived the unconditional form of (c) from $D\le M^2$ with the same constants. It found one
overstatement (that excluding (FLAT$_0$) would require improving the logarithm), a misstated comparison with Lemma 2 in
(b), a missing range $1\le p<\infty$ in the citation of Lei, Zhang and Zhao, and an overly broad necessity claim in
Remark (iii) after Proposition 14B; I checked each by hand and repaired the text. That audit could not retrieve sources
and worked from the extracted texts of the two papers read in the original.

Proposition 14D and the divergence-free example in the Remark after Proposition 14C were first proposed in a second-model
research answer (route question on (FLAT$_0$)), which also identified the summability of the certified dissipation. I
rederived the moving-frame identity for $b'$, the three bounds, the enstrophy bound (including the boundary terms, which
the answer did not treat), the disjointness of the intervals, both cases in (c) and the passage from dyadic records to all
$t$, and the curl formula, the constant core and the bounds for the example, by hand. A second-model audit of a frozen text
(without second-model consultation inside the audit, and with web access) found (a) to (c), both Remarks' estimates and
the example correct, and pointed out that the divergence of $M^2(T_*-t)$, then presented as a consequence of (c), is
already Proposition 7(c); that the necessity sentence in Remark (ii) overstated (nonsummability is sufficient, not
necessary); that $\Theta_0R\ge1$ holds eventually under (FLAT$_0$); and that $b>0$ should be stated in the example. I
checked these by hand and repaired the text.

A later whole-report audit of a frozen text containing these repairs (without second-model consultation inside the
audit) compared every item of the Result and the Limitations with the Findings and spot-checked Propositions 14A to
14D and 22, finding no failing step (14B subject to its granted input) and no em or en dashes. It found one unsupported
inference in summaries of Proposition 14D: the lower bound for $M(t)^2(T_*-t)$ uses the flatness ratio of the next
dyadic record, not the current ratio $h(t)=M(t)/\Theta_0(t)$; monotonicity gives $h_{n+1}\le4h(t)$ but not the lower
comparison needed to replace the next-record ratio by the current one in this estimate. It
also asked for missing subsequence and hypothesis qualifiers in Result 4'' and Limitations 1'', amplitude and scope
qualifiers in two items of Limitations 2 and in Limitations 4, the exponent range in the pressure estimate of
Proposition 14B, a symbol clash ($\Gamma$ for the Newtonian kernel), corrections of three audit-status sentences in this
section, and the statement that the first missing implication is precise within the strategy of admissible
amplitude-$M$ isotropic normalizations. I checked each point against the text and made these repairs by hand; they
were not re-audited at that stage (the final consistency audits are described at the end of this section).

The Remark on the pressure impulse after Proposition 14D was proposed in a second-model research answer (route question
on (FLAT$_0$)). I rederived the impulse identity, the Nash decay, the choice of $\varsigma_n$, the lower bound $J_n\ge m_n/2$, the
antisymmetrized kernel formula, the bound of $|F|$ by $\|\nabla q\|_1$ with the cubic logarithm, the entropy, moment and
Gaussian bounds and the resulting upper bound by hand. A hostile second-model audit of that version found
the identity, the decay, the lower bound, the kernel formula and the comparison with Proposition 14C correct. It reported
gaps in the presentation: the flat-record quantifier, the construction of $q$, the cutoff justification of the identity and
the kernel substitution, the attribution of the identity, and above all the unwritten time-logarithm step of the upper bound.
It also asked that the impulse be stated as a signed quantity with the older contribution retained, and that the
comparison of $h_{n+1}$ with $h(t)$ be corrected as above. The Remark now writes these steps, following the audit's
reconstruction, which I checked by hand. A second hostile audit of the revised Remark found no false displayed
estimate and asked for three scope repairs: the record quantifier in Result 4''', the restriction of the comparison with
Proposition 14C(c) to regime (II), and a more precise closing sentence. I made these by hand; they were not
re-audited at that stage (the final consistency audits are described at the end of this section). The cited sources were not checked by me.

Three items of Limitations 2 were checked as follows: the normalization at records of $r|u^{mer}|$ (immediately before the
Holder-regularity item), and backward uniqueness for the limits of Proposition 14B(c) and the Gaussian frequency of $q$
(just after the BMO-class Liouville route). The first, with its
test on the two fields of the Remark after Proposition 14C, was worked out by hand. The other two come from a
second-model research answer (route question on (FLAT$_0$)); I rederived the single-time Biot-Savart step, the
frequency identity $dN/ds=-2\|Z\|^2/H+2\langle Z,\mathscr R\rangle/H$ with $Z=(A-N)F$, $A=-\Delta_\xi+\frac12\xi\cdot\nabla_\xi$,
$H=\|F\|^2_{L^2(\mu)}$ and $\mathscr R$ the transport and stretching remainder, the resulting inequality and the scalar-rate
exponents by hand. The exterior backward uniqueness and unique continuation theorems of Escauriaza, Seregin and Sverak
enter only that Limitations item, in the versions reported by the answer, which were not read. A hostile
second-model audit of a frozen text containing the three items (without second-model consultation inside the audit,
and without access to the sources) found the field estimates, the internal backward-uniqueness argument and the
frequency inequality correct. It found that the divergence of the weighted supremum does not give finite attained
records (the data class does not bound $\|r\,u^{mer}(0)\|_\infty$; it gave an explicit smooth finite-energy swirl-free
datum, which I checked) and that the bound for that rescaling holds only on the available time interval; it asked for
the definitions of the lift and of the Gaussian measure, for a description of the divergent transport term as a
divergent available bound, and for the consequence $\kappa_n\to\infty$ in the backward-uniqueness item. It also asked
for qualifier repairs: the conditional status of Proposition 14B in the opening paragraph, the label of Result 4',
the quantifier "every weak limit of an admissible amplitude-$M$ isotropic normalization" in Result 4'' and Limitations
1', the rigidity and scalar-rate wording in Result 4''', the quantifiers of Theorem 6 in Result 5, the description of
the pressure lower bound in Result 5' and after Lemma 15' (a lower bound for the majorizing increment integral, not for
the pressure gradient), the defect-energy summary in Result 5'', the scope of the dimensional scenario in Limitations
1', the constant of the tail bound in Limitations 1'' and two sentences at the opening of this section. It found the
status label and the statement of the first missing implication consistent with the Findings. I checked each point
against the text and made these repairs by hand. A second-model literature check of
post-2021 results on axisymmetric solutions found no theorem whose hypotheses follow from fixed data and which
excludes (FLAT$_0$) or yields a bounded nonconstant ancient limit; its statements were not re-read here, except that
the abstract of arXiv 2609.20762 v2 was read (later also its definition, main theorem, remark and the statement and outline of proof of
its key proposition, as recorded in Limitations 6) and the corresponding sentence of Limitations 6 was made precise. A
focused second-model re-audit of these repairs, of that sentence and of this section found them correct except for
three points (positivity and domain conditions in the rescaling at records of $r|u^{mer}|$, an unproved converse in
the sentence on that preprint, and a wrong item number in this paragraph), which I repaired as it proposed. It again found the status label and the first missing implication honest. A
last focused second-model audit found these three repairs correct and the status label and first missing implication
unchanged; for the Limitations 2 item on Holder regularity near the axis (added after the re-audit) it confirmed the
local estimate and the exponents but asked for the time, domain and history conditions and for the qualification that
the lower bound for the Euler length by $\tau\|u(t)\|_\infty$ needs the velocity bound on the whole space, and it found
one stale locator in this paragraph; I made both changes in the form it proposed, and these edits were not
audited again at that stage (the final consistency audits are described at the end of this section).

A hostile consistency audit of the summary (the text before the Method section) against the body by the second model found sixteen
points: omitted requirements that record sequences tend to $T_*$ and omitted ranges of $s_0$; an imprecise statement of the scale form of
Theorem 38; symbols used in the summary without a definition (now collected in the notation paragraph); a description of the selection rules
as computable that the body does not support (the summary now says explicit, and states that algorithmic computability with certified errors
is not claimed); an imprecise paraphrase of (L2LS); omitted parameters and hypotheses in the summaries of Propositions 14B, 14D, 22, 24, 36
and 39, Lemma 34, Corollary 35 and Theorem 37; an overbroad sentence on Type I bounds; a conflation of the cut-off ball and the meridional
disc in the summary of the toroidal branch; and notation collisions. All were checked against the body by hand. Fifteen were repaired as
proposed or in an equivalent form; of the notation collisions, the renamings were made except for the two uses of $A$, which are explained in
the notation paragraph, and the exponents $p$ and thresholds $N$, which are defined where they occur. A second audit of these repairs by the
same model confirmed the new statements, with explicit checks of the scale separation $\ell_nD_n/M_n\to\infty$ in (C), the identity
$2\pi Q_\rho=\int_{T_\rho}|\eta|$, the lower bound for $\kappa_n$ and the toroidal constants, and asked for three scope repairs (the uses of
$A$ and $A_n$, the range of $s_0$ in the summary of Corollary 35, and the regime (II) hypothesis in the summary definition of admissible
normalizations); these were made. A third audit by the same model checked these repairs, the sentence on the total meridional
vorticity mass in the opening paragraph (the inclusion of the record disc in the whole space, the scale invariance of the
mass and the places where the $L^1$ contraction is proved) and the literature sentences at the end of Limitations 7 (including
the transfer of the vorticity criterion to $[0,T_*)$ by scaling and time translation), and found all of them correct. The two
logarithmic criticality results cited at the end of Limitations 7 were read in their arXiv abstracts only, and the vorticity
criterion cited after them in the statement of the paper; none of their proofs was checked.

The Remark (a windowed source condition) after Theorem 32 and the corresponding clause in the opening paragraph were added last. Three
hostile audits by the second model, one for each of its three parts (the upper bound by the windowed source, the localized lower bound
from Proposition 30, and the work form), checked the selection of the restart time from the time-integrated bound of Lemma 9', the
restart of the homogeneous propagator at that time, the Chebyshev selection with the two constants, the coefficients of both lower
bounds, and the order of quantifiers in $n$ and $\lambda$, and found all of them correct. I checked the constants and the arithmetic by
hand. The paragraph of that Remark on the improvement of both selections along the sequence (the tails $\varepsilon_n$ and the shorter
windows $2\delta_n'$) was added after these three audits; a fourth audit by the same model found its estimates and the sharpened
opening clause correct and asked to separate the signed work, whose lower bound holds only on a selected subinterval of the shorter
window, from the inward work and to treat the case $\varepsilon_n=0$ separately; both changes were made. The scalar consistency example
and the remark on the uniform sharpness of $\delta_n$ at the end of that Remark were proposed by the second model, asked whether
the listed inequalities can force the windowed bound; I checked every inequality of the example by hand and the exponents by a
symbolic computation. A fifth audit by the same model, of the example, the sharpness remark and the paragraph on the shorter windows,
found the example and the sharpness remark correct within their stated scope and the case $\varepsilon_n=0$ correctly handled; it asked
to state that the window asymptotic of the example holds for every fixed $\lambda$ and for the selected $\lambda_n$, to write the exact
contraction identity of the example, and to say only that the listed inequalities do not imply the condition. It also found that a
parenthetical deriving the forms with $M_n^2/A_n$ from those with $M_n^2/D_n$ argued in the wrong direction; the forms are true, by
keeping the local mass bound of Proposition 30 before using $A_n\le\sqrt2D_n$. I checked each point by hand and made all four changes.
The sentence in item 1 of the Limitations on the windowed condition was added at the same time.
The Remark (the windowed conditions concern a thin cylinder about the axis) and the corresponding sentence in item 1 of the
Limitations were added after that. A hostile audit by the second model found the localization (a), the bounds and the sufficient
condition of (b) and the divergence in (c) correct, and asked to state that the mean radius bound controls the source only on the selected
subinterval (not on the whole window), that the divergence in (c) concerns the total and not the swirl dissipation, that the equivalences hold
up to the stated errors, and to choose the radius $\rho_n$ through $\lambda_n$ so that the case $\varepsilon_n=0$ is covered; all four changes were
made, and I checked each inequality by hand. A recheck of the entered text by the same model found these four points correctly
implemented and the bound on the vorticity mass away from the axis and the bound on the radius of the record point correct; it found that
the localization had been claimed for both sufficient conditions of the preceding Remark although it is proved only for the record mass and
the windowed source (not for the inward work), and that the sentence in the Limitations attached the mean radius bound to the source in the
cylinder, while it is proved for the source integrated over all space; both statements were corrected. The localization of the adjoint-weighted inward work was proved
afterwards, in part (c) of the Remark (compression at small radial Reynolds number): on the selected intervals $[s_n,t_n]$, the work outside
$\{r<cD_n/M_n^2\}$ is at most $(G\varepsilon_nE_0/(4c))M_n^2/D_n=o(M_n^2/D_n)$ for every fixed $c>0$. A mean radius bound for the source
restricted to the cylinder is not proved. A further hostile audit checked item (d) of that Remark: the mass inequality, the constants and the comparison with the inward work over all space are correct; four wording repairs (the citation of the identity in the proof of Lemma 40, a compactly supported cutoff, the boundary-layer statement restricted to the model weight, and the crude bound described as an upper bound only) were applied. Lemma 40A was derived by hand; its identity was also checked numerically at one instant, with the time derivatives given by the swirl equation and the adjoint equation, for explicit smooth test fields (a divergence-free meridional field from a Stokes stream function, a swirl field vanishing linearly at the axis and a weight vanishing quadratically there), where the two sides agree to about fourteen digits. A hostile audit by the second model confirmed the identity, the coefficients $1$ and $3$, the consequence with the constant $1/\sqrt2$, the reduction of the sufficient condition after Lemma 40 to three quantities and item (d) of the thin-cylinder Remark in the form given; it asked that the terminal datum be treated as a trace off the axis, that the cutoffs be removed in the order stated, and that the weighted Hardy counterexample be read as a statement about the completion of the square in Lemma 40 only. These repairs are in the text, and the relation between Lemmas 40 and 40A was checked by hand. The identity of the Remark (power weights) was derived by hand and checked numerically in the same way for $a=0,\frac12,1,\frac32,2$ (agreement to at least eleven digits). A hostile audit by the second model confirmed the identity with its coefficients and sign for $0\le a\le2$, the case $a=0$ with its constants, and the window selection; it supplied the interpolation inequality for $0<a\le1$ (checked here by hand, including the constant and the endpoint cases), and asked that the uniqueness of $a=1$ be stated for the factorization only, that a factor $2$ be restored in the elementary comparison, that the gradient bound on the window be justified (by the historical definition of $D$), and that the hypothesis be stated at the selected times, for the same adjoint weights, as an integral over all space. These repairs are in the text. The Remark (compression at small radial Reynolds number) was derived by hand, except that the reduction to the Reynolds-weighted work in (b), the bound $r(u^\theta)^2\le G^{3/2}D_n^{1/2}$ in (d) and the second weight in (e) were proposed by the second model in a research consultation and checked here by hand; the restriction of the rate in (d) by Leray's lower bound is our own. Two hostile audits by the second model confirmed the absorption inequality with its constant (optimal for the pointwise step), the coefficients $1$ and $3$ at $\theta=\frac12$, the inclusion of the compression set in $\{r>(\theta/(3D_n))^{1/2}\}$ (for fixed $\theta>0$), the elementary bound with the factor $\frac14$, the quadratic in (b), and all constants and exponents in (d) (including $\frac16$, $7$, $12$ and $-\frac13$); they showed by an explicit family of swirl fields that no weighted radial Hardy inequality under the stated properties of the weight improves the threshold, and asked that the size statements be phrased as comparisons of upper bounds, that the converse in (b) be restricted to a nonzero denominator, that the Feynman-Kac formula be stated as an unnormalized expectation with a survival indicator and the Khasminskii hypothesis as a bound uniform in the starting time and point along killed paths, and that the exponential bound be read as the divergence of an upper bound only. These repairs are in the text. The localization of the uncontrolled work to the shell $\{(6D_n)^{-1/2}<r<\rho_n^*\}$ in (c) was derived by hand; a further hostile audit confirmed the pointwise and integral bounds, the coefficient $3+K/c$ and the emptiness criterion, and asked that the degenerate cases $G=0$ and $\varepsilon_n=0$ be treated separately and that the conclusion be tied to the choice of $s_n$. These repairs are in the text. The necessary condition on the terminal swirl energy in item (d) of the Remark (the windowed conditions concern a thin cylinder about the axis) and the failure of Khasminskii's hypothesis in the steady strain in (e) of the Remark (compression at small radial Reynolds number) were first observed in the parallel line of work and were checked here by hand, the asymptotic constant and the threshold $\frac34$ also numerically. A consistency audit by the second model of all material added in this revision confirmed the identities, signs and constants and asked for corrections of statements around them: the decay of the barrier weight for the swirl core was claimed although the exponential factor is not controlled (now stated as requiring an additional estimate); the conditions of the Remark (power weights) were said to be unrelated although Hölder's inequality orders them (the ordering, with its constant, was checked here by hand); the endpoint $a=2$ was misstated; a localization of the inward work was said to be missing although it is proved later; two conditions were described as equivalent where only an implication holds; the fixed initial time, the historical records, the meaning of one symbol, the observation point of the cutoff-strain example and the normalization of a source bound were made explicit; and bare references to item (d) were made unambiguous. These repairs are in the text. The Remark (the memory part without transport) was added last. Its bound was first proved by hand with the constant $5$ in place of $\frac3{16}$ (the same bilinear splitting, without the zero-mean step and with a cruder estimate of the four-dimensional integral); the second model checked that proof step by step and supplied the two sharpenings (superharmonicity of $|x-y|^{-1}$ on $\mathbb R^4$ and the zero-mean step), which I checked by hand together with the constants. A referee pass on the entered text by the same model found the normalizations, kernels, the four-dimensional integrals and the constant $\frac3{16}$ correct, and asked to state the model class with joint smoothness and a common compact support in time, to say that only the nonnegative majorants are extended in time, to restrict "every swirl history" to the model class, and to replace a statement about what is known for the transported kernel by a statement of what the proof would require; these repairs are in the text, together with the precise route from the source bound to GOAL through the splitting of the Remark (a windowed source condition) and Lemma 31. A second pass by the same model found the repaired text correct. The passage of that Remark on transport by a linear strain with arbitrary time dependence (constant $\frac38$) was proved by the second model in a separate consultation and checked here by hand step by step (the kernel with its Jacobian factor, the majorant, the two scaling identities and the two time changes); a referee pass on the entered text found it correct and asked to make explicit which constant the conditional consequence in the Limitations uses; this is in the text. The paragraph after it (a general steady drift without a time-uniform absolute bound; a lower bound of order $\log\log T$ with negative work) was proved by the second model in a research consultation and checked here step by step: the divergence-free form of the drift, the generator and the barrier identity by symbolic differentiation; the barrier inequalities and boundary values, the survival, moment and Cauchy-Schwarz steps, the reflected kernel bound, the work sign and the final constant by hand (the constant also in exact rational arithmetic); and the global Lipschitz bound analytically, with a numerical cross-check. A referee pass on the entered text by the same model found the computations correct and asked for the density equation with its source, explicit justifications of the barrier inequalities and of the probabilistic step, two changes of notation, and a narrower statement of scope (absolute rather than signed work, a lower bound only, infinite kinetic energy of the drift, independence of the prescribed swirl); these repairs are in the text. The paragraph after it (the positive part of the memory work, a lower bound of order $\log N/\log\log N$ along a family of drifts) was proved by the second model in a further research consultation and checked here step by step: the cone generator identities, the barrier bracket, the forward equation and the identities for the swirl energy and for $q^2$ by symbolic differentiation; the bracket inequality also by sampling on a grid; and the kernel formula, its sign and bounds, the drift-independent Nash bound, the routing estimate, the survival and Cauchy-Schwarz steps, the assembly of the error terms, the choice of horizons and the rescaling by hand. A referee pass on the entered text by the same model found it correct and asked for the realization of the two semigroups at the axis (now by exhaustion with Dirichlet domains), one exponential rate common to the routing and cone-exit errors before the horizons are chosen, explicit constants in the kernel and cone-exit bounds, the quantifiers of the rescaled statement, a change of notation and a narrower closing statement; these repairs, each checked here, are in the text. The two bounds after it, for globally inflowing and for globally outflowing drifts, were developed in a parallel line of work on this problem and refereed there by the second model (whose referee also supplied the uniform form for inflowing drifts); I checked both here step by step: the half-line integral and the kernel bound $\mathcal K\le\frac12DG\min\{1,\ell/\rho\}$ with its two pieces, the potential $\mathcal H$ and its value at $a$, the bound $b^r\mathcal H'\le\frac12k$ under weak outflow, the Itô step, the comparison with the dimension-zero Bessel process and its survival probability, the propagation of $|q|\le D$ and the time integral. A referee pass by the second model on the paragraph stating them confirmed the constants and asked for the hypotheses on the drift to be stated explicitly, for the regularity of $\mathcal H$ at the axis, for a supermartingale proof of the propagation of $|\Gamma|\le Dr^2$ in place of a maximum principle, for the support condition on the source to be kept in the summary (with an example, checked here to leading order, showing that it cannot be dropped) and for a corrected description of the comparison in (ii); it also observed that a finite-energy drift with either global sign vanishes, which I checked and added. The bound for arbitrary drifts with the outflow charge, from the same parallel work, was checked here: the identity $\mathcal H'=\rho k/(2\theta)$ on both pieces and the stopped Itô step with an integrand of both signs. The bound in terms of the initial circulation mass after them was proved by the second model in a research consultation and checked here step by step: the two energy identities with their axis terms, the mass identities, the Nash step with duality, the bound $r^2f\le D\|\Gamma\|_\infty$ and the two time integrals. A referee pass by the second model on that paragraph confirmed the constants and both time integrals and asked for the time reversal in the duality step for time-dependent drifts, for the threshold of improvement obtained from the time integral up to $T$ (which is $T>t_*$, not $T>3t_*$), for the word meridional in the finite-energy remark (an axisymmetric field with only an angular component satisfies both sign conditions) and for the remark that finite energy does not give $\Gamma\in L^1$; these repairs were checked and made. A further referee pass on the summary of these bounds in the Result section and on the bound with the outflow charge confirmed the identity for $\mathcal H'$, the truncated time integral and its threshold, and asked for the stopped identity to be split into nonnegative parts before the limit, for the exact hypotheses in the summary (the cap on the weight, the support of the source, the weak-outflow condition, the hypotheses of the transport) and for the summary to include the outflow charge and the circulation-mass bound; these repairs were made. The statement on steady circulations and the streamline identity after the circulation-mass bound were obtained in a research consultation with the second model and checked here step by step: the polar form of the generator, the equation for $p$, the bound on the angular exit time, the martingale step, the residual of the steady equation for the perturbed Burgers configuration and the identity for $F$ along streamlines. A referee pass on that paragraph and on the repaired summary confirmed the steady statement (with the remark that the polar angle has a limit at its exit time) and asked for the description of the perturbed Burgers configuration to be corrected (its drift differs from the Burgers drift also by an axial shear above the perturbed set, since the radial integral of the stream function of that shear is, to first order, $-4$ times the integral of the perturbation of the exponent, which is nonzero, and it is not a steady Navier-Stokes solution), for the streamline identity to be stated for the time integral along trajectories and for several hypotheses of the summary to be stated in it; I checked the shear identity and the failure of the meridional equation in the shear region and made these repairs. A consistency referee pass of the second model over the whole Remark (the memory part without transport), item 8 of the Result and the Limitations sentences found no mathematical error and asked for scope and notation repairs: the choice of the starting time of the record windows in the route to GOAL, the model class and uniformity quantifiers in the summaries, the support restriction behind the numerical constants and the weighted form of the outflow-charge bound for the Navier-Stokes drift, the definition of the stopping times and of the strain factor, two cross-references, the signs for which the linear strain lies in the two classes, a convention on local notation, the observation that a bounded steady circulation with finite circulation mass vanishes for every drift considered (from the drift-independent smoothing bound), and an explicit construction of the perturbed Burgers configuration; I checked these points (for the construction, the reduction of the steady swirl equation to $\partial_zS=J$, the sign of $b^r$, the expansion of the shear integral and the residual of the meridional equation above the perturbed set) and made the repairs. The decomposition of the produced vorticity for the streamline source with diffusion was checked by hand. A follow-up referee pass of the second model over these repairs confirmed the perturbed Burgers construction (including the exact form of the integral of the shear), the vanishing of bounded steady circulations of finite mass and the diffusion identity, and asked for precision repairs: the hypotheses of the vanishing statement, the regularity of the lifted function in the diffusion identity, the distinction between the steady source and the vorticity it produces, the two thresholds in the comparison of the circulation-mass bound with the trivial bound, the referents of a summary sentence, a quantifier, and the measure convention; I checked each and made them. The truncation of the two examples to compactly supported drifts was obtained in a further second-model consultation; I checked it step by step (the curl form of the truncation and its velocity, Lipschitz and energy bounds, the coupling of the two diffusions up to the exit from the ball and the resulting bound for the change of the work, the generator identity for $r^2+z^2$ and the two moment bounds, the choice of radii, the preservation of parity and hence of the sign in the first example, and the comparability of the Lipschitz constants in the second) before entering it. A hostile referee pass of the second model over the entered text confirmed these steps and the constants, and pointed out that the parabolic rescaling turns the truncated families into families of energy one, so that a coefficient depending only on the energy is excluded as well; it also asked for the meridional form of the drift, the condition that the ball contain the source, an explicit exponential radius and the notation for the kernel. I checked the scaling of the energy and the elementary lower bound for the scale-invariant energy at the records, and made these repairs. A second referee pass over the repaired text confirmed the general moment bound with its stopping argument, the stream function of the routed drift, the lower bounds for the energies and the record bound, and asked for the excluded inequalities to be stated explicitly, for the finite-energy clause in the list of properties to be reworded (the truncated examples have finite energy) and for the work of a general source to be defined; these repairs were made.

Final consistency audits. After all the edits described above, the second model audited, as a hostile referee and against the body, the Overview, the Result section, the Limitations section and the Method and Verification sections, with instructions to compare every stated result with its proof (hypotheses, quantifiers, constants and directions of inequalities), to flag any claim stronger than what is proved, and to flag undefined symbols. It reported no mathematical defect in the items of the Overview and the Result section. It asked for the following repairs, each checked here against the body before it was made: in the Overview, the sufficient one-sided condition of Corollary 6A(b) is now stated with its measure and normalization, and a task label was replaced by a reference to the goal; in Limitations 4, the statement that no local energy bound is used now excepts the localized energy inequality in the proof of Proposition 14A(a), and the remark on the constant flow is restricted to estimates from the local gradient alone; in this section, the audit status of parts (e) and (f) and of Corollary 52(d) is corrected, and a locator in the Remark (the memory part without transport) is made precise. Item (e) of the Remark (radial transport required by the dual-weighted quartic) and the passages of the Overview and of Limitations 1' that depend on it were refereed twice; the second referee confirmed the derivation, including the constants $6561$ and $\frac{1024}{243}$, and asked for a rewording of the How to resume paragraph, which was made in the form it proposed. Sections 11 and 12 were then audited in the same way, for defects between passages: citations against the cited statements, stale statements, notation and overclaims. The audits identified notation and scope defects, including the incorrect claim that the pointwise-source argument certifies only the displayed window lengths. The repairs, each checked here against the body, were: a definition of the $L^2$ mean oscillation in the proofs of Proposition 36 and Theorem 37 (the oscillation of Lemma 2 is pointwise); the scope of the averaged window condition in the Remark (compression at small radial Reynolds number), which yields (L2M) and not (VL1); local declarations where the letters $q$, $v$ and $H$ carry the meaning of Lemma 40A or the octave index of Proposition 51 instead of the meaning fixed at the start of Section 12, and the endpoint $t^{(N)}$ in Theorem 49(c); and, after Corollary 56, the length $\min\{t,\lambda_1\}$ of the backward window and the statement that the pointwise source bound, with the coarser estimate of its time integral, certifies windows up to the displayed length, which replaces a claim that it certifies only those. Item (f) of the Remark (radial transport required by the dual-weighted quartic) was added after these audits. Its algebra (the minimum of the quadratic form, the maximum of $f$, the sign of the form outside $[-2p,-2]$) was checked by a symbolic computation and, independently, by hand. A referee then confirmed this algebra and the lower bounds of item (f), and found its original exclusion of upper bounds obtained by discarding a term of fixed sign too broad: for $\beta>0$, completing the square yields such an upper bound whenever the weighted integration is justified. That exclusion and the corresponding parenthesis in How to resume were rewritten in the form the referee proposed, the upper bound was added, and the completed square and the minimum of its constant were checked by a symbolic computation. A second referee confirmed the rewritten passages, with the integration for $\beta>0$ left as a stated condition. Finally the Setting and Sections 1 to 10 were audited in the same way. The repairs, each checked here against the body, were: a separate letter for an angular scalar in the proof of Lemma 3; the reason the energy certificate fails under (UP) (Lemma 2 and the definition of (UP), not Proposition 7); in the Remark (rules in (B)), item (iii) now states only that comparability of local first radii at the prescribed levels $\frac1{32}$ and $\frac14$ can fail (for its example the localized condition holds at the smaller upper level $\frac1{32}$), replacing a claim that (L2LS) cannot be localized; in the Remark on the scope of Proposition 14B, the conclusion drawn from scalar rates; at the end of Section 7, a claim of dynamical self-consistency of (UP) replaced by the absence of a contradiction; a named pressure bound in Lemma 15'; a pointer to Proposition 22 at the start of Section 9A; and, in Section 9B and in the Result section, a claim that critical norms cannot address (FLAT), now restricted to compatibility at a single time. A re-check of these repairs confirmed them and found passages in the Result and Limitations sections and at the start of Section 9A that repeated two of the repaired statements (the reason the energy certificate fails, the scope of the Liouville case of Proposition 14B) or described the end-time shift of Theorem 12 imprecisely; they were repaired in the same way. After these audits, item (g) of the Remark (radial transport required by the dual-weighted quartic) was added (an explicit family showing that the mean in item (b) has no universal upper bound and that the datum term there is needed); its potential identity, response kernel and constants were checked by a symbolic computation and by hand. A hostile referee re-derived every step (the Leray projection, the energy and circulation constants, the viscosity-independent lifespan behind the scaling, the uniform convergence of the dual, the limits and the kernel bound) and found no error; on its advice the cutoff is required to vanish near the axis, the claim is stated for a fixed cutoff and constants independent of $t$, the remark that small $a$ makes (b) fail without $V(s_0)$ was added, and the scope sentence now says that no second-case crossing is established. Item (h) (the Burgers vortex, showing that a fixed circulation bound and a vanishing datum term do not bound the mean when the meridional velocity contains a harmonic strain) was added next; the transformation of the dual equation and the steady swirl equation were checked by a symbolic computation, the constants by hand; a Monte Carlo evaluation of the representation (with the indicator of the inner torus as cutoff and twelve units of backward time, where $V(s_0)/Q_\psi$ is negligible) gave means between $0.9$ and $146$ for $\lambda$ from $\frac14$ to $16$, above $\frac49$ and consistent with (b). A hostile referee re-derived every step and constant and found no error; on its advice $G_0>0$ is fixed, the scaling statement is made explicit, the numerical parenthesis was moved out of the item, and the last sentence was narrowed. Item (i) (finite energy and a fixed circulation bound) was added last; its existence input was checked against the statement of the cited desingularization theorem (bounded head law, common support, fixed speed), and the axis formulas for Hill's vortex, the scaling, the change of frame, the transformed dual equation and the circulation bound by hand. A first hostile referee found the construction correct modulo routine details; these (the quadratic rate of the meridional correction, by the reflection symmetry and an energy estimate, and the convergence of the weighted integrals in the iterated limit, by fourth moments, the Feynman-Kac formula and stopped coupling) were then written out and checked by hand, including the generator bound for $1+|Y|^4$, the stationarity of the Burgers profile, the reduction of the limiting dual to that of (h) and the barriers for the dual and the circulation, and a second hostile referee found them correct, asking only that the bound $0\le\rho\le1$, the restriction to the chosen interval, the normalization of the ring bounds and the size of $R$ for given $T$ be stated, which was done. An independent read of the outlined version, made before the details were written out, found no defect; its one precision remark (the growth of the integrand of $V(s_0)$) is met by taking the integrands against the five-dimensional measure, as in the item. After items (e) to (i) were complete, a further hostile consistency audit by the second model compared them with the summaries in How to resume (a), in this paragraph and in the Overview (hypotheses, directions of inequalities, constants, status words and item letters) and found no defect. The clause of item 2 of the Limitations on the signed comparison with the swirl-free evolution of the same meridional datum was checked by hand (the difference equation, the cancellations in the pairing with the adjoint, the backward energy estimate, the pointwise bounds and the constant of the response bound) and by a hostile referee of the second model, who found no error in the equations or constants and asked for four precision repairs (the class of the terminal field, the description of $K$, the regime (II) qualification of the record test and a narrower statement of what is not established); all four were applied. The closing sentences of item (h) of the Remark on the radial transport required by the dual-weighted quartic (in (g) and (h) the source $P_\psi$ stays bounded or vanishes) and the matching clause of How to resume (a) were checked by hand (the $z$-independence of $q$ in (h), the axis bounds for $q^{(n)}_v$ and its axial derivative, the conservation of the dual mass, and $\partial_z(h^2)=-105z\,d^{-9/2}\ne0$ on the support of $\chi$) and by a hostile referee of the second model, who found the calculations correct and asked for narrower conclusions (bounded source in (g) only for fixed $a$; in (h) only the ratio $W_4/Q_\psi$ is unbounded; no statement about the crossing cutoffs of (a)); these were applied. The sentences of item (i) of that Remark on the source in the ring family ($\delta^{-2}P_\psi\to0$ in the iterated limit) were checked by hand (the scaling of $D^2\beta_R$, the axis formula for $a$, the global bounds for $h$ and $\nabla h$, the equation for $\partial_zh$ obtained through incompressibility, the Feynman-Kac bound and the moment bound) and by a hostile referee of the second model, who found no substantive defect and asked for an explicit smallness restriction on $\delta$ and a precise description of the limit of $\delta^{-4}Q_\psi$; both were applied. In the same clause of Limitations item 2, the component formula for $(\nabla b^0)^T\mathfrak a$ and the statement that the gradients are taken in $\mathbb R^3$ were added after an outside reading by a second reader of the same kind as the author, who re-derived the clause and found no defect; the sentence on $L^1$ estimates was proved by hand (the continuity of the Fourier transform of integrable fields, the mean of the ring-averaged test field and the Duhamel formula for the example), following a second-model answer obtained by that reader, and was checked by a hostile referee of the second model, who confirmed the argument and asked for the hypothesis $w\ne0$ and an explicit restriction of the example to the linear system; both were applied. The energy bound for the axial derivative $\partial_z\Psi_k$ in the Remark on the shell-tested form of Corollary 58 (and its $L^4$ consequence) was derived by hand, following a second-model answer obtained by the same second reader, who also re-derived it, and was checked by a hostile referee of the second model, who confirmed every step and the constants and asked only that the dependence on the kernel norms be named; this was applied.
